# Appendix: every finding, release 1.22
One line per finding, grouped by plugin and then by how serious it is. `file:line` is where to look. Each finding was written by one reviewer and then checked by a second one who was asked to disprove it; findings the second reviewer disproved are not in this list.
Totals: 561 findings, 22 serious, 204 medium, 335 minor.

## Hazard Helper (10: 0 serious, 6 medium, 4 minor)

### Medium
- **Five ways to start the same pack build; the Hazard main-window button is the one to drop** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/data_tab.py:292`  
  build() in build_nd_pack.py is started from: (1) Hazard main window, Data & Output tab, group "7. Data source - offline pack or download", button "Build offline pack..." (data_tab.py:292-298, handler _on_build_pack l.431-488): a plain build, no detection of stale layers or newly chosen servers, no DWR/HIFLD folder, and a sources_file argument build() ignores (U2-B-5); (2) the "Suite data and servers" window button, labelled "Build / update the offline pack..." in Hazard but "Update data..." in the Runner and Screening copies (suite_data.py:227 in each), which lists the missing, old and incomplete layers first (l.688-751); (3) Processing tool ndhazard:buildpack (chain_algorithms.py:242) for h  
  What you see: Two buttons in the same Hazard plugin start the same hour-long download with different confirmation texts and different behaviour; a user who presses the Data & Output one never sees which layers were stale or the layer-files folder. In the Runner and Screening the same button has a different name ("Update data...").  
  Fix: Delete the Data & Output button and _on_build_pack (data_tab.py:292-298 and 431-488: about 65 lines, two message boxes); give the suite-data button the same text in the three plugins; keep ndhazard:buildpack for batches and the Updater as the scheduled route; drop the docstring lines 53-54. Saves one button, one handler, one confirmation dialog and one of two names for the same action.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-3.
- **The Hazard suite's own test double hides GDAL, so the four 'needs GDAL' tests fail instead of being skipped** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/fake_qgis.py:115`  
  install() puts a stand-in 'osgeo' package into the running program when real GDAL does not load. Every GDAL guard in the Hazard suite then asks the same question the stand-in answers yes to, so the guard never fires and the test runs against stand-ins that create no files and return empty answers. I ran `python3 ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/run_tests.py` on a machine with no working GDAL: the result is '78 tests, 5 failed', four of them from this (osm_layer_files_go_into_the_pack_instead_of_overpass fails with FileNotFoundError on a GeoPackage that was never written, the_3dhp_national_file_is_cut_to_the_pack_box with "'Fake' object is not subscriptable", packfiles_write_a_layer_file_into_the_p  
  What you see: The command the plugin's own metadata.txt:114 advertises as needing no QGIS reports five failures that are not defects, so the one report Nick or a reviewer would look at to see whether the suite is healthy is red for the wrong reason, and a real regression hiding among those five would never be noticed.  
  Fix: Have the guards ask the stand-in, not the module name: in fake_qgis.install() set a flag such as FAKE_OSGEO = True when the stand-ins go in, and make _have_gdal()/the real-QGIS guard return False when it is set (or have them call a real function, e.g. `ogr.GetDriverByName("GPKG") is not None` and `osr.SpatialReference().ImportFromEPSG(4326) == 0`, which a stand-in cannot satisfy). Then those four   
  Effort: small. Id T1-TV-1.
- **Most of the Hazard plugin's result-making code has no test at all; the same holds for the Updater's own modules and a few Runner cores** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/run_tests.py:1`  
  Every Hazard test file is a release-note test (test_v22xx / test_v2300 / test_suite116): they cover what changed in 2.28-2.30 and the shared damsuite. Not one test imports consequences.py, checks.py, crosscheck.py, damage_curves.py, evacuation.py, ensemble.py, extent.py, facilities.py, linear.py, popsource.py, scenarios.py, sensitivity.py, series.py, termination.py, temporal.py, webexport.py, mapbook.py, rasimport.py, reproduce.py, review_exchange.py, reviewpkg.py, quickcreep.py, watch.py, classify_tab.py, dvsheet.py, formfill.py or submittal.py (14,417 lines in 17 of them); pipeline.py (3,262 lines) is touched only by test_v2280.py:183 (`_flood_cut_off_step`), classification.py only by test  
  What you see: The number Nick reads (the ND class, the consequence counts, the evacuation and sensitivity sheets) comes from code that only the GUI exercises; a change there is caught by nobody until a dam comes out wrong, which is exactly what the Absaraka comparison in 1.22 found by hand.  
  Fix: Do not try to test everything; add one golden-result test per plugin that runs the classification on a small synthetic dam folder (the Runner's tests/synthetic.py valley plus a few structures) and pins the ND class, the counts and the summary lines, so a change anywhere in pipeline/classification/consequences/structures shows up; then one import-and-smoke test per untested module list above.  
  Effort: large. Id T1-T-3.
- **Release numbers of all four plugins are pinned as literals in the Hazard test suite (and one own-version literal in each other suite)** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_suite116.py:29`  
  The Hazard suite hard-codes the release number of every plugin and compares it to damsuite/versions.py RELEASE. A bump of ANY plugin (even a Runner-only 3.20.4) fails this Hazard test until the literal is edited, and the same literal is used in 7 more asserts of the file (lines 76, 142-143, 156, 170, 178, 193-194, 211). Each other suite pins its own number too: nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3200.py:906 `assert md == "3.20.3" == wf.VERSION`, :909 `changelog.strip().startswith("3.20.3 ")`; nd_dam_screening/tests/test_window_028.py:201 `assert ver == "0.28.3"`; nd_data_updater/tests/test_qt6.py:52 `assert g["version"].strip() == "1.4.3"`. The repo already has the right pattern (test_v2280.py:22-  
  What you see: Every release needs five test edits in four folders before the suites go green; a forgotten one makes the suite report a failure that is not a defect, which teaches whoever runs the tests to ignore red results.  
  Fix: In test_suite116.py build RELEASE from each sibling's metadata.txt when present (as test_v3140.py:140-151 does) or simply from VS.RELEASE, and assert the structure (four keys, X.Y.Z strings) instead of the values; replace the three own-version literals with `== metadata version` only (the equality to wf.VERSION / dri.plugin_version() already proves the chain).  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-1.
- **Tests that cannot run print ok: skips are counted as passes in all four suites** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_v2300.py:513`  
  The harnesses (Hazard run_tests.py:42-49, Runner run_tests.py:1586-1593, Updater run_tests.py:38-46) know only ok and FAIL. A test that returns early because QGIS, GDAL, bash, a sibling plugin or a data file is missing is printed `ok` and counted in "78 tests, 0 failed". Hazard: test_v2300.py:501-502 and :513-514 (no real QGIS), test_osm_layers.py:92-93 and :123-136 (no GDAL / no PyQGIS), test_v2290.py:358-362 and :396-399 (no GDAL). Runner: test_cli.py:691-693, 720-722, 764-766, 825-827, 928-930, 997-999 (no bash), test_hecras_model.py:235, 327, 371, 424, 444, 462, 490 (no GDAL). Screening (exit 0 and a printed OK): test_inventory.py:32-33, test_dam_lines.py:10-11, test_chain.py:13-14, test  
  What you see: A machine without GDAL or without the sibling plugins reports every suite green while the pack writer, the window, the hand-off parameter names and the chart-file identity were never checked; the known Hazard failure `the_window_opens...` only shows because the brief's run used a real QGIS.  
  Fix: Add a `class Skip(Exception)` to each registry and let the harness print `skip <name>: <why>` and a `N tests, F failed, S skipped` line; replace the `print(...); return` pairs with `raise Skip(why)`; for Screening let the new runner (T1-T-2) treat a printed `SKIP` line the same way.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-4.
- **The Hazard menu test says it needs real Qt but never checks, so it always fails without QGIS** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_v2301.py:19`  
  Unlike its neighbours in test_v2300.py, this test has no `if MODE != "real": return` and does not even call fake_qgis.install(). Without a real PyQGIS the QMenu it builds is a stand-in whose actions() list is empty, so texts is [] and the test fails every time. My run of the documented command gives: FAIL the_suite_menu_holds_each_shared_entry_once, AssertionError: []. This failure is not in the brief's list of known failing tests, so it has simply been living in the suite.  
  What you see: The suite can never reach '0 failed' in the mode the plugin documents, which is the fastest way to teach whoever runs it that a red line means nothing; the duplicate-menu bug this test was written for (nine suite menu entries instead of three) would also go unnoticed if it came back.  
  Fix: Add the two lines its neighbours use at the top of the test: `MODE = fake_qgis.install()` at module level and `if MODE != "real": return` as the first line of the test, so it runs under a real QGIS and skips (or, with T1-T-4 done, reports 'skip') otherwise.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-TV-2.

### Minor
- **Three 11-line compat shims keep the pre-2.17 import paths alive; 12 import sites still use them** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/offline_pack.py:4`  
  ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/offline_pack.py, ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/suitegpkg.py and nd_data_updater/ndpack/offline_pack.py (identical) copy another module's namespace so old imports keep working. Five releases later the old path is still used inside the same plugin: estimate.py:293 and 320, suite_algorithms.py:87, 89 and 364, data_tab.py:395, runnerculverts.py:104 and 1118, suiteexport.py:86 and 88, download.py:637, checks.py:183, build_nd_pack.py:82 (both copies). Nothing outside these plugins imports them.  
  What you see: None for the engineer. For whoever maintains the code, `from .offline_pack import describe` leads to a file that is not where the code is, and the star-copy hides which module a name really comes from.  
  Fix: Change the 12 import lines to `.damsuite.offline_pack` / `.damsuite.suitegpkg` (build_nd_pack.py already has the `from .damsuite ...` form at line 81) and delete the three shim files: 33 lines, three modules, one indirection.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-10.
- **Two test lines end in 'or True', so they can never fail** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_osm_layers.py:153`  
  A check written as 'A or True' is true whatever A says, so both lines pass even when the thing they describe is broken. The first one is the only check in the suite for the rule in the comment above it (that an unrelated layer must not be taken for a mobile-home park or a place layer, the mix-up that would put the wrong houses in a population count); the second is the only check that the Screening engine setting keeps its starting value. Both read, to anyone scanning the file, as covered.  
  What you see: Two rules that look tested are not: the layer-role rule protects which houses are counted behind a dam, and nobody reading the suite would know the check is switched off.  
  Fix: Delete the trailing ' or True' from both lines, run them, and fix or remove whichever then fails for a real reason.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-TV-5.
- **test_suite116 (the packdiff test file) copies the RELEASE table literally and will fail at every release bump** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_suite116.py:29`  
  The file imports versions as VS yet re-declares the release table (lines 29-30), asserts equality with it (line 70) and asserts a literal version in a message (line 170). The useful check, that each plugin's metadata.txt agrees with versions.RELEASE, already exists in nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3140.py:135-151 and test_v3200.py:906-907 without a literal copy. The local RELEASE is then passed to SC.check / step_rows in eight more places where VS.RELEASE would serve.  
  What you see: None for the engineer. Every release needs a hand edit of this test or it goes red; a red test that is 'expected' hides a real failure in the same file (the only tests of packdiff, cascade, loss and priority).  
  Fix: Delete lines 29-30, use VS.RELEASE where RELEASE is passed (lines 76, 142, 143, 156, 178, 193, 194, 211), and at line 170 assert `VS.RELEASE["ndhazard"] in row["text"]`. Saves 2 lines and a per-release chore.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-12.
- **About 90 temporary folders are left behind by one run of the four suites, one of them outside the temp directory** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_v2300.py:73`  
  mkdtemp() without rmtree: Hazard 21 (test_v2300.py lines 73, 219, 245, 282, 297, 423, 453, 484, 530; test_v2290.py 104, 127, 340, 363, 400, 471; test_suite116.py 50, 51, 241; test_osm_layers.py 94, 138; test_v2281.py 45), Runner 8 (test_v3203.py 22, 43, 54, 61, 67, 79, 110; test_nd_hazard.py 132), Updater 16 (test_runreport.py:38 `_pack()` called 16 times), Screening about 40 module-level folders (`work = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=...)` in test_popup.py:33, test_window_028.py:35, test_v0280.py:47, test_handoff.py:21, test_runchain.py:22 ... plus the 11 fake HOME folders at test_popup.py:13 etc.). test_v3202.py:34 also calls `A.download(..., "/tmp/anuga_home_dl/mm", ...)`, and anuga_setup.downl  
  What you see: On a machine where the suites are run often the temp folder fills with nddam_*/popup_*/v3203_* folders holding small GeoPackages and rasters; on Windows a stray C:\tmp appears.  
  Fix: Use the existing Tmp context manager (or `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` as the Updater's other tests do) in the listed tests; for the module-level Screening folders register `atexit.register(shutil.rmtree, work, ignore_errors=True)` right after mkdtemp; point test_v3202 at a mkdtemp path.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-5.

## Breach Runner (224: 8 serious, 85 medium, 131 minor)

### Serious
- **Breach start point always jumps to the midpoint of the nearest crest line (any dam's), bypassing the DWR match and the 500 m cap** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1032`  
  BREACH_POSITION is a required number with default 0.5 (line 491), so from the Processing dialog it is never empty. The branch at 1022 (`parameters.get("BREACH_POSITION") in (None, "") and dist > 1.0`, which moves the dam point onto the line picked by DWR structure number or the nearest within CREST_SNAP_M = 500 m) can never run from the dialog. Instead the branch at 1032 always runs: it takes every line within `radius` (0.75 x MAX_EXTENT_MI = 45 mi by default, line 870), picks the one with the nearest VERTEX (`_point_along_nearest`, line 112) and puts the breach at 50 % of its length. The DWR id match, the 500 m limit and the picked line `best` are all ignored for the breach location. CREST_  
  What you see: A dam whose own DWR line is missing, or whose inventory point sits nearer another dam's line vertex, gets its breach placed at the midpoint of ANOTHER dam's crest, possibly kilometres away, while the log says only 'Breach starts at 50% along the crest line'. Even for a dam with its own line, the point the engineer placed on the crest is silently replaced by the line's midpoint. The flood then starts in the wrong place: wrong depths, arrival times and receptors.  
  Fix: Make BREACH_POSITION optional with no default (empty = move the dam point onto the picked line, as line 1022 intends), and when a position is given measure it along `best` (the line chosen by _pick_crest_line, with its DWR match and 500 m cap) instead of the nearest-vertex line over all lines in the 45 mi box. Say in the log which line and why.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-2.
- **The breach is moved onto a crest line the code has just rejected as not this dam's** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1032`  
  _pick_crest_line refuses a line more than CREST_SNAP_M (500 m) from the dam point, and refuses it precisely because it is not this dam's crest. The block that follows ignores that decision: it re-reads the same layer and puts the breach half way along the nearest line with no distance limit at all, and the search box is 0.75 x 'Largest domain side' (up to 45 miles by default). The same block also overrides the DWR structure-number choice, so the crest that is raised can be one line while the breach starts on another. 'Breach position' is declared with a default of 0.5 and is not optional (algorithms.py:491), so this branch runs in every dialog run, and the matching 'the dam point is moved on  
  What you see: For a dam whose own crest line is missing from the DWR dam lines layer, the breach is started on another dam's crest line miles away and the whole inundation map, depth, velocity and arrival rasters are for the wrong place, while the log says in one line that the crest line was not used and in the next that the breach starts half way along it. Nothing is reported as a warning.  
  Fix: Use the line _pick_crest_line chose, and only that line: replace lines 1032-1040 with a block that runs only when best is not None, taking the position along best (no second qio.lines read). Make BREACH_POSITION optional with no default so the 'move the dam point onto the crest' path at 1022-1027 can happen, or drop that path and always place the breach on the chosen line. Add a test that runs app  
  Effort: small. Id R5-BV-2.
- **Queue tool crashes on its first dam: prepare() is handed the wrong feedback object** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:580`  
  _one() passes `fb`, a `_Feedback` adapter (algorithms.py:220, which only has progress / log / cancel), into RunDamAlgorithm.prepare(). prepare() calls `feedback.pushInfo(...)` unconditionally (algorithms.py:1263 "Model CRS ...", and 1255/1266 for the dam.json notes). That is an AttributeError. The caller at line 647 catches only QgsProcessingException, BreachInputError and ValueError, so the error is not caught. The only test of the queue (tests/test_v3200.py:1107) replaces _one with a fake, so the real path was never exercised.  
  What you see: 'Run a queue of dams from a list' fails with a Python traceback on the first dam, every time. The queue file is left saying that dam is 'running'. The tool, the 3.20.0 headline feature, cannot be used.  
  Fix: Pass the real `feedback` to alg.prepare(p, context, feedback) (the adapter is only for workflow.run_dam), or give _Feedback pushInfo / pushWarning / reportError pass-throughs. Also catch `Exception` around _one() so one dam's error is recorded as failed instead of ending the queue.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-1.
- **'Run a queue of dams from a list' stops on the first dam with a Python error** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:580`  
  The queue hands the per-dam setup the small adapter object it uses for the model's own progress and log callbacks, instead of the QGIS feedback object that prepare expects. prepare always calls feedback.pushInfo, so every dam raises AttributeError: '_Feedback' object has no attribute 'pushInfo'. The queue only catches QgsProcessingException, BreachInputError and ValueError around _one (587-590), so the error is not caught: the whole tool stops. No test covers it because the only queue test replaces _one with a stub (tests/test_v3200.py:1106 'a._one = fake_one'), and the wizard, which calls the same prepare, correctly passes QgsProcessingFeedback() (wizard.py:245).  
  What you see: The queue tool, one of the headline 3.20 features and a button on the popup's Dam tab, cannot run a single dam: it fails immediately with "AttributeError: '_Feedback' object has no attribute 'pushInfo'", which means nothing to an engineer, and the first dam is left marked 'running' in _queue/breach_queue.json.  
  Fix: Pass the real feedback to the setup, which runs on the main thread anyway: at algorithms_release320.py:580 use alg.prepare(p, context, feedback) (fb stays for the model callbacks in the worker thread). As a belt-and-braces measure give _Feedback pushInfo / pushWarning / reportError / isCanceled that forward to self.fb. Then change the queue test so it exercises the real _one at least once.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-BV-1.
- **Queue and batch treat a cancelled run's per-pass Depth (Max).tif as a finished dam and skip it** `nd_breach_runner/core/batchqueue.py:275`  
  Since 3.18.0 workflow.run_dam writes 'Depth (Max).tif', 'Velocity (Max).tif' and 'Arrival Time.tif' into the dam folder after EVERY finished LISFLOOD-FP pass (workflow.py 1063-1079, write_result_rasters). The queue's premise in its docstring, 'the run it was in the middle of wrote nothing final', is no longer true. pending() first puts a dam left 'running' back to 'waiting' and then, in the same loop, asks done_check (is_done), which only tests that Depth (Max).tif exists. The point-layer batch has the same test (algorithms.py:1601 'already has Depth (Max).tif') and runblock.has_run (runblock.py:61-64) tests the same three per-pass files. SKIP_DONE defaults to True in both tools (algorithms_  
  What you see: Nick cancels the dam queue (or QGIS dies) while dam 7 of 40 is in its second domain pass. He runs the queue tool again to carry on, as the log told him to. Dam 7 is marked 'skipped: already run (the dam folder has the final rasters)' and is never finished. Its folder holds the pass-1 rasters: a smaller domain, a shorter clock, no WSE raster, no run_report.json, no dam.json 'modelled' status. The queue summary and the CSV say every dam is done. The hazard classification and the products tools the  
  Fix: Make is_done (and the batch's skip test, and has_run for the tools that need a FINISHED run) test completeness, not the depth file: dam.json's latest 'modelled' status for this dam (damsuite.dam_record latest('modelled')), or _breach_model/run_report.json with a 'run_finished' key. At the least require all four FINAL_NAMES including 'WSE (Max).tif', which is not written per pass. In pending(), a d  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-1.
- **The first finished pass makes the dam folder look like a finished run: a cancelled dam is later skipped as "already run" and its cut-off rasters stay as the result** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1069`  
  Since 3.18.0 every finished LISFLOOD-FP pass writes "Depth (Max).tif", "Velocity (Max).tif" and "Arrival Time.tif" into the dam folder with the final names, which is good for not losing work, but nothing on disk says they are from an unfinished run (report["products_pass"] only reaches run_report.json, which a cancelled run never writes). The queue's skip test and the window's readiness test both ask only whether those files exist. The same per-pass write also deletes the previous run's ND 5.2 classification files (STALE_ON_RERUN) at pass 1, before the new run is known to work.  
  What you see: Cancel the queue (or let a dam die) while that dam is on its second domain pass, then run the queue again with the same list, which is what the tool tells you to do: the dam is reported "skipped - already run (the dam folder has the final rasters)" and is never run again, and its pass-1 rasters - a domain the model was still growing - stay in the folder as its results. In the window, every results button (products, consequences, ND hazard classification, hand-off, submittal) lights up on those p  
  Fix: Write the per-pass rasters under the model folder (or mark the folder, e.g. _breach_model/pass_in_progress.json, removed when the run finishes) and copy them into the dam folder under the final names only at the end of the run; have batchqueue.is_done and runblock.has_run require a finished run (run_report.json, or dam.json's "modelled" status) and not just the file names. Move the STALE_ON_RERUN   
  Effort: medium. Id R2-BV-2.
- **A re-run never removes the previous run's rasters, so a sunny-day run is given the flood run's no-breach baseline** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1391`  
  Only the four final rasters and, when the run has one, the Baseline and Incremental rasters are rewritten. Nothing deletes the ones a previous run of the same dam left. A sunny-day run with no ambient flow produces no baseline, and a flood run in which the dam does not fail (the early return at 736-748) writes no rasters at all. The downstream tools find the files by name, with no check that they came from the same run.  
  What you see: Run the PMF flood case first and then the sunny-day case into the same dam folder, which is the normal order of work: the dam folder keeps "Baseline Depth (Max).tif" and "Incremental Depth (Max).tif" from the flood run next to the sunny-day depth. "Life safety and damage" then computes the incremental consequences of the sunny-day flood against the flood run's no-breach case, and the hand-off passes that same stale baseline to ND Dam Hazard, with no warning anywhere. In the no-breach case the wh  
  Fix: In write_result_rasters, delete every result raster the new run will not write (BASELINE_NAMES, INCREMENT_NAMES and the FINAL_NAMES kinds not in `arrays`) together with STALE_ON_RERUN, and do the same in the no-breach early return; or write the scenario into the file names. Either way the folder then holds one run's results only.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-BV-3.
- **preview() writes its own top-of-dam guess into the dam object; the nested tool then runs it as a given crest** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1556`  
  preview() changes the caller's DamData in place: when no top of dam was given it sets dam.top_of_dam_ft to the HIGHEST DEM cell within 30 m of the dam point (np.nanmax), the estimate the 3.20.3 notes say 'can pick up a nearby hill or road' and which run_dam itself no longer uses (it takes the 98th percentile or the crest-line median). core/nested.run_nested calls preview and then run_dam with the same dam object, so run_dam sees top_from_dem False and treats the preview's guess as a value the engineer gave: no 'Top of dam not given: estimated' warning (nested.py:300 only strips that warning from the coarse run, so the author expected it), RAISE_CREST (on by default) raises the dam line in th  
  What you see: A nested run of a dam with no TOP_FT models the embankment as tall as the highest DEM point within 30 m (a road, a tree, a hillside spur), raises the crest line to it, and the run report never says the top of dam was estimated. The same dam run with 'Run breach model for one dam' gives a different crest, a different raise and the warning. Nobody can see why the two runs differ.  
  Fix: preview() must not change the dam it was given: work on a copy (dam = copy.copy(dam) at the top) or restore top_of_dam_ft before returning. Use the same estimate as run_dam (98th percentile within 30 m, or the crest-line median) so the estimate and the run agree. Add a test that preview leaves dam.top_of_dam_ft None.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-2.

### Medium
- **A LISFLOOD-FP path that does not exist is silently swapped for another engine** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:611`  
  find_lisflood() does not tell apart 'no path given' from 'a path was given but is not a file'. In the second case it quietly substitutes whatever detect_lisflood() finds: an installed LISFLOOD-FP, the bundled one, or the built-in numpy engine (nd_data.py:298). No message is logged. The same applies to find_anuga (line 621).  
  What you see: A mistyped or moved executable path (a very common thing after an update) is accepted without a word; the run goes ahead with a different program, possibly the slow built-in engine with different numerics, and the engineer only finds out by reading the manifest.  
  Fix: When a path was given (parameter or setting) and it is not a file, raise a QgsProcessingException naming it, or at least feedback.pushWarning('LISFLOOD-FP at X not found; using Y').  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-4.
- **Dam folder CSVs silently override the inflow, spillway and baseline files the engineer chose** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:724`  
  For the one-dam tools prepare() passes nd_data.dam_input_files(folder) (line 1270), and flood_inputs() lets those files win over the parameters. The parameter texts ('Flood scenario: PMF inflow hydrograph CSV') do not say this, and it contradicts the suite rule in provider.py:36-37 ('a choice the user made is never replaced'). Nothing is logged about which file was used. (The batch tool's texts do say 'for dams without their own inputs/pmf_inflow.csv', so there it is by design.)  
  What you see: An engineer who tests a revised PMF inflow by picking a new CSV in the dialog gets the old inputs/pmf_inflow.csv modelled again, with no message; the incremental results look updated but are not.  
  Fix: Let a file given in the dialog win over the dam folder's; when the folder's file is used, say so in the log ('PMF inflow: <path> (dam folder)').  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-5.
- **Tool help texts carry version history and 'never run in a real QGIS' developer notes** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1136`  
  The help of the main tool ends with a changelog paragraph and the statement that two of its options were never run in QGIS; the sensitivity help ends 'Nothing here has been run in a real QGIS or against a real LISFLOOD-FP' (1324); animation says 'has not been tested in QGIS' (algorithms_animation.py:245); HEC-RAS import 'was not tested against real files' (algorithms_hecras.py:103-104); an output is labelled '(v3.4.0, roughness option only)' (algorithms_tools.py:211). The release-1.22 summary records the suite running in real QGIS 3.34, so these notes are at best unverifiable. The Validation record help (algorithms_verification.py:145-146 and docstring line 7) refers to a tool called 'Compar  
  What you see: Nick reads in the tool's own help that its features were never tried, and is sent to a tool name he cannot find in the menu.  
  Fix: Cut the version-history and 'not run' sentences from the five help strings (they belong in release notes), rename the output label, and change the Validation record help to the benchmark tool's real display name.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-12.
- **A DEM layer that cannot be loaded gives a Python error, and in the queue it ends the whole queue** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1249`  
  prepare() calls dem.crs() one line before the None check that was written for exactly this case, so a DEM whose file has moved or cannot be opened (parameterAsRasterLayer then returns None) raises AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'crs'. In the queue, _one is guarded for only three exception types, while the run itself is guarded for six (algorithms_release320.py:676-682), so this error escapes the loop, the remaining dams are never started, and the queue file keeps that dam as 'running'.  
  What you see: The DEM VRT points at a drive that is not mounted, or one tile is missing. Instead of 'No DEM' the user gets a Python traceback, and in the queue dams 7 to 40 are simply not run even though the tool promises that one dam failing is recorded and the queue goes on.  
  Fix: Move the None check above the first use (if dem is None: raise QgsProcessingException with the message that names the DEM parameter), and in the queue's _one catch Exception, record that dam as failed with the message, and carry on.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-AV-3.
- **Batch and Queue are two tools for the same job (many dams through rundam)** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1441`  
  'Run breach models for a layer of dams (batch)' (114 parameters) and 'Run a queue of dams from a list' (102 parameters, algorithms_release320.py:524-725) both run rundam over many dams with their own ThreadPoolExecutor, their own worker count rule (1567-1570 vs 598-599, identical), their own thread-safe feedback wrapper (1575-1584 vs 620-629, identical), SKIP_DONE and WORKERS. The only real difference is the input: a point layer with 13 F_* field pickers versus a list of IDs read from dam.json, which 'Get data for this dam (or all dams)' now writes for every dam.  
  What you see: Nick sees two tools and two menu entries that do the same thing, must choose between them, and gets slightly different behaviour (R5-B-1) depending on which he picks.  
  Fix: Give QueueAlgorithm an optional DAMS point layer plus ID field as a second way to build the ID list (dam values then come from dam.json as they already do) and delete BatchAlgorithm, _apply_run_result and BATCH_FIELDS (lines 1397-1400, 1417-1438, 1441-1760; keep BATCH_FIELD_KEYS for Cascade). Saves about 330 lines, one tool, one Plugins-menu entry, one window button and 13 F_* parameters.  
  Effort: large. Id R5-B-2.
- **Batch's PRESET silently overrides the cell size and durations typed in the same dialog** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1535`  
  Only the batch tool has a 'Resolution preset' (1486-1490), defaulting to 'Quick screen (50 m)'. Unless the user picks 'Custom', the preset replaces CELL, DURATION_HR and DURATION_MAX_HR that sit further down the same dialog (and that the suite settings file may have set). The preset numbers are hard-coded here and nowhere else; rundam, queue and cascade have no preset, so a batch run of the same dams at default settings uses 50 m while the one-dam tool uses 10 m. The second tuple value (_sim_time_hr) is read and then discarded (1541 sets sim_time_hr = None).  
  What you see: A batch left at its defaults runs at 50 m and is not comparable with the one-dam run at 10 m; a CELL the engineer typed is ignored with only a log line saying so.  
  Fix: Delete PRESET and lines 1485-1490, 1534-1544 (CELL, DURATION_HR and DURATION_MAX_HR already exist and are settings-file aware). If presets are wanted, put them in the wizard or the suite settings so they apply to every run tool. Saves one parameter and about 15 lines, and the dead _sim_time_hr value.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-7.
- **Batch re-implements the one-dam setup instead of reusing it, and has drifted** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1618`  
  BatchAlgorithm.processAlgorithm (lines 1527-1760, 234 lines) rebuilds the dam from the layer and dam.json itself (1612-1664), while QueueAlgorithm._one (algorithms_release320.py:574-582) simply calls RunDamAlgorithm.prepare per dam. The two paths have drifted: batch never sets self._crest_ids (rundam does at 1264), so _pick_crest_line gets an empty id set and the DWR dam line is picked by nearest-only; batch never falls back to dam.json's dam_length_ft for the crest length (rundam 1205-1209); NID_ID is not read. The help text says batch 'Runs Run breach model for one dam for every dam point', which is no longer exactly true.  
  What you see: The same dam can get a different crest line (and so a different breach point and raised crest) and a different crest length in a batch run than in the one-dam run, with nothing in the log saying why the two differ.  
  Fix: Make BatchAlgorithm build its per-dam parameter dict (DAM_ID, DAM_POINT, HEIGHT_FT ... from the layer fields) and call RunDamAlgorithm().prepare(p, context, fb) exactly as QueueAlgorithm._one does; delete lines 1612-1664. Saves about 50 lines and removes the drift. (Or merge Batch into Queue, see R5-B-2.)  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-B-1.
- **The batch throws away everything apply_features has to say about each dam** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1660`  
  apply_features returns the notes about what it read and what it refused: 'Dam crest line: nearest line (120 m from the dam point)', 'Dam crest line not used: the nearest crest line is 900 m from the dam point', how many structure footprints and points were read, how many culvert/bridge/notch structures, the crossing count, and 'Buildings: ... the structures layer has no polygon footprints ... the model keeps bare terrain'. The one-dam tool and the cascade print them; the batch discards the whole list.  
  What you see: In a 50-dam batch the log never says, per dam, whether the crest was found and raised, how many receptors were read, or that an option the engineer ticked was quietly dropped, so when a batch result differs from the same dam run on its own there is nothing in the log to explain it.  
  Fix: for n in self.apply_features(...): feedback.pushInfo('{}: {}'.format(dam_id, n)) - the batch already prefixes the dam.json notes that way two lines above.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-AV-3.
- **Two menu entries and two tools in the Runner classify the same dam folder** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_ndhazard.py:337`  
  The Breach tools menu offers 'ND hazard classification (Section 5.2)...' (the Runner's own nd_hazard-based tool with 29 parameters for field mapping) and, in the same group, 'Hand the results to the hazard classification...' (5 parameters, runs the Hazard Helper, whose class the suite itself calls the one with the structure attributes: sensitivity.py:503-504 'The official classification is ND DWR's; ND Dam Hazard classifies with the structure attributes'). Both read the same Depth (Max).tif and Velocity (Max).tif. The Runner's own tool is also the engine of the sensitivity 'preview_class' column, which the report labels a preview that 'can over-classify'.  
  What you see: Nick sees two classification entries in one menu, picks one, and gets a class that can differ from the other (R3-B-2). The 29-parameter tool is the harder one to fill in and gives the less complete answer.  
  Fix: Remove the 'ND hazard classification (Section 5.2)...' menu entry and the ndbreach:ndhazardclass tool from the Runner (algorithms_ndhazard.py, 772 lines, 29 parameters, 1 menu entry, 6 output files), keeping nd_hazard.classify only as the engine behind the sensitivity preview and the Screening. If a Runner-only install must still classify, keep the tool but take it out of the menu and label it 'pr  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-5.
- **The queue cannot run the flood scenario: there is no normal pool box and the dam folder's value is never read** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:553`  
  With per_dam=False the queue dialog has no normal pool box at all, and because _one() uses the plain RunDamAlgorithm the dam folder's dam.json normal pool is not filled in either (the same gap as the data inputs). flood_inputs therefore passes a normal pool of None, and core/flood.py:142-146 refuses the dam: 'the flood scenario needs the normal pool elevation or a spillway rating'.  
  What you see: Choose 'Flood: PMF inflow' in the queue and every dam is recorded as failed with that message unless its folder happens to hold spillway_rating.csv, so the incremental (ND 6.4) runs, the ones that need a queue most, cannot be done this way at all.  
  Fix: Read each dam's normal pool from its dam.json inside _one (the same damjson lookup the one-dam tool gets through the provider), or accept a normal pool column in the dam list; and say in the help what the flood scenario needs per dam.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-AV-2.
- **The dam queue ignores each dam's own data layers, so a queued run differs from the same dam run on its own** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:574`  
  The per-dam fill of empty inputs from the dam folder (DEM, NLCD land cover, structures, roads, rail, the DWR crest line, crossings) lives in a wrapper that works inside processAlgorithm. The queue builds a bare RunDamAlgorithm and calls prepare() directly, and 'queue' is not in DATA_INPUTS, so that fill never happens. Only the numbers and the dam point come from each dam.json (algorithms.py:1198-1234). Every dam in the queue is therefore modelled with the one DEM and the one set of layers picked in the queue dialog, and the DEM stays a required parameter there because make_optional is never applied.  
  What you see: Nick runs 'Get data for this dam' for 40 dams, so every folder has its own DEM, land cover and DWR dam line, and then starts the queue, whose own help says each dam's values come from its dam.json. The queue makes him choose one DEM for all 40 and silently drops the rest: no crest line (so 'raise the dam line' does nothing and the top of dam is estimated from a handful of cells), one Manning's n everywhere, no roads or structures (no culvert candidates, no receptor decision about where to stop t  
  Fix: In the queue's _one(), fill the per-dam inputs before prepare with suite_data_tools.fill_parameters using provider._RUN_MAPPING and the dam's own folder, log the notes it returns, and stop if a dam has no DEM (fail that one dam, not the queue). Equivalently, add 'queue' to DATA_INPUTS and run each dam through the data-aware subclass. Either way, log for each dam which DEM and which layers it used.  
  Effort: medium. Id R2-AV-1.
- **Queue dams do not get the dam folder's data (DEM, structures, roads, crest line) that the one-dam tool gets** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:574`  
  _one() instantiates the raw RunDamAlgorithm from algorithms.py, not the provider's settings_aware / data_aware subclass, and 'queue' is not in provider.DATA_INPUTS (provider.py:55-64). So the per-dam filling of DEM, RECEPTOR_POINTS/ROADS/RAIL, NLCD_RASTER, CREST_LINE and CROSSINGS from <dam>/data (what 'Get data for this dam' writes) never happens in a queue run; one statewide layer of each must be given to the queue dialog and the queue's DEM is required (add_model_params). The help ('Every dam's own values come from its dam.json ... so no dam layer is needed') does not say this.  
  What you see: After running 'Get data' for 50 dams, the engineer runs the queue expecting each dam to be modelled on its own clipped DEM and layers; instead every dam runs on whatever single DEM and layers were picked in the queue dialog (and with none picked, with no receptors or crest line at all), so the queue's results differ from the one-dam tool's for the same dam.  
  Fix: In _one() use the provider's wrapped rundam class (the ALGORITHMS entry whose name() is 'rundam') or call suite_data_tools.fill_parameters(...) per dam before prepare(); say in the help what the queue reads per dam.  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-A-7.
- **A queue run does not fill any dam's inputs from its dam folder, although its help says it does** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:574`  
  The per-dam filling of empty inputs from <Dams folder>/<Dam ID> (DEM, land cover, structures, roads, railroads, the DWR dam line for CREST_LINE, bridges for CROSSINGS and the normal pool from dam.json) is done by the wrapper the provider puts around the one-dam tool, inside processAlgorithm. The queue builds a plain RunDamAlgorithm and calls prepare directly, so none of that runs, and 'queue' is not in DATA_INPUTS either (the comment there lists the tools left out on purpose and does not mention the queue, which is one dam folder at a time and belongs in the list). Settings-file defaults for parameters the queue does not declare (normal pool, partial breach depth and so on) are dropped the s  
  What you see: The same dam gives different results in the queue than in 'Run breach model for one dam': no dam crest line, so the smoothed crest is not raised and breach water flows back into the reservoir basin; no structures, roads or railroads, so the domain stops growing on a different rule and nothing is reported as flooded; no land-cover roughness. The log says none of this, and the help promised the opposite. (This is separate from the crash in R5-BV-1, which has to be fixed first before any of it is v  
  Fix: In _one, fill the parameters the same way the provider does before calling prepare: p, notes, _folder = suite_data_tools.fill_parameters(alg, p, context, provider._RUN_MAPPING, {"NORMAL_POOL_FT": "normal_pool_ft"}, provider._run_dam_folder), push the notes to the log, then prepare. Keep the per-dam DAM_ID so the dam folder is found. Alternatively move the filling out of data_aware.processAlgorithm  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-BV-3.
- **The queue gives every dam all the cores while its log says one thread each, the setting its own notes call catastrophic** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:613`  
  The queue never sets the per-dam thread count. Each dam's settings keep threads = 0 (algorithms.py:631), which leaves OMP_NUM_THREADS unset so LISFLOOD-FP uses every core (core/lisflood.py:390-391). With the standard two dams side by side that is two solvers each taking the whole machine, which core/batchqueue.py:11-12 and its warning text call the one setting measured as much slower. The batch tool does it right: 'if workers > 1 and not base_st.threads: base_st.threads = 1' and then passes that number to the log line (algorithms.py:1569-1571). The queue calls worker_note(workers) with no thread count, so the helper falls back to its assumed 1 and prints 'Running 2 dam(s) at a time, 1 thread  
  What you see: The queue, the headline feature of 3.20.0, runs slower than one dam at a time on a small machine, the measured 1.76x speed-up in its help text is not what happens, and the log states the opposite of what is running, so nothing tells the engineer to change it.  
  Fix: In _one (or just after reading WORKERS) set the per-dam threads to 1 when more than one dam runs at a time, as the batch does, and pass that number to batchqueue.worker_note so the warning can fire.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-AV-1.
- **'Sign off the fatality rate table' writes to a temporary file by default, so the signed table is never used** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:789`  
  An optional QgsProcessingParameterFileDestination shows '[Save to temporary file]' by default in the dialog (createByDefault is true), so parameterAsFileOutput returns a path under the Processing temp folder, not ''. The 'empty = the plugin's own file' fallback only happens when the engineer explicitly picks '[Skip output]', which nobody does for an output they want.  
  What you see: The engineer fills in the nine rates, signs, clicks Run; the log says a file was written (to a temp folder) and that 'Loss of life will be estimated from this table'. The consequences tool, with RATE_FILE left empty, still reads the unsigned core/fatality_rates.json and every report keeps saying 'loss of life is not estimated'. The temp file is gone after QGIS closes.  
  Fix: Call setCreateByDefault(False) on OUTPUT and treat an empty value as the plugin file (as the text promises), or make the plugin file the explicit default value; log plainly when the table was written somewhere other than the file the consequences tool reads.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-3.
- **On Windows every ANUGA run opens a black console window that the user can close and kill the run with** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:163`  
  Everywhere else in the Runner a child program is started with the Windows flag that hides its console window, and one of those places even says so in a comment: lisflood.py:396-399 ('3.11.0: no console window on Windows', flags = 0x08000000 if os.name == 'nt' else 0), animation.py:863 for ffmpeg, anuga_setup.py:176 for micromamba and report_docx.py:1311 for pandoc. The two calls that start the ANUGA interpreter were never given the same treatment: _launch at anuga_model.py:163 and check_anuga at anuga_model.py:293 call subprocess with no creationflags at all.  
  What you see: On Windows a black command window pops up in front of QGIS for the whole ANUGA run, which can be hours, and again for a moment every time the user runs 'Check the model programs'. The window belongs to the ANUGA process, so a user who closes it to get it out of the way kills the run and loses the work, and the Runner then reports 'ANUGA stopped with code 1'. The LISFLOOD-FP tier in the same plugin has no such window, so the two tiers behave differently for no reason the user can see.  
  Fix: Do what lisflood.py:396-399 does. Add 'flags = 0x08000000 if os.name == "nt" else 0' and pass creationflags=flags to the Popen at anuga_model.py:163 and to the subprocess.run at anuga_model.py:293.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-BV-1.
- **The ANUGA worker quietly changes the flow scheme and the downstream boundary, and the run record still names the one the user asked for** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_worker.py:111`  
  Two fallbacks in the worker swap one piece of physics for another without recording it. At lines 111-112 the chosen flow algorithm is replaced by DE0 if ANUGA rejects it, and at lines 146-149 the transmissive (free outflow) boundary is replaced by a Dirichlet boundary held 50 m below the lowest ground if ANUGA has no transmissive boundary, which the comment right above says reads about 0.1 ft shallower at the edge. Neither writes a note or a flag into the stats dictionary at lines 257-261. Worse, line 260 records 'algorithm' as the value out of the job file, which is what was requested, not what was used, and verification.py:710-711 copies that into the run's version record as 'ANUGA <versio  
  What you see: If ANUGA will not accept the algorithm the Runner asked for, the run finishes normally and the run report, the verification record and the provenance all state the algorithm the user chose while the water was actually routed with a different scheme. A reviewer reading the report cannot reproduce the result, and nobody is told anything went differently. The boundary swap changes the reported depth at the edge of the model with the same silence.  
  Fix: In the worker, keep what was actually set: on the fallback at line 112 record the real name (for example used = 'DE0') and put that in stats['algorithm'] at line 260, plus a line in a new stats['fallbacks'] list; do the same for the boundary at line 148. Show stats['fallbacks'] with the ANUGA notes the Runner already prints (anuga_model.py:274-281).  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-BV-2.
- **The dam queue runs two dams at ALL cores each while its log says 'one thread each'** `nd_breach_runner/core/batchqueue.py:244`  
  The queue tool has no THREADS parameter and _one() never sets st.threads, so prepare() leaves threads at 0 and lisflood.py does not set OMP_NUM_THREADS: each LISFLOOD-FP process takes every core. With WORKERS = 2 (the default) two solvers each use all cores at once. The point-layer batch guards against this (algorithms.py:1569-1570 'if workers > 1 and not base_st.threads: base_st.threads = 1'); the queue tool, written to be 'two dams at one thread each', does not. worker_note(workers) is called without the real thread count and prints the default THREADS_PER_DAM = 1, so the log states a configuration that is not running.  
  What you see: The queue, whose whole reason (module docstring) is the measured 1.76x gain from two dams at one thread each, instead oversubscribes the machine, the case the same docstring measured as 'far worse than either'. The log line says 'Running 2 dam(s) at a time, 1 thread(s) each', so Nick has no way to see it.  
  Fix: In the queue tool's _one(): if workers > 1 and not st.threads: st.threads = batchqueue.THREADS_PER_DAM (as the batch does), and call worker_note(workers, st.threads). Better: have worker_note take the thread count the run will actually use and refuse a default argument.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-5.
- **A CSV with semicolons and decimal commas is read as different numbers, and unreadable rows are dropped in silence** `nd_breach_runner/core/breach.py:225`  
  Semicolons are turned into commas before the line is split, so a semicolon-separated file is split on its decimal separators as well. The row "1,5;200,0" becomes the four fields 1, 5, 200, 0 and is read as time 1 with discharge 5. Semicolon separators and decimal commas are the same export convention, so the one format the replace() was added to support is exactly the one it corrupts. Nothing downstream catches it: the times still increase, so read_user_hydrograph's check passes (breach.py:245), and a hydrograph that is too small gets no warning (only one bigger than the releasable storage does, breach.py:794). Rows that cannot be parsed at all are skipped with no count and no message; only   
  What you see: An engineer who exports the breach hydrograph, the spillway rating or the PMF inflow from a tool that writes semicolons and decimal commas gets a run that finishes normally on numbers that are not the ones in his file, and a file with a few bad rows loses them without being told. The flood, the depths and the consequences are all computed from the wrong curve.  
  Fix: Look at the first data line before replacing anything: if it contains a semicolon, split on semicolons and treat commas as decimal points; otherwise split on commas or whitespace as now. Count the lines that were skipped, put the count in the notes, and refuse the file when more than one or two were dropped.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-AV-2.
- **The chain tool writes each dam's run into the dam's own folder, replacing the single-dam results** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascade.py:108`  
  out_root is the Dams folder (algorithms.py L1816 and L1857), so for an ordinary ID like DWR-2801 the chain run's folder is exactly the dam's own folder, the one the single-dam tool uses (algorithms.py L1185-1188). The downstream dam is re-run with a changed scenario (the upstream wave added to its inflow, or a sunny-day breach timed to the wave) with write_final forced on (L112), so Depth (Max).tif, Velocity (Max).tif, Arrival Time.tif, the run report and the dam.json run entry of that dam are replaced by the chain result. Nothing in the tool's help text (algorithms.py L1771-1780) or its log says so; only Cascade_Report.md lists the folder afterwards.  
  What you see: After running 'Cascade failure of dams in a chain', the downstream dam's own PMF or sunny-day results are gone. Every later step that reads the dam folder (hazard hand-off, consequences, compare runs, the cascade check, the Suite home list) silently shows the chain scenario as that dam's result.  
  Fix: Run the chain's dams under the Cascade_<first dam> folder (for example Cascade_A/B/), or refuse to overwrite a dam folder that already holds results and say so in the help text and the log.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-2.
- **Breach timing of the third dam in a chain is measured from the wrong clock** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascade.py:167`  
  For a dam with pool data the wave from the dam just upstream is added to its inflow, so its run's clock is the upstream run's clock, and its breach time (res.t_ref_hr) is in that clock. L168 subtracts the FIRST dam's t_ref, which is only right when the upstream run shared the first dam's clock. Take A -> B -> C where B has no pool data and C has: B runs as a sunny-day breach timed by L200 (its clock starts when A's wave arrives, this_offset_h hours after A's breach), then C's offset is computed as res.t_ref_hr - first_t_ref_h and the A-to-B travel time this_offset_h is left out.  
  What you see: In a three-dam chain with a middle dam that has no pool data, C's 'breach initiation X h after the first dam's' in the Decisions list and C's arrival times in Cascade Arrival Time.tif are too early by the time the flood took to travel from A to B (often hours).  
  Fix: Use the previous run's values, which are already kept: t_offset_h = this_offset_h + res.t_ref_hr - this_t_ref_h. For the second dam this equals the present formula, so nothing else changes.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-3.
- **Cascade hand-off overwrites the downstream dam's upstream-inflow files without saying which dam they came from** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascadecheck.py:244`  
  The files are always named upstream_breach_inflow.csv and pmf_inflow_plus_upstream.csv inside the OTHER dam's inputs folder, and write_hydrograph replaces whatever is there (os.replace, L215) with a two-column file whose header carries no source. When two upstream dams both reach the same dam (a confluence, or the engineer runs the check for dam A and later for dam B), the second run silently replaces A's wave and A's sum with B's. The module's own stated rule is never to make the next run milder by accident; a sum that holds only the later of two waves is the smaller flow.  
  What you see: Nick points INFLOW_CSV at pmf_inflow_plus_upstream.csv and cannot tell whose breach wave is in it, or that an earlier one was replaced. The job JSON that names the source sits in the upstream dam's folder, not next to the file.  
  Fix: Put the upstream dam ID in the file names (for example upstream_breach_inflow_DWR-2801.csv) and keep a small sidecar JSON or a first comment line naming the source dam and date; refuse to overwrite a file from a different upstream dam, or sum all recorded waves.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-6.
- **Run Comparison reports '0 structures change zone' when the chart file is not signed off** `nd_breach_runner/core/compareruns.py:167`  
  The "we have zones" flag is set from the chart argument BEFORE the code tests whether the charts can actually be used. A chart file that is present but not signed off makes charts.chart() raise, so charts becomes None, every structure gets an empty zone and nothing is ever counted as changed; but counts["zones"] is still True, so the report takes the branch that prints the all-zero counts and never prints the saved chart_problem.  
  What you see: Point the Compare-two-runs tool at a chart file nobody has signed off (the Hazard Helper's sign-off tool writes exactly such a draft, and the CHARTS parameter takes any file) and Run Comparison.md says '12 structure(s) tested; 0 change zone (0 to a higher zone, 0 to a lower one)'. Nick reads that as 'the finer grid moved nobody across a danger boundary, it bought nothing', which is the one answer the tool exists to give, when in truth no danger zone was worked out at all. The reason is in the JS  
  Fix: Set the flag after the test (counts["zones"] = charts is not None, just after the except block), and in markdown_lines print counts.get("chart_problem") whenever it is present, not only in the no-zones branch.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-BV-1.
- **Loss of life is printed in bold from an unsigned, from-memory fatality table** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:716`  
  The suite's rule (lifeloss.py, fatality_rates.json, damsuite.loss) is that loss of life is not a number until a named engineer signs a rate table. The consequences tool bypasses that: when no FATALITY_CSV is given (the default in the Processing tool, algorithms_consequences.py L317 and L358) it falls back to DEFAULT_FATALITY_TABLE, a table the code itself calls approximate and from memory, and writes the result as 'Estimated loss of life' in bold in the Results table of Consequences Summary.md, as estimated_lol in the JSON and as fatality_rate / est_lol / inc_lol in every row of Consequences (Structures).csv. A few sections lower the same report's PAR section (lifeloss.markdown_from_summary)  
  What you see: Nick gets a report with a bold loss-of-life figure (for example 'Estimated loss of life 1.37') that no engineer has signed off and that the suite's own policy says should not exist, plus a later paragraph saying loss of life is not estimated. Whoever copies the headline table into a submittal carries an unvetted number.  
  Fix: When fatality_table is None do not compute or print loss of life: leave fatality_rate / est_lol / inc_lol blank, write 'not estimated (no fatality table given)' in the Results table and the JSON, and drop DEFAULT_FATALITY_TABLE. Better still, make the signed RATE_FILE the only source of fatality rates for this tool too, so there is one rule.  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-A-1.
- **The Results table's 'Structures flooded' incremental cell counts structures with extra dollar damage, not structures the breach floods** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:938`  
  The third column of the row labelled 'Structures flooded' is filled with the count of structures whose DOLLAR damage went up, not the count of structures the breach floods. A structure with no value in the layer has zero damage by construction, and 'no structure value' is one of the data gaps the tool reports, so a structure layer without values (an address-point or building-footprint layer, which is the common case here) gives zero incremental damage for every structure. The population-at-risk and loss-of-life rows on the same table use the real incremental figures, so the one row disagrees with the rest.  
  What you see: For a structure layer with no values, Consequences Summary.md says 'Structures flooded | With breach 37 | Without breach 0 | Incremental 0'. Nick reads that the breach floods no additional structures while the next rows of the same table say the breach adds 92 people at risk.  
  Fix: Put the count of structures the breach newly floods in that cell (structures wet with the breach and dry without it, which `ev["wet"] & ~eb["wet"]` already gives), and give structures_with_incremental_damage its own row labelled 'Structures with more damage'.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-AV-2.
- **The incremental column of 'Structures flooded' counts dollar damage, not structures the breach floods** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:938`  
  In the report's headline table the third column of the 'Structures flooded' row is a count of structures whose DOLLAR damage went up. A structure the breach floods but the layer gives no value to has zero damage with and without the breach, so it is not counted; neither is a structure already flooded just as deeply without the breach. The JSON summary names the field honestly; only the table puts it under the wrong label.  
  What you see: With a structure layer that carries no value field - which the tool supports and reports as 'no structure value: no dollar damage counted for them' - Consequences Summary.md reads 'Structures flooded | 12 | 2 | 0', so the incremental effect of the breach looks like no extra structures flooded although ten more are under water. That count is the number ND REG_05.2026 6.4 is about.  
  Fix: Count it from the wet flags instead: add int((ev["wet"] & ~eb["wet"]).sum()) to the incremental summary and print that in the row, or leave the cell blank as the 'Structures destroyed' row already does.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-BV-3.
- **Two loss-of-life paths in the Consequences tool; the unsigned one still prints a number** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:948`  
  consequences.py keeps its own fatality table (lines 148-157), its own severity thresholds (sev_med 3 ft / 7 ft2/s, sev_high 20 ft / 75 ft2/s), its own four warning classes and its own PAR depth, and multiplies PAR by those rates into est_lol / inc_lol columns and a bold summary line. lifeloss.py (3.20.0) implements the suite rule that loss of life is not a number until an engineer signs core/fatality_rates.json, with damsuite.loss's thresholds (40 / 160 ft2/s, 13 ft) and three warning bands. The code comment at 847-852 says the second is reported BESIDE the first on purpose, but that leaves one report with two severity tables, two warning schemes, two fatality tables, and a bold figure of ex  
  What you see: Nick runs 'Life safety and damage estimate' and gets Consequences Summary.md with '**Estimated loss of life** **0.37**' in the results table and, further down, a section saying 'Loss of life is NOT estimated' because no engineer has signed the rate table. The Processing log also prints 'estimated loss of life 0.37' (algorithms_consequences.py:377-381). A county reader cannot tell which statement is the suite's position, and the number comes from an unverified table.  
  Fix: Delete the built-in fatality path: DEFAULT_FATALITY_TABLE, _severity_name, parse_fatality_table, load_fatality_csv, _table_array, warning_class, warning_labels (lines 148-157 and 317-368, 62 lines), the sev_* and warning_edges_min fields of ConsequenceParams, the rate / lol / severity / warning_class arithmetic in evaluate (539-545), the est_lol, base_lol, inc_lol, fatality_rate, severity, warning  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-B-1.
- **The Runner carries its own ND 5.2 classification tool beside the hand-off, on an 864-line rule file copied unpinned from the Screening** `nd_breach_runner/core/damjson.py:288`  
  The GUI report lists three tools that classify the same rasters under ND 5.2: ndhazard:classify (the authority, 43 parameters), ndbreach:ndhazardclass ("ND hazard classification (Section 5.2)", 29 parameters) and nddam:hazardclassification (step 8, 34 parameters). The Runner's window and menu offer both "ND hazard classification (Section 5.2)..." and "Hand the results to the hazard classification..." (popup_logic.py:70,73). The Runner's rule file core/nd_hazard.py (864 lines) is byte-identical to the Screening's, yet no FILES.json hash or test pins it; ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/rulecompare.py:4 relies on it being "byte-identical in both". classified_preview and PREVIEW_RESULT exist only to stamp   
  What you see: Two buttons in the Runner window give a hazard class; one is a preview that writes a "classified" status into dam.json and can disagree with ND Dam Hazard's class for the same rasters. A fix to the ND 5.2 rule in one plugin can silently miss the other copy.  
  Fix: Remove ndbreach:ndhazardclass from the Runner: algorithms_ndhazard.py (772 lines), core/nd_hazard.py (864 lines), damjson.classified_preview and PREVIEW_RESULT (25 lines), one menu entry, one window button and 29 parameters. The Runner keeps "Hand the results to the hazard classification" (ndbreach:classify to ndhazard:classify); the preview stays in the Screening's step 8, where dams that never r  
  Effort: medium. Id R2-B-6.
- **Above the top of the spillway rating the discharge is extrapolated from the wrong elevation, and nothing says it happened** `nd_breach_runner/core/flood.py:58`  
  Beyond the last row of the rating the discharge is extrapolated as a weir law whose head is measured from the BOTTOM row of the table rather than from the spillway crest. Ratings are normally tabulated from normal pool upward, with zero discharge for the first rows, so z[0] sits below the crest and the head used is too large and its relative growth too slow. For a table that starts 10 ft below a crest with 10 ft of tabulated head, the pool 5 ft above the table gets about 24% less discharge than a weir law anchored at the crest. The class already works out where the crest is (Spillway.crest_m, flood.py:61-64) but q() does not use it, and route_flood never records that the pool went above the   
  What you see: In a PMF that rises above the supplied rating the routed no-breach outflow is under-estimated and the no-breach pool over-estimated, which makes the incremental effect of the failure look larger than it is. Neither the run report nor the warnings say that part of the routing was outside the curve the engineer supplied, so he cannot tell.  
  Fix: Measure the extrapolation head from the crest: q[-1] * ((wse_m - crest_m) / (z[-1] - crest_m)) ** 1.5. And have route_flood note, once, that the pool rose above the top of the rating, giving the peak pool and the top of the table, so the note reaches the report.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-AV-3.
- **A breach time after the no-breach routing has stopped is silently moved to the end of that record** `nd_breach_runner/core/flood.py:226`  
  With trigger 'time', the breach is placed at the routing step nearest the hour the user typed. The no-breach routing (lines 199-208) ends as soon as the inflow is over and the outflow has receded, which can be many hours before the user's time; k_b is then clipped to the last step and the breach starts there, at a lower pool, with no note or error. The summary's breach_initiation_hr shows the substituted time, but nothing says it was changed.  
  What you see: The engineer asks for a breach at, say, hour 48 of the PMF and gets one at the hour the routing happened to stop, with a smaller pool and a smaller flood; the only clue is a different number in the summary table.  
  Fix: Before clipping, if fi.trigger_value * 3600.0 > ts[-1], raise BreachInputError('the breach time ({} h) is after the flood has passed the dam ({} h); choose an earlier time') or, at least, append a note to the result. Three lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-BV-2.
- **A breach time later than the routed record is moved to the end of it without a word** `nd_breach_runner/core/flood.py:227`  
  The no-breach routing loop ends as soon as the inflow record is over and the outflow has fallen below 0.2% of its peak (flood.py:207-208), so the time array `ts` usually stops a few hours after the storm, not at the 240 h limit. When the engineer chooses the "breach at a given hour" trigger with an hour beyond that, searchsorted returns an index past the end of the array and the min() on line 227 silently moves the breach to the last routed step. By that time the pool has normally fallen back toward normal pool, so the breach starts from a much lower head than the engineer intended. The other two triggers are handled properly: the pool-elevation trigger returns a "no breach in this flood" re  
  What you see: A flood run set to breach at hour 48 can quietly breach at hour 20 from a lower pool: a smaller peak outflow, a smaller flood, smaller depths downstream and a smaller incremental effect than was asked for, with no warning anywhere. The only trace is breach_initiation_hr buried in the run report.  
  Fix: When fi.trigger_value * 3600 is past ts[-1], stop with a BreachInputError that gives the requested hour and how long the inflow was actually routed (and suggest the peak-pool or elevation trigger). If a soft path is preferred, keep the clamp but append a note to `notes` saying the breach was moved, to what hour, and what the pool was then, so it reaches the run report and the warnings list.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-AV-1.
- **Two HEC-RAS plan-HDF readers, one per plugin, with different abilities and different results** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras.py:409`  
  The import half of hecras.py (lines 378-1007: Catalog, discover, read_mesh, _first_reach, SpatialHash, cell_spacing, rasterise, about 630 lines) reads the same .p##.hdf datasets ('Cells Center Coordinate', 'Maximum Water Surface', 'Maximum Face Velocity', the Water Surface time series) as ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/rasimport.py (1028 lines, read_area at 294, _spacing at 349). They were written separately: the Hazard reader falls back to GDAL's multidimensional HDF5 driver when h5py is missing (rasimport.py:33-34, class _GdalMD at 137); the Runner's stops and tells the user to pip-install h5py. The Runner's velocity is the mean of the face maxima (hecras.py:714-715), the Hazard's the largest face m  
  What you see: On one QGIS, 'Import HEC-RAS 2D results as rasters' (Runner) fails without h5py while the Hazard plugin's HEC-RAS import reads the same file; the same HEC-RAS run imported through the two plugins gives two different velocity rasters and two sets of file names ('HECRAS Velocity (Max).tif' vs 'Velocity_ftps.tif').  
  Fix: Keep one reader: move the Hazard rasimport reader (the one with the GDAL fallback and real-file history) into damsuite and have the Runner's import tool call it, then delete hecras.py lines 378-1007 and the h5py-only path. Saves about 630 lines, the h5py install step for Runner users, and leaves one import tool with one set of output names instead of two.  
  Effort: large. Id R4a-B-1.
- **The plan always writes results every minute, however long the run** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:279`  
  build_model sets only m.comp_interval (line 1809); the output, mapping and detail intervals keep their fixed defaults. The simulation window can be as long as 336 hours (line 1802) and the computation interval can come out at 2, 3, 5 or 10 minutes (line 1808: Courant x cell / velocity, with cells up to 500 ft and a diffusion-wave Courant of 2), so the output interval is neither tied to the length of the run nor to the computation step it must be a multiple of.  
  What you see: A long flood window with the default 100,000 cells asks HEC-RAS for about 20,000 output times: a results file of many gigabytes and a much slower compute, for an animation nobody asked for at one-minute detail. And when the computation interval works out longer than a minute, the plan asks for output more often than HEC-RAS computes, which it objects to at compute time.  
  Fix: Choose the intervals from the window and the computation step: the smallest HEC-RAS interval that is a whole multiple of the computation interval and gives roughly 200 to 500 output times over sim_hours (mapping the same or coarser), and show them in the README table next to the time step.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-AV-2.
- **build_model is one 555-line function; three more in this unit are over 160 lines** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1352`  
  build_model runs from 1352 to 1906 (555 lines) with ten phases already marked by '# ---' comments and progress() calls, sharing some eighty local variables (P, d, n_left, A, B, perim, z, zx0, zy0, scell, tcell, wcell, ...). Every test of it needs GDAL and builds a whole run (9 _has_gdal guards in tests/test_hecras_model.py, 3 in test_hecras_structures.py), so no phase can be tested alone. Also over the mark: structure_connection 869-1029 (161 lines), animation.render_run 889-1059 (171), anuga_worker.main 57-263 (207).  
  What you see: Nothing visible today; a defect in one phase (reservoir outline, boundary line, breach centre) can only be found by building a full model and opening it in HEC-RAS, which the help text says has not been done.  
  Fix: Cut build_model along its existing phase comments into functions that take and return a small dict (crs/units, perimeter+dam ends, terrain paths, reservoir ring, weir profile, boundary line, points, structures, timing, assemble Model). No lines saved, but each phase gets a unit test without GDAL where it is pure (choose_crs already is), and the GDAL ones become 40-line functions.  
  Effort: large. Id R4a-B-10.
- **A hand-typed 2D cell size has no upper limit on the number of cells** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1706`  
  cell comes straight from the CELL_FT parameter (algorithms_hecras.py:277, minimum 0 and no maximum) and, unlike the automatic path, is not passed through nice_cell's 25 to 500 ft range (line 1458-1461). Nothing checks the resulting count: a 2 ft cell over a 15 mile inundation makes an in-memory raster of hundreds of millions of cells and as many computation points, which mesh_check then feeds to scipy's Voronoi. The only guard is for too FEW cells.  
  What you see: A typo in the cell size (2 instead of 200) makes QGIS take gigabytes of memory and freeze or die during the '2D computation points' step, with no message and the export lost, instead of a one-line 'that cell size gives too many cells'.  
  Fix: After computing gnx and gny, stop with a clear message when gnx * gny (or len(pts)) is above a limit of a couple of million: 'a {:g} ft cell gives about {:,} cells in the 2D area; use a larger cell size'. Clamping a typed cell to the nice_cell range would do as well.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-AV-3.
- **HEC-RAS simulation window leaves out the breach lead time in flood scenarios** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1801`  
  `latest_arrival_hr` is written by workflow.py:1460 from `_final_arrays`, which subtracts `t_ref_hr` (workflow.py:363), so it counts hours AFTER breach initiation. The HEC-RAS clock, however, starts at the beginning of the inflow record (Simulation Date from 00:00, inflow series from t=0), and in a flood scenario the breach comes `breach_initiation_hr` later (`hy.t_ref_s`, set in hecras.load_run:361). The arrival term therefore mixes two time references and is short by the breach lead time. The other candidate, `dur_hr`, is the length of the routed outflow record, which ends 60 s after the outflow reaches zero (flood.py:315-320), so it does not necessarily reach the latest arrival either.  
  What you see: For a flood-scenario export where the breach happens well into the inflow and the outflow record ends soon after the reservoir drains, the plan's simulation window ends before the flood reaches the far end of the 2D area. HEC-RAS finishes normally, the maximum-depth map is cut short downstream, and no note says so. Sunny-day exports (t_ref = 0) are not affected.  
  Fix: Add the lead time to the arrival term: `t_ref_hr = hy.t_ref_s / 3600.0` and `sim = max(6.0, 1.25 * max(dur_hr, t_ref_hr + float(arr) + bvals["formation_hr"] + 2.0))`. Optionally print the window in the README row 'Simulation' as 'breach at X h + latest arrival Y h'.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-A-1.
- **Flood export can start the HEC-RAS pool at the breach level and breach at hour zero** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1828`  
  In a flood run the recorded 'starting water surface' IS the pool at breach (flood.py:254 calls br._common(dam, wse_b, ...)), so trig_ft and wse0_ft in breach_values (hecras_model.py:771-774) are the same number and the sunny-day test at line 1641 always matches: the trigger is set 0.01 below the starting pool. The only thing that keeps the export honest is the later override at line 1828, and that needs settings['flood']['normal_pool_ft']. A flood run started from a spillway rating alone has that as None (flood.py:141-146 takes the pool from the spillway crest instead), and the override is also skipped when the inflow CSV cannot be found.  
  What you see: For such a run the exported model starts the reservoir at the level it had when the breach began and breaches at hour zero, while the whole PMF inflow is still poured in from hour zero. HEC-RAS then gives a bigger peak outflow and a wider flood than the plugin's run, and neither the README nor the warnings say the breach no longer waits for the flood.  
  Fix: Work out the starting pool before the trigger test: take settings['flood']['normal_pool_ft'], else the spillway crest from the run report, else keep the breach pool but add a note that the model breaches at the start. Apply the 0.01 ft drop only when report['scenario'] is not 'flood'.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-AV-1.
- **The engineer's signed fatality rate table lives inside the plugin folder, so updating the plugin throws it away** `nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:57`  
  The only place the suite keeps the signed rates is a file inside the installed plugin, and the plugin ships its own unsigned copy of that same file. Installing a new release of the Runner replaces the plugin folder, so the signed table - the engineer's name, the date, the source and all twelve rates - is overwritten by the empty shipped one. Nothing copies it anywhere else, even though damsuite.loss's own message tells the engineer to sign a table 'in Suite settings', where it is not kept.  
  What you see: After a plugin update the Consequences report and the Processing log quietly go back to 'Loss of life is NOT estimated', and a county reader cannot tell a withdrawn sign-off from a reinstalled plugin. The engineer has to enter and sign the twelve rates again from memory, with no copy left to check them against.  
  Fix: Write the rate table beside the shared suite settings file (damsuite.settings.current_path()) and read the plugin copy only as a fallback; at the least, have the dialog save a second copy outside the plugin folder and warn when the plugin copy is unsigned while a saved one exists.  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-BV-4.
- **A fatality rate typed as a percentage in the rate file is used as written, with no range check on reading** `nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:84`  
  When the engineer types the rates into the Settings page or the 'Sign off the fatality rate table' tool, a value outside 0 to 1 is refused and the cell is left empty, so the table cannot be used.
