But nothing re-checks the numbers when a table is READ. lifeloss.load accepts any file with a "rates" object, status_text only asks whether a signer is named and whether all nine cells hold a number, and damsuite.loss multiplies the population at risk by whatever number is in the cell. The plugin invites hand editing: the Settings page has an 'Open the rate file' button (dialog.py L769-775) that opens the JSON in a text editor, and the Consequences tool's RATE_FILE parameter accepts any file. The mo  
  What you see: An engineer who edits the file and writes 75 where 0.75 was meant (the published tables are often printed as percentages) gets a report saying 'Loss of life with the signed-off rate table: night 2,850.0' for 38 people at risk, presented as the signed number, with no warning anywhere. The same happens with a rate table written by any other tool.  
  Fix: In lifeloss.load, run the rates through parse_rates (or the same 0-to-1 test) and refuse the file with a sentence naming the bad cell, as load already refuses a file with no "rates" object; and have status_text report a table with an out-of-range cell as not usable.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-AV-1.
- **A rate table signed only in 'checked by' is written as signed off but never used** `nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:122`  
  With REQUIRE_DIFFERENT_CHECKER False, a name in 'checked by' alone makes signoff_status return 'signed off by <checker>'. write_rate_file then stores status 'signed off' but signed_off_by '' (L183 keeps only the signer box). The reader that decides whether rates may be used, damsuite/loss.py rate_table_status L135-138, requires signed_off_by to be non-empty, so the table is refused: 'A fatality rate table is present but nobody has signed it off; it is not used.' Both the Settings page (dialog.py L754) and 'Sign off the fatality rate table' (algorithms_release320.py L802) pass the two boxes straight through, so this happens whenever the engineer fills the second box only.  
  What you see: The engineer sees 'Fatality rate table written: ... (signed off by J. Smith (one engineer; ...))' and, in the next line of the same page, 'Loss of life is not estimated. A fatality rate table is present but nobody has signed it off.' The file says status 'signed off' with an empty signer, and the rates are never applied.  
  Fix: In write_rate_file store the name signoff_status accepted as signer (signed_off_by or checked_by) in signed_off_by when the status is signed, or make signoff_status return 'not signed off' when signed_off_by is empty so the two readers agree.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-4.
- **Two different NLCD-to-Manning tables: the Runner's and the Screening's disagree on cropland, pasture, developed land and the default** `nd_breach_runner/core/nd_data.py:21`  
  The same land cover becomes roughness through two tables: codes 21, 23, 81 and 82 differ (cropland and pasture/hay cover most of North Dakota), the unknown-class default is 0.06 in the Runner and 0.05 in the Screening, and the Screening table lacks 51, 72, 73, 74. The Screening uses its table in step 7 (run_breach_sim.py:541), the Runner uses its own in every run and in "Manning's n from NLCD". Neither report says which table was used.  
  What you see: The screening flood and the Runner's run of the same dam on the same land cover differ in depth and arrival time for a reason the engineer cannot see; "the screen said X, the run says Y" has one more hidden cause.  
  Fix: One table and one default in the shared package (damsuite/units.py, or a small damsuite/manning.py), imported by nd_data.manning_lut and solver_io.manning_from_nlcd; delete one 20-line table and the second default.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-3.
- **nd_hazard.py is a byte-identical hand-maintained copy in two plugins while damsuite already exists for shared modules** `nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py:6`  
  cmp confirms nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py and nd_dam_screening/core/nd_hazard.py are identical (864 lines each). The copy is kept in step by a rule in two docstrings ('NEVER edit them here', hazard.py:6) and by nd_dam_screening/tests/test_nd_hazard_adapter.py:28-33, which fails if the bytes differ, plus a pinned MODULE_VERSION == "1.1.0" at line 36. The suite already has a mechanism for exactly this: damsuite/ is copied into all four plugins and checked by FILES.json hashes (BRIEF). nd_hazard.py was written with no package-relative imports precisely so it can live anywhere.  
  What you see: No direct effect today; the trap is a fix applied to one copy and not the other, after which the Runner and the Screening give one dam two classes. The identity test only catches it when both plugins are on the developer's machine.  
  Fix: Move nd_hazard.py into ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/ (master) and import it as `from ..damsuite import nd_hazard as nh` in the Runner and `from .damsuite import nd_hazard` in the Screening; delete nd_dam_screening/core/nd_hazard.py and the byte-identity block and MODULE_VERSION pin in test_nd_hazard_adapter.py. Saves 864 lines, one hand-copy rule and one environment-dependent test; FILES.json the  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-3.
- **nd_hazard and the Hazard Helper classify the same inputs differently in three cases** `nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py:397`  
  Two rule sets classify a dam in three files: ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/classification.py (classify_nd and classify_tm11, with structures.py and linear.py deciding per feature) and nd_hazard.py, byte-identical in nd_breach_runner/core and nd_dam_screening/core (the Runner's ND 5.2 preview, compare-runs zones and its classification tool; the Screening's step 8). They disagree where the data are thin: (a) a structure with no habitability evidence is habitable in nd_hazard (L396-398) and so can make the dam High (L645-663), while the Helper lists it as 'Unverified' and never raises the class for it (classification.py L56-58, L111-113); (b) an overtopped road with no AADT is Medium by assumption in nd  
  What you see: For a bare structure layer, or a county road with no traffic count, the Runner's preview and the Screening tool say one class and the Hazard Helper says another for the same dam and flood. Nick has to work out which tool to believe, and the sensitivity and probabilistic reports' 'preview class' columns can disagree with the hand-off's class for a reason that is not the breach uncertainty.  
  Fix: Keep one rule set: move the per-feature rules (habitability default, unknown AADT, US/State highway) into damsuite and have both classification.py and nd_hazard.py call them, or at least document the three intended differences in nd_hazard's docstring and in the preview_class note so a reader is not surprised.  
  Effort: large. Id R3-A-5.
- **Two rule engines for ND 5.2 (Hazard Helper classification.py vs nd_hazard.py) and they disagree on roads and on chart borderlines** `nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py:573`  
  Section 5.2 is implemented twice: ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/classification.py classify_nd (lines 44-213) with linear.nd_road_class, and nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py classify (668-762) with road_class / rail_class / structure_finding (the Screening uses a copy of the latter through nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py). The rules have already drifted: the Hazard Helper makes any overtopped US or State highway at least Medium whatever its AADT; nd_hazard has no US/State rule, so the same highway with AADT 300 is Low. The ACER zone is also looked up two ways from the same digitized table: the Hazard Helper interpolates the table (constants.py TableCurve, 'linear between the tabulated velocities') w  
  What you see: The same dam folder classified from the Runner's own 'ND hazard classification (Section 5.2)' tool and from the Hazard Helper (via 'Hand the results to the hazard classification') can come back Low in one and Medium in the other when a US or State highway with low traffic is overtopped, and structures just inside a chart boundary can land in different zones. Nick has no way to know which answer the suite stands behind.  
  Fix: Make nd_hazard the one rule engine (after R3-B-3 moves it into damsuite): have linear.nd_road_class, the rail rule and the structure zone test in the Hazard Helper call nh.road_class, nh.rail_class and nh.DangerChart.zone, keeping the Hazard Helper's own report, criteria table and TM 11 LIJ count on top. If the US/State-highway rule is policy, add it to nh.road_class once so all three plugins appl  
  Effort: large. Id R3-B-2.
- **A second probabilistic study leaves the first study's Runs/index.json behind, so the class-probability tool reads the old runs** `nd_breach_runner/core/probabilistic.py:1188`  
  The per-run folders Probabilistic/Runs/run_NNN are created and filled by every probabilistic study, but the two files the ND Dam Hazard class-probability tool actually reads - Runs/index.json and each run folder's run.json - are only rewritten, and old ones only deleted, when the optional 'write each run's rasters' box is ticked. That box is off by default. Run a study once with it on and again with it off (new seed, different number of runs, changed settings) and the index and the run.json files still describe the first study while the rasters and the report are the second one's.  
  What you see: The hazard class probability for that dam is then computed from a study that has been replaced, with no warning anywhere: ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/ensemble.py reads Runs/index.json and, where it cannot, simply uses every run folder it finds. The probability table and the Probabilistic report for the same dam no longer come from the same runs, and nothing on screen says so.  
  Fix: Deal with Runs/ on every study, not only when the option is on: call _finish_run_rasters always and let it write rasters only when run_rasters is set; when the option is off, delete the previous study's Runs/index.json and run.json files and say in the log that the class-probability inputs were removed.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-BV-2.
- **'Products from rasters' overwrites the run's outflow hydrograph plot with a blank zero line** `nd_breach_runner/core/products.py:809`  
  products_from_rasters has no hydrograph, so it makes a stand-in of two zero values, and write_products always writes hydrograph.svg into the model folder it was given. With no output folder chosen (the tool has no such parameter, algorithms_tools.py:400-412) that folder is the dam's own _breach_model, so the real run's hydrograph plot is replaced by a flat line at zero cfs, still titled 'Outflow from the dam'.  
  What you see: Nick re-makes the flood polygons for a dam he modelled last week ('Products from rasters' on the dam folder). The polygons are fine, but the dam folder's outflow hydrograph plot is now a blank zero line. The submittal report builder embeds that same file (core/report_docx.py:2491), so the next submittal for that dam carries a figure saying the dam released no water.  
  Fix: In products_from_rasters, pass no hydrograph (make write_products' plot optional when hy is None) or write the plot under a different name such as hydrograph_from_rasters.svg, and never touch _breach_model/hydrograph.svg when it already exists.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-AV-2.
- **'Products from rasters' still writes GeoJSON and GeoPackage with no CRS when the raster's CRS has no EPSG code** `nd_breach_runner/core/products.py:811`  
  3.20.3 fixed the run path: write_geojson receives the working CRS as WKT, crs_name looks up the EPSG code, and a CRS with no code goes beside the file as a .prj. products_from_rasters (the ndbreach:products tool, algorithms_tools.py:423) does the EPSG lookup itself and passes the result, None, when the raster's CRS is a custom or ESRI one. write_geojson then gets crs=None: no 'crs' member, and _is_wkt(None) is False so no .prj either. write_gpkg gets crs=None and makes layers without a spatial reference.  
  What you see: Nick runs 'Products from rasters' on a dam folder whose DEM was in a county LiDAR project's custom CRS (the case the 3.20.3 notes describe). 'Flood Polygons (Max Depth).geojson', 'Flood Extent.geojson' and 'Arrival Zones.geojson' come out with no CRS and QGIS / ArcGIS read the metres as degrees: the layers land off the coast of Africa, the exact symptom the release says it fixed.  
  Fix: Pass the raster's CRS as WKT and let the shared code decide: crs=g.crs_wkt (crs_name handles 'EPSG:' strings, the EPSG lookup and the .prj sidecar). Add this path to the 3.20.3 regression test.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-3.
- **proposed.py and nd_counties.py are shared copies outside the hash-checked damsuite; the Runner carries 230 lines of the Screening's tool 19 it never calls** `nd_breach_runner/core/proposed.py:1`  
  Both copies are byte-identical today, but the suite's rule for shared code (FILES.json hashes) covers only damsuite/. proposed.py (507 lines), nd_counties.py (128) and nd_counties.geojson sit in core/ of both plugins; nd_counties is pinned by one test (tests/test_v3131.py:25) that runs only when the plugins sit side by side, proposed.py by nothing. Because the Runner's copy must stay identical, it carries stage_storage, describe, build, json_safe_spec, side_of_point, _seed and _label_wet (about 230 lines) that only the Screening's "19 - Propose a dam" uses.  
  What you see: The Screening measures a proposed dam's storage and the Runner breaches it on the same inserted surface; an edit to insert_embankment in one plugin would give the two a different dam until someone compares the files by hand. No wrong result today.  
  Fix: Move proposed.py, nd_counties.py and nd_counties.geojson into damsuite (one copy, hash-checked, next to damsuite/units.py so proposed.py's own FT/AF_M3/AC_M2 go too) and delete the two core/ copies: one plugin loses 635 lines and a GeoJSON, and the Runner stops shipping tool-19 code.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-7.
- **provenance.py's digest code is copied byte-for-byte in damsuite/chain.py with a 'change it here too' note, and the model folder path is spelled in four places** `nd_breach_runner/core/provenance.py:25`  
  file_digest, _canonical, digest_of, FULL_HASH_LIMIT and PIECE (35 lines) exist in provenance.py and again in damsuite/chain.py, which is vendored inside this plugin (nd_breach_runner/damsuite/chain.py), so provenance could simply import them. The agreement is kept only by a test and a comment. The path "_breach_model/run_manifest.json" is spelled independently in workflow.MODEL_FOLDER + provenance.MANIFEST_NAME, damjson.MANIFEST_REL/REPORT_REL (42-43), products.py:807 and chain.MODEL_FOLDER/MANIFEST_NAME.  
  What you see: A change to the hashing in one copy makes ND Dam Hazard's chain check report the Runner's own rasters as DIFFERENT (a false alarm in the reviewer's package check); a renamed model folder would leave dam.json's "manifest" and "run_report" entries pointing at files that do not exist.  
  Fix: In provenance.py: from ..damsuite.chain import file_digest, digest_of, FULL_HASH_LIMIT, PIECE, MANIFEST_NAME and delete the copies (35 lines). Build damjson.MANIFEST_REL/REPORT_REL from workflow.MODEL_FOLDER and the manifest name; products.py:807 use workflow.MODEL_FOLDER (it already imports FINAL_NAMES from there).  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-4.
- **Near-dam refine (REFINE_NEAR) overlaps the Nested tool and cannot deliver the FV1 velocities it is named for with either shipped engine** `nd_breach_runner/core/refine.py:330`  
  The option exists to replace the unreliable near-breach ACC velocities with FV1 ones (refine.py 1-10). The bundled LISFLOOD-FP (bin/README.txt: 8.2, 'reports itself as 8.1.0') writes no .maxVc for FV1, so lines 330-337 drop velocity from the merge and keep the ACC values; the built-in engine runs the 'fv1' keyword with its own inertial scheme (builtin_lisflood.py 354-356). With both engines the refine therefore re-runs the same physics on a finer grid and only depth, WSE and arrival change. The Nested tool (ndbreach:nested, algorithms_nested.py) already runs a fine grid for the first stretch below the dam and merges with the same rules: refine.py 241-251 calls nested._side and nested._scatte  
  What you see: Three advanced settings whose label promises FV1 near-dam velocities and whose result, on every engine the plugin ships, is the ACC velocities plus a warning and a second run's wait; two features (Nested, Refine) for 'fine grid near the dam'.  
  Fix: Remove REFINE_NEAR, REFINE_DIST_MI, REFINE_CELL (algorithms.py 529-537 and 700-703), refine.py (414 lines), the workflow block 1343-1378 and its report glue (workflow.py 302, 1471-1472, 1714-1720, 1793-1794, 1870-1872); tell users who need a fine grid near the dam to use the Nested tool. Saves about 470 lines, 3 settings on 8 tools and one report section; the 18 refine tests in test_v340.py, test_  
  Effort: medium. Id R1-B-1.
- **Result-file names copied into report_docx instead of imported** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1528`  
  The Word report keeps its own copies of file names that other modules own: the ten raster names are workflow.FINAL_NAMES / BASELINE_NAMES / INCREMENT_NAMES (workflow.py:89-96); the six hazard file names are algorithms_ndhazard.OUTPUT_NAMES (25-30) and again workflow.STALE_ON_RERUN (93-95), and a test (test_ndhazard_tool.py:514) exists only to assert the two copies are equal; EAP_PREVIEW_SVG / EAP_OLD_SVG (1492-1493) are products.EAP_PREVIEW_NAME / EAP_OLD_NAME (25-26); MODEL_DIR is workflow.MODEL_FOLDER (88), hecras.MODEL_FOLDER (55), damsuite/chain.MODEL_FOLDER (35) plus three string literals (products.py:807, algorithms_ndhazard.py:309, algorithms_provenance.py:36).  
  What you see: A rename in workflow.py (where the rasters are actually written) leaves the Word report looking for the old name: the Results section loses its raster table, the checklist row 'Depth, velocity, arrival-time and WSE rasters produced' turns to 'No' and the file is listed under 'missing', although the raster is in the folder.  
  Fix: Make one stdlib-only names module (core/names.py or extend damsuite/chain.py) holding MODEL_FOLDER, FINAL_NAMES, BASELINE_NAMES, INCREMENT_NAMES, EAP names and the hazard output names; import it in workflow, products, hecras, algorithms_ndhazard, algorithms_provenance and report_docx (FILE_DESC can be built from it). Delete the five copies and the equality test. Saves about 30 lines, four duplicat  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-1.
- **Every elevation at or above 1,000 ft is rounded to the whole foot in the submittal** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1652`  
  fmt_val drops all decimals once a number reaches 1,000, and it is the formatter for the dam and breach tables (sec_dam 2145-2160, BREACH_KEYS at 1611). North Dakota dam crests sit between about 900 and 2,500 ft, so I ran the code: a top of dam of 1472.62 ft prints as '1,473', a starting water surface of 1234.5 ft prints as '1,234' (half a foot low), while 999.44 ft still prints as '999.4'. The heights computed from those elevations (Hw, Hb, breach bottom) keep their tenths because they are below 1,000.  
  What you see: In the Word submittal the reviewer reads a crest elevation that is up to half a foot away from the value the run used, and the numbers no longer add up: top of dam 1,473 minus breach bottom 1,430 is 43, while the same table prints the water depth above the breach bottom as 42.5 ft. The same quantity in the run report markdown and in the Verification and Validation records keeps its decimals, so the documents in one package disagree.  
  Fix: In fmt_val keep one decimal above 1,000: replace the '{:,.0f}' branch with '{:,.1f}' and strip a trailing '.0', or drop that branch and let the next line format with 1 decimal for anything at or above 100. Values where whole units are wanted (cells, storage in ac-ft) are integers and are not affected.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-BV-1.
- **The submittal report always calls the model LISFLOOD-FP, even when the built-in engine produced the results** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:2273`  
  The 'Hydraulic model' section writes the model name as fixed text. The run report and run manifest do record which program ran (workflow.py:1521 stores st.lisflood_exe, which is core/builtin_lisflood.py when the built-in engine was used, and lisflood.is_builtin / lisflood.describe_exe exist to tell them apart), but the submittal report never looks. The same fixed name is repeated through the document: the summary says 'after N LISFLOOD-FP run(s)' (line 2114), the run table is captioned 'LISFLOOD-FP runs' (2314), the mass balance 'LISFLOOD-FP mass balance' (2336), the gauge table 'Discharge past the gauges (LISFLOOD-FP)' (2474), and the assumptions discuss 'LISFLOOD-FP's inertial solver' (278  
  What you see: When the plugin falls back to its own built-in engine (the normal case when no LISFLOOD-FP is installed for this computer), the Word report Nick submits and signs states that LISFLOOD-FP produced the depths, velocities and arrival times. A reviewer cannot tell from the report which program was actually run, and the only hint is the executable path buried in the run-manifest appendix.  
  Fix: Read the engine from the run report or manifest once in _Report.__init__ and keep it in one attribute (lisflood.is_builtin / lisflood.describe_exe on the recorded path), then use that name in the Model row and in the captions at 2114, 2314, 2336 and 2474 instead of the literal text.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-AV-1.
- **The wizard button is greyed 'needs a DEM' although the wizard's terrain page downloads the DEM** `nd_breach_runner/core/runblock.py:38`  
  The 3.20.0 readiness check treats the wizard as a run tool, so without a DEM the 'Guided setup for one dam (wizard)...' button is disabled with 'needs a DEM: Download DEM for this dam' (both captures show it grey; the configured status line says exactly that). But the wizard itself has 'Download DEM for this dam' on its Terrain page (3.18.0, wizard.py 604-609) and validates the DEM only on that page. The guided first run is blocked by the thing it was built to guide.  
  What you see: A new user with a dam folder but no DEM yet cannot open the guided setup; they must find the collapsible DEM box in the header (the one squeezed in R6-A-1) first. The one button meant for a first run is the one that is grey on a first run.  
  Fix: Take "wizard:" out of RUN_TOOLS in runblock.py (keep it subject to the suite set-up and Dams folder checks only), and add a test in test_v3200.py that state('wizard:', dem='') is ok when the Dams folder and dam are set.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-4.
- **screenhandoff.apply_to, method_index and report_lines have no caller; three places re-implement the fill with three different rules, plus an extra button** `nd_breach_runner/core/screenhandoff.py:303`  
  The module's docstring documents apply_to as the API, but a grep of the whole plugin including tests finds no call to apply_to, method_index or report_lines (58 lines). Instead the fill is written three times: dialog._apply_handoff (1106-1123) sets only the grid size whenever the Advanced button "Use the screening's values" is pressed; popup_logic.defaults (360-381) fills CELL only while the window still shows the starting 10 m, and METHOD/SCENARIO by its own index rule; wizard_logic (235-245) fills cell/method/scenario only when unchanged from the wizard's start.  
  What you see: The same screening value reaches the run by three different rules. The Advanced-tab button sets one value (the grid) although its label says "values"; the run tool's own dialog fills all three when it opens anyway, so the button is a second way to set one of them.  
  Fix: Either delete apply_to, method_index and report_lines (58 lines), or make popup_logic.defaults and wizard_logic call apply_to (one rule) and drop the window's "Use the screening's values" button and _apply_handoff (dialog.py 654-658, 1106-1123, about 25 lines, one button fewer on Advanced).  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-5.
- **Snapshot times taken from .mass rows by index are one interval off with the built-in engine** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:675`  
  _snapshots assumes mass row k and depth snapshot k are written in the same time step. That holds for LISFLOOD-FP, but the built-in engine (core/builtin_lisflood.py, chosen with the 'builtin' word or the provider setting, and accepted by run_suite because BUILTIN_EXE is a real file) writes snapshot 0 at t = 0 (`next_snap = 0.0`, line 406) and its first mass row at t = massint (`next_mass = massint`, line 404). So mass row k holds the time of snapshot k+1. In case_ritter (S = 20 s, T = 60 s) the 20 s snapshot is labelled 40 s and the 40 s snapshot 60 s; only the last snapshot falls off the end of the mass array and gets the right time, so the graded criteria (taken from per[-1]) are right by a  
  What you see: With the built-in engine the Verification Record's Ritter detail table shows t = 40, 60, 60 s, and the plot draws the model's 20 s profile against Ritter's 40 s curve, so the figure in a signed record shows a gross mismatch that does not exist. A reviewer would conclude the engine is broken.  
  Fix: Match snapshots to mass rows by time, not by index: `row = int(np.argmin(np.abs(mass[:, 0] - idx * interval_s)))` and use mass[row, 0] only when it is within half an interval of the nominal time; otherwise keep idx * interval_s.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-2.
- **The eight verification cases have no test that runs them** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:740`  
  Lines 575-1515 (about 940 lines: _run_lisflood, _mass_rows, _snapshots, _snapshot_at, _walled_dem, _ray_radius, _front_position and the eight case_* functions) are exercised only by the three @model_test functions (test_verification.py:614-690), which are skipped unless the NDBREACH_LISFLOOD environment variable points at an executable (598-602). The fast test of run_suite replaces every case with a stub (_stub_cases, 228). Yet the plugin ships a LISFLOOD-FP binary (nd_breach_runner/bin/linux-x64/lisflood, bin/windows-x64/lisflood.exe) and algorithms.py already resolves 'the one bundled with the plugin'. The analytic functions (ritter, stoker, manning) are well tested; the code that reads th  
  What you see: A change to LISFLOOD-FP's .mass column layout, to lisflood.write_inputs, or to a tolerance would be found only when Nick runs 'Verification against analytical solutions' and gets ERROR or FAIL rows in a record meant for a reviewer.  
  Fix: In test_verification._lf() fall back to the bundled binary for the current platform so the five LISFLOOD cases run in quick mode (the docstring says about a minute) in the normal test pass; add plain unit tests for _front_position and _ray_radius on small synthetic arrays and for _mass_rows on a three-line .mass file.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-10.
- **A radial case that detects a fault makes the whole verification suite report 'could not run' and writes nothing at all** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1289`  
  _ray_radius returns a not-a-number value when the flood never drops below the flood-edge depth along a ray (verification.py:1194-1195), which is exactly what happens when the flood reaches the edge of the plane, the fault the case's own edge-depth criterion is there to catch. That value flows into the plot's y limits at line 1289, and plot_svg then raises 'cannot convert float NaN to integer' in _ticks (line 300). I reproduced this. write_plots is the very first thing write_outputs does (line 1795) and nothing catches it, so the error goes up through run_suite to algorithms_verification.py:130, which turns it into 'The verification suite could not run'.  
  What you see: After several minutes of model runs Nick is told the suite could not run, with a message about converting a number, and the output folder holds no record at all: no Word record, no markdown, no HTML, no CSV, no JSON. The one case that found a real problem with the installed model is the case that destroys the evidence.  
  Fix: Drop non-finite values before setting the limits in case_radial (use numpy's nan-aware minimum and maximum, and fall back to no ylim when nothing is finite), and wrap each case's plot in try/except inside write_plots so one bad plot only loses that figure.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-AV-2.
- **Verification Record calls the code under test 'LISFLOOD-FP' even when the built-in engine ran** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1588`  
  When find_lisflood returns lisflood.BUILTIN_EXE (the 'builtin' word or the provider's built-in setting; algorithms.py:606-610), run_suite runs every LISFLOOD case through the numpy engine (lisflood.run -> _run_builtin), but the record identifies it as 'LISFLOOD-FP executable: .../core/builtin_lisflood.py' with a SHA-256 of a Python file, the cover says 'LISFLOOD-FP', every case's Solver row says 'LISFLOOD-FP acceleration (local inertial), default settings as the plugin runs it' (line 769), and the summary table's Model column says LISFLOOD-FP. _lisflood_version (line 643) looks for 'LISFLOOD-FP version' and the built-in log's first line is 'ND Dam Breach Runner built-in engine ...', so no ve  
  What you see: A reviewer reading the record believes LISFLOOD-FP was verified on this computer when a different code was; a FAIL would be blamed on LISFLOOD-FP and a PASS taken as evidence for it. The wrong model name goes into a document meant to be signed.  
  Fix: In machine_info and write_record use lisflood.describe_exe(lisflood_exe) for the label and cover row; when lisflood.is_builtin(ctx.lisflood_exe) set each case's 'model' to 'built-in engine' and the Solver set-up row accordingly, and read the engine name from the log's first line in _lisflood_version.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-3.
- **Verification suite: a Word record that cannot be replaced sinks the whole run and loses the JSON** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1813`  
  write_outputs writes the Word record before Verification_Results.json and does not catch the OSError that Docx.save raises when os.replace cannot overwrite the target (report_docx.py:1012). On Windows that happens whenever the previous 'Verification Record.docx' in the same output folder is still open in Word, which is the normal way to re-run the suite into one folder. The exception goes up through run_suite to algorithms_verification.py:128-130, which turns any OSError into 'The verification suite could not run'. The code path in the algorithm for 'The Word record could not be written; the markdown and HTML summaries are in ...' is unreachable because write_record never returns in that cas  
  What you see: After several minutes of model runs the tool tells Nick the verification suite could not run, although every case ran and the markdown, HTML and CSV are on disk. Verification_Results.json is not written at all, and a stray 'Verification Record.docx.tmp' is left in the folder. The PASS/FAIL summary and counts are never returned to Processing.  
  Fix: In write_outputs, dump the JSON before calling write_record, wrap write_record in try/except OSError, append the reason to res.notes and leave res.files['docx'] unset so the algorithm's existing 'Word record could not be written' branch runs. In Docx.save, remove the .tmp file in a finally/except when os.replace fails (see R4b-A-4).  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-1.
- **A verification run that cannot write the Word record is reported as a run that did not happen** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1814`  
  write_record is called with nothing around it and before the JSON is written, so any OSError from doc.save (a locked or full output folder, a path Word cannot be written to) travels out of write_outputs and out of run_suite. algorithms_verification.py:120-121 catches it and raises 'The verification suite could not run: ...'. Because res.files['docx'] is only set after write_record returns, the fallback the author wrote for exactly this case, 'The Word record could not be written; the markdown and HTML summaries are in ...' (algorithms_verification.py:131-133), can never run.  
  What you see: After the models have run for a minute or more, Nick is told the verification suite could not run, with no mention of the markdown, HTML and CSV summaries that are sitting in the output folder, and Verification_Results.json is never written at all.  
  Fix: Write the JSON before the Word record, and wrap write_record in try/except OSError, recording the message in res.notes and leaving res.files['docx'] unset. The algorithm's existing else branch then gives the right message and points at the folder.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-BV-2.
- **Verification record laid out twice (markdown and Word) and the two have drifted** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1832`  
  summary_markdown (1712-1769, 58 lines) and write_record (1832-1976, 145 lines) each walk the same case list and build the same record: summary table, computer and software table, per case the description, error, reference, criteria table, per-time table, notes, plot, then references. The Runner already has report_docx.add_markdown, written to insert the tool's own markdown into a Docx (used for Cascade_Report.md, consequences*.md). Because the two layouts are maintained separately they differ: the Word record has a Set-up table, the 'Basis of the criteria' bullets and an 'All measures' table; the markdown/HTML summary has none of them. write_outputs also renders summary_markdown twice (1798,  
  What you see: A reviewer who opens Verification_Summary.html (written in the same folder) sees no basis for any tolerance and no set-up; only the Word file has them. Any future change to the record must be made twice or the two disagree further.  
  Fix: Add the set-up table, basis bullets and all-measures table to summary_markdown; make write_record build the cover, TOC and sign-off and insert add_markdown(summary_markdown(res, plot_marks=True)) with the @@PLOT@@ paragraphs replaced by doc.image. Render summary_markdown once in write_outputs. Saves about 90 lines and keeps HTML and Word identical.  
  Effort: medium. Id R4b-B-3.
- **Both files parse the tool's own HTML back into data** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:2014`  
  benchmark.write_outputs (benchmark.py:111-160) has the depth-class matrix as a dict (metrics['depth_class_matrix']) but writes only a flat CSV without it plus an HTML page; read_benchmark_folder then recovers the matrix by parsing the HTML with an HTMLParser and a layout heuristic (first header cell empty, at least three columns). Likewise sensitivity.write_outputs (sensitivity.py:487-528) builds markdown in memory, writes only the HTML, and report_docx._html_items (1867-1874) regex-parses that HTML for the 'What the spread means' and 'Check these' lists. Two HTML parsers (~50 lines) exist only to undo the tool's own HTML rendering.  
  What you see: If the HTML layout changes or the file is regenerated differently, the Validation Record prints 'Not available: Benchmark_Report.html holds no depth-class matrix' and the submittal's sensitivity section drops the spread and check lists, while the numbers exist in the folder. The code's own warning text admits this ('or the file was edited').  
  Fix: Have benchmark.write_outputs also save Benchmark_Summary.json (the metrics dict as is) and sensitivity.write_outputs save Sensitivity_Report.md next to the HTML. read_benchmark_folder reads the matrix from the JSON; report_docx.sec_sensitivity inserts the .md with the existing add_markdown. Delete _Tables, the matrix heuristic and _html_items. Saves about 55 lines and both HTML parsers.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-2.
- **The resume key includes settings that do not change the flood (threads, paths, products), so Resume starts over after an ordinary change** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:268`  
  key_settings removes only three keys. The checkpoint key therefore changes when the thread count changes, when the LISFLOOD-FP or ANUGA path changes (a reinstall, or the bundled build on another computer), when input_files holds a different absolute path (the Dams folder or the DEM moved to another drive), or when PRODUCTS, ANIMATE, KEEP_FILES or CONTOURS_HR are toggled. None of these change a LISFLOOD-FP result. The parallel batch sets threads=1, the one-dam tool defaults to 0, so a dam cancelled in the batch and continued with 'Run breach model for one dam' + Resume never matches. The log then says only 'No matching checkpoint (nothing saved, or the settings changed): starting over.' and d  
  What you see: Hours of finished LISFLOOD-FP passes are thrown away after a change that could not have affected them, and Nick is told the settings changed without being told which one. Resume works only when every incidental setting is reproduced exactly.  
  Fix: In key_settings pop the keys that do not enter the flood: threads, lisflood_exe, anuga_python, input_files, products, animation, snapshot_frames, keep_arrays, keep_model_files, arrival_contours_hr, write_final (keep gauges if their CSVs are needed). When a checkpoint exists but the key differs, log the keys whose values differ (both dicts are JSON; a one-line diff) so the user knows what to put ba  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-6.
- **run_dam is one 1,009-line function with ten nested closures** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:522`  
  The run from DEM check to manifest is a single function of 1,009 lines (522-1530) with fourteen "# ---" phases and ten closures that read about thirty shared locals. Nothing below the hydrograph can be run or tested without a solver, which is why preview() exists as a copy (R2-B-1). lisflood_run (968-990) and load_lisflood (992-1005) build the same LisfloodSettings twice. render_report (1771-1931) is 161 lines.  
  What you see: No direct effect. Every fix inside it (3.20.3 touched four places in this function) risks the other phases, and a fix made here is not made in the copy (R2-B-1 is the result).  
  Fix: Split along the existing "# ---" headers into functions on a small run-context object (crest, hydrograph, domain loop, baseline, ANUGA, refine, rasters, products/record); give lisflood_run and load_lisflood one _lf_settings(bnds, sim_s, gauges, snap) helper (saves about 12 lines). Line count stays, but each phase becomes callable from preview and from tests.  
  Effort: large. Id R2-B-2.
- **Crest-line median uses a 3 m tolerance even when the crest window is read at the DEM's coarse cell, so the line is ignored on the DEMs it was meant for** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:636`  
  3.20.3 reads the 120 m window around the dam at the DEM's own pixel when that is coarser than 3 m, but the test for 'a cell on the crest line' kept a fixed 3 m band: max(3.0, min(cs, 3.0)/2.0) is always 3.0. On a 30 m or 40 m DEM the window has 3 or 4 cell columns and a cell centre lies within 3 m of the line about one time in seven, so vals.size >= 3 almost never holds and the code falls back, silently, to the 98th percentile within 30 m of the point (1 to 4 cells). The warning at 676-683 then says the estimate 'is probably LOW ... Give the top of dam, or the dam crest line', because dem_crest_from no longer says 'crest line'.  
  What you see: With a 30 m DEM and the DWR dam line given (CREST_LINE fills itself from the dam folder since 3.13.0), the top of dam is still estimated from a handful of cells at the point and the report tells Nick to give the crest line he has already given. The more stable estimate the release added is not used where it matters most.  
  Fix: Scale the band with the cell the window was read at: tol = max(3.0, 0.5 * crest_cell) (half a cell catches every cell the line passes through), and when the line still yields fewer than 3 cells say so in the report instead of silently falling back. Do not tell the user to give a crest line when crest_line_xy is set.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-4.
- **With structure or road layers given, the class-stop rule can never stop the domain: every edge is refused before the rule is applied** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1119`  
  impacts.edge_decisions returns `need` as exactly the edges whose info says extend=True, and line 1112 keeps only those. So by the time the class-stop block runs, every remaining edge has extend=True. The test at line 1119 can therefore never be true when receptor layers were given, so the no-breach run and the flow path are never prepared, and judge_edge returns "edge kept (extended): receptors downstream (rule (b))" for every edge without ever looking at the depth or the rise. The depth part of the rule only ever runs when no receptor layer was given at all.  
  What you see: An engineer who gives the structure, road and rail layers (the normal way to run this tool) and switches the class-stop rule on gets nothing from it: the domain keeps growing exactly as before, and the run report's "Class-stop rule" section lists every edge as "receptors downstream" with no station table. The case the rule was written for - a house lies beyond the edge but the flood there is only half a foot deep, so the class cannot change - is refused instead of accepted.  
  Fix: Decide once, in one place: judge the class-stop rule on the edges BEFORE line 1112 removes any (or move the receptor filter after it), and let a below-threshold station run accept an edge, using the receptor information as the note beside the decision rather than an automatic refusal. The unreachable test at 1119 then becomes a plain `if True`-style preparation step and can be dropped.  
  Effort: medium. Id R2-BV-1.
- **The final raster write is not guarded: a result raster still open in QGIS makes the whole run fail at 93 percent** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1388`  
  GeoTiffWriter deletes the existing 'Depth (Max).tif' and creates it again in place. On Windows a GeoTIFF that QGIS has loaded cannot be deleted or re-created (sharing violation), so the writer raises. The per-pass write catches that and logs it, but the final write at 1388 does not, so the exception leaves run_dam after the solver, the baseline and ANUGA have all finished: no report, no products, no dam.json entry, no manifest. The state file does say lisflood_done, so Resume would recover, but RESUME is an advanced switch that defaults to False (algorithms.py:591) and nothing tells the user that.  
  What you see: Nick keeps the previous run's depth layer in the map to compare, re-runs the dam, and after the hours of modelling the tool stops with a GDAL permission error at 'writing rasters'. He has to find the advanced Resume switch to get the results without running the solver again; most users will simply run it all again.  
  Fix: Wrap the final write like the per-pass write: on failure keep going (report, products, dam.json), add a warning that names the file, says to close the layer in QGIS and re-run with Resume (the solver results are kept), and keep the pass rasters on disk. Better still, write to a new name beside the locked file and record it in the report.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-7.
- **preview() is a drifted hand copy of run_dam's set-up and writes its own crest guess into the dam** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1556`  
  preview() (1533-1594, 62 lines) re-implements the first 300 lines of run_dam: the DEM window, the top-of-dam estimate, inflow placement, flow trace and simulation time. It has drifted: it reads at min(cs, 3.0) without the 3.20.3 coarse-cell rule (603-606), ignores a crest line, uses the highest cell within 30 m that run_dam's own comment calls unstable, and stores the guess in dam.top_of_dam_ft. nested.py:202 calls preview(dam, ...) and nested.py:228 then calls run_dam(dam, ...) with the same object, so run_dam sees a top of dam "given" (top_from_dem is False at 667).  
  What you see: In "Nested fine grid near the dam" with no top of dam given, the fine run models the crest as the highest DEM cell within 30 m of the point (a road or hill counts), the "Top of dam not given: estimated from ..." warning and the 3.20.3 coarse-DEM warning never appear, and with RAISE_CREST on (the default) the dam line is raised to that value and the report lists it as a given top of dam. The run-time estimate and wizard also plan from a different crest than the run uses.  
  Fix: Pull run_dam's crest/top-of-dam block (597-684) and its inflow + trace block (805-856, 915-918) into two functions that both run_dam and preview call, and have preview work on a copy of dam (or return the estimate instead of writing it). Deletes the 62-line copy; one estimator, one set of warnings.  
  Effort: medium. Id R2-B-1.
- **The window may open shorter than its fixed header needs, so the DEM box rows are squeezed over each other** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:116`  
  Yes, the layout code explains the cramped 'Download a DEM' group in runner_main_dam_0_00_Dam.png (and in the clean capture). 3.20.0 set an explicit minimum height of 560 px on the dialog. When a window has its own minimum, Qt stops using the layout's computed minimum, so the window can be shown or resized below what its widgets need; only the tab scroll areas (TAB_MIN_H 170) and the log (44) can shrink legitimately. The DEM group is a 7-row QGridLayout placed in the header, outside every scroll area, so when the window is short Qt's layout engine takes the missing height from the biggest non-scrolling item: the group's rows are compressed to about 17 px, the combo boxes and line edits are cl  
  What you see: On a first run (no DEM yet) the DEM download group, the one thing a new user must use, is unreadable: 'Area', 'Resolution', 'Dam point', 'County' and 'Folder' overprint their fields and the Source / Area / County drop-downs are cut in half. On a 768 px laptop the window opens at the 560 px minimum and it is worse.  
  Fix: Keep the header to the three dam rows and put the 'Download a DEM' group somewhere that scrolls: on the Data tab next to 'Get data for this dam' (where it belongs by function), or inside its own QScrollArea. Then remove setMinimumHeight and let the layout's own minimum stand (the scroll areas keep their 170 px minimum, so the window still fits 768 px). If the group must stay in the header, start i  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-1.
- **The 'Download a DEM' box is a second copy of the download tool's dialog, and the forced minimum height is what squeezes it** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:116`  
  Yes, the layout code explains the cramped box. Qt only computes a window's minimum height from its children when the window has no minimum of its own; dialog.py:116 gives it one (560 px), so the real need of the children (3 header rows, 2 note rows, the 7-row DEM grid, the models line, the tab bar plus the 170 px scroll area, the ready line, the 44 px log, Close: about 700 px) is ignored. The window opens at 639 px (gui_report runner_main: size [820, 639], min_size [820, 560]) and Qt's grid then shrinks the tallest rows first: the combos and edits of the DEM box (lines 307-329) are cut to about half height while the labels keep theirs, which is what both screenshots show (clean and configure  
  What you see: The first thing under the dam header is a clipped, half-readable group of fields on every open of the window; the engineer types the dam point and folder, presses 'Download DEM...' and gets the same questions again in the tool's dialog.  
  Fix: Replace lines 258-447 with one 'Download DEM for this dam...' button (plus the 10 m / 1 m choice) that opens nddam:downloaddem through wizard_logic.dem_download_params, as the wizard does; keep _dem_downloaded for the result. Then drop setMinimumHeight at 116 (the window fits 768 px without the box). Saves about 150 lines, 13 controls and the layout defect.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-B-1.
- **Opening the window rewrites the shared settings workbook, although nothing was changed** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:133`  
  _autosave_settings has a guard for exactly this (_loading), but __init__ clears _loading at line 133 and only then loads the settings file at line 135. suite_settings.SettingsGrid.load ends with 'if self.on_change: self.on_change(self.settings)' (suite_settings.py:442), and dialog.py:817 wires on_change straight to _autosave_settings, so every open of the window loads the workbook and writes it straight back. The same happens when the user picks a file (_settings_file_edited at 823).  
  What you see: The settings workbook the three plugins share is rewritten every time the Runner window opens, so its modified date and its 'written by / on' line change although the engineer changed nothing, and when the file is read-only or open in Excel on a shared drive the log fills with '!!! settings not saved' for a file nobody edited.  
  Fix: Load the settings file before clearing _loading in __init__ (move line 133 below the try block), or have _load_settings_file suppress the autosave while it loads, so the file is only written when a value in the grid is actually changed.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-BV-2.
- **The DEM box promises '~90 s/dam' for a 1 m DEM while offering a 30 mile radius, which is tens of gigabytes** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:393`  
  The grey note under the box states a fixed time for each source and resolution and never looks at the buffer, while the buffer starts at 30 miles (popup_logic.DEM_HALF_MI, half the run tool's 60 mile domain; the capture shows '30.00 mi') and can be set to 50. A 30 mile radius at 1 m is about 60 by 60 miles of 1 m cells, roughly ten thousand million cells and tens of gigabytes, which is more than ten times the statewide 10 m download that does get a warning. The only confirmation in _open_dem_download is for the State area; the Dam area at 1 m, which is the box's own default area, gets none.  
  What you see: An engineer who wants fine terrain picks '1 m', reads '~90 s/dam', and starts a download that cannot finish and fills the dam folder's disk, with no warning and no estimate that matches what was asked for.  
  Fix: Work the note out from the numbers already on the screen: area from the buffer and the chosen cell, then cells, file size and a time from them, and refresh it when the buffer changes as well as the source and resolution. Ask the same 'this can take a long time' question whenever the estimated size passes a limit, not only for the State area.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-BV-3.
- **Picking a county on the map silently switches the DEM download from USGS 3DEP to ND DWR LiDAR** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:444`  
  The button is called 'Counties on the map' and the log line only says 'Select one and the county list follows it', but selecting a county also forces the Source to 'ND DWR LiDAR' and the Area to 'County'. Both sources accept a county (nd_dam_screening/algorithms/download_dem.py: SOURCE and SCOPE are independent), so there is no reason to change the source.  
  What you see: A user who only wanted to pick their county on the map ends up downloading county-wide ND DWR LiDAR tiles over HTTPS instead of the USGS 3DEP clip they had chosen, which is a far bigger and slower download, and the FTP path row appears for no reason.  
  Fix: Set only the county (and, if you must, the Area) in _county_from_map and leave dem_source alone; say in the log which county was taken.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-AV-4.
- **For a dam that has no folder yet the DEM box leaves the Folder empty, so 'Download DEM...' refuses with 'Choose a folder for the DEM.'** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:470`  
  _prefill_dem_box fills the DEM box's Folder (and Dam point) only when <Dams folder>/<Dam ID> already exists on disk. For a dam ID that has no folder yet, which is the case the Dam tab itself describes ('No folder for dam X in the Dams folder yet - the first run creates it'), the Folder box stays empty and the download button stops with a warning. The folder is perfectly well known at that moment, and the download code already creates it (os.makedirs(folder, exist_ok=True) on line 532).  
  What you see: On a true first run the status line says 'needs a DEM: Download DEM for this dam', and pressing the download button only answers 'Choose a folder for the DEM.' The user has to work out the dam's data folder themselves, and a folder they then browse to is not passed as the Dams folder (line 551), so the DEM is not recorded in the dam's dam.json either.  
  Fix: In _prefill_dem_box, fill the Folder from pl.dam_data_dir(folder) whenever the Dams folder and the dam ID are both set, whether or not the folder exists yet; _open_dem_download already creates it. Keep the dam point as it is (it genuinely is unknown without a record).  
  Effort: small. Id R6-AV-1.
- **The signed fatality rate table is written inside the plugin's own folder, so a plugin update throws it away and loss of life silently stops being estimated** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:754`  
  'Save and sign the rate table' writes the engineer's rates and signature into core/fatality_rates.json inside the installed plugin, and lifeloss.load() with no path reads only that same file. QGIS replaces the whole plugin folder when the plugin is updated or reinstalled, and the shipped file is unsigned, so the signature and the numbers are gone after an update with no message.  
  What you see: After the next plugin update the engineer's signed rates vanish, the window says 'Loss of life is not estimated' again and consequence reports quietly drop the loss of life figures until someone notices and signs the table a second time.  
  Fix: Write and read the table in a place that survives an update: the suite data folder (damsuite.suitedata) or the QGIS profile folder, and fall back to the shipped plugin copy only when that file does not exist. Say in the window which file was written.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-AV-2.
- **The signed fatality rate table is written inside the plugin folder, so the next plugin update wipes the engineer's sign-off** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:754`  
  The Advanced tab's rate grid reads and writes one fixed file: nd_breach_runner/core/fatality_rates.json, inside the installed plugin. That file ships in the plugin package (it is in the checkout, empty and unsigned), so installing a new release or re-unzipping the plugin replaces the engineer's signed table with the empty one. The window offers no other place to keep it and no way to point at one, although the consequences tool already has a RATE_FILE parameter for a table kept elsewhere, and the Settings tab has exactly this pattern (a settings file chosen by the user and remembered) for the suite settings workbook.  
  What you see: An engineer types the nine rates, signs them, and runs dams. After the next update the window's rate grid is empty again, the reports go back to saying loss of life is not estimated, and nothing says why or that the sign-off was lost.  
  Fix: Keep the rate table beside the suite settings file (or in the suite data folder, damsuite.suitedata) and give the box the same 'File... / New...' row the Settings tab has, remembering the path as the settings file is remembered; pass that path to the consequences tool's RATE_FILE. Until then, add one line under the grid saying the file lives in the plugin folder and is replaced by an update, plus   
  Effort: medium. Id R6-BV-1.
- **The 'Breach tools' submenu is a hand-copied list of the window's tools and has already drifted (5 tools missing)** `nd_breach_runner/plugin.py:107`  
  plugin.py:107-140 is a second list of the tools that popup_logic.TABS (popup_logic.py:21-95) already holds with the same labels. Compared programmatically: 31 menu entries vs 36 window buttons; the 3.20.0 tools queue, cascadecheck, crossings, compareruns and signrates are in the window but not in the menu, and getdamdata's label differs ('(or all dams)'). The submenu opens tools with nothing filled in (plugin.py:303-309), while the window opens the same tools with the dam filled in (dialog.py:1179-1214) and the Processing toolbox lists all 34 anyway: three places for one set. Lines 97-106, 143-151 and 156-160 are a fallback for 'no QMenu' that no Qt build needs.  
  What you see: A menu called 'Breach tools' that lacks five of the tools; a user who starts from the menu gets the tool without the dam and does not learn that the window would fill it in.  
  Fix: Delete plugin.py:94-163 and tool_actions/tools_menu in __init__ and unload (about 75 lines, 31 menu entries and 3 separators); the window and the toolbox keep every tool. Update tests/test_popup.py:514-516 which asserts len(tools) > 30. If a menu list must stay, build it from popup_logic.TABS so it cannot drift.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-2.
- **The grid size (Advanced tab and the screening's cell) is filled into 'Run breach model' only; Estimate, Sensitivity, Nested and Probabilistic open at 10 m** `nd_breach_runner/popup_logic.py:317`  
  defaults() puts CELL (and DURATION_HR / DURATION_MAX_HR) only for 'rundam'. The four other run tools (estimate, sensitivity, nested, probabilistic all inherit RunDamAlgorithm and have CELL) get no CELL from the window's grid size. handoff_defaults() then tests already.get('CELL') == 10.0, and for those four tools 'already' has no CELL at all, so float(None or 0.0) is 0.0 and the screening's cell size is never applied either, although the comment on line 362 says every run tool takes CELL and the 3.20.0 changelog says the hand-off fills the empty CELL of the run tools. Only METHOD and SCENARIO reach them. The Advanced tab's heading (dialog.py:637) says these are 'The values the run tools open  
  What you see: After the screening ran a dam at 20 m, or after the engineer set 20 m on the Advanced tab, 'Estimate the run time' estimates a 10 m run (four times the cells, a wrong time), and Sensitivity, Nested and Probabilistic open at 10 m while the main run is 20 m, so their results are not comparable with the run they are meant to vary. Nothing says so; the dialog simply shows 10.  
  Fix: In defaults(): put CELL from S['grid_m'] for every alg in HANDOFF_TOOLS (the set already names the five run tools), not only for 'rundam'. In handoff_defaults(): treat a missing CELL in 'already' as the starting value: `float(already.get('CELL', DEFAULT_SETTINGS['grid_m']) or 0.0)`. Add a test for 'estimate' and 'sensitivity' next to the 'rundam' one in test_v3200.py.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-2.
- **A CLI run writes no dam.json, so the suite does not know the dam was modelled** `nd_breach_runner/scripts/ndbreach_cli.py:991`  
  The QGIS rundam / batch tools set st.dam_record so that workflow writes <dam folder>/dam.json after the run (the values used, the dam point, a 'modelled' status with tool and version). The CLI builds RunSettings itself and leaves dam_record None, so the guard on workflow.py:1494 skips the record. The docstring promises the same outputs as in QGIS. The run report and rasters are written, so the run looks finished.  
  What you see: After a terminal run the Runner window's record line says 'dam.json: none yet (written by the next run ...)', the screening hand-off and the suite's next-action logic have no 'modelled' entry, and ND Dam Hazard's suite view (which 'reads the same dam.json records') does not show the run's height, storage, point or version. The batch command has the same gap for every dam.  
  Fix: In prepare(): build the same {'point': {...}, 'read': [], 'top_source': 'parameter'/'record', 'nid_id': ...} dict the QGIS tool builds (algorithms.py around line 1193, self._rec_info) and set st.dam_record = it; the x / y / crs are known (pt and setup.frame). Add a test in tests/test_cli.py that a run leaves dam.json with status 'modelled'.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-A-5.
- **Two representations of the LISFLOOD-FP patch, and the .patch file has drifted (no MinGW edits)** `nd_breach_runner/scripts/patch_lisflood.py:99`  
  patch_lisflood.py carries nine edits including the 3.11.0 MinGW ones in utility.cpp (lines 54-70: <malloc.h>, _aligned_malloc, _aligned_free). scripts/lisflood-nd-breach.patch (95 lines) has only the six older edits: grep finds _aligned_malloc 0 times in the .patch and 4 in the script. Yet the script sends people to the .patch as 'the intended changes', core/lisflood.py:9 names the .patch as the plugin's patch, and bin/README.txt says the one patch is patch_lisflood.py. The authoritative copy and the documented copy disagree.  
  What you see: Someone rebuilding LISFLOOD-FP from the .patch (the file the plugin's own messages point to) gets a build without the Windows fixes and no hint why.  
  Fix: Delete scripts/lisflood-nd-breach.patch and point patch_lisflood.py:100 and core/lisflood.py:9 at the script (it is idempotent and reports what it could not apply). Saves one 95-line file and the drift.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-8.
- **The built-in engine's 'conserves volume' test checks a column the engine always writes as zero** `nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3110.py:49`  
  The engine's .mass line has twelve columns (header at builtin_lisflood.py 393-395: Time Tstep MinTstep NumTsteps Area Vol Qin Hds Qout Qerror Verror Rain-Inf+Evap). The computed volume error goes into column 10 (Verror); column 9 (Qerror) is the literal 0.0 on every line. The test v3110_check_runs_with_the_builtin_engine_and_conserves_volume reads column 9, so its assertion is true whatever the engine does. lisflood._read_mass (line 303) correctly reads column 10, so the Runner's own check is fine; only the test is empty.  
  What you see: A mass-balance regression in the built-in engine (the only engine on a Mac, and the fallback everywhere) passes the test suite and reaches the engineer; the run report's volume-error line would be the first place anyone could notice.  
  Fix: Read column 10 and sum it over all rows (or compare the final Vol in column 5 with the injected volume from the .bdy file) and assert that against 1% of the injected volume. One line.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-BV-1.
- **Six Runner tests and six Screening scripts crash on a missing GDAL or scipy where their neighbours skip politely** `nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3200.py:265`  
  The Runner suite guards some GDAL tests and not others. On a machine without a working GDAL, `python3 -m nd_breach_runner.tests.run_tests` prints '451 tests, 12 failed'; six of those are only the unguarded imports above (v3200_units_metadata_on_a_raster_beats_the_crs_guess, v3200_the_crossings_tool..., v3200_the_compare_tool..., v3200_the_cascade_tool..., v3203_the_dem_pixel_size..., v3203_a_geojson_keeps_its_crs...). The Screening scripts do the same with scipy and GDAL: test_crs_017.py and test_dwr_https.py end in ModuleNotFoundError, test_dwr_kinds_019.py, test_ponds.py, test_proposed.py and test_storage.py in a scipy error, while the LISFLOOD-dependent scripts (test_chain.py:13, test_eng  
  What you see: Twelve red lines in the Runner and six crashed Screening scripts on a machine that is simply missing a library, with no way to tell them apart from a real regression; anyone comparing today's run with yesterday's has to know by heart which failures are 'normal'.  
  Fix: Move test_hecras_model.py's _has_gdal() into the Runner's tests/registry.py (and a _has_scipy() beside it), call it at the top of the four unguarded tests, and do the same in the six Screening scripts with the 'print(... skipping); sys.exit(0)' pattern those scripts already use for LISFLOOD.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-TV-3.
- **The wizard opened from the plugin menu ignores the suite's Dams folder and proposes a folder in the home directory** `nd_breach_runner/wizard.py:410`  
  From the Runner window the wizard is started with the window's Dams folder, but the menu entry 'Guided setup for one dam (wizard)...' calls run_wizard with nothing, so the answers start with an empty Dams folder and page 1 proposes ~/ndbreach_dams. The plugin already knows the suite's Dams folder (popup_logic.suite_dams_folder, which the window uses on the same screen).  
  What you see: A new user who starts from the menu, which is where the guided setup is advertised, runs the dam into a folder in their home directory instead of the suite's Dams folder. The Runner window, ND Dam Hazard and the screening then do not see that dam, and the work has to be moved or run again.  
  Fix: In the DamPage, fall back to popup_logic.suite_dams_folder() before ~/ndbreach_dams, or pass the suite's Dams folder from plugin._open when it opens the wizard.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-AV-3.
- **The wizard's dam list silently stops at 5,000 dams; the suite's DWR inventory has 10,088** `nd_breach_runner/wizard.py:489`  
  Since 3.13.0 the wizard starts on the suite's DWR dam inventory (the configured capture shows 'The DWR dam inventory of the suite data folder was added to the project'). That layer has 10,088 rows (ND_Dam_Suite_Pack/dwr/DWR_dams.gpkg, counted). With County = '(all of North Dakota)' the loop breaks after 5,000 listed features and says nothing: the note reads only 'Fields matched: 9.' The dam the Runner window handed over is then looked for in that list (initializePage, lines 425-443); when it is among the 5,088 unlisted dams the match fails and the page falls back to an empty form with only the dam ID. The loop is also getFeatures() with no request on a statewide layer, run on the GUI thread   
  What you see: About half the DWR dams cannot be picked from the wizard's list unless the user first chooses the county; a dam handed over from the Runner window may open with blank height / storage / location fields although it is in the inventory. No message explains either.  
  Fix: Remove the cap, or when it is hit append 'showing 5,000 of N dams: choose a county' to the note. For the county case, build a QgsFeatureRequest with setFilterRect from cty.box([county]) (transformed to the layer CRS) and setSubsetOfAttributes to the mapped fields, so only that county's rows are read; for the all-state case use setSubsetOfAttributes and setFlags(NoGeometry).  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-3.

### Minor
- **The LISFLOOD-FP / ANUGA parameter texts and the 'none was found' error are pasted four times** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:575`  
  The LISFLOOD_EXE and ANUGA_PYTHON descriptions are repeated in add_model_params (575-582), CheckAlgorithm (1999-2003), SelfTestAlgorithm (algorithms_selftest.py:91-95) and VerificationSuiteAlgorithm (algorithms_verification.py:60-64), with small wording drift in the ANUGA text; the 'Set the LISFLOOD-FP executable in Processing > Options ... none was found' error is in algorithms.py:637-640 and algorithms_verification.py:86-89. A provider setting SET_BUILTIN ('Use the built-in flood engine', provider.py:112-114) exists beside typing 'builtin' into LISFLOOD_EXE (algorithms.py:606-610): two ways to choose the same engine.  
  What you see: Two places to turn on the built-in engine; a wording fix lands in one of four dialogs.  
  Fix: Add a _Base.add_model_paths() that declares the two files once and a LISFLOOD_MISSING constant for the error; drop SET_BUILTIN (provider.py:111-114, 130; algorithms.py:43, 606-607) since 'builtin' in the path field does the same. Saves one provider setting and about 25 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-11.
- **DWR structure-number matching of the crest line works only in the one-dam tools** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1016`  
  _crest_ids is set only in prepare() (line 1264). BatchAlgorithm and CascadeAlgorithm call apply_features() without setting it, so for every batch or cascade dam the crest line is picked by nearest-within-500 m only, never by the dam's DWR number.  
  What you see: In a batch, two dams close together can both be raised and breached on the same (nearer) crest line even though the DWR lines layer names each one.  
  Fix: Set self._crest_ids = _dwr_ids(dam_id, rec) before apply_features() in the batch and cascade loops.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-11.
- **WORKERS help says '0 = one per CPU core' but the code uses cores minus one** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1496`  
  The parameter text promises one worker per core; the code (1568, and algorithms_release320.py:599) gives cpu_count - 1. The same text and the same rule are pasted in Batch and Queue, and the '1.76x faster' measurement is repeated three times (1496-1498, release320 544-547 and 558-560).  
  What you see: An engineer who sets 0 on a 4-core machine expects 4 dams at once and gets 3; nothing in the log explains the difference.  
  Fix: Say what the code does ('0 = one fewer than the CPU cores') or make the code do what the text says; put the rule in one helper in core/batchqueue.py (worker_count(raw)) used by both tools, and keep the 1.76x note in one place (the queue help).  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-6.
- **Batch preset tuples carry an unused 'sim time' value** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1535`  
  The middle value of each preset (12, 24, 36 h) is unpacked and never used; sim_time_hr is set to None regardless. The 'Custom (use cell and time settings below)' wording suggests the presets set a duration, which they do not.  
  What you see: None today; a maintainer may 'fix' a preset's duration by editing a number that does nothing.  
  Fix: Drop the unused value or apply it.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-12.
- **Batch summary CSV is written only at the very end; a killed QGIS leaves no table** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1724`  
  Rows are kept in memory and the CSV is overwritten once after all dams. A QGIS crash or kill during a multi-hour batch leaves no Batch_Breach_Runs.csv at all; a re-run with SKIP_DONE then writes a table whose finished dams are 'skipped' rows with no numbers.  
  What you see: After an overnight batch that did not reach the end, the per-dam folders are there but the one-table overview is lost and cannot be rebuilt without re-running.  
  Fix: Append each row as it finishes (open the CSV once, flush per row), or rebuild rows for skipped dams from each folder's run_report.json. (The queue tool already does the per-dam bookkeeping; see Lens B.)  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-8.
- **Batch's CLASSIFY branch duplicates the 'Hand the results' tool's batch merge** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1744`  
  Lines 1741-1759 are the same classify_batch + CSV filter + merge_csv into Batch_Breach_Runs_Classified.csv as ClassifyAlgorithm.processAlgorithm (algorithms_tools.py:370-381). The CLASSIFY / CLASS_ALG / CLASS_MAP trio is declared three times (Batch 1500-1505, Sensitivity 1343-1348, Probabilistic algorithms_probabilistic.py:75-80).  
  What you see: Two ways to classify a batch (a tick box inside batch, or the separate hand-off tool), with the same result file name, so the engineer cannot tell which one wrote it.  
  Fix: Remove CLASSIFY, CLASS_ALG, CLASS_MAP and lines 1741-1759 from BatchAlgorithm and tell the user in its help to run 'Hand the results to the ND hazard classification' (MODE = batch) afterwards. Keep the trio only where per-case classification is needed (sensitivity, probabilistic). Saves 3 parameters and about 25 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-5.
- **Two cascade tools, one of them a 103-parameter copy of the run dialog** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1764`  
  'Cascade failure of dams in a chain' (103 parameters, 1764-1870, sunny-day only, layer fields only, no dam.json, tested in one file) sits beside the 3.20 'Cascade check: does this flood reach another dam?' (7 parameters, algorithms_release320.py:232-376), whose own help has to explain that the other one 'is the separate tool that models a chain in one go'. The cascade check plus a normal run of the downstream dam with the written CSV covers the same engineering question with the tools Nick already uses.  
  What you see: Two 'Cascade' entries in the menu; the big one asks for 103 values and ignores dam.json.  
  Fix: Retire CascadeAlgorithm (1764-1870, CORE_ALGORITHMS, plugin.py:113, popup_logic entry) and keep the cascade check, whose help already says how to run the downstream dam. Saves one tool, one menu entry, one button and about 110 lines. If chain-in-one-go must stay, build it on QueueAlgorithm so dam values come from dam.json.  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-B-13.
- **A dam point with no geometry stops the whole cascade with a raw Python error** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1824`  
  The cascade tool reads every dam of the chain without checking that the feature has a point. A row with an empty or missing geometry (common in a hand-edited or joined dam layer) makes asPoint() raise, and nothing in the tool catches it: the try/except around the model run comes later (1855-1862), so the error escapes as a bare Python message. The batch tool already handles the same case and reports the dam.  
  What you see: One bad row in the chain layer ends the whole cascade run with a Python error that does not name the dam, instead of a line saying which dam has no point.  
  Fix: Copy the batch guard into the loop at algorithms.py:1824: if g is None or g.isEmpty(): raise QgsProcessingException("{}: the dam has no point".format(dam_id)) - or skip that row with feedback.reportError and go on, as batch does.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-BV-4.
- **Cascade tool has no dam.json fallback and no check for missing height or storage** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1829`  
  Unlike the batch tool (lines 1618-1633, which fills from dam.json and raises a plain BreachInputError when height or storage is missing), the cascade builds DamData with None values straight from the layer, never reads dam.json, and takes `g.centroid().asPoint()` (line 1825) without a null-geometry check. The error only appears later, deep in the core, as a Python exception.  
  What you see: A dams layer without a height or storage field (or with a NULL) gives a Python traceback instead of 'no dam height: ...'; the 'Get data for this dam' values in dam.json, which every other run tool uses, are ignored by the cascade.  
  Fix: Reuse the batch tool's per-dam block: damjson.load(folder) + damjson.fill(...) and the required-value check, and skip or name a feature with no geometry.  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-A-6.
- **A cascade writes its dams' results to differently named folders than the rest of the suite uses** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1838`  
  Two different naming rules for the same dam. _safe_name (algorithms.py:107-109, the rule damjson.folder_name copies and the whole suite uses) keeps spaces; workflow._safe (core/workflow.py:1623-1624) turns every space into an underscore. So the cascade reads a dam's input CSVs from '<Dams folder>/Lake Ashtabula/inputs' while core/cascade.py writes that dam's rasters to '<Dams folder>/Lake_Ashtabula'.  
  What you see: For any dam ID with a space in it, a cascade leaves a second folder per dam. 'Get data for this dam', the one-dam tool, the consequences tool and the hazard batch all look in the first folder, so the dam still looks un-run and a later batch runs it again.  
  Fix: Use damjson.folder_name (the suite rule) in core/cascade.py, or pass each dam's folder in from the tool the way run_dam already receives it.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-AV-4.
- **'Estimate the run time' is a copy of rundam's 124 parameters for a 20-line body** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:1940`  
  EstimateAlgorithm inherits every one of rundam's 124 parameters (the initAlgorithm override at 1954-1955 is a no-op) and only swaps the body for workflow.preview plus lfmod.calibrate. The wizard already shows 'a live run-time estimate' (popup_logic.py:24), so the engineer has two estimate paths and a third 124-field dialog to fill.  
  What you see: A full 124-parameter dialog must be filled in just to get a number; a value typed there is not carried to the real run.  
  Fix: Add one boolean 'ESTIMATE_ONLY' (advanced) to RunDamAlgorithm that runs the preview-and-calibrate branch and returns, and delete EstimateAlgorithm (1940-1980), its menu entry (plugin.py:114) and popup button (popup_logic.py:30). Saves one 124-parameter tool, one menu entry, one button and about 40 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-3.
- **'Check the model installation' is a subset of the self-test and its advice is stale** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:2013`  
  CheckAlgorithm (1984-2032) runs check_lisflood and check_anuga. SelfTestAlgorithm's core/selftest.run_all runs the same two checks ('LISFLOOD-FP executable', 'ANUGA environment', core/selftest.py:1022-1029) plus everything else, and RUN_MODEL off keeps it quick. Check's failure text tells a Windows user to build LISFLOOD-FP with a macOS shell script although the plugin ships binaries in bin/windows-x64 and bin/linux-x64 and has a built-in engine (find_lisflood falls back to nd_data.detect_lisflood at 611-614).  
  What you see: Two installation checks in the menu; the simpler one gives advice that does not apply to the engineer's computer.  
  Fix: Delete CheckAlgorithm (1984-2032), its registration in CORE_ALGORITHMS (2035-2036), menu entry (plugin.py:138) and popup button (popup_logic.py:87). Saves one tool, one menu entry, one button, about 50 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-4.
- **Field-synonym matching is written five times** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_consequences.py:199`  
  The same 'chosen field, else the first synonym present in the layer' loop appears in algorithms.py apply_crossings (823-827) and apply_features (921-928), algorithms_consequences._fields (199-208), algorithms_ndhazard._fields (464-473) and algorithms_release320._fields_by_name (75-81).  
  What you see: None directly; a fix to one copy (e.g. case handling or logging what matched) misses the other four.  
  Fix: Add qgis_io.match_fields(alg, parameters, context, names, synonyms) and call it from the five places. Saves about 30 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-8.
- **Hazard classification and HEC-RAS import exist in more than one plugin** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_ndhazard.py:330`  
  ND 5.2 classification is offered by ndbreach:ndhazardclass (772 lines, 29 parameters), nddam:hazardclassification (34 parameters, Screening core/hazard.py) and ndhazard:classify (43 parameters, the plugin the Runner's own 'Hand the results' tool calls); core/nd_hazard.py (864 lines) is byte-identical in Runner and Screening. HEC-RAS 2D plan import exists as ndbreach:hecrasimport (core/hecras.py, 1007 lines) and ndhazard:hecrasplan (rasimport.py, 1028 lines) with no shared code; benchmark (ndbreach:benchmark vs nddam:comparereference) and run-time estimate (ndbreach:estimate vs nddam:estimateruntime) are also doubled across providers.  
  What you see: The toolbox shows three 'hazard classification' tools and two HEC-RAS importers; the engineer has to know which one is the authority (the help says ND DWR's is, which means ndhazard:classify).  
  Fix: Keep ndhazard:classify as the one classifier and make the Runner's menu entry 'ND hazard classification' open the hand-off tool (ClassifyAlgorithm) that already calls it; drop NDHazardAlgorithm (algorithms_ndhazard.py, 772 lines, one tool, one menu entry) or keep it only as an explicit 'preview when ND Dam Hazard is not installed'. For HEC-RAS, make one of the two importers call the other's core m  
  Effort: large. Id R5-B-14.
- **Small QGIS helpers copied between tool modules instead of living in qgis_io** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_release320.py:48`  
  _raster_crs is a verbatim copy of ConsequencesAlgorithm._raster_crs (algorithms_consequences.py:210-226), which algorithms_ndhazard.py:331 reaches by aliasing a method of another tool class. _attr (consequences 47-53) is identical to algorithms._attr_of (88-95). _geom_kind (ndhazard 266-272) is identical to qgis_io.geometry_kind (49-57). The grid centre / radius for the spatial filter is computed four times (release320._box 68-72, consequences 237-239 and 270-272, ndhazard 485-487). _safe_name (algorithms.py:107-109) duplicates core/damjson.folder_name (54-58).  
  What you see: None directly; maintenance only.
