  Fix: Move _raster_crs, _attr, grid_box into qgis_io.py next to geometry_kind and num; import them in the four tool modules; make algorithms._safe_name = damjson.folder_name. Deletes about 50 lines and one cross-class alias.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-9.
- **Same Qt SVG rendering is forced onto the GUI thread for the report but run in a worker thread for the verification suite** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_report.py:15`  
  SubmittalReportAlgorithm disables threading because report_docx._svg_qt (report_docx.py:1328-1346) paints an SVG with QPainter on a QImage. VerificationSuiteAlgorithm (algorithms_verification.py) has no such flag and calls the same svg_to_png with the default backends ('qt' first) from write_record (verification.py:1938) inside the Processing worker thread. Either the Qt rendering is thread-safe (QImage painting is, per Qt's threading documentation) and the report needlessly blocks QGIS, or it is not and the verification suite risks a crash. The report build also walks the whole dam folder with os.walk and getsize (file_rows, lines 3106-3138) and may run inkscape or LibreOffice subprocesses   
  What you see: While the submittal report is built QGIS is frozen (no progress bar movement, no cancel) for as long as the folder walk and figure conversions take, up to minutes when an external converter is tried; the verification suite does the same Qt work in the background with no visible problem.  
  Fix: Pick one model: drop ALG_NO_THREADING from the report (QPainter on QImage is supported from worker threads and the verification suite already relies on it), or add the flag to the verification algorithm if a Qt crash was actually observed. Record the reason in the comment.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-6.
- **Stale guards, base-class detours and in-function imports left from earlier wiring** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_selftest.py:48`  
  algorithms.py:2047 registers SelfTestAlgorithm, so the 'not listed yet' fallback never runs; the module docstrings of algorithms_selftest.py (9-11) and algorithms_verification.py (10-12) still give 'Wiring' instructions that were done. SelfTestAlgorithm (64-69) and SetupAnugaAlgorithm (algorithms_anugasetup.py:28-33) subclass _ToolBase and then override group()/groupId() back to _Base's values. ProductsAlgorithm imports EAP_MAP_CHOICES and _floats inside its methods (algorithms_tools.py:408, 416) although the module imports from .algorithms at line 13. EstimateAlgorithm.initAlgorithm (algorithms.py:1954-1955) only calls super.  
  What you see: None; maintenance only.  
  Fix: Delete the fallback (lines 52-56 become 'return list(algorithms.ALGORITHMS)'), the two Wiring paragraphs, the four group()/groupId() overrides (subclass _Base instead), the two local imports and the empty override. About 25 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-15.
- **Products tool: the fallback arrival-zone list differs from the parameter default** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_tools.py:426`  
  When the text is cleared the tool uses (0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8) while the parameter default and the run tools (algorithms.py:664) use 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8.  
  What you see: Zones re-made with an emptied box lack the 15-minute zone the run itself drew.  
  Fix: Use the same tuple as run_settings (0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8).  
  Effort: small. Id R5-A-13.
- **Arrival-zone default differs between the run tool and the Products tool** `nd_breach_runner/algorithms_tools.py:426`  
  The parameter default lists six zones starting at 0.25 h, but the fallback used when the field is cleared is (0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8) (and core/products.py:781 defaults the same way), while the run tool's fallback is (0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8) (algorithms.py:664) and workflow.RunSettings has a third copy. ConditionDemAlgorithm likewise retypes the DEM_Z choice list (algorithms_tools.py:200-201) instead of using ZUNITS, and gives CELL a minimum of 1.0 m (204) where the run tools allow 0.5 m (algorithms.py:510).  
  What you see: Clearing the arrival-zone field in 'Flood polygons and EAP map from a dam's rasters' drops the 15-minute zone that the same dam's run produced, so the two sets of arrival polygons do not match.  
  Fix: Define DEFAULT_CONTOURS_HR once in core/workflow.py and use it for the parameter default and both fallbacks; import ZUNITS in ConditionDemAlgorithm and give CELL the same minimum as add_model_params.  
  Effort: small. Id R5-B-10.
- **PNG writer, hillshade and hex-colour parser exist in the Runner's animation.py and again in the Screening plugin** `nd_breach_runner/core/animation.py:285`  
  nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:34 has its own pure-numpy png_bytes (RGBA, no filter) and :29 its own _hex; nd_dam_screening/core/gridutil.py:143 has its own hillshade with the same azimuth/altitude model as animation.hillshade (205-226). Both plugins carry damsuite, which is where one copy of each belongs.  
  What you see: None; two PNG encoders and two hillshades to keep correct.  
  Fix: Move png_bytes (with an RGB/RGBA switch), hillshade and _hex into damsuite and import them in both plugins. Saves about 40 lines in one plugin and makes the two plugins' map backgrounds the same.  
  Effort: medium. Id R4a-B-12.
- **lzw_decode and write_gif live in animation.py but serve only the tests** `nd_breach_runner/core/animation.py:375`  
  render_run writes GIFs through GifWriter directly; nothing in the plugin decodes a GIF. The only callers of lzw_decode (375-419, 45 lines) and write_gif (483-493) are tests/test_animation.py:129, 243, 256 and 293. Test helpers are shipped inside a 1098-line production module.  
  What you see: None; 56 lines in the plugin that only the test suite exercises.  
  Fix: Move lzw_decode and write_gif into tests/test_animation.py next to its _read_gif helper. animation.py drops to about 1040 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-4.
- **Unused imports, an unused timer, an unused parameter, a no-op line and a GDAL-2 guard** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:18`  
  anuga_model.py: `sys` (line 12) appears only inside a string literal at 291 and `Grid` (18) is never used; `t0` in _launch (161) is assigned and never read (the timing that is reported comes from _run's own t0 at 248). anuga_worker.py:234 `def ras(v, dry_nan=True)` never uses dry_nan. hecras_model.py:1724 `edge |= inside & False` is a no-op (ORs an all-False array). hecras_model.py:1071-1074 guards SetAxisMappingStrategy with `except AttributeError` for GDAL < 3.0, which QGIS 3.34 (GDAL 3.x) never has.  
  What you see: None; small reading noise in the files a maintainer opens first when ANUGA or the HEC-RAS builder misbehaves.  
  Fix: Delete the two imports and the t0 line in anuga_model.py, the dry_nan parameter, hecras_model.py:1724, and the try/except around SetAxisMappingStrategy (call it directly). Saves about 8 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-6.
- **The ANUGA job builder and worker have no tests; every HEC-RAS builder test is skipped without GDAL; one test pins a version literal** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:57`  
  Grep of nd_breach_runner/tests: anuga_model is touched only by test_v3180.py:267-304 (worker_env and _launch's environment) and a source-text check in test_v341.py:79-82; mesh_polygons, inlet_line, clip_lines_to_polygon, write_job and _run (the 150 lines that decide what ANUGA meshes) have no test. anuga_worker.py has none at all, although bilinear, fill_nan and the rasterise-back block (lines 219-244) are plain numpy/scipy and need no ANUGA. All build_model tests sit behind _has_gdal() (9 guards in test_hecras_model.py, 3 in test_hecras_structures.py), so on a test machine without GDAL the 555-line builder is untested. tests/test_hecras_model.py:336 asserts the literal 'Program Version=6.10  
  What you see: A change to the ANUGA mesh rules or the worker's output grid can ship without any test noticing; the only check is a full ANUGA run.  
  Fix: Add a numpy-only test of mesh_polygons/inlet_line/write_job on the synthetic DEM used by test_animation (it has a Grid and a flooded mask already) and of anuga_worker.bilinear/fill_nan imported as a module; make test_hecras_model.py:336 compare with PROGRAM_VERSION. The GDAL skips are acceptable but should be reported as skips by run_tests, not as passes.  
  Effort: medium. Id R4a-B-15.
- **mesh_polygons tests whether the inflow cell is outside the DEM and then uses it as an array index anyway** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:73`  
  Line 70 checks dem.inside(r, c), which only makes sense if r and c can fall outside the DEM. Two lines later (r, c) is handed to terrain.largest_component, which indexes the label array with it (terrain.py:357, lab[seed_rc]), and at line 77 fine_mask[r, c] is indexed directly. With a row or column past the end of the grid numpy raises IndexError; with a negative one it silently wraps round to the far side of the grid and the mesh is grown from the wrong place. Nothing clips r and c.  
  What you see: If the breach inflow point ever falls outside the DEM (a DEM that does not cover the dam, a mismatched coordinate system), the ANUGA tier does not say so. It either stops with a raw Python message such as 'index 4312 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 3000', which tells the user nothing about the DEM, or it builds the mesh around a point on the opposite edge of the DEM.  
  Fix: Keep the result of the check: set ok_cell = dem.inside(r, c), use 'terrain.largest_component(region, (r, c) if ok_cell else None)' at line 73 and 'if ok_cell and fine_mask[r, c] else None' at line 77, and raise a clear ModelError saying the inflow point is outside the DEM when ok_cell is false.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-BV-3.
- **ANUGA worker and import check open a console window on Windows** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:163`  
  QGIS is a GUI process with no console. On Windows, a console program (the conda python.exe) started from it without CREATE_NO_WINDOW gets a new console window, even with stdout piped. The sibling modules already handle this: anuga_setup._run passes `creationflags = 0x08000000` (anuga_setup.py:175-176) and animation uses `_no_window()` (animation.py:861-864). The ANUGA launcher and `check_anuga` (anuga_model.py:293, `subprocess.run([python, "-c", code], ...)`) do not.  
  What you see: On Windows, a black console window appears when an ANUGA run starts and stays on screen for the whole run (hours); a second one flashes every time the ANUGA interpreter is checked. Closing that window kills the run.  
  Fix: Pass `creationflags=0x08000000` on Windows in `_launch` and `check_anuga`, e.g. reuse the same helper as animation._no_window(): `kw = {"creationflags": 0x08000000} if sys.platform.startswith("win") else {}` and `subprocess.Popen(..., **kw)`.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-A-2.
- **If the ANUGA output reader thread dies, the launcher waits forever** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:169`  
  The Popen is in text mode with the default strict decoding (`text=True`, no `errors=`). If the worker writes a byte sequence the QGIS-side locale cannot decode (for instance a traceback with a path in another alphabet when the two interpreters disagree on the console encoding), `for ln in proc.stdout` raises inside the daemon thread, `lines.put(None)` is never reached, and the main loop keeps polling the empty queue until the user cancels. The process may already have exited. anuga_setup._run avoids this with `errors="replace"` (anuga_setup.py:178).  
  What you see: The ANUGA run shows the last progress message and never finishes or fails; the user has to cancel and gets no error text. Unlikely on an English Windows, possible with non-ASCII paths or mixed console encodings.  
  Fix: Open the pipe with `errors="replace"` and make the sentinel unconditional: wrap the loop in `try/finally: lines.put(None)`. Also stop the wait when `proc.poll() is not None` and the queue has drained.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-A-3.
- **check_anuga ignores its workdir argument, so three callers create and delete temp folders for nothing; installed() has no caller** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:285`  
  The body never reads `workdir` (no cwd, no file written). Yet anuga_setup.check (198-208) does tempfile.mkdtemp + shutil.rmtree around the call, selftest.check_anuga_env passes os.path.join(workdir, 'anuga_check') (selftest.py:746), and the Check tool and the CLI pass a TemporaryDirectory (algorithms.py:2021, ndbreach_cli.py:1425). Separately, anuga_setup.installed() (211-216) is called from nowhere in the repository (the '.installed(' hits are damsuite.versions.installed).  
  What you see: None visible; a few lines that mislead the next reader into thinking the check writes files.  
  Fix: Drop the workdir parameter from check_anuga; anuga_setup.check becomes a one-line wrapper (remove the tempfile/shutil code); delete installed(). Saves about 15 lines and three pointless temp folders per installation check.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-5.
- **The ANUGA check can end in a raw Python error instead of saying what is wrong** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_model.py:299`  
  Everything else in check_anuga returns a message, but these two lines assume the last line of standard output is exactly the two-word version line. Empty output, or any banner the environment prints after it (a conda or DLL notice), makes [-1] or the two-field format raise IndexError, and the try above only covers the subprocess call, so nothing catches it; anuga_setup.check and installed pass it straight on.  
  What you see: 'Check the model programs' and 'Set up ANUGA' stop with a Python error dialog rather than 'PROBLEM ANUGA ...', so the user is told nothing about their ANUGA environment.  
  Fix: Parse defensively: toks = (out.stdout or "").split(); if len(toks) < 2, return {'ok': False, 'message': (out.stdout + out.stderr)[-1500:] or 'the interpreter printed nothing'}; otherwise format from toks[:2].  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-AV-4.
- **The list of QGIS Python variables to strip exists twice, and the two lists differ** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_setup.py:166`  
  anuga_model.DROPPED_ENV (line 142) is ('PYTHONHOME', 'PYTHONPATH', 'QGIS_PREFIX_PATH', 'PYTHONNOUSERSITE'); the 3.18.0 fix added PYTHONNOUSERSITE there (and worker_env, _launch and check_anuga all use it, tested in tests/test_v3180.py:267-304). anuga_setup._clean_env, which builds the environment micromamba runs in, keeps its own three-name copy and was not updated. The actual ANUGA checks after setup go through worker_env, so no run is affected today.  
  What you see: None today; the next variable added to one list will be missed in the other, as PYTHONNOUSERSITE already was.  
  Fix: In _clean_env replace the literal tuple with `from .anuga_model import DROPPED_ENV` (a plain constant, no Qt). One list instead of two.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-7.
- **'Remove the private ANUGA environment' deletes the whole NDBREACH_ANUGA_HOME folder** `nd_breach_runner/core/anuga_setup.py:261`  
  `remove()` is documented as 'Delete everything this module created' but deletes the folder named by `data_dir()`. When the user sets `NDBREACH_ANUGA_HOME` (anuga_setup.py:65-68) to an existing folder that also holds other things, the REMOVE option of the Set up ANUGA tool (algorithms_anugasetup.py:60-62 calls `anuga_setup.remove()`) deletes those things too. The module only ever creates `bin/`, `env/` and `root/` under home.  
  What you see: A user who pointed the ANUGA home at, say, a shared tools folder and later ticks 'Remove the private ANUGA environment' loses everything else in that folder, with no confirmation beyond the checkbox.  
  Fix: Delete only what the module made: `for sub in ("bin", "env", "root"): shutil.rmtree(os.path.join(home, sub), ignore_errors=True)`, then remove `home` itself only if it is now empty. Return True when any of the three existed.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-A-6.
- **The queue tool prints the dam-list note one letter per line** `nd_breach_runner/core/batchqueue.py:108`  
  read_list returns one note string, but the queue tool treats it as a list of lines and loops over it, so Python iterates the characters of the string.  
  What you see: Every run of "Run a queue of dams from a list" starts with about forty log lines of one character each (d, a, m, space, I, D, s, ...) before the real messages, so the first thing the engineer sees in the Processing log is a column of letters instead of "dam IDs from the column \"Dam ID\"".  
  Fix: In algorithms_release320.py use `feedback.pushInfo(notes)` (guarded by `if notes:`), or make read_list return a list of notes and keep the loop. One line either way.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-BV-4.
- **The queue counts dams that were run and did not fail as 'already run'** `nd_breach_runner/core/batchqueue.py:233`  
  SKIPPED is used for two different things: a dam whose folder already held results, and a dam that was modelled and did not breach. summary_text labels the whole SKIPPED count 'already run', and the same word is used in the queue CSV's Status column.  
  What you see: A county queue of 40 dams where 12 do not fail in the flood reports '40 dam(s): 28 done, 12 already run', so Nick reads it as twelve dams he had done before and never notices that twelve dams were modelled and produced no flood. The per-dam Message column is the only place that says so.  
  Fix: Add a separate status (for example NO_BREACH = "no breach") for a dam that was run and did not fail, count it on its own line in summary_text, and keep it in FINISHED so it is not run again.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-AV-4.
- **The Screening's benchmark.py embeds the Runner's benchmark functions by hand copy** `nd_breach_runner/core/benchmark.py:23`  
  Lines 23-108 of the Runner's benchmark.py (_wet, _err, compare_fields, verdict, difference_arrays) are lines 46-134 of nd_dam_screening/core/benchmark.py, verbatim, with a docstring telling the developer to re-copy by hand and a test (nd_dam_screening/tests/test_tools_benchmark.py) that runs both copies on the same arrays to catch drift. Same pattern as R3-B-3; same remedy exists.  
  What you see: None today; a fix to the F or verdict thresholds in one plugin and not the other would make 'Compare with a reference model' in the Runner and tool 'compare_reference' in the Screening grade the same comparison differently.  
  Fix: Move _wet, _err, compare_fields, verdict and difference_arrays into damsuite/benchmark.py; both plugins import them; delete the copied block and the cross-copy test. Saves 86 lines and one hand-copy rule.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-9.
- **The numpy trapezoid/trapz compatibility shim is written out eleven times across the suite** `nd_breach_runner/core/breach.py:148`  
  The same two-line hasattr shim (needed because QGIS 3.28/3.34 ship numpy 1.x, which has no np.trapezoid) appears in breach.py 148-149, 180 and 203, flood.py 106, hydrology.py 105-106 (as _trapz), and outside the unit in hecras.py 115, anuga_worker.py 253-254, verification.py 256 (a second _trapz), nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py 435 and two tests.  
  What you see: None now; when numpy 2 removes np.trapz for good there are eleven places to find instead of one.  
  Fix: One trapz(y, x) in units.py (or damsuite/units.py, since the Screening needs it too), imported where the shim stands; delete the nine inline copies. Saves about 15 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-7.
- **Hydrograph.resampled builds a grid x breakpoints matrix; the guard checks the wrong dimension** `nd_breach_runner/core/breach.py:167`  
  The nearest-breakpoint distance is computed as a dense (len(grid) x len(orig)) float64 matrix. The guard only skips it when there are 5,000 or more breakpoints; the grid side (duration / 30 s, called from lisflood.write_inputs) is unguarded. Routed hydrographs are long enough to trip the guard, but a user CSV (METHOD_USER) with a few thousand rows over a long record does not: 4,000 rows over 240 h is 28,800 x 4,000 = 115 M doubles, about 0.9 GB for the difference and another 0.9 GB for np.abs before .min runs.  
  What you see: With a long, dense user hydrograph the run can stop with a MemoryError (or a long freeze while the machine swaps) at the point the LISFLOOD-FP inputs are written, after the breach routing has already been reported as done.  
  Fix: Use np.searchsorted(orig, grid) and compare the grid point with its two neighbouring breakpoints to get the nearest distance in O(n log n), with no guard needed.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-A-3.
- **The breach formulas exist twice: breach.py and the Screening plugin's breach_hydro.py, and one shared function has already drifted** `nd_breach_runner/core/breach.py:314`  
  Nine top-level names are shared with nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py (checked with an AST diff): _positive (breach.py 297-308 = breach_hydro.py 47-58, identical), tr60_peak_cfs (377-404 = 136-163, identical), embankment_area_ft2 (372-374 = 120-122, identical), froehlich_2008 (314-321 vs 62-67), froehlich_1995 (324-332 vs 70-77), froehlich_1995_peak (335-338 vs 267-268), mlm_1984 (341-350 vs 107-117) and curvilinear_shape (695-711 vs 166-183) differ only in wording, vtg_1990 (353-369) and von_thun_gillette (84-93) carry the same Cb table under two names, and tr60_hydrograph (714-762 vs 187-213) differs by 64 lines. About 91 lines are byte-identical (sort/comm). A coefficient correction   
  What you see: The Screening's hydrograph and the Runner's for the same dam can disagree after a one-sided fix, and nothing in the output says which copy was corrected.  
  Fix: Move the formula layer (froehlich_2008, froehlich_1995, froehlich_1995_peak, mlm_1984, vtg_1990, tr60_peak_cfs, embankment_area_ft2, curvilinear_shape, _positive; about 120 lines, breach.py 297-404 and 695-711) into damsuite (for example damsuite/breach_formulas.py beside damsuite/units.py) and import it from both plugins. Saves about 100 lines in breach_hydro.py and removes the drift.  
  Effort: medium. Id R1-B-2.
- **The ESRI ASCII grid reader and writer exist three times (grid.py, builtin_lisflood.py, Screening solver_io.py); the built-in engine's standalone mode that justifies its copy is never used** `nd_breach_runner/core/builtin_lisflood.py:51`  
  builtin_lisflood._read_asc/_write_asc (51-72) repeat Grid.read_asc/write_asc (grid.py 100-125) line for line, and the module redefines NODATA (37) and G (32), so that it can run alone via main() (510-526, 'python builtin_lisflood.py -v run.par'). Nothing does: a grep of every .py, .sh, .md and .txt finds no such call, and lisflood._run_builtin imports it as a module (lisflood.py 351, 364). The Screening plugin has a third copy, nd_dam_screening/core/solver_io.py 28-45 (write_ascii_grid/read_ascii_grid), which reads six header lines blindly and handles no xllcenter.  
  What you see: None today; a header fix (xllcenter, a 5-line header) made in grid.py does not reach the engine or the Screening.  
  Fix: In builtin_lisflood import Grid and NODATA from .grid and G from .units; delete _read_asc, _write_asc, the local G/NODATA, main() and the __main__ block (about 50 lines). The Screening copy is that plugin's decision; note it to the Screening reviewer.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-5.
- **No test names the built-in engine, so its Manning raster, start file, gauge, snapshot and closed-edge branches run untested; the GDAL mosaic, gap-fill and writer functions have only fakes** `nd_breach_runner/core/builtin_lisflood.py:327`  
  No test file imports builtin_lisflood (grep: 0). It is driven only by lisflood.check_lisflood on a 20 x 100-cell synthetic channel with default settings (test_v3110.py 42-50), which writes no Manning grid, start file, gauge, snapshot, closed edge or line source, so run_par 327-333, 353, 369-390, 396-399, 425-428, 435-438 and the closed branches of Model._edge (251-264) are exercised only if a model test happens to run on a machine without the bundled LISFLOOD-FP (run_tests.py 718-719 returns NDBREACH_LISFLOOD or "", which lisflood.resolve_exe 77-84 turns into the bundled binary when it is present, as it is on Linux and Windows x64). gdal_io.build_vrt, fill_gaps, nlcd_to_manning_raster, GeoTi  
  What you see: A regression in the engine's gauges (the Nested tool's interface flow comes from them) or in the DEM mosaic tool reaches the engineer before any test.  
  Fix: One test that runs builtin_lisflood.run_par on a 30 x 30 grid with a .n.asc, a .start file, one gauge, saveint snapshots and a closed N edge, asserting the .discharge file, one .wd snapshot and the mass balance; one GDAL test that build_vrt's two tiny GeoTIFF tiles, fill_gaps a hole and reads the result with read_grid.  
  Effort: medium. Id R1-B-13.
- **The built-in engine records the maximum velocity on one time step in five** `nd_breach_runner/core/builtin_lisflood.py:470`  
  Maximum depth, time of maximum and arrival are updated on every time step (builtin_lisflood.py:456-469), but the running maximum of the cell speeds is updated only when the step number divides by five, plus once after the loop. The face discharges of the other four steps out of five are never looked at, so a short velocity peak at a cell can fall between samples. Nothing in the engine's log, its stats dict or the module docstring says the velocity is sampled rather than tracked.  
  What you see: On a run made with the built-in engine (which is what a user with no LISFLOOD-FP installed gets) the "Velocity (Max)" raster and everything taken from it can read below the engine's own peak: the depth times velocity numbers of the ND 5.2 hazard charts, and the road and rail closure flag in crossings.py, which uses depth x velocity at 7 ft2/s. The effect is largest just below the breach, where the velocity peak is shortest.  
  Fix: Update vmax on every step, as depth and arrival already are. If that measurably slows the engine, keep the interval but put it in the stats dict and in the engine's log line so the report can say the velocities are sampled every nth step.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-AV-4.
- **Two different folder-name sanitizers for the same dam ID** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascade.py:108`  
  The chain tool names run folders with workflow._safe, while the single-dam tools and the chain tool's own input lookup use algorithms._safe_name (algorithms.py L1188 and L1838). The two agree for IDs like DWR-2801 but not for IDs with spaces or punctuation: 'Absaraka Dam' reads its inputs from Dams/Absaraka Dam/inputs/ and writes the chain run to Dams/Absaraka_Dam/.  
  What you see: For a dam whose ID contains a space, the chain run's results, run report and dam.json entry land in a second folder with a slightly different name, next to the real dam folder, and the Suite home shows two dams.  
  Fix: Use one sanitizer (move _safe_name into workflow or damsuite and call it from cascade.py).  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-13.
- **The cascade report does not mention the dams below the first one that holds** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascade.py:262`  
  When a downstream dam does not overtop, the loop stops and only the dams reached so far are in out.runs. Both the report's header line and its 'Runs' list are built from out.runs alone, so for a four-dam chain that stops at the second dam the report names two dams and never says that the third and fourth were given and not considered. The Processing log has the same gap.  
  What you see: Nick selects four dams, the report is headed 'Dams upstream to downstream: A, B', and nothing says what happened to C and D. He has to work out from the Decisions list that the chain stopped at B rather than that he selected the wrong dams.  
  Fix: Keep the whole chain in the report: after the loop, list the dams that were never reached as 'not modelled (the chain stops at <dam>)', the way a dam whose run failed is already listed.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-AV-3.
- **Two hydrograph adders and a fifth hydrograph CSV writer in the two cascade modules** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascadecheck.py:198`  
  cascade.py sums two hydrographs on the union of their time steps in three numpy lines; cascadecheck.py does the same in 25 lines of pure Python (_at 181-195, add_hydrographs 198-205) because its docstring forbids numpy, although the Runner cannot run without numpy. cascadecheck.write_hydrograph (208-216) is the fifth function in core that writes a 'time_hr,discharge_cfs' CSV (hydrology.py:705, workflow.py:1233, hecras.py:170, breach.py:153), and read_hydrograph (165-178) the matching reader. The two cascade modules are also two tools for one question (chain run vs 'does the flood reach another dam?'), which the docstrings explain, but the arithmetic should not exist twice.  
  What you see: None today; a change to how the Runner writes or sums a hydrograph (header, units) would need five edits and could leave the cascade hand-off file in an old format.  
  Fix: One core/hydrograph.py with read_csv, write_csv and add (numpy), used by cascade, cascadecheck, breach, hecras, hydrology and workflow. Saves about 30 lines in the two cascade modules and four more writers elsewhere.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-14.
- **Summing two hydrographs is quadratic in their length** `nd_breach_runner/core/cascadecheck.py:204`  
  _at scans the whole list for every time, so add_hydrographs costs (len a + len b) x (len a + len b) Python steps. The Runner's breach hydrograph is written at every model step (breach.py routed_hydrograph: 10 s steps growing to 60 s over up to 240 h, so several thousand to 14,000 rows), and the downstream dam's pmf_inflow.csv can itself be a copied Runner hydrograph.  
  What you see: Two long hydrographs take tens of seconds to a few minutes of pure Python inside the cascade check, with no progress shown; the common case of a short PMF table is a few seconds.  
  Fix: Use numpy.interp on the union of times (the Runner already depends on numpy), or walk the two sorted lists once.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-10.
- **Small helpers copied around the Runner core: CSV quoting, receptor counts, nearest-cell sampler, acre constant, unused imports** `nd_breach_runner/core/compareruns.py:108`  
  Within the unit: `_cell` CSV quoting is identical in lifeloss.py:439, compareruns.py:301 and crossings.py:248 while consequences.write_rows_csv already uses csv.DictWriter for the same job; `_receptor_counts` is written twice with the same four keys (sensitivity.py:230-238, probabilistic.py:775-782); the nearest-cell sampler is written three times (consequences._sample, compareruns.sample_nearest, impacts.Receptors.sample); the acre constant 4046.8564224 is typed out in sensitivity.py:98 and :441, compareruns.py:136 and selftest.py:832 although units.ACRE exists and probabilistic.py:1305 uses it; `CFS` is imported and never used in cascade.py:34 and sensitivity.py:40.  
  What you see: None; about 40 lines to maintain in several places, and a constant that could be mistyped in one of them.  
  Fix: Add Grid.sample_nearest(x, y) to grid.py and use it from the three samplers; one receptor_counts(rec, grid, arrays, arrival_m) in impacts.py; csv.writer instead of the three `_cell`; units.ACRE in the four places; drop the two CFS imports. Saves about 40 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-13.
- **Run A is clipped to run B's extent in the comparison, with no note** `nd_breach_runner/core/compareruns.py:127`  
  Everything is computed on the finer run's grid; cells of the coarser run outside that grid are dropped and cells of the finer grid outside the coarser run read NaN and are counted as dry. The Runner grows its domain per run, so two runs of one dam at different cell sizes often have different extents. The stats a_flooded_ac, b_flooded_ac, wet_only_in_a_ac and the difference raster then cover only the overlap, and markdown_lines says only 'compared on the X m grid'.  
  What you see: The comparison's 'A flooded N ac' can be smaller than A's own run report says, and water A put beyond B's domain never shows as 'wet only in A'. The engineer reads a smaller difference than exists.  
  Fix: Build the comparison grid on the union of the two extents, or state in the report how many acres of each run lie outside the compared area.  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-A-7.
- **Three engines compare two floods: benchmark.compare_fields, compareruns.difference and the sensitivity cell-size check** `nd_breach_runner/core/compareruns.py:137`  
  benchmark.compare_fields (39-79) scores two depth rasters (fit index F, hit rate, wet acres, depth bias). compareruns.difference (120-147) computes its own wet-area statistics for the same two rasters and never reports F. sensitivity.compare_grids (88-101) wraps benchmark for the cell-size check and reports F and 'class_stable_grid', but no difference raster and no zone table. So 'Compare two runs' and the Sensitivity SENS_CELL check answer the same question (what did a finer grid change?) with different numbers and different files.  
  What you see: Nick reads 'fit index F = 0.83, class_stable_grid: yes' in Sensitivity_Report.html and 'deepest increase 0.4 ft, 3 structures change zone' in Run Comparison.md for the same two grids and cannot relate them; the two tools look like two different methods.  
  Fix: Have compareruns.difference call benchmark.compare_fields on the arrays it has already aligned (drop its 10 lines of own statistics, gain F and hit rate in Run Comparison.md), and have the sensitivity cell-size check call compareruns.difference and compareruns.zone_changes for its finer run (writes the difference raster and the zone table into Sensitivity/). One comparison engine, both reports say  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-B-8.
- **A missing velocity raster puts every structure at zero velocity in the zone table** `nd_breach_runner/core/compareruns.py:188`  
  The caller passes velocity None when a run folder has no Velocity (Max).tif (algorithms_release320.py _maybe). zone_changes then uses velocity 0, which lands most wet structures in 'low danger', whereas nd_hazard.structure_finding (L646-652) treats an unknown velocity as borderline and counts it potentially lethal. Comparing a Runner folder (velocity present) with an imported folder (velocity absent) therefore reports zone 'changes' that are only the missing raster.  
  What you see: The 'Structures whose danger zone changes' table says structures dropped from high danger to low danger when in fact one run had no velocity data; a_velocity_fps is blank in the CSV but the markdown table does not show it.  
  Fix: When a run has no velocity grid, write zone 'velocity unknown' (or follow nd_hazard and call it high danger, borderline) and say so in the report instead of using 0.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-8.
- **Conditioned terrain re-warps and re-edits the whole domain for the initial water and the roughness grid** `nd_breach_runner/core/conditioning.py:579`  
  read() warps the DEM from the (statewide) source and applies every edit, then initial_depth() warps the same bounds again and applies every edit again just to get the initial water grid that the first call already computed and threw away (Edits.apply returns it); manning() warps the DEM a third time when roughness footprints exist. workflow.py:971 and 996 call initial_depth right after read for the same bounds and cell on every domain iteration (up to max_iterations = 6, plus the baseline run).  
  What you see: A run with ponds or tailwater stages spends two or three GDAL warps of the full model domain per iteration where one would do: for a 10 m domain cut from a county LiDAR mosaic that is tens of seconds to a minute of extra waiting per pass, several minutes over a run that has to grow its domain.  
  Fix: Cache the last (bounds, cell) -> (edited grid, init grid, counts) in ConditionedTerrain.read and have initial_depth() and manning() reuse the cached base grid and init when the key matches.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-A-4.
- **Result files written without an encoding fail on Windows for non-ASCII text** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:896`  
  QGIS on Windows uses cp1252 as the default text encoding. The structures CSV carries user text (structure ids, occupancy and foundation text from the layer) and the markdown carries road names from the crossing table; a character outside cp1252 (a curly apostrophe or an accented name from a parcel or OSM layer) raises UnicodeEncodeError, which is a ValueError, not the OSError the tool catches (algorithms_consequences.py L364-366), after the CSV is partly written. lifeloss.py and compareruns.py already pass encoding='utf-8'; these do not.  
  What you see: The Processing tool stops with a Python traceback and a partly written Consequences (Structures).csv; the markdown and JSON are missing.  
  Fix: Pass encoding='utf-8' to every open(..., 'w') that writes a result file, as lifeloss.py and compareruns.py do.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-9.
- **assess_dam_folder has no caller; assess_run_result is used only by a test** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:1110`  
  A grep over all four plugins, tests and damsuite copies finds no call of assess_dam_folder, and assess_run_result only in tests/test_consequences.py L397. The Processing tool calls read_dam_folder and assess directly. Both functions pass neither rate_table nor sim_end_hr, so if someone did call them they would get the PAR section from the plugin's own file and no 'how long wet' bound, differing from the tool.  
  What you see: None today; a future caller would get a slightly different report than the Processing tool for the same folder.  
  Fix: Delete assess_dam_folder, and either delete assess_run_result with its test or route the tool through it so there is one entry point.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-A-12.
- **consequences.assess_dam_folder and assess_run_result have no production caller** `nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:1110`  
  assess_dam_folder (1110-1127) is referenced nowhere else in the four plugins (grep of every .py, tests included). assess_run_result (1130-1144) is called only by tests/test_consequences.py; workflow.py never imports consequences, and the Consequences tool calls read_dam_folder plus assess directly (algorithms_consequences.py:326, 356). Both wrappers also skip the rate_table / crossings / sim_end_hr arguments the tool passes, so anyone who did pick them up would get a summary without the 3.20.0 sections.  
  What you see: None; 35 lines that look like a supported entry point and would give a lesser result if used.  
  Fix: Delete assess_dam_folder and assess_run_result and the assess_run_result test; the tool already shows the two-call pattern. Saves 35 lines and one misleading docstring ('For the workflow').  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-10.
- **Crossings table groups receptors with a Python scan of every receptor per road** `nd_breach_runner/core/crossings.py:162`  
  For every (kind, ident) group the code runs a Python comprehension over ALL receptors (numpy string scalar compares, about a microsecond each), and the 'seen' list membership test is itself a linear scan per sample: O(groups x N) twice. Receptors are road and rail samples every 30 m (impacts.LINE_SPACING_M). A rural dam with 50 roads and a few thousand samples is fine; a dam above a town with 2,000 road features and 20,000 samples is tens of millions of comparisons.  
  What you see: After every run the consequences step (consequences._crossing_summary) sits for a minute or more on the crossings table for an urban dam, with no progress message; the result is correct, just slow.  
  Fix: Build the groups in one pass: a dict keyed by (kind, ident) whose values are lists of indices, appended while iterating np.nonzero(sel)[0] once; iterate the dict in insertion order. Same output, O(N).  
  Effort: small. Id R1-A-2.
- **The dam-folder naming rule exists twice (damjson.folder_name and algorithms._safe_name); _num and the plugin labels are re-defined** `nd_breach_runner/core/damjson.py:54`  
  The one rule that decides which folder a dam's results go into is written twice and kept equal by a comment. screenhandoff._num (77-82) repeats damjson._num (173-178); screenhandoff.SCREEN_TOOL/SCREEN_LABEL (44-45) repeat damsuite.versions.PLUGINS/LABELS["ndscreen"].  
  What you see: If one copy of the folder rule changes, the batch writes to one folder and the window, provider and popup (which use damjson.folder_name) look in another.  
  Fix: algorithms._safe_name = damjson.folder_name (delete 3 lines); screenhandoff imports damjson._num and versions.LABELS; about 12 lines fewer.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-12.
- **Four functions of 170 to 222 lines (route_flood, run_par, run_nested, hydrograph_from_settings) whose numbered stages are not functions** `nd_breach_runner/core/flood.py:130`  
  flood.route_flood is 222 lines (130-351), builtin_lisflood.run_par 206 (302-507), nested.run_nested 185 (173-357), hydrology.hydrograph_from_settings 171 (528-698). None mixes GUI with computation, but each is already divided by numbered comments into stages that are not callable on their own, so the with-breach routing loop, the engine's gauge set-up or the nested hand-over cannot be unit-tested without running the whole scenario.  
  What you see: None directly; a fix in the with-breach loop (flood.py 264-291) or the gauge geometry (builtin_lisflood.py 369-390) can only be tested through a full run.  
  Fix: Split along the existing comments: route_flood into _route_no_breach (192-217), _breach_start (219-249), _route_with_breach (251-307) and the assembly; run_par into _load_inputs (315-357), _gauge_cells (369-390), the time loop and _write_outputs (491-507); run_nested into its five numbered steps; hydrograph_from_settings into _timing (536-576), _rainfall (578-603) and the rest. No lines saved, but  
  Effort: medium. Id R1-B-11.
- **Two identical functions read a user hydrograph CSV (breach.read_user_hydrograph and flood.hydrograph_from_csv), and FloodInputs.from_files repeats the first half a third time** `nd_breach_runner/core/flood.py:354`  
  flood.py 354-364 is breach.py 241-252 with a label argument; workflow.py 1638-1639 calls one then the other on consecutive lines. FloodInputs.from_files (flood.py 82-85) repeats the read, the monotonic check and the t - t[0] shift once more.  
  What you see: None; two places to fix the next CSV quirk (a header with units, a trailing blank line).  
  Fix: Give read_user_hydrograph a what="hydrograph" parameter, delete hydrograph_from_csv and change its one caller (workflow.py:1639); have from_files call the same helper. Saves 11 lines and a function name.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-6.
- **GeoTiffWriter leaves its .work.tif behind when the re-projection fails** `nd_breach_runner/core/gdal_io.py:276`  
  With a target CRS the writer first creates path + '.work.tif' in the dam folder, then warps it to the final path. If the warp raises (GS.checked turns a None result into RuntimeError, or GDAL raises in exception mode) the function exits before drv.Delete(tmp), so the temporary file stays next to the outputs.  
  What you see: After a failed write the dam folder holds stray 'Max Depth (ft).tif.work.tif'-style files that look like outputs, and a later successful run overwrites them silently (os.path.exists check) rather than cleaning up.  
  Fix: Put the Warp in try/finally with drv.Delete(tmp) in the finally (guarded by os.path.exists), or write the work file under tempfile.mkdtemp() instead of beside the output.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-A-5.
- **Dead functions and constants: handoff.describe, products.DV_LIMIT, damjson.LABEL, batchqueue.CANCELLED (always 0), runblock.log_lines, stale DEFAULT_IDS, workflow._Skip** `nd_breach_runner/core/handoff.py:117`  
  Grepped across all four plugins and tests: handoff.describe (117-123) is never called; products.DV_LIMIT (59) is never read (impacts.DV_DANGER_FT2S is the live 7 ft2/s); damjson.LABEL (33) is never read; batchqueue.CANCELLED (51) is never assigned, the queue tool marks a cancelled dam WAITING with the message "cancelled" (algorithms_release320.py:703,718); runblock.log_lines (163-167) is called only by a test although its docstring says the run tools log it; handoff.DEFAULT_IDS (39-40) lists ndhazard:classifydam, ndhazard:rundam, ndhazard:batchclassify and ndhazard:batch_classify, which Hazard never registered (it has classify and batch); workflow._Skip (336) is never raised.  
  What you see: The queue's summary line always says "0 cancelled" even after a cancel. Otherwise none.  
  Fix: Delete them (about 30 lines); have batchqueue.summary_text drop the cancelled count or have the queue tool mark CANCELLED.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-10.
- **Helpers defined but re-spelled inline (handoff), and three copies of the same small blocks (key_settings, batchqueue item dict)** `nd_breach_runner/core/handoff.py:234`  
  handoff.py defines _is_folder_param and _param_text and then writes the same expressions out again inside build_parameters. workflow.key_settings pops the edits keys buildings, embankments and crest_raise in three identical blocks (290-298) and uses _REFINE_KEYS (302) twenty-seven lines before it is defined. batchqueue builds the item dict literal in three places (120, 166-167, 209-210).  
  What you see: None.  
  Fix: Call the two helpers; loop over ("buildings", "embankments", "crest_raise") in key_settings and move _REFINE_KEYS above it; add a _item(dam_id) helper in batchqueue. About 15 lines fewer.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-13.
- **hecras.py re-declares the run folder and file names that workflow.py and damsuite/chain.py already define** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras.py:55`  
  '_breach_model' is declared in damsuite/chain.py:35 (MODEL_FOLDER, the shared one), workflow.py:88, hecras.py:55, report_docx.py:1488 (MODEL_DIR), damsuite/suite_home.py:24 (RUNNER_FOLDER) and as a literal in products.py:807, algorithms_ndhazard.py:309 and algorithms_provenance.py:36; 'run_report.json' is a literal in a dozen places. The HEC-RAS export tool already distrusts hecras's copy: algorithms_hecras.py:63 passes workflow.MODEL_FOLDER explicitly as the model_folder argument.  
  What you see: None while all copies agree; if the run folder name ever changes, the HEC-RAS tools stop finding runs with a 'Not a finished breach run' error while the Runner itself still works.  
  Fix: hecras.py imports MODEL_FOLDER from damsuite.chain (workflow.py should too), drops HYDROGRAPH_CSV/RUN_REPORT_JSON in favour of workflow's names, and load_run/export_run lose their model_folder parameter. Saves 3 constants and 2 parameters in this unit; the same import fixes the other copies.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-8.
- **The numpy trapz/trapezoid compatibility shim is written out seven times in the Runner core** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras.py:114`  
  The same one-liner (numpy 2 renamed trapz to trapezoid; QGIS 3.34 still ships numpy 1.x, so the shim is needed) appears in hecras.py:115, anuga_worker.py:253, hydrology.py:106, flood.py:106 and breach.py:148, 180, 203, and again in nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py:435.  
  What you see: None; when the shim can go (numpy 2 as the floor) it has to be found and removed in eight places.  
  Fix: One `def integrate(y, x)` in core/units.py (or damsuite/units.py) used by the six in-plugin copies; anuga_worker keeps its own because it runs outside the plugin. Saves 6 lines and leaves one place to delete later.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-11.
- **Two HEC-RAS tools overlap: the model builder already computes the export tool's breach table but does not write it** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras.py:366`  
  'Export the breach to HEC-RAS (hydrograph and breach table)' (ndbreach:hecrasexport, 3 parameters) and 'Build a HEC-RAS 2D dam-breach model from the run' (ndbreach:hecrasmodel, 11 parameters) both exist to carry a run into HEC-RAS. The builder's breach_values (hecras_model.py:740-796) derives its values from the same breach_parameter_rows the export writes, but the model folder gets no breach table and no .u## hydrograph block; the export tool's own help ends by pointing the user to the build tool (algorithms_hecras.py:42-44).  
  What you see: Nick sees two HEC-RAS tools in the tool list and has to learn which one he needs; a user who built the model and then wants the hydrograph block has to run a second tool with a second output folder.  
  Fix: Have build_model call write_hydrograph_files and write_breach_table into its own folder (two calls) and list them in the README, then delete HecRasExportAlgorithm (algorithms_hecras.py:25-80) and export_run. One tool fewer (35 to 34 in the Runner), about 65 lines fewer; the hydrograph CSV stays in _breach_model for a user who has no DEM.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-13.
- **hecras.py keeps a second import entry point that only the tests call** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras.py:977`  
  The Processing tool does not use these: algorithms_hecras.py:161-174 calls read_mesh, grid_spec and rasterise directly and builds its own Grid. Grep of the whole repository: import_results and import_as_reference (998-1007) are called only from tests/test_hecras.py (372, 505); to_grids only from import_results; FIELD_NAMES from nowhere; 'from .grid import Grid' (line 49) serves only to_grids. The module docstring (lines 14-16) still advertises to_grids and import_as_reference as the API.  
  What you see: None today; a maintenance trap: two code paths for one import, and the tested one is not the one the tool runs.  
  Fix: Delete FIELD_NAMES, to_grids, import_results, import_as_reference and the Grid import (about 35 lines), and point the two tests at grid_spec + rasterise as the tool does. Saves 35 lines and one untrue docstring.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-2.
- **hecras_model.py re-implements four geometry helpers that its own package already has, and uses both versions in one function** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:647`  
  The module imports conditioning as cond and calls cond.densify at 848, yet defines its own densify (647-659) and uses it at 1612 and 1652. point_segment_distance (679-690) is cond.polyline_distance(...)[0] (conditioning.py:67-91) with the same clipping formula. ring_area (641-644) is terrain._area (terrain.py:335-337) line for line. _sample (1255-1277) is Grid.sample_bilinear (grid.py:71-94) on a bare array plus geotransform, same corners, same nearest-cell fallback, same outside test.  
  What you see: None in results; two densify functions with different conventions in one 555-line function is where the next off-by-one in the dam line or boundary will come from.  
  Fix: Use cond.densify (append the first point for the closed=True call at 1652), cond.polyline_distance()[0], terrain._area (make it public), and wrap the sampled array in a Grid to call sample_bilinear. Deletes about 50 lines from hecras_model.py.  
  Effort: medium. Id R4a-B-9.
- **Five of the twelve HEC-RAS builder options can be set from nowhere** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1054`  
  The Processing tool passes seven options (algorithms_hecras.py:306-312: side_buffer_ft, end_buffer_ft, cell_ft, target_cells, equation, base_flow_cfs, structures); the CLI has no HEC-RAS build command. terrain_cell, max_terrain_cells, extent_depth_ft, manning_n and courant are read through o[...] in build_model (1419-1423, 1533-1536, 1805, 1839) but no window, tool or command line can set them.  
  What you see: None; options that look configurable in the code but are not, so a reader looking for 'where do I change the Courant number' finds a knob that nothing turns.  
  Fix: Either expose the two an engineer might want (courant, manning_n) as advanced parameters, or, simpler, make all five module constants and delete them from DEFAULTS and the options plumbing. Removes 5 options and the matching o[...] lookups.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-14.
- **Every reprojected raster in the HEC-RAS builder is doubled in memory by an unnecessary conversion to 64-bit** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1220`  
  _warp asks GDAL for a Float32 in-memory raster (outputType=gdal.GDT_Float32 at line 1212) and then copies it into a float64 array, so both exist at once at four and eight bytes a cell. build_model calls it for the layout terrain at line 1555 with a cell size chosen to allow about 20 million cells, which is 80 MB for the GDAL copy plus 160 MB for the float64 copy, before the reservoir search then reshapes it again at lines 1557-1559. Nothing in the function needs 64-bit: the values are elevations and depths read from a Float32 raster.  
  What you see: Building a model for a large reservoir takes a few hundred megabytes more of QGIS's memory than it needs to, for a few seconds longer, and on a smaller machine the build can fail for lack of memory at the 'reservoir' step rather than finishing.  
  Fix: Read the band as float32 ('a = out.GetRasterBand(1).ReadAsArray()' already gives float32) and release the GDAL dataset ('out = None') before returning. The nodata test at line 1221 and everything downstream work unchanged on float32.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-BV-5.
- **The HEC-RAS builder spells out the depth raster's file name instead of using the one name the rest of the plugin shares** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1368`  
  workflow.py:89 defines FINAL_NAMES['depth'] = 'Depth (Max).tif' as the one place that name lives, and the sibling HEC-RAS import tool uses it (algorithms_hecras.py:146, workflow.FINAL_NAMES['depth']). The builder writes the name out by hand instead, and damsuite/suite_home.py:25 and report_docx.py:1528 keep two more copies.  
  What you see: None while all the copies agree. If the result raster is ever renamed, the HEC-RAS build tool stops with 'the dam folder has no Depth (Max).tif' on a run that finished perfectly, and the import tool next to it keeps working, which is a confusing pair of messages to debug.  
  Fix: At hecras_model.py:1368 use workflow.FINAL_NAMES['depth'] (the module already imports from the same package) and put the same name in the error message.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-BV-4.
- **Reservoir flood fill runs over the whole layout terrain, one dilation per cell of path** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1575`  
  `can` has the shape of the whole layout terrain `z` (up to about 20 million cells, hecras_model.py:1549-1559; the block reduction `k` is 1 whenever `res_r / 300` is smaller than `scell`, which is the case for every reservoir with a search radius of a few thousand feet). `_flood_fill` grows the region by one cell per iteration and touches the full array about eight times per iteration, so a reservoir basin a few hundred cells long costs several hundred passes over 20 MB arrays, while the fill is by construction confined to the disk of radius `res_r` around the dam (`within`).  
  What you see: The 'reservoir' step (progress 50%) of Build HEC-RAS model can sit for tens of seconds to a couple of minutes on a long flood extent with a fine DEM, with no progress change; the result is correct.  
  Fix: Crop before filling: compute the bounding box of `within` (at most about 600 cells across because `rcell >= res_r / 300`), run `_flood_fill` on `can[r0:r1, c0:c1]` with the seed shifted, and paste back. Or reuse `terrain.largest_component(can, seed)` (scipy.ndimage.label, already used by the ANUGA tier), which is one pass.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-A-5.
- **Three leftover lines that do nothing in the export and the ANUGA launcher** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1804`  
  hmax_ft is worked out in the simulation-timing block and never used again (only vmax_fps feeds the time step). 'edge |= inside & False' ORs in an all-false array, so it changes nothing. The t0 in _launch is never read; the run time is measured again in _run (anuga_model.py:248).  
  What you see: Nothing changes for the user today, but the next person to touch the time step will think the maximum depth is part of it, and will look for the launcher's timing that is not there.  
  Fix: Delete the three lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-AV-5.
- **The HEC-RAS weir coefficient 2.6 is declared three times in one module; Model defaults are always overwritten** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1846`  
  2.6 appears as STRUCT_WEIR_COEF (101), as Model's default (271) and as a bare literal for the dam weir (1846); Manning's n 0.06 appears as Model's default (265) and as the fallback in build_model (1839, `or 0.06`), with AnugaSettings carrying its own 0.06 (anuga_model.py:29). The values agree today, but nothing ties them, and build_model overwrites every Model default it sets.  
  What you see: None today; a reviewer who changes the HEC-RAS weir coefficient in one place changes the road weirs but not the dam weir, or the other way round.  
  Fix: One DAM_WEIR_COEF (or reuse STRUCT_WEIR_COEF) used at 1846; drop the Model defaults that build_model always replaces (weir_coef, manning_n, cell) or make build_model use them instead of literals. Saves 3 declarations.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-16.
- **_write_tif in hecras_model.py has no caller** `nd_breach_runner/core/hecras_model.py:1984`  
  Grep of the whole repository finds no call to hecras_model._write_tif (the only other '_write_tif' names are unrelated local helpers in ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/selftest.py:71 and tests/test_v3200.py:915). The terrain is written by write_terrain (1946), which superseded it.  
  What you see: None; 18 lines of GDAL code nobody runs or tests.  
  Fix: Delete lines 1984-2001. Saves 18 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4a-B-3.
- **Duplicated helpers inside the unit: the two-column CSV parse loop (breach vs hydrology) and densify (conditioning vs proposed)** `nd_breach_runner/core/hydrology.py:143`  
  hydrology.read_cumulative_csv 142-153 is breach.read_csv_series 223-235 retyped. conditioning.densify (98-107) and proposed.densify (nd_breach_runner/core/proposed.py 102-111, byte-identical in the Screening copy) have the same signature and algorithm; proposed's guards a zero-length line and a zero spacing, conditioning's does not (CrestRaise.apply calls it at conditioning.py:476 with 0.5 * grid.dx, so a one-point crest line reaches np.interp on an empty cumulative array only because the len(line) < 2 check above it catches it first). hecras_model.densify (647-660) is a third, list-based version.  
  What you see: None; a parsing fix for one CSV kind (semicolons, a units header) has to be made twice.

- **The engineer sign-off rule is implemented twice (lifeloss.signoff_status is a copy of chartsign.signoff_status)** `nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:105`  
  lifeloss.py lines 47 (REQUIRE_DIFFERENT_CHECKER), 66-67 (_norm_name) and 105-123 (signoff_status) repeat chartsign.py lines 69, its _norm_name and 448-469 branch for branch, differing only in the status words. The lifeloss docstring says 'chartsign.py is the pattern' and that both must follow the same rules; two copies is how they stop doing so.  
  What you see: None today; if the suite decides a second checker is required, one of the two sign-off tools would enforce it and the other not.  
  Fix: One function in damsuite (for example damsuite/signoff.py: signoff_status(signer, checker, signed_word, unsigned_word, require_different)) plus one REQUIRE_DIFFERENT_CHECKER; lifeloss and chartsign call it. Saves about 25 lines and keeps the rule in one place.

- **lifeloss.py has two renderers of the same report section and an output writer nothing calls** `nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:338`  
  markdown_lines (31 lines) and markdown_from_summary (33 lines) print the same section from two shapes of the same data; only markdown_from_summary is used in production (consequences.py:994), markdown_lines is called only by tests/test_v3200.py:348. lifeloss.write_outputs (415-436) and rows_from_csv_rows (267-278) have no caller anywhere in the four plugins (grep of all .py including tests). Meanwhile consequences.write_outputs re-implements the PAR CSV loop itself (1047-1060) instead of calling lifeloss. The private _cell quoting helper (439-441) exists only for the dead writer.

- **read_results parses the same .mass file three times through two near-identical readers; source_volume re-implements Hydrograph.volume_until; the edge-letter tuple is defined twice** `nd_breach_runner/core/lisflood.py:486`  
  _read_mass (289-308) and _read_series (311-326) are the same split-and-float loop, one returning scalars and one arrays; read_results opens the file at 486, 511 and again inside late_inflow (515 -> 335). source_volume (536-542) is Hydrograph.volume_until (breach.py 176-180) for a dict. terrain.EDGES (367) and lisflood.EDGE_LETTERS (31) are the same tuple, and builtin_lisflood.py:124 types it a third time.

  units.py defines FT, ACRE, CFS, ACFT and is imported by 22 Runner files, yet read_results types the digits out 12 times (lisflood.py 492, 493, 495, 496, 499, 500, 507, 516, 517, 525, 526), terrain.py 6 times (111, 121, 124, 141, 144, 147), conditioning.py 3 times (322, 371, 391) and nested.py once (341); 53 such literals in core/ in all. conditioning.py:550 likewise types nodata=-9999.0 instead of grid.NODATA while importing Grid from the same module and never using it.

- **The ACER chart polygons ship as two JSON copies generated from a table that is already shared in damsuite** `nd_breach_runner/core/nd_hazard.py:235`  
  acer_tm11_charts.json (identical in nd_breach_runner/core and nd_dam_screening/core) is not a primary record: it is the output of damsuite.tm11_curves.default_chart_file() (tm11_curves.py:185-229), which builds the polygons from the CURVES table signed by SIGNED_OFF_BY on SIGNED_OFF_ON. The table, the signer and the date therefore exist in three files per plugin (tm11_curves.py, the JSON, and the Hazard Helper's chartsign writer), and ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_v2281.py:59 exists only to check the sibling JSON copies still match. damsuite is already present in every plugin, so nd_hazard could build the ChartSet from the table directly.  
  What you see: None today. When the engineer re-signs or corrects the curves (chartsign) the JSON copies and the table can disagree, and the 'signed off by ... SHA-256 ...' line in every classification report then names a file that no longer matches the table it claims to come from.  
  Fix: After R3-B-3, let nd_hazard.load_charts(path=None) call tm11_curves.default_chart_file() when no path is given (a ChartSet from a dict, the same code that reads the JSON) and delete both acer_tm11_charts.json files, CHART_FILE, and the sibling-copy test in test_v2281.py:59-70. Keep load_charts(path) for a chart file the Hazard Helper's sign-off tool writes. Saves 2 data files, one constant and one  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-4.
- **Unused imports in nine of the seventeen files (twelve names), including numpy in outlets.py** `nd_breach_runner/core/outlets.py:30`

  What you see: None; they mislead a reader about what a module depends on (gaps.py looks as if it edits terrain; outlets.py looks numeric).  
  Fix: Delete the twelve names and the local import (about 12 lines). pyflakes lists them all.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-10.
- **Test-only and compat code in probabilistic.py and nd_hazard.py** `nd_breach_runner/core/probabilistic.py:185`  
  Dist.cdf (23 lines) and ProbSpec.from_text (24 lines) are called only by tests; the tool builds its spec with ProbSpec.from_parts (algorithms_probabilistic.py). LEGACY_RUNS_FOLDER and the branch at 1210-1213 look for a lower-case 'runs' folder from studies begun with 3.1.x; the release is 3.20.3 and a resumed study already needs the same seed, dam and settings. nd_hazard.save_charts (262-274, in both copies) is also test-only: the Hazard Helper writes chart files through tm11_curves.chart_file, not through nd_hazard.  
  What you see: None; about 80 lines that must be kept working and read for nothing.  
  Fix: Delete Dist.cdf, ProbSpec.from_text, LEGACY_RUNS_FOLDER with its branch, and nd_hazard.save_charts, with their tests. Saves about 80 lines across the two files (save_charts twice while R3-B-3 is not done).  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-11.
- **run_probabilistic is 286 lines; consequences.assess 172 and run_sensitivity 164** `nd_breach_runner/core/probabilistic.py:951`  
  One function does the base run, decides which breach methods can be drawn, draws the samples, builds the run keys, resumes finished runs, runs every sample, computes the statistics, re-classifies resumed runs and writes the rasters (951-1236, 286 lines, with nested closures). The same shape appears in consequences.assess (687-858, 172 lines: evaluation, row building, gap counting, notes, summary, roads, PAR, crossings) and sensitivity.run_sensitivity (304-467, 164 lines). None of them mixes GUI code, but each is too long to read against the tests, and probabilistic.py as a whole is 1551 lines.  
  What you see: None directly; this is where a fix to one step (for example the resume logic at 1065-1070 and 1206-1220, which appears twice) is easy to get wrong without noticing.  
  Fix: Split run_probabilistic into three plain functions that already have natural boundaries in the code: _prepare_study (990-1094: base run, methods, samples, keys, time estimate), _run_samples (1095-1176) and _summarize (1178-1234), each taking and returning the ProbabilisticResult. Same for assess: pull the row loop (732-763) and the gaps/notes block (765-818) into helpers. No behaviour change, no n  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-B-7.
- **A locked Breach Products.gpkg aborts write_products before the EAP map and the hydrograph plot are written** `nd_breach_runner/core/products.py:276`  
  write_gpkg deletes the previous 'Breach Products.gpkg' in place. When that GeoPackage is loaded in QGIS on Windows, os.remove raises PermissionError, the exception leaves write_products, and workflow.run_dam turns it into one warning ('Could not write the vector products / map'). The GeoJSON files written before it survive, but everything after the GeoPackage in write_products never runs: the EAP preview, hydrograph.svg and gauges.svg are not written and the HTML report has no plots. write_gpkg also leaves the OGR dataset open when any later step raises.  
  What you see: Nick keeps last run's products layer in the map, re-runs the dam, and the run report has no outflow hydrograph plot and no map; the only clue is one warning about the GeoPackage.  
  Fix: Write the GeoPackage last and inside its own try, so a locked file costs only the GeoPackage; write it to a temporary name and os.replace it, and close the dataset in a finally. Name the locked file in the warning and say to close it in QGIS.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-11.
- **PNG/JPEG header reader written twice in the suite** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:256`  
  ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/chartsign.py:525-560 has its own image_info that walks the same PNG IHDR and JPEG SOF markers to get width and height; the Runner's version (256-309) does the same plus the DPI chunks. The suite already shares code through the damsuite package (byte-identical in all four plugins), which is where one copy belongs.  
  What you see: None today; two parsers of the same binary formats to keep correct (the JPEG marker loops differ in which markers they skip).  
  Fix: Move report_docx.image_info and read_image into damsuite (for example damsuite/images.py), import it in report_docx and in chartsign.py, delete chartsign.image_info. Saves about 35 lines in Hazard.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-11.
- **Unused library knobs, unused functions and a dead result variable** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:400`  
  Grepping all four plugins and their tests: Docx.is_empty_since (400), SubmittalResult.as_dict (1725) and verification.stoker_velocity (212) have no caller at all; ritter_velocity (144) and Docx.bullets(ordered=True) (467) are called only by tests; the run-property flags u, strike, sup in _rpr (81) and tabs, ind_left, ind_hanging in _ppr (152-153) and table(zebra=, header_rows=) (526-527) are never passed by any caller. In sec_flood the variable `wrote` is set four times (2241, 2243, 2253, 2263) and ignored because the function returns True (2264). Line 2428 `if not (feats and unit_scale != 1.0) and not feats:` reduces to `if not feats:`. The METHOD_LABELS try/except import is written twice (  
  What you see: None today; every unused knob is code a future change must keep working and the dead `wrote` suggests the Flood section can be dropped when it cannot, which misleads the next editor.  
  Fix: Delete the unused methods, flags and table options; in sec_flood drop `wrote` or return it; simplify line 2428 to `if not feats:`; one `_method_label()` helper; make find_hazard/find_consequences unconditional and apply the include_consequences switch only in sec_receptors. Saves about 70 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-6.
- **Docx.save leaves a .docx.tmp behind when the target cannot be replaced** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1009`  
  The temporary file is written next to the target and os.replace is not guarded. When the target .docx is open in Word (Windows denies the replace) the PermissionError is raised to the caller as intended, but the complete .tmp copy stays in the dam folder or verification folder. The file-list walker (line 3115) hides .tmp files, so the leftover never shows in later reports and is not cleaned by anything.  
  What you see: Each failed save adds a multi-megabyte 'Submittal.docx.tmp' or 'Verification Record.docx.tmp' to the folder that Nick submits or archives.  
  Fix: Wrap os.replace in try/except OSError: remove tmp (ignoring errors) and re-raise. Alternatively write the tmp with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(dir=d, delete=False) and clean it the same way.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-4.
- **Five SVG-to-PNG converters where one or two are needed** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1278`  
  The report runs inside QGIS, where Qt's QSvgRenderer is always present and is tried first (1328-1346). The four fallbacks (cairosvg 1349, rsvg-convert 1354, inkscape 1364 with two command-line forms, LibreOffice 1377 with the Draw-module probe _soffice_has_draw 1405-1422) plus their detection in available_svg_backends (1425-1445) are about 100 lines, needed only when the code runs outside QGIS (tests, scripts/ndbreach_cli.py). The LibreOffice path already needed a 3.9.0 bug fix and its own test (test_v390.py:476-493). The algorithm help text (algorithms_report.py:33-35) tells the user to install these programs.  
  What you see: The help for the Submittal report names four external programs Nick never needs inside QGIS; when a figure is not embedded the error list carries up to five backend messages (2054).  
  Fix: Keep Qt and cairosvg (pure Python, pip-installable for runs outside QGIS); delete _svg_rsvg, _svg_inkscape, _svg_soffice, _soffice_has_draw, their detection and the test_v390 LibreOffice test; shorten the help text to 'embedded when QGIS can render them'. Saves about 75 lines, one test and three names in the help.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-9.
- **report_docx.py is a 1,480-line document library plus a 1,770-line report in one file** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1485`  
  Lines 1-1083 are a generic OOXML writer (Docx, inspect_docx), 1086-1272 a markdown converter, 1274-1482 an SVG-to-PNG converter, and 1485-3252 the submittal report. verification.py (2,230 lines) imports the whole 3,252-line module only for Docx and svg_to_png (1835, 2133), so the two largest modules of the Runner depend on each other's unrelated halves. verification.py in turn bundles validation_record (1980-2230, 250 lines), a separate tool that reads benchmark.py's folder and shares nothing with the analytic suite but _signoff and _fmt. No function exceeds 150 lines, but sec_results (2370-2519) is exactly 150 and checklist_items (2904-3052) 149.  
  What you see: None at run time; for maintenance a fix to the Word writer and a fix to the checklist land in the same file, and the verification module cannot be read without the report module.  
  Fix: Split report_docx.py into core/docx.py (Docx, add_markdown, svg_to_png, inspect_docx; about 1,480 lines) and core/submittal.py (the _Report class and build_submittal); move validation_record, read_benchmark_folder, guess_dam_id and BENCH_* into benchmark.py, which writes what they read. No lines saved; two modules of 3,252 and 2,230 lines become four of 1,480, 1,770, 1,980 and about 900.  
  Effort: medium. Id R4b-B-8.
- **Eight submittal options nobody in QGIS can set** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:1690`  
  SubmittalOptions declares 23 options; its only non-test caller, algorithms_report.py:76-88, sets 15. `date`, `author`, `page` and `update_fields` are set by nothing in the repository, tests included; `include_toc`, `max_table_rows`, `svg_backends` and `created` are set only by tests. The Docx constructor likewise takes margins_in, font, language that no caller ever passes.  
  What you see: None visible; the options exist only as untested paths (for example a4 pages with the hard-coded 6.3 in figure widths at 2063 were never exercised in a real report).  
  Fix: Delete date, author, page, update_fields from SubmittalOptions and margins_in, font, language from Docx; keep include_toc, max_table_rows, svg_backends, created as module constants or explicit test hooks. Saves eight settings and about 15 lines; or, if any is wanted, expose it in algorithms_report.py so it is reachable.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-7.
- **A figure that cannot be embedded puts the local folder path into the submittal** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:2069`  
  src is the absolute path that _Files.find returned, so the paragraph prints the whole path from the drive letter down. Everywhere else the report names files relative to the dam folder (os.path.relpath at 2065, 2743 and in the file list), and this is the only place that does not.  
  What you see: When no SVG converter is available, the submittal that goes to the state carries a line such as 'EAP inundation map (not embedded; the file is C:\\Users\\nick\\Dams\\DWR-2801\\EAP Inundation Map (preview).svg)', showing the engineer's own folder layout instead of a file name.  
  Fix: Print os.path.relpath(src, self.folder) with the separators turned into forward slashes, as the checklist and file list already do, and keep the absolute path only in res.figures for the log.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-BV-4.
- **The 'Flood scenario' section is printed even when it has nothing in it** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:2264`  
  sec_flood carefully tracks whether it wrote anything in the variable `wrote` (lines 2240, 2242, 2252, 2263) and then returns a hard True at line 2264, so the variable is never used (pyflakes flags it too). run_section only drops a section and its heading when the section returns a false value, so this section is always kept. The scenario flag comes from the run report's own `scenario` field (line 1961), not from the flood block, so a run report that says 'flood' but has no flood block, no flood settings, no inflow volume and no incremental results produces a heading with nothing under it.  
  What you see: In that case the submittal report shows a numbered section 'Flood scenario' that is completely empty, and it is listed in the table of contents, which looks like something was lost. Every other section correctly disappears when it has no content.  
  Fix: Change the last line of sec_flood to `return wrote`.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-AV-3.
- **Arrival-zone 'units taken as metres' note can never fire in the case it was written for** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:2428`  
  The condition is logically identical to `not feats`. The sub-expression `(feats and unit_scale != 1.0)` was meant to cover the case where flood polygons exist but their acres/geometry ratio fell outside 0.05-20 (lines 2397-2400), leaving unit_scale at 1.0 while the geometry is not in metres; in that case the note is suppressed and the 'Area reached (ac)' column is computed with the wrong unit and no warning. Arrival Zones.geojson carries no acres property of its own (products.py:614), so the report has no other source for those areas.  
  What you see: If the acres check fails, the arrival-time table shows wrong areas with no note saying they were derived from geometry under an assumed unit; when flood polygons are absent the note shows, which is the only case the code handles.  
  Fix: Track whether the scale was actually derived: `scaled = False` ... set True inside the 0.05-20 branch; then `if not scaled: self.note(...)`.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-A-5.
- **Two Yes/No boxes on the submittal dialog can only remove wanted sections** `nd_breach_runner/core/report_docx.py:3197`  
  Both sections already leave themselves out when there is nothing to show: sec_sensitivity returns False when there is no Sensitivity folder, no cascade report and no probabilistic file (2709-2761), and sec_repro returns False when there is no run manifest and no run report (2834-2858), and run_section then rolls the heading back. The two switches, exposed as 'Include sensitivity, cascade and probabilistic results' and 'Include reproducibility (run manifest, run settings)' at algorithms_report.py:53-57, therefore only ever remove content the folder does hold.  
  What you see: Two more Yes/No rows to read on an already long Submittal report dialog, and the only thing they can do is leave the sensitivity work or the run manifest out of a submittal that should show them.  
  Fix: Delete the two parameters from algorithms_report.py and the two options from SubmittalOptions, and call run_section for both sections unconditionally. The dialog loses two rows and the report keeps the same output for every folder.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-BV-5.
- **screenhandoff drops an unreadable recorded value silently and then says the Screening 'has not recorded' it** `nd_breach_runner/core/screenhandoff.py:152`  
  The module docstring (lines 28-30) promises that a recorded cell size outside 0.5-500 m or an unknown method 'is reported as unreadable rather than quietly ignored, because a silently dropped hand-off is the bug this module exists for'. _result_values does the opposite: when the key is present but cell_from / method_from / scenario_from return None, nothing is noted and the loop breaks. read() then lists the key under 'missing', and missing_text says 'ND Dam Breach Screening has not recorded the grid size it ran', which is false: it recorded one the Runner could not read.  
  What you see: If a screened result ever carries a value in another spelling or unit (a cell in feet, a method label from another release), the window tells Nick the Screening did not record it, so he looks in the wrong place. Today the Screening writes exactly the Runner's keys (damrecord_io.py:204-233), so this only bites on a foreign or hand-edited dam.json.  
  Fix: When k is in result and parse returns None, append a note: '{key} in dam.json is {value!r}, not a cell size between 0.5 and 500 m / not one of the Runner's breach methods', and do not list the field as missing.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-9.
- **The screening hand-off never reaches the run report, although the code for it exists** `nd_breach_runner/core/screenhandoff.py:328`  
  The hand-off values (the grid size, the breach method and the scenario the Screening actually ran) are shown in the window and used to fill empty inputs, but the section written for the run report is never added to it. The report records the cell size and method that were used and not that they came from ND Dam Breach Screening, nor what the Screening failed to record.  
  What you see: Somebody reading run_report.md, the HTML report or the submittal a year later cannot see that the 20 m grid and the piping method came from the screening run of that dam, so the choice looks arbitrary and cannot be traced.
