  Fix: Read screenhandoff.read(out_folder) once in run_dam, keep it as report['screening_handoff'], and add screenhandoff.report_lines(report['screening_handoff']) to render_report next to the dam.json section; or delete the function if the window is meant to be the only place it shows.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-AV-5.
- **selftest.MIN_QGIS duplicates metadata.txt's qgisMinimumVersion** `nd_breach_runner/core/selftest.py:55`  
  The minimum QGIS version is written in metadata.txt (what QGIS enforces) and again as MIN_QGIS in selftest.py (what the self-test reports). plugin_version() in the same file already reads metadata.txt line by line, so the second copy is avoidable.  
  What you see: None today; if the minimum is raised in metadata.txt the self-test would still say 'the plugin needs QGIS 3.28 or newer' and pass on a QGIS the plugin no longer loads in.  
  Fix: Read qgisMinimumVersion from metadata.txt in the same loop as plugin_version() and turn '3.28' into 32800; delete MIN_QGIS. One constant fewer.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-16.
- **Sensitivity factor defaults typed twice, and the Probabilistic tool's width default disagrees with them** `nd_breach_runner/core/sensitivity.py:163`  
  The sensitivity tool hard-codes its default factor strings in algorithms.py instead of deriving them from sensitivity.default_cases (the same tool already derives SENS_MAX_CELLS from sensmod.DEFAULT_MAX_CELLS at 1340). The Probabilistic tool, which varies the same three quantities, uses a different default spread for breach width (0.5 to 1.5 against 0.7 to 1.4) with the same justification text (Froehlich scatter) in both docstrings.  
  What you see: Nick runs Sensitivity and Probabilistic on one dam and the width range differs between the two reports for no stated reason; a future change of the sensitivity default in one place leaves the other behind.  
  Fix: One WIDTH_FACTORS / TIME_FACTORS / MANNING_FACTORS triple in sensitivity.py used by default_cases, by the SENS_* defaults in algorithms.py (join the tuple into the string) and by probabilistic.default_width/time/manning (min, 1.0, max). Removes two typed-out copies and one unexplained difference.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-15.
- **Sensitivity keeps every case's arrays in memory and then stacks them** `nd_breach_runner/core/sensitivity.py:416`  
  Each case's four float64 arrays stay on its RunResult for the whole study, then four stacks copy them again and np.median makes a further copy. At the default cell budget of 4,000,000 cells (L45) one run holds 4 x 32 MB; with the base and up to twelve cases that is about 1.7 GB held, another 1.7 GB in the stacks and more for the median, roughly 3.5 GB inside the QGIS process. probabilistic.py avoids exactly this with float32 .npy files read in blocks (its docstring says why).  
  What you see: On a laptop with little free memory a full sensitivity study of a large domain can end in a MemoryError or a QGIS crash after all the model runs have finished.  
  Fix: Save each case's arrays as float32 .npy as probabilistic._save_run does and build the envelope rasters block by block (blockwise_stats already exists), or at least drop r.final_si after each case is added to running min/max and keep only depth for the median.  
  Effort: medium. Id R3-A-11.
- **Unit constants re-typed instead of imported from units.py: FT, the acre, WET_M** `nd_breach_runner/core/structures.py:110`  
  structures.py defines its own FT although it could import units.FT like its neighbours; the acre in square metres is typed as a literal six times in workflow.py and products.py while units.ACRE exists; the wet threshold 0.003 m is defined in workflow, again in stoprule (which cannot import workflow because workflow imports it) and as a default in damjson.extent_ft.  
  What you see: None today; a changed wet threshold in workflow would leave the class-stop rule and dam.json's extent using the old one.  
  Fix: from .units import FT, ACRE in structures.py, workflow.py and products.py; move WET_M to units.py and import it in workflow, stoprule and damjson. Removes nine literals and two definitions.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-11.
- **culvert_candidates compares every road segment within 45 miles against every flow-path point in pure Python** `nd_breach_runner/core/structures.py:262`  
  st.road_lines is every road feature within 0.75 x MAX_EXTENT (45 mi at the 60 mi default) of the dam, returned whole by qgis_io.lines. culvert_candidates then runs a triple Python loop with a function call per pair: road segments x flow-path points (about 270 points for the 5 mi trace at 30 m). With the suite's statewide roads pack a 45 mi circle holds tens of thousands of road segments, so this is on the order of 10 million _seg_cross calls, plus an O(n) scan of `out` and `given` per hit. There is no bounding-box test: a road 40 miles away is compared vertex by vertex against the path.  
  What you see: After 'tracing the flow path' the run sits for a long time before the first LISFLOOD-FP pass starts, with the progress bar at 3 percent and no message; the wait grows with the roads layer, not with the dam.  
  Fix: Before the loops, compute the path's bounding box padded by merge_m and skip any road whose own bounding box misses it (a numpy min/max per road); then skip segments the same way. That removes almost every road and keeps the result identical. Optionally vectorise _seg_cross over the path's segments with numpy.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-10.
- **Dead code: six of the seven unit helpers, the shared damsuite/units.py nobody imports, expand_bounds' limits argument and piping_hydrograph** `nd_breach_runner/core/units.py:13`  
  ft_to_m, acft_to_m3, m3_to_acft, ac_to_m2, cms_to_cfs and cfs_to_cms (units.py 13-38) have no caller in any of the four plugins or their tests (grep -w: 0 uses each); only m_to_ft is used (11 places). The shared damsuite/units.py (copied into all four plugins, hash-checked by FILES.json) defines the same five constants under other names (ACRE_M2, AF_M3, CFS_M3S, MILE_M) plus the same helpers, and no file in the repo imports it (grep for damsuite.units / damsuite import units: none). terrain.expand_bounds(bounds, edges, step_m, limits=None) (terrain.py 379) never reads limits and both callers pass three arguments (workflow.py 1182, run_tests.py 674). breach.piping_hydrograph (690-692) is call  
  What you see: None; dead weight that a reader has to rule out, and a shared module that looks authoritative and is not.  
  Fix: Delete the six helpers (18 lines), the limits parameter, and piping_hydrograph (tests call routed_hydrograph(dam) directly). Either delete damsuite/units.py from all four copies (about 55 lines x 4 and a FILES.json entry) or add G and US_FT to it and make core/units.py a five-line re-export; today's state, two unit modules with different names for the same numbers, is the worst of both.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-4.
- **Second SVG line-plot writer and third tick-step helper** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:314`  
  products.line_plot_svg (products.py:490-547) draws the same kind of plot (axes, grid, legend, vertical marks) for the hydrograph and gauge figures and the probabilistic module; verification.plot_svg (314-430) is a superset of it (NaN breaks, markers, dashes, axis limits, tick decimals). Each has its own 'nice step' helper: verification._nice_step (284), products._nice (335) and a third animation._nice (animation.py:634).  
  What you see: The hydrograph figure and the verification plots in the same Word submittal use different tick labelling and legend styles; a fix to one plot writer (for example the legend overlapping the data) does not reach the other.  
  Fix: Move plot_svg and _nice_step/_ticks to products.py; make hydrograph_plot, the gauge plot (products.py:685) and probabilistic.py:1364 call it with the dict series form; delete line_plot_svg, products._nice and animation._nice. Saves about 70 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-4.
- **Two number formatters and two status-colour tables for the same Word reports** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:549`  
  report_docx.fmt_val (1641-1660) and verification._fmt (549-572) format the numbers of two Word reports from the same plugin with different rules: 2500 prints as '2500' in the submittal and '2,500' in the Verification Record; 1234.5 as '1,234' and '1234.5'; 0.000123 as '0' and '1.23e-04'. STATUS_SHADES is defined in both files with the same hex colours (report_docx 59, verification 78) and 'E2EFDA' appears again as a literal at verification.py:2187. verification._trapz (256-261) is a copy of hydrology._trapz (hydrology.py:105).  
  What you see: The same quantity is printed differently in the submittal report and the verification/validation records that go in the same submittal package; a reviewer sees '1,234' in one and '1234.5' in the other.  
  Fix: Keep one formatter in report_docx (add fmt_val's missing small-number branch), import it in verification and delete _fmt; one STATUS_SHADES (report_docx) keyed by lower-case status; use hydrology._trapz or numpy. Saves about 35 lines and one duplicated colour table.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-B-5.
- **The Verification Record's software table shows three rows all labelled 'SHA-256' with nothing saying which file each belongs to** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1887`  
  machine_info deliberately indents the fingerprint rows with two spaces so they read as belonging to the file above them (' SHA-256' and ' size, modified', lines 1603-1605). Both the Word record (line 1887) and the markdown summary (line 1731) strip that indent, and the record can carry three such groups (the LISFLOOD-FP program, the ANUGA interpreter and the ANUGA worker script), so the table ends up with three identical bold 'SHA-256' rows and three 'size, modified' rows.  
  What you see: In the one table of a signed record whose job is to identify the software by its fingerprint, the reader has to count rows to work out which hash belongs to which program.  
  Fix: Keep the grouping instead of stripping it: either leave the leading spaces in place (Word preserves them) or indent those rows with a prefix such as an en dash, in both the Word table and the markdown table.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-AV-4.
- **Each skipped ANUGA case gets its own almost empty page in the Verification Record** `nd_breach_runner/core/verification.py:1891`  
  A case that was skipped is a bare case dict with no description, set-up, criteria, measures or plot (run_suite 1643-1647), yet the loop gives it a level-1 heading with a forced page break, a one-row result table and a note. With no ANUGA interpreter, which is the normal install, three of the eight cases are skipped.  
  What you see: The Verification Record that goes to a reviewer has three pages holding one line each ('not run: no ANUGA interpreter'), and three entries in the table of contents for work that was never done.  
  Fix: In write_record, write the cases that ran as now, and gather the skipped ones into one short 'Cases not run' section with a two-column table of case and reason. The summary table at the top already lists them.  
  Effort: small. Id R4b-BV-3.
- **The Runner's release number is written in three places and one test pins the literal, so both must be edited at every release; a feature version is printed into the run report** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:58`  
  The version lives in metadata.txt:8, damsuite/versions.py RELEASE["ndbreach"] and workflow.py:58 VERSION_FALLBACK. test_v3200.py v3200_the_release_is_3_20_0_everywhere_it_is_written asserts the literal "3.20.3" twice (906, 909), so it fails on the next release until someone edits it (its name still says 3.20.0; it has been edited once already). refine.py:374 and workflow.py:1794 print "(v3.4.0)" into the user's run report, a feature version that means nothing to the reader.  
  What you see: A reader of the report sees a stray "v3.4.0" next to results from version 3.20.3; developers get a red test at every release.  
  Fix: VERSION_FALLBACK = versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"] (one place left besides metadata.txt); make the test compare md == versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"] == wf.VERSION with no literal and read the changelog's first token from md; drop "(v3.4.0)" from the two report strings. Saves one edit per release.  
  Effort: small. Id R1-B-12.
- **VERSION_FALLBACK is a fourth hand-kept copy of the release number, and test_v3200 pins the literal so it breaks every release** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:58`  
  The release number lives in metadata.txt, damsuite/versions.RELEASE, this literal and the test. Can it go stale? Yes: it is read only when metadata.txt cannot be opened, so a forgotten bump shows nothing in QGIS; only the tests (test_v3180:247 "keep the fallback literal in step with metadata.txt", test_v3200:906) catch it, and test_v3200 is written to fail on the next release until its literal is edited (its name still says 3_20_0). metadata.txt is also read by three separate readers: workflow.metadata_version (61-76), selftest.plugin_version (204-214) and damsuite.versions._metadata_version.  
  What you see: None while the tests run. A lagging fallback would stamp the previous release number into a run report and dam.json on a machine whose metadata.txt is unreadable.  
  Fix: VERSION_FALLBACK = versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"] (damsuite is already imported at line 56), compare to that in test_v3200 instead of a literal, and have selftest.plugin_version call workflow.metadata_version. One literal and ten lines fewer; one place to bump besides metadata.txt.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-8.
- **Checkpoint state.json is written in place, not atomically, so a crash during the write loses the whole resume** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:442`  
  The file is truncated and rewritten in place. If QGIS is killed or the machine goes down in that moment, state.json is empty or cut short; on the next run json.load raises ValueError, which __init__ swallows, resumed is False, and the run starts from pass 1 with the message 'No matching checkpoint'. batchqueue.save (124-133) already does it right with a .tmp and os.replace.  
  What you see: After a crash the resume that 3.x promises is lost without explanation; all finished LISFLOOD-FP passes run again.  
  Fix: Write to self.path + '.tmp' and os.replace it, exactly as batchqueue.save does.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-A-8.
- **Internal feature version numbers printed in the engineer's run report** `nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:1794`  
  Three report strings carry the plugin version in which the feature first appeared.  
  What you see: A report written by 3.20.3 says "Near the dam (v3.4.0)" and "Class-stop rule (v3.2.0)", which reads like a version mismatch to someone who does not know the changelog.  
  Fix: Drop the parenthesised versions from the three strings.  
  Effort: small. Id R2-B-9.
- **_escape_tab_mnemonics is a shim copied from Hazard that no Runner tab title needs** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:93`  
  The docstring describes a Hazard tab ('Setup & Run'); the Runner's tabs are Dam, Model, Data, Results, Checks, Advanced, Settings (popup_logic.TAB_ORDER plus two literals) and the settings grid's inner tabs come from the same TAB_ORDER, none with '&' (grepped every addTab in the Runner: the only '&' titles are suite_home.py's deliberate mnemonics, not this dialog). The copy in nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:61 differs only in its docstring.  
  What you see: None; 13 lines that run on every open and change nothing.  
  Fix: Delete dialog.py:93-104 and the call at 131.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-15.
- **What in this unit has no test, and one test that checks the source text instead of the behaviour** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:258`  
  No test in the four plugins references: dialog.py's DEM box (_dem_download_box, _show_counties, _county_from_map, _update_dem_info), the rate-table box (_life_loss_box, _fill_rates, _save_rates, _open_rate_file), _refresh_ready (the 'why is this button grey' logic), _refresh_handoff, _apply_handoff, _open_other, _model_paths, the settings-file methods (_pick_settings_file, _load_settings_file, _autosave_settings); plugin.py run_load_layers; scripts/patch_lisflood.py (no test at all); ndbreach_cli.apply_layers (structures, channels, ponds, gauges from GeoJSON). tests/test_v3200.py:88-93 covers the DEM-box collapse by hasattr and by searching dialog.py's text for 'setCheckable(True)', which pa  
  What you see: The parts of the window most visible to the user (the DEM box, the grey-button reasons, the rate table) can break without a test noticing.  
  Fix: R6-B-1, R6-B-12 and R6-B-13 remove most of the untested methods. For what stays (_refresh_ready, the settings-file methods, run_load_layers, apply_layers), drive them through the fake Qt that test_popup.py already uses to build NDBreachRunnerDialog, and replace the source-text assertions in test_v3200.py:88-93 with a call that toggles the box.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-B-17.
- **_to_lonlat and _gdal_translate are byte-identical in dialog.py and wizard.py; the path-equality idiom is copied five times** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:450`  
  dialog.py:450-457 and 476-483 are the same text as wizard.py:95-102 and 115-122 (the wizard's docstring even says 'as dialog.py does'). dialog.py already imports wizard (line 1159). The expression os.path.normcase(os.path.abspath(a)) == os.path.normcase(os.path.abspath(b)) is written out at dialog.py:489-490, popup_logic.py:504-506, wizard.py:193-197, wizard_logic.py:435-436 and suite_fill.py:47-51.  
  What you see: None today; a fix to one copy (for example a different GeoTIFF compression or a CRS edge case) will miss the other.  
  Fix: Keep the two helpers in wizard.py only and import them in dialog.py; add one same_path(a, b) to popup_logic and use it in the five places. Saves about 25 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-5.
- **When the download tool returns no file name, the window's DEM box does nothing and says nothing** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:555`  
  The wizard hit this case and handled it in 3.18.0: wizard_logic.finish_dem_download falls back to find_downloaded_dem (looking for the file the tool wrote since the button was pressed) and, when there is still nothing, writes 'No DEM was downloaded ... The DEM choice is unchanged' on the page. The window's box, written in 3.9.0, has neither: it returns an empty string with no fallback, no log line and no message, so the DEM is not recorded in dam.json, not added to the map and not put in the DEM choice.  
  What you see: The engineer presses 'Download DEM...', the tool runs, and the window looks exactly as it did before, with nothing in the log to say whether the download failed, was cancelled, or worked and was then thrown away.  
  Fix: Use wizard_logic.dem_download_params and wizard_logic.finish_dem_download from the window too (the same change R6-B-1 asks for), so the window gets the wizard's fallback and its message; at the very least log one line in the empty case.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-BV-4.
- **'Use the screening's values' duplicates the automatic hand-off, and the hand-off text is shown twice** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:654`  
  popup_logic.defaults (324-325) already fills CELL, METHOD and SCENARIO from the screening whenever a run tool opens and the window still shows the starting grid size (handoff_defaults, 334-381). The Advanced tab's button (654-660, _apply_handoff 1105-1122) only copies the cell size, which the automatic path covers; and _refresh_handoff (1081-1103) writes the same sentence into handoff_note on the header (192-195) and handoff_label on Advanced (650-652).  
  What you see: A button whose effect the user cannot tell from what happens anyway, and the same grey sentence in two places.  
  Fix: Delete handoff_btn, _apply_handoff and handoff_label (dialog.py:650-660, 1105-1122 and the label lines of _refresh_handoff); keep the header note and the automatic hand-off. Saves about 40 lines, one button, one label.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-13.
- **Two ways to sign the fatality rate table: the Advanced tab's grid and the 'signrates' Processing tool** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:667`  
  dialog.py:667-777 (110 lines, 9 rate cells, 3 name edits, 2 buttons on the Advanced tab) and algorithms_release320.py:728 onwards (a Processing tool with a button on the Checks tab, popup_logic.py:91-93) both end in lifeloss.write_rate_file on the same core/fatality_rates.json. The tool asks for the nine rates typed as 'high/under15 = 0.9, medium/15to60 = 0.03' in one text box, which is the harder of the two forms; the window's grid shows the status line ('Loss of life IS estimated' or not) that the tool only logs.  
  What you see: Two buttons in one window for one sign-off; an engineer who used one does not know whether the other shows the same table.  
  Fix: Delete the signrates tool, its TABS row (popup_logic.py:91-93) and its tests in test_v3200.py (parse_text); keep the Advanced tab's grid. Saves one Processing tool, one button and about 95 lines in algorithms_release320.py.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-12.
- **A DEM chosen as a project layer that was later removed stays as a cryptic item and the run buttons stay enabled** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:970`  
  _fill_dem keeps whatever the saved state holds. When the saved value is a layer id (the DEM box stores layer.id() at line 511, e.g. 'dem_10m_a1b2c3...') and that layer is no longer in the project, the id is added as an item with the id as its text and stays selected. settings()['dem'] is then non-empty, so runblock says the DEM is there and every run button is enabled; the tool dialog receives an id that resolves to no layer.  
  What you see: After removing the DEM layer from the project (or reopening the project without it) the DEM row shows a string like 'dem_10m_5070_f3a2...' and 'Run breach model' is green; the tool then opens with an empty DEM and the user has to work out why.  
  Fix: In _fill_dem, when findData fails and keep is not an existing file (os.path.isfile(keep.split('|')[0]) is False), do not add it: log 'the DEM layer ... is no longer in the project', select '(choose in the tool)' and let the dam folder's DEM apply.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-7.
- **The wizard always opens at 10 m and writes that cell size into the run tool, so the window's Advanced grid size is overruled** `nd_breach_runner/dialog.py:1180`  
  The window hands the wizard the Dams folder, the dam and the DEM, but not the grid size, the simulated time or the ANUGA choice from the Advanced tab. The wizard starts at 10 m and build_rundam_params always writes CELL, so the run tool opens at 10 m even when the window shows 20 m. This is the same gap as finding R6-A-2 on a different road.  
  What you see: An engineer who sets 20 m on the Advanced tab and then uses the guided setup gets a 10 m run without being told, four times the cells and a much longer run than they asked for.  
  Fix: Pass the window's settings into run_wizard (at least grid_m) and let wizard_logic.initial_answers use it as the starting cell size, exactly as the screening hand-off does.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-AV-5.
- **'Dam suite home...' and 'Suite data and servers...' are in the Runner's own menu and in the shared ND Dam Suite menu** `nd_breach_runner/plugin.py:77`  
  The captured menus (gui_report 'menus') show 'ND Dam Breach Runner' with 7 entries, two of which ('Dam suite home...', 'Suite data and servers...') are also in 'ND Dam Suite', which is built for exactly that purpose (plugin.py:173-185, 'one menu for the three plugins'). Hazard repeats the same two (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/plugin.py:92, 97) and Screening one (nd_dam_screening/plugin.py:85).  
  What you see: The same window reachable from two menus; five redundant entries in the Plugins menu across the suite, which is what makes the menu long.  
  Fix: Delete the two QActions and their unload blocks (plugin.py:77-87 and 347-365), and the same in Hazard (2) and Screening (1): about 30 lines and 5 menu entries; the shared ND Dam Suite menu keeps them.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-3.
- **Five 3.20.0 tools are missing from the 'Breach tools' menu that claims to hold every tool** `nd_breach_runner/plugin.py:107`  
  The menu is a second hand-written copy of popup_logic.TABS. The window lists 35 tools; the menu lists 30: queue, cascadecheck, crossings, compareruns and signrates (all added in 3.20.0) are not in it, while metadata.txt 3.20.1 says 'Every tool is still there'. The two lists have already drifted once.  
  What you see: Plugins > ND Dam Breach Runner > Breach tools has no 'Run a queue of dams', 'Cascade check', 'Road and railroad crossing table', 'Compare two runs' or 'Sign off the fatality rate table'; they are reachable only from the window or the Processing toolbox.  
  Fix: Build the submenu from popup_logic.TABS (one separator per tab), so the menu and the window cannot differ; or, since the window already lists everything, drop the submenu and keep the four window entries (fewer menu lines).  
  Effort: small. Id R6-A-6.
- **'Model paths' and 'Help (README)' exist twice: menu entries in plugin.py and buttons on the Checks tab, with duplicated code** `nd_breach_runner/plugin.py:164`  
  dialog.py:613-622 adds 'Model paths (Processing options)...' and 'Help (README)' buttons to the Checks tab (seen in runner_main_dam_0_04_Checks.png), backed by _model_paths (1292-1301) and _open_help (1303-1306); plugin.py:164-172 adds the same two to the menu, backed by _settings (311-315), which is _model_paths without the refresh. Two code paths for two actions.  
  What you see: Two more entries in the Plugins menu that do what the window's Checks tab does.  
  Fix: Delete the two menu actions and _settings (plugin.py:164-172, 311-315); the window's Checks tab keeps them. Saves 2 menu entries and about 15 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-4.
- **_dialog_alive and check_versions are copied into the three plugin.py files and have drifted** `nd_breach_runner/plugin.py:226`  
  Diffed: _dialog_alive exists in three variants (Runner:226, Hazard:160 without the sip import fallback, Screening:133 with the try nested the other way); check_versions (Runner:204, Hazard:130, Screening:163) differs more: Hazard and Screening carry a _versions_checked run-once guard, the Runner has none and instead a _schedule_version_check (195-202) the other two lack. Same job, three maintenance points, outside the shared damsuite package that exists for exactly this.  
  What you see: None today; a fix to the version check in one plugin (for example the run-once guard) does not reach the others.  
  Fix: Add damsuite/pluginbase.py with dialog_alive(dlg) and check_versions(iface, key, log_tag, versions=None) (with the schedule and the run-once guard), and call them from the three plugin.py files. Saves about 80 lines across the suite.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-B-18.
- **Receptor layers are read in a 90 mi box and transformed vertex by vertex in Python, once per dam** `nd_breach_runner/qgis_io.py:114`  
  qgis_io transforms every vertex with a Python call to QgsCoordinateTransform.transform (lines 44, 76, 91, 114, 136) instead of one C++ `geometry.transform(tf)` per feature, and apply_features reads with `radius = 0.75 * st.max_extent_mi * MILE` (algorithms.py:870), 45 mi with the default MAX_EXTENT of 60 mi, whatever the dam's size. The batch tool repeats this for every dam (algorithms.py:1660).  
  What you see: With the statewide roads and structures layers, each dam's set-up spends seconds to minutes in Python before the model starts; a 100-dam batch spends a noticeable share of its time just reading receptors.  
  Fix: Transform with `g = QgsGeometry(f.geometry()); g.transform(to_work)` and read vertices from the transformed geometry; derive the read radius from the first domain and the extension cap actually reachable, or cache the receptor read for the batch.  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-A-10.
- **The CLI keeps hand copies of three algorithms.py tables marked 'keep in step', with nothing that checks them** `nd_breach_runner/scripts/ndbreach_cli.py:114`  
  Lines 114-143 duplicate algorithms.py:76-85, 1397-1400 and 1403-1414 because algorithms.py imports qgis and the CLI must run without it. They are identical today (compared), but no test references BATCH_FIELDS or STRUCT_FIELD_SYNONYMS in either place, so the next column added to the QGIS batch table will not reach Batch_Breach_Runs.csv from the CLI.  
  What you see: None today; a later column or synonym added in QGIS silently differs in the command line's CSV.  
  Fix: Move the three tables into core (STRUCT_FIELD_SYNONYMS to core/structures.py, BATCH_FIELDS and the F_* keys to core/nd_data.py beside FIELD_SYNONYMS) and import them in both algorithms.py and the CLI. Saves about 30 lines and the comments.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-9.
- **Compiled bytecode for the tests (and the whole plugin) is committed to git** `nd_breach_runner/tests/__pycache__/run_tests.cpython-312.pyc:1`  
  The repository tracks __pycache__/*.pyc for Python 3.12 and (untracked but present) 3.13, including the test modules' bytecode and the Qt doubles, and there is no .gitignore, so every test run dirties `git status` with new .pyc files (the working tree already shows untracked cpython-313 files) and every release commit re-adds stale bytecode.  
  What you see: The plugin zips carry a few hundred useless files, diffs between releases are noisy, and a reviewer cannot see from `git status` which test files really changed.  
  Fix: Add a root .gitignore with `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc`, run `git rm -r --cached` on the tracked caches in one commit.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-9.
- **The Runner's test harness is also its biggest test file, and it cannot be started the way the other two can** `nd_breach_runner/tests/run_tests.py:24`  
  Running `python3 nd_breach_runner/tests/run_tests.py`, the form the Hazard harness documents and the form anyone would try first, stops at once with ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'nd_breach_runner'; only `python3 -m nd_breach_runner.tests.run_tests` from the folder above works. On top of that the harness file holds 65 tests of its own, so the file that is supposed to be the small runner is the largest test file in the suite, and the shared helpers in it (close, dam, fixtures) are only reachable by importing the runner.  
  What you see: The three harnesses behave differently enough that whoever runs them has to remember a separate recipe per plugin, and one of the two obvious commands fails with an error that looks like a broken plugin rather than a wrong working folder.  
  Fix: Copy the four-line sys.path block from ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/run_tests.py:16-18 to the top of the Runner's harness, above the core imports, and move its 65 inline tests into a test_core.py next to the others so run_tests.py is only the runner.  
  Effort: medium. Id T1-TV-4.
- **The three run_tests.py harnesses differ in small ways and their docs are stale** `nd_breach_runner/tests/run_tests.py:1581`  
  Hazard (run_tests.py:39-40) and Updater (run_tests.py:36-37) accept test-name fragments on the command line and return the failure count; the Runner takes no filter (all 451 tests run for every check, about 27,000 lines of tests) and returns 1, although Hazard's registry.py:7-8 says it is 'the same layout as the ND Dam Breach Runner's tests/'. nd_data_updater/README.md:201 says '64 tests' (there are 75); nd_data_updater/tests/run_tests.py:1 says '(1.4.0)' and Hazard's says '(v2.28)'; test_cli.py:697 additionally runs `shellcheck` when it is installed, so the same test is stricter on one machine than another.  
  What you see: Whoever runs the tests gets three slightly different tools; the Runner developer cannot run one failing test on its own, and the documentation gives the wrong count.  
  Fix: Give the Runner's main() the same `only = [a for a in argv if not a.startswith("-")]` filter and `return failed`; delete the release numbers and counts from the harness docstrings and the Updater README (or print the count).  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-11.
- **The two damsuite version floors compare version strings as text, so they cannot fail for the versions they guard** `nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3130.py:152`  
  String comparison: "1.9.0" >= "1.10.0" is True (the character '9' sorts after '1'), so a damsuite 1.9.x without solvegate would pass this half of the assert, and the same at test_v3140.py:134 (`SUITE_VERSION >= "1.10.0"`). test_v3120.py:173 shows the correct form: `tuple(int(x) for x in damsuite.SUITE_VERSION.split(".")) >= (1, 8, 0)`. Today SUITE_VERSION is 1.16.x so both pass for the right reason, but the checks are dead weight.  
  What you see: None today; the test would stay green if an old damsuite copy were ever pasted in, which is what it exists to catch.  
  Fix: Use the tuple comparison from test_v3120.py:173 in both places (or a `_ver()` helper like test_v3140.py:140).  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-10.
- **Tool shells with no direct test, and a test that pins the release literal** `nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3180.py:247`  
  The processAlgorithm bodies of BuildVrt, FillGaps, NlcdManning and Benchmark (algorithms_tools.py) are never exercised: tests only check they are registered (test_v390.py, test_v3200.py) and test the core functions. SetupAnugaAlgorithm (algorithms_anugasetup.py) and NestedRunAlgorithm (algorithms_nested.py) are not named by any test (core anuga_setup and run_nested are). HydrographAlgorithm and ClassifyAlgorithm appear only in run_tests.py's registration list. compat.py has no test. The test above makes the hard-coded release number VERSION_FALLBACK = "3.20.3" in core/workflow.py:58 fail every release until the literal is edited, so a release number lives outside damsuite/versions.py by desi  
  What you see: A regression in the four data-preparation dialogs (parameter reading, output naming) is not caught before a release; each release needs one extra hand edit.  
  Fix: Add one fake-QGIS run per untested shell (the pattern of test_v3200.py for the 3.20 tools). Replace VERSION_FALLBACK with damsuite.versions.RELEASE['ndbreach'] and drop the pin at test_v3180.py:247.  
  Effort: medium. Id R5-B-16.
- **A test and core/workflow.py pin the release number '3.20.3'; both must be edited by hand every release** `nd_breach_runner/tests/test_v3200.py:906`  
  damsuite/versions.py:41 is the one place a release number should live, but core/workflow.py:58 hard-codes VERSION_FALLBACK = "3.20.3" and this test (named v3200_the_release_is_3_20_0_everywhere) pins the literal twice. Every release the literal is changed in metadata.txt, versions.py, workflow.py and the test; forgetting the test makes the suite red, forgetting workflow.py makes a run whose metadata.txt cannot be read report the previous version.  
  What you see: A failing test or a wrong version in run manifests after the next release.  
  Fix: Set VERSION_FALLBACK = versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"] in core/workflow.py and have the test compare metadata.txt's version to versions.RELEASE without a literal (drop the startswith check). Saves two edits per release.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-16.
- **wizard.py's _classes() is a 635-line function holding six classes, and dialog.py imports the GUI module only for a plain-Python helper** `nd_breach_runner/wizard.py:264`  
  Lines 264-899 define DamPage, TerrainPage, ScenarioPage, ModelPage, ReviewPage and Wizard inside one function so the module imports without Qt; the Qt-free part already lives in wizard_logic.py, and the tests build the pages through fake_qgis anyway (tests/test_wizard_logic.py:463). _find_models (206-220: provider settings, then nd_data.detect_*) has no Qt in it and is what dialog.py:1160 reaches into wizard for.  
  What you see: None directly; the pages cannot be imported, subclassed or inspected one at a time, and a fault in one page is reported as 'something went wrong in the wizard' through the 635-line wrapper.  
  Fix: Move _find_models to core/nd_data.py beside detect_lisflood / detect_anuga_python and import it from there in both dialog.py and wizard.py; define the six classes at module level (wizard.py is only ever imported inside QGIS). No lines saved, but the longest function in the unit goes away and each page becomes a unit.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-B-14.
- **The cell size's limits and the run defaults are restated in five places, and the limits disagree** `nd_breach_runner/wizard.py:780`  
  The same setting has minimum 0.5 and no maximum in the tool (algorithms.py:509-510), 0.5-500 in the window's Advanced tab (dialog.py:197), 1-200 in the wizard (wizard.py:780, wizard_logic.py:595) and 1 upwards in the CLI (ndbreach_cli.py:161). Likewise popup_logic.DEFAULT_SETTINGS:113-114 restates the tool's CELL / DURATION_HR / DURATION_MAX_HR defaults (10 / 0 / 96), popup_logic.DEM_HALF_MI and DEM_CELL_M (410-411) restate damdata.DEFAULT_DEM_HALF_MI / DEFAULT_DEM_CELL_M (damsuite/damdata.py:87-88, the comment admits it), and the CLI TEMPLATE model block (409-415) restates workflow.RunSettings (core/workflow.py:120-163). All values agree today; the limits do not.  
  What you see: A 0.5 to 0.9 m cell the Advanced tab and the tool accept is refused by the wizard with 'The cell size must be at least 1 m'.  
  Fix: Put CELL_MIN / CELL_MAX beside RunSettings in core/workflow.py and read them in the four places; have popup_logic import damdata's two constants; build the CLI template's model block from RunSettings(). Saves about 15 lines and the disagreement.  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-11.
- **The list of dam fields (ID, height, storage, ...) is written out four times with slightly different labels** `nd_breach_runner/wizard_logic.py:44`  
  The same twelve dam fields appear as wizard_logic.DAM_FIELDS (F key, tool parameter, label), algorithms.BATCH_FIELD_KEYS:1403-1414 (F key, label with 'field' in it), ndbreach_cli.BATCH_TO_DAM:122-126 plus DAM_ALIASES:146-148 (F key, DamData attribute) and core/damjson.PARAM_FIELDS:35-40 (tool parameter, dam.json field, label). Adding one dam field means editing four tables in three languages of naming.  
  What you see: None today; a field added to the batch tool but not to the wizard (or dam.json) is not offered or not saved, and the labels a user sees differ between the wizard and the tool.  
  Fix: One table in core/nd_data.py next to FIELD_SYNONYMS with (F key, tool parameter, DamData attribute, dam.json field, label); derive the other three from it. Saves about 40 lines and one place to add a field.  
  Effort: medium. Id R6-B-10.
- **wizard_logic re-implements popup_logic's dam-folder helpers and its own number cleaner** `nd_breach_runner/wizard_logic.py:253`  
  wizard_logic.py imports popup_logic as pl (line 22) yet dam_folder_dem (253-266) is pl.dam_data_dem(pl.dam_folder(...)) (popup_logic.py:128-144) and dam_folder_of (324-330) is pl.dam_folder(a['dams_folder'], DAM_ID). clean_number (68-87) is the same cleaner as ndbreach_cli.num (442-454) and qgis_io.num (147) minus the isNull branch, and popup_logic._number (438-443) is a fourth; core has ten more private _num helpers.  
  What you see: None today; the four copies of the 'NULL / nan / text' rule can diverge.  
  Fix: Replace the two functions with pl calls (saves ~20 lines) and keep one clean_number in core (for example core/units.py) imported by wizard_logic, qgis_io, popup_logic and the CLI (saves ~30 lines).  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-6.
- **wizard_logic copies Screening's DEM file-name rule character by character** `nd_breach_runner/wizard_logic.py:308`  
  Lines 308-321 restate nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:539-549 (local_tif_stem) so find_downloaded_dem (396-416) can guess the file the tool wrote. The download cannot run without Screening at all (wizard.py:648 checks the algorithm exists), so the rule can be imported from Screening instead of copied; if Screening renames its output the wizard's fallback silently finds nothing and the note says 'No DEM was downloaded' although the file is there.  
  What you see: A future Screening change to its file name turns a successful download into 'No DEM was downloaded' in the wizard.  
  Fix: Import local_tif_stem from nd_dam_screening.core.dem_download inside find_downloaded_dem and delete dem_stem and dem_file_name (14 lines, plus their tests in test_v3180.py).  
  Effort: small. Id R6-B-7.

## Screening (197: 9 serious, 65 medium, 123 minor)

### Serious
- **Tool 0 never writes the structure_points layer; the log says it did** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/data_package.py:177`  
  dump() opens two QgsVectorFileWriter objects on the same GeoPackage at the same time (lines 176-177) and interleaves writes. QgsVectorFileWriter starts an SQLite transaction on the file for the first writer, so the second writer's deferred CREATE TABLE fails with 'database is locked', every wp.addFeature() returns False, and the layer is never created. The return value of addFeature is not checked (line 201) and the summary reuses the structures count for the points layer (lines 207-208). Reproduced with the installed QGIS 3.34.4 / GDAL 3.8.4 using the same writer options and order as this code: 200 polygons written, 0 points, 'structure_points' absent from the file, while addFeature on the   
  What you see: Every package built by tool 0 (also one rebuilt from an ND Dam Hazard export, which does have the layer) lacks structure_points, yet the run log reads 'structure_points (523,000)'. The window checks for the layer (core/workflow.py:1363-1366) and falls back to the polygon structures, so ND Dam Breach Runner's rundam gets no RECEPTOR_POINTS (runner_handoff.py:272-277 only passes a point layer) and the Runner's receptor-based results are missing without any message.  
  Fix: Write the layers one after the other: finish 'structures' (del w) before creating the structure_points writer, either by looping over the source twice or by collecting the point features in a list while writing structures and writing them after del w. Check the return of addFeature() and raise a QgsProcessingException (or at least count the failures) instead of reporting n for both layers.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-1.
- **New shallow-basin rule (1.22) treats a full reservoir as a puddle when it was found as flat water** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/estimate_storage.py:217`  
  wnote is set both for a closed depression and for hydro-flattened flat water (storage.flat_water_mask returns a note at storage.py:369-370). For a depression, pool_elev is the basin floor and crest minus pool is the basin depth, which is what the rule intends. For flat water, pool_elev is the water surface (storage.estimate_storage line 183), so span is only the freeboard between the water and the DEM crest; the DEM cannot see the depth of the water at all. A reservoir that is full to within half the dam height of its crest (the normal case for a small ND dam) therefore passes the test and its DEM-measured, inventory-calibrated curve is replaced by the assumed quadratic of storage.inventory_  
  What you see: For a 30 ft dam whose water surface sits 10 ft below the crest and that has no NHD polygon within the search distance, tool 3 now discards the measured curve, writes est_pool_m at the dam-point elevation and est_crest_m one dam height above it, marks stor_method 'inventory curve' with an ASSUMPTION note that misstates the depth, and the peak breach flow in tool 5 changes (the help text itself says this rule moves the peak flow). Tool 7's reservoir-in-grid engine gets the shifted start elevation   
  Fix: Apply the shallow test only when the mask came from storage.depression_mask (keep a flag from the branch at lines 185-197), or for flat water compare span + res.max_depth_m (the estimated water depth) with SHALLOW_BASIN * h. Also make the note say 'the DEM basin spans X ft below the crest' rather than 'is X ft deep'.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-2.
- **Report records never carry hz_basis, hz_border, hz_gaps, kind, h_src, stor_src, stor_notes, status, source** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/reports.py:13`  
  core/report.py reads rec["hz_basis"] (line 230, the conservative-rule label and card), rec["h_src"] / rec["stor_src"] (147-149, the ESTIMATED review reason), rec["kind"] (276), rec["stor_notes"], rec["status"], rec["source"] (304, 344, 384) and writes hz_border, hz_gaps, kind, h_src, stor_src into summary.csv (SUMMARY_COLS 355-363). Its only caller, algorithms/reports.py, builds every record from the fixed FIELDS list above, which has none of these names, although all of them exist on the tool-8 layer (hz_basis/hz_border/hz_gaps in hazard_classification.py NEW lines 25-32; kind/h_src/stor_src/source from prepare_dams.py lines 17-23 and stor_notes from estimate_storage.py line 25, all carried  
  What you see: In every real run: summary.csv has empty hz_border, hz_gaps, kind, h_src and stor_src columns, so ND Dam Hazard's portfolio dashboard (portfolio.py:136 SCREEN_FIELDS) reads blanks for the border and data-gap columns; the 'ESTIMATED height / storage (no DWR value)' review reason and warning card of 0.19.0 never appear; when the conservative no-chart method was used the class is shown as plain 'High' with no 'conservative' label or card; every dam page says 'Structure kind: DWR dam', including LiD  
  Fix: Add the nine names to FIELDS (or build FIELDS as the union of rp.SUMMARY_COLS and the keys report.py reads) and make test_report.py or test_qgis_tools.py run tool 10 on a tool-8 layer and assert summary.csv has a non-empty hz_border / hz_basis for a dam that has one.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-1.
- **Self-test deletes a work folder the user chose when 'Keep' is off** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/selftest.py:139`  
  WORK is a folder-destination parameter that accepts any folder the user picks in the Processing Toolbox dialog. When the run ends and KEEP is off (the default), the tool removes that whole folder with everything in it, not only what the self-test wrote. The code only uses mkdtemp when WORK is empty; it never remembers whether the folder was its own. (The window's All steps > 14 passes only ENGINE and REPORT, workflow.py:1452 and LAYOUT '14', so from the window the folder is QGIS's temporary one; the exposure is the Toolbox dialog, where the folder picker invites choosing an existing folder.)  
  What you see: A user who points the self-test at a folder of his own (for example his screening work folder, to look at the synthetic outputs) and leaves 'Keep' unticked loses the folder and all his results in it when the test finishes. ignore_errors=True means no message is shown.  
  Fix: Remember whether the tool made the folder (made_tmp = not work ...) and call rmtree only when made_tmp is True; when the user named a folder, write into a fresh sub-folder nddam_selftest_<time> under it and delete only that sub-folder. Relabel KEEP as 'Delete the temporary folder afterwards' so it reads true for both cases.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-1.
- **The hand-typed county download boxes miss 48 of the 53 counties, by up to 34 miles** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:26`  
  I compared all 53 boxes with the county outlines the rest of the suite uses (core/nd_counties.geojson, the same file prepare_dams uses to decide which county a dam is in). Only 5 boxes cover their county. McLean's box stops at 101.65 W while the county runs to 102.39 W, so about 34 miles of the county, and 49% of its outline, is outside the box; Sioux is short 34% of its outline, Morton 20%, Logan, Cass and Grand Forks 12 to 18 miles each. The comment says the boxes are padded so a dam on the county line is inside; for 48 counties the opposite is true.  
  What you see: "Download the terrain for a county" quietly returns a DEM that stops short of the county. A dam in the western end of McLean County, along the Lake Sakakawea shore, has no terrain in the county DEM, so step 7 fails for it with "breach injection point falls outside the terrain" or runs on a clip that is mostly no-data, and the DEM tab draws a rectangle that looks like the county so nothing on screen says anything is missing. The same box picks the ND LiDAR tiles, so the LiDAR download misses the   
  Fix: Delete COUNTIES, county_names and county_features and take the extent from the outlines the suite already ships: in download_dem use nd_counties.names() and nd_counties.box([county], pad_deg=0.05), in tiles_for use the same box, and let the dialog draw nd_counties.features(). One county list for the whole suite, and the clip then covers the county. Until that is done, every box should at least be   
  Effort: small. Id S1-BV-1.
- **County download boxes stop short of 48 of the 53 counties** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:54`  
  The hand-typed COUNTIES table says it is padded outward, but checked against the suite's own county outlines (core/nd_counties.geojson, the file tool 1 and the window use to pick a county) 48 of the 53 boxes end INSIDE the county. The worst: McLean's west edge is 55 km inside the county (-101.65 vs the outline's -102.386), Morton 34 km (west), Sioux 26 km (west), Logan 20 km (west), Cass 19 km (west, -97.45 vs -97.706), Grand Forks 19 km (west), Dunn 19 km (south), Ward 17.5 km (north), Slope 17 km (east), Pembina 15 km (west). The table is used for the USGS 3DEP county download (algorithms/download_dem.py:145 `box = dd.COUNTIES.get(county)`) and for the ND DWR LiDAR tile pick (dem_download.  
  What you see: A county-wide DEM download (3DEP or DWR LiDAR) does not cover the whole county and nothing says so. Dams in the missing strip then have no terrain: tool 2 clips fail with 'less than half of the terrain clip has data', tool 7 reports those dams FAILED or a flood cut at the DEM edge, and a McLean or Morton county screening silently leaves out a third of the county.  
  Fix: Delete the hand-typed table and derive the boxes from the shipped outlines: core/nd_counties.py already has box(counties, pad_deg) (line 113); use nd_counties.box([county], pad_deg=0.05) for county_names()/COUNTIES and nd_counties.box(nd_counties.names(), 0.05) for STATE. Add a test that every county box contains the geojson box of that county.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-1.
- **Tool 18 merge fails on any GeoPackage QGIS wrote (rtree triggers need ST_IsEmpty)** `nd_dam_screening/core/merge.py:61`  
  18_dams_all.gpkg is a byte copy of 3_dams.gpkg, which step 3 writes through a Processing sink (estimate_storage.py, parameterAsSink), i.e. GDAL's GPKG driver with its default spatial index. Such a file carries the triggers rtree_<table>_<geom>_insert / update1..4, whose WHEN clauses call ST_IsEmpty(), ST_MinX() and friends. Those SQL functions exist only when GDAL/Spatialite registers them; plain sqlite3 has not got them, so the INSERT raises sqlite3.OperationalError 'no such function: ST_IsEmpty' (reproduced here with a table that has the standard trigger). The unit test builds its GeoPackages with tests/_gpkg.py, which creates no rtree, so the test passes while the real tool cannot.  
  What you see: Step 18 'Merge dams and ponds' stops with 'merge failed: no such function: ST_IsEmpty' on every real work folder, so the Simple tab's 'Both' chain cannot get past the merge and steps 5-10 never see dams and ponds together.  
  Fix: Before the INSERT, drop the destination's rtree triggers and virtual table and its gpkg_extensions row (SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='trigger' AND name LIKE 'rtree_%'), then let QGIS rebuild the index on demand; or do the append with QgsVectorFileWriter / the GPKG provider instead of sqlite3. Add a test fixture that includes the four standard rtree triggers.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2b-A-2.
- **Review 'Move breach point' never moves the point in a QGIS-written layer** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:215`  
  SqliteApply.move_point rewrites the geometry column with plain sqlite3. The breach-point layers of steps 4 and 16 are written by Processing sinks (breach_direction.py:117, find_ponds.py:387), so they have the GeoPackage rtree update triggers, which call ST_IsEmpty(); sqlite3 raises 'no such function: ST_IsEmpty' (reproduced). apply() catches it and records 'not written: ...' for each layer; dir_conf is therefore never set to 'reviewed' either. The window (dialog.py:2322) passes no QGIS-backed applier, so SqliteApply is always the one used. Attribute-only UPDATEs (accept, exclude, set_type) are fine because the triggers watch only the geometry column.  
  What you see: The reviewer's new coordinates are logged in review_decisions.csv and a box says 'not every layer could be written: 4_breach_points.gpkg: not written: no such function: ST_IsEmpty', but the breach point stays where it was; the next runs of steps 5-8 use the old point while the review log says it was moved.  
  Fix: Same as S2b-A-2 (drop or bypass the rtree triggers for the UPDATE and NULL the extent), or implement the QGIS-backed Apply the module docstring already anticipates (QgsVectorLayer.dataProvider().changeGeometryValues) and pass it from the window.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2b-A-3.
- **Opening a settings file overwrites its 'window' rows with the window's current values before reading them** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1512`  
  SettingsGrid.load() (suite_settings.py:442-443) calls on_change before it returns. on_change is _grid_changed -> _autosave_settings, which copies the window's CURRENT choices (engine, scenario, simulated hours, preset, both loading cases, skip done, pregrow, ...) into the in-memory settings object with window_settings_to and saves the file. Only then does line 1514 read the 'window' rows back with window_settings_from, so it reads what was just written. _loading is False on every path that reaches here (window open at line 221, 'File...' at 1472, typing a path at 1463). Reproduced with the repo's own qgis_mock: a file with window rows simh=24, scenario=1 (sunny day), engine=0 (LISFLOOD ACC)   
  What you see: The run choices a colleague saved in a shared settings file, or that Nick edited on the 'window' rows in Excel, are silently thrown away every time the window opens or the file is picked, and the file itself is rewritten with the window's values. The grid's tool rows (e.g. HEAD_FT) are read correctly, so it looks as if the file loaded. Two engineers 'sharing' a settings file will each keep overwriting the other's run options.  
  Fix: In _load_settings_file, set self._loading = True around self.grid.load(p, quiet=quiet) (so _autosave_settings is a no-op during the load), then read win = wf.window_settings_from(self.grid.settings), apply it, and only afterwards save once via _autosave_settings. Add a test: a file with window rows differing from the window must change the window, not the file.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-1.

### Medium
- **Tool 11 blames the reference folder when it is the scenario or the tool-7 folder that is empty** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/compare_reference.py:168`  
  When the tool-7 output folder is used instead of the results layer, the dams are found by the file ending _<scenario>_depth.tif. If the scenario picked does not match the rasters (sunny day chosen, top-of-dam rasters on disk), or the folder given is the wrong one, the list of the plugin's own dams is empty and the only message the user gets names the reference folder and explains how to lay out reference files. No log line says how many tool-7 rasters were found, because the count is only printed after this check (line 173).  
  What you see: The engineer re-arranges and renames his HEC-RAS or DSS-WISE reference folder, runs the tool again and gets the same error, while the real cause is one combo box (the scenario) or the tool-7 folder. os.listdir on a folder that does not exist also fails with a raw Python error instead of a plain message.  
  Fix: Check 'ours' first: if it is empty, raise a message naming the folder and the scenario ('no <dam>_sd_depth.tif in <simdir>: tool 7 wrote the td rasters, or give the results layer'), and wrap os.listdir so a missing folder is reported in the same words.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-AV-3.
- **Tool 20 reuses an existing DEM even when you asked for a bigger area or a different source** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/download_dem.py:106`  
  The name the reuse check looks for is built only from the area kind, the cell size, the dam ID (or county) and, when there is no dam ID, the point: core/dem_download.py:539-552. The buffer in miles, the dam point when an ID is given, and the chosen source (USGS 3DEP or ND DWR LiDAR) are not part of the name, and the sidecar it checks for records only source, CRS, units and cell (core/dem_download.py:577-591), which nothing reads back. So any earlier file for that dam ID and resolution satisfies the check. Tool 2 does this properly for its per-dam clips: cm.clip_reusable compares the extent, the cell AND the source, and re-cuts when any of them changed (algorithms/_common.py:447-470).  
  What you see: An engineer whose flood ran off the edge of the DEM raises the buffer from 10 to 25 miles, runs tool 20 again, and gets 'reusing existing DEM' with the old small file; the next run hits the same wall. The same happens when the source is switched from 3DEP to the ND DWR LiDAR at the same resolution: the 3DEP file is handed back, recorded in dam.json as the dam's DEM, and the LiDAR is never downloaded.  
  Fix: Put the buffer (and the point) in the stem, or keep a .json beside the GeoTIFF with extent, cell and source and compare it the way cm.clip_reusable does; include the source key in the name so a change of source never matches.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-AV-1.
- **Running tool 3 (or tool 5) again on its own output keeps the old numbers and says nothing** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/estimate_storage.py:144`  
  QgsFields.append refuses a name the fields already hold (I checked this in QGIS: it returns false and the count does not grow). If the chosen dams layer already carries est_pool_m, stor_method, stor_notes and the rest - which is exactly what tool 3 wrote last time - the output gets no new columns, so the 17 freshly computed values are appended past the end of the row and are silently dropped; I confirmed the writer accepts such a feature and keeps the old values. Tools 7 and 7b avoid this by going through cm.extend_fields, which reuses a field that already exists (algorithms/_common.py:126-135); tools 3 and 5 are the two that append blindly.  
  What you see: An engineer who re-runs tool 3 with a different shape factor or search distance on 3_dams.gpkg (or picks the 'Dams with storage' layer already in the project) gets a run that reports no error and a layer in which every storage value, method, confidence and note is the one from the previous run. The same happens for tool 5 re-run on its own output: the breach parameters and hydrograph fields stay as they were.  
  Fix: Build the output fields with cm.extend_fields(dams.fields(), NEW_FIELDS) and write by field name (the setter it returns) instead of by position, in both tools.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-AV-2.
- **'Run one dam' on a pond outside the size limits says the ID did not match** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/find_ponds.py:609`  
  The size filter runs after the ID match and drops the chosen pond silently (n_size only). results stays empty, so the final check reports the wrong cause.  
  What you see: A user who picks a 60-acre pond (MAX_AC default 50) or one under 0.05 ac in 'Run one dam...' gets 'None of the water bodies matches the chosen IDs (POND-...)' and goes looking for an ID problem; the message never mentions the size limits.  
  Fix: Exempt ONLY_IDS ponds from the size filter (a named pond is wanted), or count 'matched but outside the size limits' separately and say so in the exception: 'POND-... matched but is 60.2 ac, above your largest water body (50 ac)'.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-5.
- **Tool 8 help still says the shipped chart file is not digitized and the default method stops** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/hazard_classification.py:86`  
  Since 0.26.1 (metadata.txt changelog) the shipped core/acer_tm11_charts.json is signed off and the default criterion runs out of the box; the help text was not updated. Tool 9 (line 101) points to tool 8 for this, so the wrong text is the only explanation a user sees.  
  What you see: The tool's help panel tells the engineer the default lethality method will stop and that he should switch to the conservative method. Following it changes the result (every inundated habitable structure counts as lethal) for no reason.  
  Fix: Replace the sentence with the current state: the shipped chart file is signed off (2026-10-12) and runs; a chart file without a signing engineer is refused with a message naming the conservative method.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-6.
- **One unreadable depth or velocity raster aborts the whole tool 8 run** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/hazard_classification.py:565`  
  The primary-scenario assess() is not wrapped. The second-scenario call (lines 574-581) is wrapped and degrades to a warning, and tools 6 and 16 explicitly 'keep the batch going', but here a RuntimeError from GDAL (a depth_tif that was moved, deleted or damaged, typically on a dam whose status is 'cached' from an earlier run) or the QgsProcessingException from hz.Grid propagates out of the per-dam loop.  
  What you see: A county run of hundreds of dams ends in tool 8 with an error naming one file. No OUT layer, no IMP layer, no dam.json updates and no report for any dam until the user finds and deletes or re-makes that one raster and runs tool 8 again.  
  Fix: Wrap the assess() call (and the scenario handling that follows) in try/except Exception: feedback.reportError, set hz_class 'NoData', hz_conf 'none', hz_notes 'flood raster not readable: <error>', and continue with the next dam, as the scen2 branch already does.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-3.
- **Tool 13 inventory diff cannot read DWR's GeoPackage export: dams get made-up IDs and nothing is reported** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/inventory_diff.py:41`  
  core/inventory.py has resolve_fields() and FIELD_CANDIDATES precisely so that the long field names of the DWR GeoPackage / service export (structure_id, dam_name, max_dam_height, max_pool_vol, hazard_class, structure_status) are found when the 10-character dBase names (structure_, max_dam_he, ...) are absent; tool 1 calls it, and resolve_fields' own docstring names this exact case. Tool 13 does not: it maps a role to None whenever the dBase name is missing, so for a long-name export every role is None. inventory.standardize then falls back to its position-based ID ('id': _txt(g("ID")) or f"{tag}{idx}", inventory.py:136) and leaves height, storage, status and class all blank.  
  What you see: Run tool 13 on two of DWR's GeoPackage exports and every dam is given the invented ID DWR-DWR0, DWR-DWR1, ... in row order. The two inventories are therefore matched up by row position instead of by structure number, and because every value reads blank the tool announces '0 dams differ' and writes an inventory_changes.csv with no rows, so a real height, storage or class change in the new export is never seen and no dam.json is flagged for a refresh.  
  Fix: Use inv.resolve_fields(inv.DWR_DEFAULTS, names, inv.KIND_DAM) the way tool 1 does and push its notes to the log; if the ID role still cannot be resolved, stop with a clear message instead of numbering the dams by row.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-AV-2.
- **Tool 13 looks up the DWR fields by their dBase names only, so on the GeoPackage export of the same inventory it reports no changes at all** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/inventory_diff.py:41`  
  Tool 1 resolves the field names through inventory.resolve_fields, which knows both spellings (structure_ / structure_id, max_dam_he / max_dam_height, and so on). Tool 13 skips that helper and keeps only the chosen name when the layer happens to have it. On the GeoPackage or service version of the same export not one role matches, so every role becomes None: the ID falls back to the row counter (dam_id "DWR-DWR0", "DWR-DWR1", ...), and height, storage, status, class and name all read as missing.  
  What you see: Compare two GeoPackage DWR exports and the tool prints "0 dams differ" and writes an empty changes file, even when heights and storages really changed, because every value on both sides reads as blank. The dams are also renumbered by row order, so the point layer and any dam.json note can be attached to the wrong dam. The same layer works correctly in tool 1, so the user has no reason to suspect the diff.  
  Fix: Call the helper that already exists: `fld, notes = inv.resolve_fields(inv.DWR_DEFAULTS, names, inv.detect_kind(names, src.sourceName() if hasattr(src, "sourceName") else ""))`, push the notes to the log, and take the ID prefix from inv.ID_PREFIX for that kind. About five lines, and add the raw-export case to the tool-13 test (tests/test_runchain.py:171 covers only two tool-1 layers).  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-BV-2.
- **Tool 10's dam pages and summary.csv leave out nine columns the report code needs, so 'Structure kind' always says DWR dam and the dashboard columns are empty** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/reports.py:13`  
  Tool 10 builds every record from its own FIELDS list, which does not contain kind, h_src, stor_src, stor_method, stor_notes, status, source, hz_border or hz_gaps, although tool 8 writes them all into 8_hazard.gpkg and core/report.py reads every one of them. The per-dam page therefore prints 'Structure kind: DWR dam' for a pond or a proposed dam, 'Height from: inventory' for a height the DEM estimated, and an empty 'Storage from'. In the review queue the 0.19.0 reason 'ESTIMATED height and storage (no DWR value)', the proposed-dam reason 'the pool leaves the basin' and the 'DEM storage held at the spill level' reason can never fire, because the words they look for are in fields the record doe  
  What you see: A pond's report page calls it a DWR dam, a class built on a height the plugin guessed from the LiDAR is presented as if it came from the DWR inventory, and those dams never appear in the review queue for that reason. The engineer reading index.html cannot see which dams rest on estimated inputs, and the suite dashboard that reads summary.csv gets empty hazard-gap and border columns.  
  Fix: Add "kind", "h_src", "stor_src", "stor_method", "stor_conf", "stor_notes", "status", "source", "hz_border" and "hz_gaps" to FIELDS in reports.py:13-17 (they already exist in the tool-8 layer, so nothing else changes), and give tests/test_report.py one record that carries h_src = "estimated from the DEM" so the review reason is checked.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-BV-1.
- **Tool 10's dam page always shows the top-of-dam hydrograph and peak, even when the class came from the sunny-day run** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/reports.py:14`  
  The report hard-codes the td (top of dam) scenario. When tool 7 is run with Scenario = 'sd - sunny day', the flood map is right but the breach peak, the formation time and the hydrograph chart on every dam page are read from the td_ fields, which belong to the other loading case. When tool 7 is run with 'Also run the other scenario' and the sunny-day case governs, tool 8 records that in hz_scen but reports.py reads neither hz_scen nor scen2_depth_tif, so the page shows the top-of-dam flood map and maximum depth under a class that came from the sunny-day flood. Nothing on the page names the loading case.  
  What you see: On a dam where the two loading cases differ, the page the engineer reviews and prints shows one flood's picture and numbers next to the other flood's hazard class, with no note that they do not belong together.  
  Fix: Add "hz_scen", "scenario", "scen2", "scen2_depth_tif" and "scen2_max_depth_m" to FIELDS, pick the raster, hydrograph CSV and peak/formation fields by the governing scenario (hz_scen, falling back to scenario) instead of the fixed 'td' prefix, and print the loading case as one more fact on the page.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-BV-4.
- **Tool 7 and tool 2 ship with different defaults from the window's (engine 0 vs 2, cell 3.3 ft vs 32.8 ft)** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:207`  
  The window's DEFAULT_SETTINGS says engine 2 (built-in, no install) and dem_cell_ft 32.8 (the 0.28.0 note at workflow.py:184-186 explains that 3.3 ft resampled a 10 m source to 1 m, 9 million cells). The tools themselves default differently: run_breach_sim.py:207 and estimate_runtime.py:47 have ENGINE defaultValue=0 (LISFLOOD-FP ACC, which needs an executable), and terrain_cache.py:55/62 has CELL_FT 3.3. The same default is thus defined in two places with two values.  
  What you see: Anyone who opens tool 7 from the Processing toolbox or runs it with qgis_process (the headless path 1.22 just extended) gets LISFLOOD-FP and the run stops at 'executable not found' (the preflight itself calls engine 0 without an exe a FAIL at preflight.py:286); tool 2 from the toolbox on 3DEP still builds the 9-million-cell clip the window stopped building in 0.28.0.  
  Fix: Set the tool defaults to the window's values: ENGINE defaultValue=2 in run_breach_sim.py:207 and estimate_runtime.py:47, CELL_FT default 32.8 in terrain_cache.py:55 and its fallback at 62. Three one-line edits; one default instead of two.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-3.
- **Tool 7's processAlgorithm is 442 lines with six nested closures; nothing in it can be tested on its own** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:285`  
  One method (lines 285-726) reads 40 parameters, builds the per-dam cases, defines the per-dam run (run_scenario, worker), the result-row builder (on_done, 95 lines) and the polygon writer as closures over about 30 local variables. The per-dam logic cannot be exercised without a QGIS feedback object and a complete parameter set, so the tests (test_v0280, test_v0260, test_crs_017) can only run the whole tool. core/runner.py already holds simulate() and run_batch(), core/resume.py and core/flood_polygons.py the other halves.  
  What you see: No direct effect on a run. For Nick the effect is indirect: every fix to how one dam is re-read, re-run or written into the results table (the 0.28.0 resume and second-scenario changes both landed here) is made inside a 442-line function that only whole-tool tests cover, so regressions in the results table are found late.  
  Fix: Move run_scenario and worker into core/runner.py as run_case(case, loader, opts, path_fn, skipdone, ...) and the body of on_done into a pure core function result_row(rec, r, scen, scen2, ...) that returns a dict of field values; processAlgorithm keeps parameter reading, the case list and the sink (about 150 lines). Same for runner_handoff.processAlgorithm (lines 187-432, 246 lines): the per-dam tr  
  Effort: large. Id S3a-B-1.
- **Tools 5 and 7 read the breach-point fields blind: the wrong point layer ends the run with a raw Python error** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:362`  
  Both tools take any point layer as 'Breach points' and then index its fields by name with no check. A layer that is not tool 4's output raises KeyError inside the loop that builds the breach-point index, which sits outside every try block, so the whole run stops. core/errors.plain exists precisely to turn that into 'the layer has no field X: pick the right field in the tool's dialog', but it is only reached for failures inside the per-dam try. Tool 7b checks the field list and says what was expected; tools 5 and 7 do not.  
  What you see: Opening tool 7 from the Processing Toolbox, where the dams layer is pre-filled but the breach points are not, and picking the dams layer (both are point layers with similar names) ends the run with "KeyError: 'flow_az'" and no result, instead of 'this layer is not tool 4's output'. In tool 5 the same mistake marks every dam FAILED.  
  Fix: Before the loop, check the breach-point field names as runner_handoff.py:260 does: if 'dam_id' or 'flow_az' is missing, stop with 'Breach points: the layer has no <field>; give tool 4's output (4_breach.gpkg)'. Read the rest with cm.fval so a blank value is empty rather than an error.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-BV-2.
- **Tool 15 gives the steps a feedback that is connected to nothing: step logs lost, Cancel ignored** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_chain.py:196`  
  cap_fb is a brand-new feedback object; nothing connects it to the real `feedback`. Only pushWarning and reportError are copied into the log (lines 188-194). pushInfo and setProgress of the child tools go to cap_fb's own signals, which nobody listens to, so they appear neither in the dialog nor in the LOG file. feedback.isCanceled() is checked only between steps (line 199); the running step polls cap_fb.isCanceled(), which is never set, so Cancel has no effect until that step ends. type(feedback)() also assumes a no-argument constructor; it works for QgsProcessingFeedback (the dialog and qgis_process cases) but raises TypeError when the chain runs as a child of a model (QgsProcessingMultiStep  
  What you see: A headless or dialog run of tool 15 shows only 'Plan: ...', the chain's own lines and warnings; the per-step messages ('12 dams written', 'simulations: ok 10, FAILED 2', the pre-run checks) never appear, the progress bar stays still, and pressing Cancel during step 7 does nothing for hours.  
  Fix: Use QgsProcessingMultiStepFeedback(len(plan), feedback) as the child feedback (it forwards messages, progress and cancellation) and subclass it only to add the log() lines; or keep the subclass but forward pushInfo/setProgress to `feedback` and override isCanceled() to return feedback.isCanceled().  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-3.
- **The headless chain hands each step a disconnected progress object: Cancel is ignored and the steps' own messages never reach the chain log** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_chain.py:196`  
  cap_fb is a brand-new progress object, not the one QGIS gave the tool. Only pushWarning and reportError are forwarded into the chain log; ordinary messages and progress go to an object nobody reads, and the cancelled flag the user sets on the real object is never seen by the running step - start() only looks at it between steps. The window's chain does forward ordinary messages (dialog.py:2014-2016), so the two chains behave differently.  
  What you see: An overnight statewide run through tool 15 leaves a chain log that holds the plan, the preflight lines and the warnings, but nothing the steps themselves reported ('terrain cached: 180 ok, 2 failed', 'simulations: ok 174, FAILED 6'), and the progress bar never moves. Pressing Cancel during step 7 does nothing until that step finishes, which on a county is hours of modelling Nick asked it to stop.  
  Fix: Keep the given object and only add the log line: subclass it as now but forward pushInfo to log() as well, and pass the ORIGINAL feedback to processing.run while writing the log from a small wrapper that also returns isCanceled() from the real object (or pass QgsProcessingMultiStepFeedback(len(plan), feedback), which forwards cancellation and progress for free).  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-BV-3.
- **Tool 7b with FAIL_ON_ERROR (the default) throws away the results layer when one dam fails** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/runner_handoff.py:424`  
  FAIL_ON_ERROR defaults to True (line 177) and the tool raises when ANY dam failed (the comment at lines 173-174 speaks of a hand-off 'in which every dam failed'). In QGIS a raised QgsProcessingException means the run is not successful: the OUT sink is not loaded into the project (a temporary-layer destination, the dialog's default, is discarded with the context), and the result dict with DONE / FAILED / SKIPPED is never returned. The window opens 7b through processing.execAlgorithmDialog (dialog.py:1930) with OUT = <work>/7b_runner_results.gpkg and no FAIL_ON_ERROR value, so the file is written but not loaded, and the step then shows as done (the OUT file exists) while the dialog reported a   
  What you see: After hours of Runner time on ten dams, one 'no breach point (tool 4)' failure leaves the engineer with a red error, no 'Dams with runner results' layer (gone for good if it was a temporary layer) and no counts; rn_status, rn_class and rn_agree for the nine good dams have to be dug out of the GeoPackage by hand.  
  Fix: Keep the exception for qgis_process only (e.g. when QgsApplication.platform() is 'qgis_process', or when every chosen dam failed as the comment intends) and otherwise return the result dict with OK=false; in any case have the window pass FAIL_ON_ERROR=False in core/workflow.py's 7b defaults.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-4.
- **Tool 7b throws away its own results table when any dam fails, which is the normal case it was built for** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/runner_handoff.py:424`  
  FAIL_ON_ERROR is on by default, and the tool deliberately gives every dam a row (rn_status 'done' / 'failed: ...' / 'skipped: ...', rn_class, rn_agree, rn_inputs) before raising at the end. But a Processing tool that raises has failed as far as QGIS is concerned: its output layer is not added to the map and not reported to the caller. In the dialog the OUT box defaults to a temporary layer, so that table is not written anywhere at all; nothing else holds the per-dam rn_status, rn_class and rn_agree (the comparison HTML only covers the dams that succeeded).  
  What you see: Nick sends twenty dams to the Runner overnight, nineteen finish, one fails (the help says this is expected) and the run ends in a red error with no 'Dams with runner results' layer: he loses the table that says which dams the Runner finished and what class it gave them, and has to read the error text and the dam folders instead. The exit code for a script is the point of the 0.28.3 change, but it costs the whole table.  
  Fix: Write the results to a real file before raising, or make FAIL_ON_ERROR false by default and keep the counts (DONE / FAILED / SKIPPED) as the signal; if the non-zero exit must stay, raise only after the sink has been flushed to a named file and say in the message where the table is.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-BV-1.
- **One failed variant throws away every variant tool 9 has already run** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:125`  
  Every variant re-runs tools 5, 7 and 8 for all the chosen dams, and the result layer is built only after the last variant finishes. Any failure inside a variant - the explicit raise at line 159, a child tool refusing to start, a full disk - leaves processAlgorithm with an exception, so nothing is written. There is no per-variant try/except, although tools 6, 7, 8 and 16 all deliberately 'keep the batch going' for a single dam.  
  What you see: Tool 9 on a review queue is a run of hours (eight variants, each a flood model per dam). If the last variant fails the engineer gets an error and no sensitivity layer at all: the seven variants already modelled are lost and he has to start again from the first one.  
  Fix: Wrap each variant's three processing.run calls in try/except Exception: report the error, keep the classes collected so far and carry on with the next variant; then write the OUT layer as now, with sens_runs = the variants that succeeded and the failed variant named in sens_detail.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-AV-2.
- **Tool 9 forces 'cached clips only' terrain, piping mode and a 10 m grid on every variant** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:137`
