  run_breach_sim.py:50 TERRAIN = ["Cached clips only (tool 2)", "USGS 3DEP service", "Local DEM folder"], so TSRC 0 is the setting the 0.28.0 changelog replaced because a flood that reached the step 2 clip edge was cut off there and the dam screened too low (3,937 ac-ft dam Medium instead of High); tool 7's default and the window now follow the terrain source and the preflight warns about 'cached clips only'. Tool 9 still hard-codes it, with no parameter to change it. It also fixes MODE 0 (piping), CELL 10 m and SIMH 12 h whatever the user chose in steps 5 and 7, and reads only hz_class from each variant, never edge_flag.  
  What you see: The variant floods can be truncated at the clip edge, so each variant's class is a floor. 'class_stable = yes' can mean every variant was cut off at the same edge, and class_min / class_max can both be lower than the real range. A user who ran step 5 with overtopping gets a 'base' variant that is not his base run.  
  Fix: Add TSRC and LOCAL (and MODE) parameters to tool 9, defaulting to tool 7's (TSRC 1, 3DEP) and filled by the window from its settings; read edge_flag from each variant's output and write 'truncated' into sens_detail so a stable-but-truncated result is visible.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-A-4.
- **Tool 9 pins tool 7's terrain to 'cached clips only', so its variants run on a clip that cannot grow and can all be cut off at the edge** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:137`  
  Tool 9 hard-codes TSRC = 0 (cached clips only) for every variant run, while tool 7's own default is 1 (USGS 3DEP). With TSRC = cache, run_breach_sim.py:544 sets the largest clip equal to the starting clip, so the terrain cannot grow at all. The comment tool 7 carries at lines 195-200 says exactly what that costs: on a 3,937 ac-ft dam, cached clips only gave Medium with 0 buildings where a clip that could grow gave High with 27 buildings and 86 roads. Tool 9 keeps only hz_class from each variant, not hz_conf or edge_flag, so a truncated variant leaves no trace in the output.  
  What you see: The engineer runs tool 9 on the borderline dams and gets a class range computed on floods that stopped at the edge of the step 2 clip. The base variant can come out lower than tool 8's class for that dam, and when every variant is truncated the same way the table reports class_stable = yes: a dam whose class really does depend on the breach equation looks settled and never reaches the review queue.  
  Fix: Pass the tool-7 terrain choices through instead of pinning them: add TSRC (and LOCAL) to tool 9's parameters with tool 7's defaults, or read them from the settings file, and pass RMAX_FT with them. Together with the PRESET fix in S3b-B-1 this is the one change that makes tool 9's base variant reproduce tool 8.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-BV-2.
- **Tool 9 hard-codes tool 7's run settings and misses PRESET, so its variants run at the Quick 50 m preset whatever it asks for** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:139`  
  Tool 9 calls nddam:runbreachsim with a fixed dictionary of tool-7 settings written before tool 7 got its PRESET parameter. It does not pass PRESET, so QGIS (and the test mock) fall back to the default, 0 = 'Quick screen (50 m grid)', and tool 7 then overwrites cell, simh and simmax with 50 m / 12 h / 48 h (run_breach_sim.py:55, 299-305). The 'CELL': 10, 'SIMH': 12, 'SIMMAX': 96 that tool 9 passes are dead values. It also passes the old metre keys CELL / CORRW / RMAX instead of CELL_FT / CORRW_FT / RMAX_FT, pins SCEN to 0 and SUNNY to False (a sunny-day run is re-run as top-of-dam), and the same settings live a second time in core/workflow.py's tool-7 builder and a third time in estimate_runt  
  What you see: The engineer who ran step 7 at the Standard or Detailed preset gets a sensitivity table whose base variant was computed on a 50 m grid: its class can differ from tool 8's class for the same dam, and 'class_stable = no' can come from the grid change rather than the breach equation. The review queue then lists dams for the wrong reason.  
  Fix: Give tool 9 the same PRESET enum as tool 7 (or read tool 7's cell / simh / simmax from the settings file) and pass PRESET = 3 (Custom) with those three values and the _FT keys; take SCEN from the dams layer's 'scenario' field. Longer term, one shared `tool7_params(settings)` builder in core/workflow.py used by the window, tool 9 and tool 12 removes the three copies.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-1.
- **Tool 6 resolves field names by exact match while tool 8 ignores case: the same roads layer is read by one and not the other** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/triage.py:133`  
  Tool 8 got a case-insensitive field finder `pick` in 0.18.0 (hazard_classification.py:288-300, 'a layer with AADT is found by the default aadt'). Tool 6 kept its own exact-match copy for the same six fields (OCC, PRIM, habitable, ROAD_AADT, ROAD_CLASS, R_SOLE), so the two tools that are documented to apply 'the same rule as tool 8' read a layer differently.  
  What you see: With a roads layer whose field is spelled AADT (a DOT export, not the tool-0 package), tool 8 reads the traffic counts but tool 6 finds no AADT field, treats every overtopped road as 'unknown AADT = Medium' and refuses to clear the dam. Dams that tool 8 would class Low go to the 2D model anyway: hours of model time, in the conservative direction only.  
  Fix: Move `pick` from tool 8 into algorithms/_common.py and call it from triage.py:130-136 (and from sensitivity.py, which passes the names through). Removes the 7 duplicate lines and the drift.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-3.
- **Tool 6 ignores a roads AADT column spelled with capitals and sends the dams to the 2D model** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/triage.py:134`  
  Tool 6 matches the field names exactly, so the default 'aadt' does not find a column called AADT (ND GIS Hub county roads carry AADT / AADT_YR; see nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:396). The field is dropped with no message. Every overtopped road then has an unknown AADT, which the same rules module treats as Medium (core/nd_hazard.py:589), so the road counts and the dam cannot be cleared. The window passes no ROAD_AADT for step 6 or 8 (core/workflow.py:1297-1307), so the default is what runs. The same line drops OCC_CLS / PRIM_OCC / sole_access / habitable, and tool 6 also has no 'unknown AADT' setting, so it always assumes Medium even when tool 8 is set to something else.  
  What you see: With his own roads layer the engineer gets almost no dams cleared by the quick screen: the county then runs the full 2D flood model on dams that triage should have dropped in a second each, hours of run time for nothing, and the reason column says 'N road(s) inside the envelope' while tool 8, on the same layer, finds the AADT and clears them. Nothing in the tool 6 log says the column was ignored, although the help says the fields work 'as tool 8'.  
  Fix: Move tool 8's pick() helper into algorithms/_common.py and use it in tool 6 for OCC, PRIM, ROAD_AADT, ROAD_CLASS, R_SOLE and 'habitable', logging the spelling it found; warn when a named field is not found at all.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-AV-1.
- **Three drifted compat.py files (434 lines) and hand-rolled enum lookups outside them** `nd_dam_screening/compat.py:16`  
  Screening (161 lines), Hazard (219) and the Runner (54) each have a compat.py with its own _first; cmp/diff: none identical, 15 names shared with Hazard, 7 with the Runner, the rest unique to each. Screening's lacks the message levels, so plugin.py:8-19 re-implements them as _level() while Hazard's and the Runner's compat export MSG_INFO/MSG_WARNING/MSG_CRITICAL; dialog.py:1985-1988 resolves FlagOptional by hand and 1148-1150 and 1224-1226 resolve SelectionBehavior/EditTrigger/ResizeMode twice. The Updater has no compat at all.  
  What you see: A Qt6 (QGIS 4) fix lands in one plugin and not the others; the same enum is spelled three ways in the suite.  
  Fix: One damsuite/compat.py with the union of the three (damsuite is already copied byte-identical into all four plugins); each plugin's compat.py becomes a one-line re-export or goes, plugin.py drops _level, dialog.py uses the shared names. Saves two files and roughly 250 lines plus the drift.  
  Effort: medium. Id S4-B-14.
- **The breach-parameter sensitivity row always assumes Froehlich 2008 piping, even when the dam was run another way** `nd_dam_screening/core/breach_band.py:76`  
  Tool 5 lets the engineer pick the breach equation and piping or overtopping, and records both on the dam (brch_eq, brch_mode). The sensitivity row in tool 8 ignores both: it always computes the low, middle and high breaches in piping mode and always treats Froehlich 2008 as the middle one, and it scales the modelled flood by the change from that middle peak. For an overtopping dam the middle breach is about 30 percent narrower than the one actually modelled, so the spread is measured around a breach the run never used.  
  What you see: For any dam screened as overtopping or with another equation (Von Thun, MacDonald, Froehlich 1995), the bp_detail column prints a middle breach width and formation time that disagree with the dam's own breach fields, and the spread around it is wrong in both directions: the dam can be flagged "class depends on breach parameters" and pushed to the review list (hazard_classification.py:665-667 also drops its confidence to "review") on a comparison it never ran, or be called stable when it is not.  
  Fix: In hazard_classification.band_row read brch_mode and brch_eq from the feature and pass them through: bbd.params(vw, hb, mode=mode, mid=eq), and pass the dam's own routed peak as bbd.classes(..., mid_peak=<the run's qp>) so the bands are measured against the flood that was modelled. Where the equation is not one of SPREAD_METHODS, say so in bp_detail instead of silently using F2008.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-AV-2.
- **The breach-sensitivity row uses a breach wider than the dam, so dams are flagged "class depends on the breach parameters" for no real reason** `nd_dam_screening/core/breach_band.py:93`  
  params() takes the widest of the five equations with no check against the length of the embankment, while step 5's analyze_dam caps the width at the crest length and says so in a note. For a low dam with a lot of water the MacDonald & Langridge-Monopolis width (computed with the assumed 12 ft crest and 3H:1V faces, because the inventory has no section) runs away. Running the module on a 50,000 ac-ft reservoir behind a 15 ft dam gives: low 218 ft, mid 293 ft, high 3,395 ft forming in 4 minutes, with routed peaks of 30,205, 37,511 and 592,587 cfs. The high band's peak is 16 times the modelled one, and scale_factors turns that into a depth 5 times larger.  
  What you see: Prairie dams that are low and hold a lot of water get the note "class depends on breach parameters" and are pushed from hz_conf "medium" to "review" (hazard_classification.py:666-670) because of a breach 3,400 ft wide across a dam that is nowhere near that long. The engineer is sent to re-model dams whose spread is an artefact of one equation with no crest-length limit, and the note sits first in hz_notes where it pushes the dam's real notes out of the 254 characters.  
  Fix: Pass the dam's crest length into breach_band.params (hazard_classification already reads the dams layer, which carries crest_len_m) and apply the same cap step 5 applies: bavg = max(crest_len - z*hb, 0.3*crest_len), with the same note. Better still, call one shared helper so the cap can never be in one place and not the other.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-BV-2.
- **Screening breach routing has drifted from the Runner's ND 5.1.1 breach (equations agree, routing does not)** `nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py:314`  
  The width/time equations (Froehlich 2008/1995, Von Thun & Gillette, MLM, TR-60 peak, curvilinear shape) give the Runner's numbers and tests/test_tools_breach.py checks them. The level-pool routing does not match: Screening uses rounded weir coefficients 1.71 / 1.35 where the Runner uses 1.7115 / 1.3526; Screening floors the breach bottom width at 10% of the average width where the Runner floors it at 0; the Runner enforces a 60 s minimum formation time, Screening none; the stop rule (1% of peak vs 0.2%) and time step (tf/200 capped 20 s vs tf/300 capped 10 s) differ; Screening uses G = 9.81, the Runner G = 9.80665 (test_tools_breach.py:51 needs a 2e-4 tolerance on tf for exactly this). The d  
  What you see: For the same dam the Screening and Runner routed peaks differ for reasons unrelated to the storage curve and not said in any note. For most dams it is under 0.2% (the coefficients). For a small pond behind a tall dam (roughly under 20 ac-ft with a 30 ft breach, where bavg < z*hb) the 10% bottom-width floor makes the Screening peak visibly higher than the Runner's triangle. An engineer comparing the two hydrographs, as the Absaraka comparison did, gets a gap nobody can explain from the outputs.  
  Fix: Move the shared pieces (_positive, froehlich_2008/1995, von_thun_gillette/vtg_1990, mlm_1984, embankment_area_ft2, tr60_peak_cfs, curvilinear_shape, the two weir coefficients and G) into one damsuite module (e.g. damsuite/breach_eq.py) and import it in both breach_hydro.py and breach.py; delete the copied block and the 'copied from the Runner' header (about 120 lines in breach_hydro.py, the same a  
  Effort: medium. Id S1-B-1.
- **Runner vs Screener sheet: the screener's peak flow and model columns are always empty** `nd_dam_screening/core/comparison.py:72`  
  Tool 5 writes its scenario values with a prefix (breach_hydrograph.py:172-176 appends every SCEN_FIELDS name as "td_<name>" and "sd_<name>"), so the field on the layer is td_qp_m3s and there is no field called qp_m3s anywhere in the plugin. There is also no field called peak_qp_m3s, grid_m or dem_m: grid_m is a window setting key (core/workflow.py:220), not a layer field. runner_handoff.py:303 builds attrs straight from the input layer's own field names (attrs = {n: get(f, n) for n in names}), so all three lookups return None on every real run. The unit test hides it: tests/test_comparison.py:42-55 feeds hand-written keys qp_m3s, grid_m and dem_m that no tool ever writes.  
  What you see: In runner_vs_screener.html the "Peak breach flow (cfs) - Screener" cell is always a dash, the "(x N.NN)" runner-against-screener ratio beside the runner's peak never appears, and the "Model - Screener" cell is always blank. Comparing the two peaks is the main reason the sheet exists, so the engineer gets a sheet that compares only the class and the top of dam.  
  Fix: Read td_qp_m3s (falling back to sd_qp_m3s) for the screener peak and sim_cell_m for the screener grid, drop grid_m / dem_m or read the cell size the run recorded, and change SAMPLE_ATTRS in tests/test_comparison.py to the real field names so the test would have caught this.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-AV-1.
- **Two ways to download ND DWR LiDAR: the pasted FTP package link (untested, FTP only) and the HTTPS tile index** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:242`  
  fetch_dwr (242-296) plus parse_dwr_path (224-239) take a link copied from the map site and pull it over FTP; fetch_dwr_area (436-503) finds the tiles for a dam, county or state from the statewide index over HTTPS and is the default. The tool exposes both through DWR_PATH (download_dem.py:64-65, 'empty = tiles for the area over HTTPS') and the window has a dem_ftp box for it (dialog.py:797, 1014). fetch_dwr and parse_dwr_path have no test; fetch_dwr_area has seven. fetch_dwr_area also keeps a transport='ftp' branch (lines 450, 465-467, 491-492) that only a test ever selects.  
  What you see: A second route that needs the map site, a copied link and an open FTP port, which the plugin's own comment says is blocked on many networks: the user who fills the box gets a connection error where the empty box would have worked. One more field to understand on the DEM tab and one more tool parameter.  
  Fix: Delete fetch_dwr, parse_dwr_path and the transport parameter/FTP branch of fetch_dwr_area; remove the DWR_PATH parameter from download_dem.py (64-65, 117-120) and the dem_ftp box from dialog.py (797, 1014). Keep _ftp/ensure_index only as the index fallback _open_index already uses, or drop them too. Saves about 85 lines, one tool parameter and one window field.  
  Effort: medium. Id S1-B-9.
- **Switching the terrain source re-uses the other source's DEM and says only "reusing existing DEM"** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:539`  
  The standardised DEM file is named from the area and the resolution only, never from where the terrain came from, and tool 20 decides to re-use an existing DEM purely by that file name. The same name, for example Cass_10m_5070.tif, is what both the USGS 3DEP branch (download_dem.py:160) and the ND DWR LiDAR branch (download_dem.py:133) write, because both warp whatever they downloaded to the chosen 10 m or 1 m cell. The sidecar text file beside it records the real source, but nothing compares it with what was asked for.  
  What you see: A user who downloads a county at 10 m from 3DEP, then changes the source to ND DWR LiDAR and runs tool 20 again for the same county and resolution, is told "reusing existing DEM" and gets the 3DEP file back. Every later step then screens that county on the terrain they thought they had replaced, and the only hint is the _dem.txt file nobody opens.  
  Fix: Put the source in the stem (for example <county>_10m_5070_3dep.tif and ..._dwr.tif, source_key is already passed to write_sidecar), or read the sidecar's "Source:" line in the re-use check at download_dem.py:106 and download again when it does not match the chosen source. Either way say in the log which source the re-used file came from.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-AV-1.
- **'Wet' is defined in several places with different depths; the dams table area_ha and the extent polygon disagree** `nd_dam_screening/core/flood_polygons.py:45`  
  The flood polygons (and the hazard step) call a cell wet at 0.1 ft = 0.03048 m (hazard.DEFAULT_WET_M). Tool 7's dams table computes area_ha at 0.05 m (run_breach_sim.py:648 and :694 for the second scenario). runner.DEFAULTS wet_m = 0.05 decides the edge and cascade checks. fv_engine arrive_thresh (0.03), solver_io.read_results dry_thresh (0.03) and domain.still_growing depth_min (0.15) are three more literals for the same idea.  
  What you see: For one dam the extent polygon in 7_flood_polygons.gpkg covers more ground than the area_ha number in the dams layer for the same run, because they use different wet depths. An engineer who measures the polygon in QGIS and compares with area_ha sees two areas for one flood and cannot tell which is the screening result.  
  Fix: Make hazard.DEFAULT_WET_M the single wet depth: run_breach_sim computes area_ha as fp.wet_mask(depth).sum() * cell^2, runner.DEFAULTS sets wet_m = DEFAULT_WET_M, and fv_engine / solver_io take their thresholds from the same constant. One setting instead of five literals; area_ha then matches the polygon.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-3.
- **Tool 8 offers five lethality methods of which three are, by its own text, not to be used on its own floods: two comparison-only criteria and the 1D rule** `nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:142`  
  Tool 8 has 34 parameters (gui_report.json). Its CRIT enum lists 5 methods; 'feinberg' and 'curve' are labelled 'comparison only, not the ND authority' and the 1D rule is labelled '1D models only' while every tool 7 engine is 2D (the tool prints a warning and then runs it anyway, hazard_classification.py:279-281). Two further parameters exist only for these choices: CURVE (depth-velocity CSV) and RULE (1D lethal depth). In hazard.py about 90 lines serve them: lines 102-137 (FEINBERG, load_curve, structure_lethal), 149-155 (labels, ONE_D_WARNING), 341-366 (_comparison_finding) and 400-424 (the comparison branch of classify_nd, which re-derives hazard_class, governing and borderline_raised outs  
  What you see: A user picking a method from the list sees three choices the tool itself says are wrong for its results; the first-run default is right, but the list and two extra file/number boxes add to a 34-parameter dialog and the per-method branches in classify_nd and hazard_classification.py are code that is tested but never meant to be used.  
  Fix: Make hz.METHODS = (nh.LETHALITY_CHARTS, nh.LETHALITY_ALL_WET) for tool 8; delete COMPARISON*, FEINBERG, load_curve, structure_lethal, _comparison_finding, ONE_D_WARNING and the comparison branch of classify_nd (about 90 lines of hazard.py), the CURVE and RULE parameters and their branches in hazard_classification.py (lines 170-171, 185-186, 245-248, 279-281, 620, 643), and section 7 of test_nd_haz  
  Effort: medium. Id S2b-B-3.
- **A flood cut off in the second loading case is never flagged: incomplete.py reads scen2_edge_flag and then ignores it, and does not know tool 8's wording** `nd_dam_screening/core/incomplete.py:77`  
  With both loading cases run (settings 'both_one_dam' is True by default, so every 'Run one dam' run does both), the governing class can come from the second case. Step 7 records that case's truncation in scen2_edge_flag; reasons() looks only at edge_flag, so the row is treated as whole. The step 8 row does carry tool 8's own sentence in hz_notes, but none of the words in NOTE_WORDS appear in it, and the row is not FAILED and not NoData, so reasons() returns nothing there too.  
  What you see: The red line the Simple tab and the dam table were added for ('N of M dams incomplete - their class is a FLOOR, not an answer') stays empty for exactly the case this release is about when the truncation happened in the sunny-day run: the engineer reads a Medium that would be High with a larger clip, with nothing on the screen saying so. The HTML report still catches it, through the lowered confidence, but the window does not.  
  Fix: In reasons(), test scen2_edge_flag beside edge_flag, and add 'truncated at the terrain edge' / 'truncat' to NOTE_WORDS so tool 8's own sentence is recognised (the words already match damsuite.priority.INCOMPLETE_WORDS, so the review list's grouping stays in step).  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-AV-2.
- **A flood cut off in the second loading case gets no flag (scen2_edge_flag is read but never looked at)** `nd_dam_screening/core/incomplete.py:77`  
  Since 0.28.0 step 7 can run both loading cases and records the second one's truncation in its own column, scen2_edge_flag (run_breach_sim.py:690); tool 8 is careful to read the governing case's column (hazard_classification.py:624-625). core/incomplete.py fetches scen2_edge_flag in READ_FIELDS (line 132) but reasons() never reads it, and none of NOTE_WORDS (43-47) matches the words tool 8 writes into hz_notes for that case ('flood truncated at the terrain edge'), so nothing catches it. The window's Flags column and its red summary line both come from this function (dialog.py:2601 and 1768).  
  What you see: A dam whose sunny-day flood was the one that set the class and was cut off at the clip edge shows an empty Flags cell in the Results tab and is not counted in the red 'N of M dam(s) incomplete - their class is a FLOOR' line. Only the Confidence column's 'review' hints at it, which is the quiet failure this module was written to end.  
  Fix: In reasons(), read both columns: 'if _yes(row.get("edge_flag")) or _yes(row.get("scen2_edge_flag"))'. Add ('truncated at the terrain edge', EDGE) to NOTE_WORDS so a step-8 row's hz_notes is recognised as well, and add 'scen2_notes' to the _text call at line 79. Three lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-BV-2.
- **proposed.py and nd_counties.py are unguarded copies with no declared master** `nd_dam_screening/core/proposed.py:1`  
  cmp shows the three files are byte-identical today. nd_hazard.py and acer_tm11_charts.json are declared Runner-master (hazard.py lines 5-6) and checked by test_nd_hazard_adapter.py:25-33. proposed.py has no such statement and no test in either plugin compares the copies; the Screening uses the whole module (propose_dam.py: build, json_safe_spec, polyline_geom, side_of_point) while the Runner uses only crest_check and PHRASE, so the Screening is the master in practice and the Runner carries about 450 lines it never calls. nd_counties.py's docstring says 'the same file in both' without naming a master; only the Runner's test_v3131 checks equality, the Screening's tests do not.  
  What you see: A fix to the embankment geometry, the stage-storage rim check or a county outline in one plugin silently leaves the other behind, so the same proposed dam gets a different crest check or storage in the Runner than in the Screening, and nothing in the Screening test run says so.  
  Fix: State the master in each copy's first lines (Screening for proposed.py and nd_counties.py, Runner for nd_hazard.py as now), add filecmp checks for both files to the Screening tests (like test_nd_hazard_adapter) or to the FILES.json hash list the damsuite already uses, and reduce the Runner's proposed.py to crest_check and PHRASE if it is to stay a separate file.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-7.
- **proposed.py is the only shared module whose two copies are never checked for identity; the Runner silently skips the embankment if they drift** `nd_dam_screening/core/proposed.py:23`  
  Three modules are copied between nd_dam_screening/core and nd_breach_runner/core. Today all three are byte-identical (cmp). nd_hazard.py (+ charts JSON) is guarded by the Screening test, nd_counties.py (+ GeoJSON) by the Runner test, but no test in either plugin and no FILES.json entry (FILES.json covers only the 27 damsuite files) names proposed.py. The Screening writes the proposed-dam block with this module (propose_dam.py: build, json_safe_spec, side_of_point, polyline_geom), the Runner reads it with its own copy (insert_embankment, PHRASE, crest_check), and the Runner's first check is a string compare against PHRASE.  
  What you see: If one copy is edited (even the PHRASE text or a slope convention), the Runner will quietly model a proposed dam on the bare pre-dam ground with no warning, or insert a differently shaped embankment than the Screening measured. Nothing in the test suite would catch it.  
  Fix: Add "proposed.py" to the copy loop in tests/test_nd_hazard_adapter.py (one word) and to the Runner's test_v3131 loop, or list the three shared modules in a FILES.json like damsuite. State in proposed.py's docstring that the Screening copy is the master, as hazard.py does for nd_hazard.py.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-2.
- **The report's "conservative, chart not signed off" label can never appear, whatever the record holds** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:231`  
  The words "Conservative, no chart" exist in only one place in the suite: nd_hazard.LETHALITY_LABELS (core/nd_hazard.py:78), which is the Runner's own wording for its text report. Tool 8 of the Screening writes a different sentence into hz_basis: hazard_classification.py:274 sets basis = hz.METHOD_LABELS[crit], and core/hazard.py:146-148 words the conservative method as 'ND 5.3 conservative: every inundated habitable structure potentially lethal (no chart)'. The two strings never match, so _conservative_note() is false for every screening record and class_display() always returns the plain class.  
  What you see: Even after the missing hz_basis column is fixed (S2b-A-1), a dam that was classified High by the conservative no-chart rule is still shown as a plain 'High' on its own page and in the statewide table, with no 'conservative: TM 11 chart not signed off' label and no card telling the engineer that the ACER TM 11 charts were not used and where to sign them off. The engineer cannot tell a chart-based High from a conservative High.  
  Fix: Match on the method key, not the wording: write hz_criterion into the report record (it is already a tool-8 field) and test rec.get("hz_criterion") == nd_hazard.LETHALITY_ALL_WET; or make core/hazard.METHOD_LABELS reuse nd_hazard.LETHALITY_LABELS so the two plugins word the method the same way.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-AV-3.
- **A dam the reviewer excluded shows in the report as green "done" with class "NoData"** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:414`  
  When the Review tab excludes a dam, tool 7 writes sim_status = "excluded" (run_breach_sim.py:571) and tool 8 writes hz_class = "" with hz_conf = "excluded" and the reason in hz_reason (hazard_classification.py:520-522). _run_status puts "excluded" in the same bucket as a finished run, so the Status cell reads a green 'done'; class_display turns the blank class into 'NoData'. The statewide table's columns are ID, Dam, Status, Screened, DWR, Comparison, Peak, Bldg wet, Lethal, ac-ft and Review - none of them is hz_conf or hz_reason - and no per-dam page is written either, because reports.py:99 needs a depth raster and an excluded dam has none.  
  What you see: On index.html an excluded dam looks exactly like a dam the model failed to classify: 'done' in green, a grey 'NoData' class, an empty Review column, no link to a page and nothing anywhere saying the reviewer excluded it. The four class tiles also stop adding up to the 'Dams screened' count (excluded dams fall outside High/Medium/Low/NoData) with no tile to explain the difference.  
  Fix: Give _run_status its own 'excluded' label and style, show hz_conf (or hz_reason) as a column in the table and in SUMMARY_COLS, and add an 'Excluded by review' tile so the counts add up to the number of dams screened.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-AV-4.
- **'Copy N dam IDs to rerun' button is broken HTML and does nothing** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:442`  
  The IDs are wrapped in literal double quotes inside a double-quoted onclick attribute. The attribute value therefore ends after 'writeText([' (html.parser gives onclick='navigator.clipboard.writeText([' and the rest becomes stray attributes). Clicking runs a JavaScript syntax error.  
  What you see: On the statewide summary page the rerun button appears whenever a dam failed or was cut off, but clicking it copies nothing and never changes to 'Copied!'.  
  Fix: Quote the IDs with single quotes or &quot; (ids_js = ", ".join("'{}'".format(html.escape(str(i), quote=True)) ...)), or store the list in a data attribute and attach the handler in JS.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-6.
- **summary.csv 'review' column is text, but the Review tab and the counts line look for yes/1** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:89`  
  core/report.py writes the review column as the joined reasons ('simulation failed; confidence low') or an empty string (test_report.py:40 confirms). review.queue (line 89) and workflow.summary_counts (workflow.py:1710) both accept only 1/true/yes/y, so the real file never matches. test_popup.py:180 writes a synthetic summary.csv with 1/yes, a format the report never produces.  
  What you see: The Review tab's fourth source ('in the report's review queue') never adds a dam, so a dam the report flags only for a failed simulation, a truncated flood or a class change is missing from the tab; the Simple tab's counts line (dialog.py:1876) never says 'N flagged for review' although the report's review queue lists N dams.  
  Fix: In both readers treat any non-empty review text as flagged: flagged = bool(str(row.get("review") or "").strip()) (keep the yes/1 forms for old files), and change test_popup.py to write the real format.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-4.
- **The report's review column is a sentence, but the Review tab and the main window read it as a yes/no flag, so the count is always zero** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:89`  
  Tool 10 writes the reason text into the review column of summary.csv. Both readers test that text against the four words "1", "true", "yes" and "y", which it never is, so the report's review queue is never seen. The test that covers this (tests/test_popup.py:180) writes its own summary.csv with "1" and "yes" instead of what the report actually writes, so it passes.  
  What you see: The Review tab never lists a dam that only the report flagged: a failed simulation, a flood cut off at the terrain edge, a class that changes between sensitivity runs, an estimated height or storage. The main window's results line never says "N flagged for review" either, however many dams the report's own review queue holds, so the window and the report disagree.  
  Fix: In both places treat any non-empty value as flagged: `if str(row.get("review") or "").strip():` (the old yes/1 spellings still pass). Change the fake summary.csv in tests/test_popup.py:180 to the text report.summary really writes, and add one queue() test over a report folder.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-BV-1.
- **Reservoir-in-grid engine crashes instead of falling back when the reservoir reaches the largest clip** `nd_dam_screening/core/runner.py:243`  
  _reservoir_setup returns the string "grow" when the reservoir touches the clip edge. At the largest clip the code switches reservoir_mode off and promises injection instead, but leaves rset == "grow". Line 243 tests only `if rset:` and then does rset["reservoir"] on a string, a TypeError. Every other use of rset guards with `not isinstance(rset, str)` (lines 254, 279, 285, 378); this one does not. Reproduced with a synthetic 60 x 60 grid, engine builtin-reservoir, radius_m = radius_max_m: status FAILED, notes ['reservoir larger than the maximum clip; used injection instead', "string indices must be integers, not 'str'"].  
  What you see: With the 4th engine of tool 7 ('Built-in, reservoir in the grid') a dam whose reservoir is larger than the largest clip (the big lakes, exactly the dams this engine is meant to improve) comes out FAILED with the note 'string indices must be integers' instead of the promised injection run. The broad except at line 351 turns the crash into a FAILED row, so the batch continues but that dam has no flood.  
  Fix: Line 243: `if rset and not isinstance(rset, str):`, or set `rset = None` right after `reservoir_mode = False` at line 235. Add the case to tests/test_runner.py (reservoir reaching the edge at radius_max_m must give status ok with injection).  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-2.
- **With the automatic cell size the run-time label quotes the 50 m figure, up to 75 times too fast** `nd_dam_screening/core/runplan.py:94`  
  With the Custom preset and the Grid size box at 0 the cell size is chosen per dam (3 to 20 m, never finer than the DEM: core/domain.auto_cell, and run_breach_sim.py:484 passes cell="auto"). effective_cell correctly reports that as "no single number" by returning None, but seconds_per_dam turns None into 50 m, the fastest anchor in the table. Every automatic cell size the tool can pick is finer and therefore slower: 12 s per dam is quoted where the table itself says 120 s at 20 m and 900 s at 10 m.  
  What you see: The label beside the elapsed timer tells the engineer a county of 400 dams will take about 20 minutes when at the automatic 10 m cell it is nearer a day, so a run is started in the afternoon that cannot finish. The label does say "order of magnitude only", but this is out by a factor of ten to seventy five.  
  Fix: In run_time_text, when effective_cell returns cell_m None, either use the slowest anchor (10 m) and word it as "up to", or give a range from the automatic sizes and say the number depends on each dam's reservoir; do not let seconds_per_dam silently substitute 50 m.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-AV-3.
- **The window's "how long will this take" label is ten times faster than the preset in the same window** `nd_dam_screening/core/runplan.py:108`  
  The anchors are the per-dam figures printed in the preset names, which runplan's own docstring says are for the built-in engine. The engine box defaults to item 0, LISFLOOD-FP ACC, so the label multiplies them by the unmeasured 0.1 factor. I ran it: with the default engine and the default Quick screen preset, run_time_text(37 dams) returns "37 dam(s) x about 1 s/dam at 164 ft (50 m) = about 44 s", while the preset combo three boxes away says "Quick screen (50 m grid, ~12 s/dam)". At Detailed the label says 2 min/dam against the preset's ~15 min/dam.  
  What you see: One window shows two per-dam times for the same run that differ by a factor of ten, so neither can be trusted. An engineer planning a county reads "about 44 s" for 37 dams, starts the run before leaving for the day, and gets something between minutes and hours instead.  
  Fix: Either state the engine in the preset names and in the label, or drop the LISFLOOD factor from the label (keep it only in tool 12, where a measured rate backs it). The honest short form is to say the figures are built-in-engine times and show them unscaled until someone has timed LISFLOOD-FP on this computer, which is what the module's own comment asks for.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-BV-3.
- **The self-test's cell size and simulated time are thrown away: tool 7 runs the Quick preset and the report states a grid that was never used** `nd_dam_screening/core/selftest.py:193`  
  Tool 14 asks the user for a step 7 cell size (selftest.py:98-99, default 65 ft) and a simulated time (selftest.py:100-101, default 1 h) and hands them to tool 7 as CELL, SIMH and SIMMAX. Tool 7 has had a resolution preset since item 7, and the self-test does not pass PRESET, so tool 7 takes the default Quick preset and overwrites all three with 50 m, 12 h and 48 h. The two parameters on the self-test's own dialog do nothing, and core/selftest.py:271 then writes the numbers the user asked for into the report as though the run had used them.  
  What you see: The self-test report says 'tools 1-8 and 10 with the chosen engine on a 19.8 m grid for 1 h of flood' when the flood was actually modelled on a 50 m grid for 12 h. The run also takes far longer than the 1 h of flood the engineer asked for, and on a fresh QGIS a coarse 50 m grid over the small synthetic valley is a weaker test of the engine than the one the tool advertises.  
  Fix: Add "PRESET": 3 (Custom) to the step 7 parameters in core/selftest.py:189-196 next to CELL, SIMH and SIMMAX, and switch CELL to CELL_FT while there. One key, and the self-test then runs and reports the same grid.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-BV-3.
- **Manning's n by land cover is defined in two plugins with different values** `nd_dam_screening/core/solver_io.py:22`  
  nd_breach_runner/core/nd_data.py:21 has its own NLCD_N with references (Chow 1959, HEC-RAS 2D Table 3-1). Four shared classes differ: 21 developed open space 0.040 vs 0.035, 23 developed medium 0.120 vs 0.100, 81 pasture 0.033 vs 0.035, 82 cultivated crops 0.037 vs 0.040; the Runner also has 51, 72, 73, 74 which Screening lacks. Both tables say 'edit for your standard', so an engineer who edits one does not know about the other.  
  What you see: The same land cover gets a different roughness in the two plugins. Cultivated crops (class 82) cover most ND valleys and differ by 8%, so a Screening flood and a Runner flood of the same dam over the same NLCD raster have different depths and extents for a reason that appears in no note, and a Screening-vs-Runner comparison (tool 'Compare with a reference model') reads a roughness choice as a model difference.  
  Fix: Keep one table in the shared damsuite package (the Runner's nd_data.NLCD_N is the fuller, referenced one) and have solver_io.manning_from_nlcd import it; delete solver_io.py lines 21-24. One table, one place to edit, and the two plugins agree.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-2.
- **Dam folder-name rule copied six times across three plugins, plus a seventh rule that differs** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:476`  
  The regex re.sub(r"[^\w\- ]+", "_", ...) that names a dam's folder under the Dams folder is written out in workflow.py:479 (_runner_name), handoff.py:73 (safe_name), ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/portfolio.py:564, nd_breach_runner/algorithms.py:108, nd_breach_runner/core/damjson.py:57 and nd_breach_runner/scripts/ndbreach_cli.py:438, while damsuite/dam_record.py (the shared dam.json module) has no such function. workflow.py:1489 then defines a different safe_name (keeps '.', turns spaces into '_'), and algorithms/propose_dam.py:409 uses that one to write the proposed dam's folder (os.path.join(folder, wf.safe_name(dam_id))), whereas dam_folder_in (493), hazard_classification.py:691 and runner_handoff  
  What you see: A proposed-dam ID containing a dot (a user-typed ID field such as 'Site 3.2') is written to the folder 'Site_3.2' by tool 19 but looked for as 'Site_3_2' by the window, so Continue says the dam 'has no dam record yet'. Any future change to the rule (for example Windows-reserved names) has to be made in six files in three plugins or the plugins stop finding each other's folders.  
  Fix: Add one folder_name(dam_id) to damsuite/dam_record.py and delete the six copies (two of them in this unit: workflow._runner_name and handoff.safe_name); make propose_dam._write_record and workflow.dam_row_for call it; keep workflow.safe_name only for the one_dam/<id> work-folder names. Saves six functions (about 25 lines) and leaves one rule.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-2.
- **Every window refresh re-reads and SHA-256s whole output folders (2_terrain, 7_simulations) on the window's thread** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1086`  
  The steps' manifests record folders as inputs (defaults() passes DEMDIR = the 2_terrain folder to steps 6, 7, 9, 10 and 12, and the 7_simulations folder to step 10), and manifest._digest of a folder walks it and takes the SHA-256 of every file in it. statuses() asks for that of every finished step, and the window calls statuses() from refresh(), which runs when the window is created, whenever it is activated (dialog.py:1902 changeEvent) and at every step boundary of a chain (chain_next also calls statuses()). damsuite.chain keeps a per-session cache keyed by file time, so the cost falls on the first refresh after QGIS starts and on every refresh after new rasters appear.  
  What you see: For a county whose 2_terrain and 7_simulations folders hold gigabytes of rasters, clicking back into the Screening window (and each step boundary during a run) freezes the window, and QGIS with it, while the disk is read end to end, with no message and no way to stop it. Windows marks QGIS 'Not responding'.  
  Fix: Do not hash folders for the out-of-date test: compare the folder's file count and newest modification time (or hash only the manifest's file inputs and treat folder inputs by date), or compute the test off the window's thread and cache the verdict until the work folder changes.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2a-AV-1.
- **'Run one dam' / 'Run selected dams' find no dams in a DWR GeoPackage layer: dam_choices reads only the shapefile field name 'structure_'** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1577`  
  Before step 1 has written 1_dams.gpkg, the dam picker reads the DWR layer itself, but only through the dBase-truncated field 'structure_'. The DWR GeoPackage export (tests/test_dwr_kinds_019.py DAM_FIELDS, test_autofill_0211.py) and the dwr/ drop-in GeoPackage carry 'structure_id'; tool 1 (inventory.resolve_fields) and the preflight (any_of=(('structure_','structure_id'),)) both know this, dam_choices does not. The window's _read_rows returns None for a field the layer lacks, so every row is skipped; the bare `except Exception: pass` at line 1585 also hides a DWR layer that cannot be opened at all.  
  What you see: With a GeoPackage DWR layer (the pack's dwr clip, the dwr/ folder file or a chosen GeoPackage) and no step-1 output yet, 'Run one dam...' and 'Run selected dams...' show 'No dams to choose from yet: run step 1 (or pick the DWR or water-body layer on the Inputs tab) first.' even though the DWR layer is found and shown on the Simple tab's Found line. The engineer is told to do something already done.  
  Fix: Resolve the ID and name fields from the layer's own field list before reading: ask read_rows for the layer's field names (or extend it) and pick the first of ('structure_id', 'structure_') / ('dam_name', 'name') that exists, or call inventory.resolve_fields({}, names) and use fld['ID'] / fld['NAME']. Log the exception at line 1585 instead of passing silently.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-2.
- **Results counts line never shows the review count: it tests summary.csv's review column for yes/1/true, but the report writes a text of reasons** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1710`  
  summary_counts() reads <work>/10_reports/summary.csv and counts a dam as flagged only when the review cell is literally 1/true/yes/y. report.py fills that cell with the review reasons joined by '; ' (tests/test_report*.py: rows[2]['review'] contains 'simulation failed'), or an empty string. No value ever matches, so counts['review'] stays 0 and counts_text() never appends '; N flagged for review'.  
  What you see: The Results tab's summary line (dialog.py:1876 counts_label) says e.g. '37 dam(s): 3 High, 10 Medium, 24 Low. Screening classes only...' and never the number of dams flagged for review, although the report's review queue and the CSV have them. The engineer reading the window does not see that any dam needs a look.  
  Fix: Count a non-empty cell: `if (row.get("review") or "").strip(): counts["review"] += 1`. The existing test for counts_text can get a row with review = 'confidence review'.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-1.
- **An all-failed step 7 only stops the chain when no dam was cleared by the quick screen** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1895`  
  step_status_counts (1849-1882) counts EVERY row of 7_simulations.gpkg, and step 7 writes sim_status for dams it never modelled too: 'triage', 'noflood' and 'excluded' (run_breach_sim.py:628, the three statuses incomplete.WHOLE_STATUSES lists). So 'bad == total' - the test Chain._finished uses to stop the run at workflow.py:1997 - is only ever true when not one dam was cleared by the quick screen, released no flood, or was excluded on the Review tab. With any such dam in the table the chain takes the other branch at 2001, logs one warning line, and runs the classification and the reports on nothing. The function's own docstring (1886-1888) says this is the case it exists to prevent: 'the chai  
  What you see: In a county run with the quick screen on, if every modelled flood fails (no LISFLOOD-FP program, the 3DEP service down, a bad local DEM folder), the run does not stop: step 8 gives those dams NoData, tool 10 writes index.html and summary.csv, and the window ends with a finished chain and one warning buried in the log. Nick gets a report for a county where nothing was modelled.  
  Fix: Count only the dams that were meant to be modelled: in step_problem, modelled = total - sum(counts.get(s, 0) for s in ic.WHOLE_STATUSES), then return (bad >= max(modelled, 1), ...) and word the text 'the flood did not run for {bad} of the {modelled} dam(s) it was run for'. Three lines, and it reuses the status list core/incomplete.py already keeps.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-BV-1.
- **Between steps the chain clips the offline pack or downloads server data on the GUI thread, with no progress and no Cancel** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1970`  
  Chain._advance runs from the Run button and from each tool's completion callback (dialog._start_algorithm's on_done), i.e. on the Qt GUI thread. The tool itself goes to a QgsProcessingAlgRunnerTask, but defaults(..., prepare=True) first runs slot_origin(prepare=True) for every data slot the step needs, which reads the offline pack (R*Tree pre-selection, then zlib+JSON decode of every feature in the box) and writes <work>/0_suite_data/<slot>.gpkg through GpkgWriter, or with Data source = Download calls DD.fetch_role with no cancel callback and no size limit. The same synchronous call sits behind the step buttons (dialog.py:1921), 'Fill empty layers from the suite data' (dialog.py:1692) and th  
  What you see: When a run starts, and again before step 6 and step 8, the window and all of QGIS stop responding for the time it takes to cut structures, roads, railroads, lifelines and water bodies for the work area out of the pack (seconds for one dam, longer for a county, longest for a statewide run or a server download), with no message and no way to stop it. Windows marks QGIS 'Not responding'; an engineer who clicks again or closes the window can lose the run.  
  Fix: Do the preparation off the GUI thread: run defaults(prepare=True) inside a QgsTask (the chain already waits for a callback per step) and pass feedback.isCanceled as `cancel` to fetch_role; at minimum log a line before each clip starts and show a wait cursor. Alternatively write all clips once, in one background 'data step' at the beginning of the chain, so later steps find them ready (the prefligh  
  Effort: medium. Id S2a-A-3.

The Simple button was renamed from 'Run all' to 'Run county' in 0.28.0 (comment at 431-433) but the All-steps text still says 'Run all'. refresh() carries bookkeeping for both pairs (292-293, 1837-1851).

Nick cannot tell from the window which of the two copies a run used.

Either remove these 22 rows from the grid (add their keys to a per-tool skip list in settings_groups so full_catalog leaves them out, and let Run options be the only place), or remove them from Run options and have defaults() stop passing them so the grid rows apply. The remaining Run options rows are then only the per-computer paths (local DEM folder, executable, chart file, Runner

- **The 'enlarge the clip before the run' box cannot be switched off from the window** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:707`  
  workflow.py:1334 only passes the parameter when it is on: `if S.get("pregrow"): p["PREGROW"] = True`. The tool's own default is True (run_breach_sim.py:229-231, defaultValue=True), and so is the grid's row. Unticked, nothing is passed and the tool pre-grows anyway. test_popup.py:433-435 pins this ('switched off, the parameter is simply not passed'). The twin switch skip_done is passed when off (workflow.py:1337), so only this one is inert.

Keep on Run options only the paths of this computer (local DEM, exe, chart file, R

- **A 260-line DEM download box that only pre-fills the DEM tool's own dialog, copied into the Runner too** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:787`

  What you see: The Run options tab is twice as long as it needs to be; the same download sits in three places (Screening Run options, Runner Dam tab, Suite data), each with its own idea of the dam point and the output folder.  
  Fix: Delete the box in both windows. Keep one 'Download a DEM...' button (Run options or All steps next to '2 - Cache terrain') that opens nddam:downloaddem with DAM_POINT, DAMS_FOLDER and OUT pre-filled from the chosen dam. Saves about 260 lines and 20 controls in Screening, about 130 lines in the Runner, the ndscreen/dem_box/* QgsSettings keys and the untested methods.
