- **Three resolvers of 'the Dams folder' in one window, with different precedence** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:926`  
  _dem_box_dams (926-942) looks at the Run-options box, then the suite's Dams folder (SD.dams_folder()), then <work>/Dams. priority_folder (1238-1244) looks at the Priority tab's own box, then wf.dams_folder. wf.dams_folder (core/workflow.py:304-307) looks at the Run-options box, then <work>/Dams, and never at the suite's folder; step 8, the Results tab's record line (1877) and Open Runner (2545) use that one.  
  What you see: With the Run-options box empty and a suite Dams folder set, a DEM downloaded for dam X goes into the suite's Dams folder while step 8 writes X's dam.json under <work>/Dams and the Results tab and Open Runner look there. The same dam ends up in two folders and the home window shows one of them.  
  Fix: One function, wf.dams_folder, decides the order (window box, then the suite's Dams folder, then <work>/Dams, or whatever order the suite folder design wants) and _dem_box_dams and priority_folder call it; delete the two private resolvers (about 25 lines) and the Priority tab's box (S4-B-4).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-5.
- **Priority tab re-scans the same Dams folder the suite home's Dams tab already lists** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1252`  
  The Priority tab (lines 1187-1322: a Dams-folder box, Browse, 'Refresh the list', 'Export to Excel...', a 9-column table) reads the folder with the same damsuite.suite_home.scan that the shared suite home's Dams tab uses (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/suite_home.py:217-277: Dams folder box, Browse, Refresh, table of Dam ID, Name, Class, Existing, Screened, Modelled, Classified, Reviewed, Next). damsuite.priority is imported by nothing but this dialog and core/incomplete.py. Two windows, two Dams-folder boxes, two tables of the same dam.json files, one of them with a verdict column.  
  What you see: Nick has a 'Dams' list in the suite home and a 'Priority' list in Screening that disagree on columns and need refreshing separately; the review list exists only inside the Screening window although it concerns the whole programme.  
  Fix: Add the Verdict, Why and Flags columns (PR.rank) and the Excel export to the suite home's Dams tab, which all three plugins open, and delete the Priority tab. Removes one tab, three buttons, a Dams-folder box and about 136 lines from dialog.py; adds three columns to a table that exists.  
  Effort: medium. Id S4-B-4.
- **refresh() is not guarded while widgets are being filled: dozens of full work-folder re-reads at window open and on every settings-file / preset load** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1426`  
  _save_state and _autosave_settings return early while self._loading is True, but refresh() does not. Each layer combo is connected to _changed at line 576; in real Qt, clear() emits currentIndexChanged(-1), the first addItem emits currentIndexChanged(0), and setCurrentIndex(i>0) emits again, so one _fill_layers() triggers 22-33 refresh() calls; _apply (1368-1400) adds one per combo/checkbox/spinbox whose value changes. Each refresh re-reads the work folder: wf.statuses (file stats plus manifest digests of every done step's inputs), _run_flags (two sqlite reads), refresh_review (rv.queue reads the breach-point and pond layers), _refresh_dam_table (opens the step-8 GeoPackage of the work folde  
  What you see: With a county work folder and a shared Dams folder of a few hundred dam folders the window freezes for a noticeable time on open and after picking a settings file or preset; QGIS shows nothing meanwhile. On a small folder it is only wasted work.  
  Fix: Add 'if self._loading: return' at the top of _changed (or make refresh() return early while loading), and call refresh() once where the batch ends (after _fill_layers in __init__ it already is; _load_settings_file / _apply_preset / _add_results already call _changed() or refresh() afterwards).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-4.
- **Every layer list rebuilt fires a full re-read of the work folder, about twenty times in a row** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1426`  
  _save_state (1402-1404) and _autosave_settings (1541-1543) both stop while the window is loading, but refresh() has no such guard. Every layer combo on the Inputs tab is connected to _changed (line 576), and _fill_layers (1641-1667) clears and refills all eleven of them one after the other, so each clear and each first item sends the signal and runs the whole of refresh() again: the step states, the found-layers line, the summary CSV, the review queue (1892) and step 8's GeoPackage, feature by feature (2566-2642). That is roughly twenty full re-reads of the work folder for one rebuild, and _fill_layers runs when the window opens (line 220), when 'Refresh the layer lists' is pressed (585-586)  
  What you see: On a county-sized work folder the window sits dead for seconds when it opens, when Nick presses 'Refresh the layer lists' or applies a preset, and the step notes flicker through wrong values while it does, because the folder is read about twenty times instead of once.  
  Fix: Give refresh() the same guard the other two have: `if self._loading: return` at the top of refresh (or skip refresh in _changed while self._loading), and call self.refresh() once at the end of _fill_layers. One line plus one line, and no behaviour is lost because every caller of _fill_layers already refreshes afterwards.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-BV-1.
- **A missing settings-file path is made the suite-wide current file before it is checked; every tool of the three plugins then runs on built-in defaults without a word** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1498`  
  DS.set_current_path writes the path into the QGIS profile setting shared by Hazard, Runner and Screening (damsuite/settings.py:493-503) before the isfile check. When the file does not exist (typo, renamed, a network drive not mounted, a project opened on another computer), DS.load_current returns None (settings.py:515-518) and apply_file_defaults returns [], [] (suite_settings_tools.py:142-144): every settings-aware tool in all three plugins opens with its built-in defaults. At window open (quiet=True) the only trace is one '!!!' line in the window's Log.  
  What you see: After a settings file is moved or the path is mistyped, 'Run county', the step dialogs, and the Hazard and Runner tools all silently use the starting values instead of the engineer's thresholds, and the results look normal.  
  Fix: Call DS.set_current_path(p) only after os.path.isfile(p) succeeds; when the file is missing, keep (or clear) the previous current path and show the warning even on the quiet path, e.g. in the Simple tab's red hint line.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-5.
- **Tool messages, errors and the step-7 progress bar never reach the window: QTimer.singleShot from the task thread never fires** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2056`  
  _Feedback.pushInfo/pushWarning/reportError/setProgress (2013-2034) are called by QgsProcessingAlgRunnerTask on a QThreadPool worker thread. A QTimer.singleShot(0, callable) made there is queued to THAT thread's event loop, and QThreadPool threads have none, so the callable never runs. Verified with PyQt5 5.15 offscreen: three singleShot(0, ...) calls from a QRunnable, then 1.5 s of main-loop time -> none fired. The same binding is QGIS 3.34's. Only the Chain's own lines (written on the GUI thread: '>>> Step 7 ...', 'step 7 done (Ns)') appear in the window's Log.  
  What you see: During 'Run county' / 'Run the steps in order' the window's Log shows which step started and ended but none of the tool's own messages, warnings or errors; the step-7 progress bar (2645-2651) never appears. When a step fails, the dialog says 'see the log above and the Processing log' (2045) and the log above has nothing, so the user has to find the reason in QGIS's Log Messages panel.  
  Fix: Declare Qt signals on the dialog (e.g. logged = pyqtSignal(str); progressed = pyqtSignal(str, float)), connect them to _log / _update_progress once in __init__, and have _Feedback emit them: a signal emitted from a worker thread is delivered on the receiver's (GUI) thread. Alternatively connect fb.progressChanged (QgsFeedback's own signal) to the bar.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-2.
- **The Results dam table sorts Storage (ac-ft) as text, so the biggest dams are not at the top** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2600`  
  The storage is put into the cell as already-formatted text ('9,000', '85,000') and the table is sortable. A Qt table cell that holds text is compared letter by letter, not as a number, so '9,000' is placed after '85,000' and anything starting with a '1' comes before everything else. Nothing stores the plain number alongside the text (no item.setData with the value), so the header click can only sort the text.  
  What you see: Clicking the 'Storage (ac-ft)' header to put the largest reservoirs first gives a wrong order, and the order looks plausible, so a dam with 9,000 ac-ft can sit above one with 85,000. The same applies to the Confidence column.  
  Fix: Keep the number in the cell as well as the text: item.setData(Qt.EditRole, float(value)) for the storage column (or give the table a small QTableWidgetItem subclass whose comparison uses that value), so the header click sorts by size.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-AV-1.
- **The Screening suite has no runner: 52 standalone scripts, no single command, no aggregate exit code** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_breach_dir.py:1`  
  Hazard, Runner and Updater each have tests/run_tests.py that imports every test_*.py through tests/registry.py and exits with the failure count. Screening has nothing of the kind: each script must be started by hand with `FAKE_QGIS_FLAVOR=3.40 QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen PYTHONPATH=tests/qgis_mock:<parent> python3.12 tests/test_x.py`, 19 of the 52 do not even document that line, the mock's default flavour is 3.28 (qgis_mock/qgis/PyQt/QtCore.py:7 `FLAVOR = os.environ.get("FAKE_QGIS_FLAVOR", "3.28")`) so a plain `python3 tests/test_x.py` tests a different QGIS than the one the suite is checked against, and the three known-failing scripts (test_dem_local_024, test_dwr_https, test_suite_data_016)   
  What you see: A regression in Screening is found only if someone remembers to run that one script with the right environment; a release can go out with 49 of 52 passing and nobody knows which three, or whether it was three yesterday.  
  Fix: Add nd_dam_screening/tests/run_tests.py on the pattern of the other three: run each test_*.py in a subprocess with PYTHONPATH and FAKE_QGIS_FLAVOR set, print ok/FAIL/SKIP per script, carry a short list of scripts that need GDAL / network / a sibling plugin, and exit with the failure count; add the Run line to the 19 scripts that lack it.  
  Effort: medium. Id T1-T-2.

### Minor
- **27 hidden 'before 0.13' metre-twin parameters and 70 lines of compat code keep a pre-0.13 saved-model format alive** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/_common.py:57`  
  Every distance parameter is created twice: the feet parameter the dialog shows and a hidden metre twin for 'saved models / histories from before 0.13' (docstring, line 59). feet_or_legacy (43-54) lets the metre value win when the feet key is absent. The GUI report counts 27 hidden parameters in 13 of the 23 nddam tools (run_breach_sim 3, proposedam 3, findponds 6, terraincache 2, breachdirection 2, runnerhandoff 2 incl. WET_M at runner_handoff.py:168, ...). The plugin is at 0.28.3, fifteen minor releases later; the legacy branch of feet_or_legacy and distance_m_opt has no test (grep of tests: 0 hits).  
  What you see: Nick does not see the hidden parameters in the dialogs, but they appear in every saved model, in Processing history entries, in qgis_process --help output for each tool, and in the suite settings catalog that the window builds from parameter definitions; and two parameters can set the same distance, with the metre one silently winning when a script passes only it.  
  Fix: Replace distance_params with a single add(QgsProcessingParameterNumber(ft_key, ...)) and distance_m with plain parameterAsDouble * uu.FT; delete hide(), feet_or_legacy(), distance_m_opt()'s metre branch and WET_M. Saves 27 parameters, about 60 lines in _common.py and the '(before 0.13)' labels; a saved model from before 0.13 (2025) has to be re-saved once.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-B-3.
- **_common.py mixes QGIS helpers with 350 lines of GDAL-only terrain code that belongs in core/terrain.py; tool 2 builds a second mosaic of the same tiles** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/_common.py:279`  
  Lines 279-631 (z_unit_scale, dem_z_scale, local_mosaic, mosaic_key, clip_reusable, write_clip_meta, drop_clip, TerrainLoader, load_cached_clip) use only GDAL, numpy and core modules, but sit in a module whose top imports qgis.core, so test_crs_017 has to load the QGIS double to test a VRT mosaic. core/terrain.py (216 lines: fetch_3dep, list_local_tiles, set_unit_m) is the module they already import. Separately, tool 2 (terrain_cache.py:66-78) always rebuilds a fixed-name _local_mosaic.vrt in its output folder while TerrainLoader (540-549) builds a content-keyed _terrain_mosaic_<key>.vrt of the same tiles in _work: two mosaics of one folder, two naming rules.  
  What you see: None in a run. A user with a local LiDAR folder gets two VRT files built from the same tiles (one in the DEM folder, one in 7_sim/_work); the terrain code is harder to test and to find.  
  Fix: Move lines 279-631 into core/terrain.py (cm keeps thin aliases or callers import terrain directly); _common.py drops from 751 to about 400 lines. In terrain_cache.py replace lines 66-78 with loader = cm.TerrainLoader(out, "local", folder, log=feedback.pushInfo); vrt = loader.vrt (10 lines fewer, one mosaic per tile set).  
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-B-11.
- **3DEP tiles fetched for a larger clip pile up under _work/_3dep and are downloaded again every run** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/_common.py:596`  
  TerrainLoader.load writes the downloaded tiles to <OUTDIR>/_work/_3dep/x<dam>_<half>_t<i>.tif, warps them in memory and never deletes or reuses them; no other code references _3dep (core/runner.py removes only its own lf_* run folders, and the KEEP switch does not cover these). terrain_cache.py:113 deletes its tiles after the same kind of warp. Each time the clip grows, and on every re-run of tool 7, the same extent is fetched from USGS again and another tile set is left behind.  
  What you see: A county run leaves hundreds of GeoTIFFs (tens of MB per dam at the 20 m preset, more at 10 m) in 7_simulations/_work/_3dep that nobody told the engineer about, and a re-run spends network time downloading terrain it already has.  
  Fix: Remove src_list after the Warp (as terrain_cache does), or reuse an existing file of the same name before fetching; mention _work in the help text.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-5.
- **Seven of the eleven names _settings.py re-exports have no caller; is_line_source and TerrainCache.ID are dead too** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/_settings.py:11`  
  Grep of nd_dam_screening (tests included) finds no use of _settings.definitions, param_defs, tool_name, set_default, original, apply_file_defaults or step_of_tool; only fresh, catalog_of, settings_aware and full_catalog are called (provider.py:49, dialog.py:1034, test_preflight.py). The workflow import at line 5 serves step_of_tool only. In _common.py, is_line_source (645-646) has no caller; terrain_cache.py:20 unpacks an ID attribute that the class never uses.  
  What you see: None; it is weight that a reader of the settings adapter has to rule out.  
  Fix: Delete lines 5, 11-15, 23-24 and 37-42 of _settings.py (42 lines become about 20), _common.is_line_source, and the ID name in terrain_cache.py:20-21.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-9.
- **Feet, acre-foot, cfs and acre constants redefined in these files although core/us_units.py holds them (two spellings of the acre-foot)** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/breach_hydrograph.py:19`  
  core/us_units.py:14-17 defines FT, ACRE_M2, AF_M3 and CFS_M3S and _common.py already imports it as uu. The audited files still carry their own: _common.FT, breach_hydrograph.AF_M3 and FT_M plus a cfs literal, three literals in estimate_storage, one in breach_direction, and run_breach_sim uses two different names (rn.FT, uu.FT) for the same constant in one function. The acre-foot appears as 1233.4818375 here and 1233.48183754752 in us_units/storage (the plugin's core has 19 more FT/AF_M3 definitions: runner.py:41, flood_polygons.py:36, ponds.py:45, handoff.py:26-28, damrecord_io.py:45-47, proposed.py:24-26, ...).  
  What you see: None today (the values agree to 1e-8). The cost is that a unit mistake has to be looked for in 20 places, and the CSV written by tool 3 (estimate_storage.py:294) and read by tool 5 use constants defined in different modules.  
  Fix: Delete _common.FT, breach_hydrograph.AF_M3 and FT_M and the literals; use uu.FT, uu.AF_M3, uu.CFS_M3S, uu.ACRE_M2 (rn.FT = uu.FT too). Saves 5 constant definitions and 6 literals in these files, about 25 in the plugin if core follows.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-4.
- **Per-feature field reads in tools 3, 4, 5 re-implement cm.fval; two tools re-implement read_tif with a different nodata rule** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/breach_hydrograph.py:183`  
  cm.fval(f, names, k, cast) exists for exactly this (it also guards a QVariant NULL, line 203, as data_package._plain does at 253). breach_hydrograph defines its own get; estimate_storage (198-199, 231-234) and breach_direction (147-148) inline the two-line height/inventory parse without the NULL guard; dam_id = str(f["dam_id"]) if "dam_id" in names else str(f.id()) is written three times (estimate_storage:157, breach_direction:145, terrain_cache:89). breach_direction._dem_fn (83-91) and estimate_storage:166-170 each re-read a GeoTIFF turning nodata into NaN while cm.read_tif turns it into -9999: two nodata conventions. download_dem._record (198-208) is also the same seven lines as the Runner  
  What you see: Mostly none. One point for the correctness reviewer to confirm in real QGIS: the inline copies do not guard a QVariant NULL the way cm.fval does, so a dam whose height_m is NULL could fail tool 3 (inside the try, reported as FAILED) or stop tool 4 (line 148 is outside any try) instead of getting the DEM height tool 3 promises.  
  Fix: Use cm.fval(f, names, "height_m", float) and friends in the three tools, a cm.dam_id(f, names) one-liner for the id, and give cm.read_tif a nodata_as=np.nan option used by _dem_fn and tool 3; put _record into damsuite/damdata.py as record_dem(folder, path, source) for both plugins. Saves about 25 lines and one nodata convention.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-12.
- **Tool 0's PACKAGE route (a Hazard export repackaged) is a second way to deliver the layers the suite offline pack now provides** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/data_package.py:63`  
  Since 0.16/0.22 every data slot falls back to the suite offline pack (workflow.py:418-446), which the Updater builds with the same structures, roads, railroads, flowlines, lifelines and dams. Tool 0 still carries a PACKAGE input that reads a GeoPackage exported by Hazard's ndhazard:suiteexport tool and its metadata table (core/package.py export_info, info_lines, missing_lines; lines 98-110, 134-149, 151-155, 245-247 here). Three builders of one package: the Updater's pack, Hazard's export, tool 0. Tool 0 keeps a role the pack does not fill by design (streams, dam_lines, nlcd are MODEL_SLOTS), so only the export route is the duplicate.  
  What you see: Nick sees a tool in Hazard ('Export data package for ND Dam Breach Screening') and a first parameter in tool 0 that describe a hand-carry he no longer needs once the suite folder is adopted; the tool-0 help (lines 47-54) spends a paragraph on it.  
  Fix: Remove the PACKAGE parameter, the pk.export_info/info_lines/missing_lines calls and the 'or: a package exported by ND Dam Hazard' help paragraph from tool 0 (about 45 lines), keep pk.gpkg_layers for workflow.package_layers, and retire ndhazard:suiteexport (one toolbox entry in Hazard, suite_algorithms.py). Saves one tool, one parameter and about 85 lines across the two plugins.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-B-13.
- **Cancelling tool 0 leaves a package whose later layers are empty, and the window counts it as done** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/data_package.py:183`  
  Cancel only breaks the feature loop. dump() then finishes normally, and every remaining slot still opens its writer and breaks at once, so the GeoPackage ends up holding the layers that were reached in full, one half-written layer, and empty layers for everything after it. The tool returns its output path as if it had finished, and the window decides a step is done by the existence of that file (core/workflow.py:330-336).  
  What you see: An engineer who cancels a long package build (or whose QGIS is closed part-way) is left with a package that looks complete: step 0 shows as done, the slots report 'from the package', and tool 8 then finds no roads or no railroads at all and reports no road or rail consequences, with nothing saying why.  
  Fix: After the loops, if feedback.isCanceled(), delete the half-built GeoPackage and raise (or at least report that the package is incomplete and do not return OUT), so the step is not counted as done.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-AV-3.
- **Clip test in tool 0 rebuilds the state outline for every feature** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/data_package.py:190`  
  clip is the union of the clip layer (the ND boundary) and QgsGeometry.intersects converts it to a GEOS geometry on every call; the bounding-box shortcut does not help because nearly every feature of a statewide layer lies inside the state's bounding box. For the FEMA USA Structures layer (hundreds of thousands of footprints) this is a GEOS build of a many-thousand-vertex polygon per feature.  
  What you see: Building the package with the clip option takes many minutes longer than the copy itself.  
  Fix: Create one prepared engine once: eng = QgsGeometry.createGeometryEngine(clip.constGet()); eng.prepareGeometry(); then test eng.intersects(g.constGet()) per feature.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-8.
- **Tool 0 writes the text 'NULL' into the data package's occupancy fields instead of leaving them empty** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/data_package.py:216`  
  The same function passes the habitable and mobile flags through _plain but not the two occupancy values, although the module's own comment says an empty value in QGIS is not None. An empty OCC_CLS or PRIM_OCC therefore fails the 'is None' test, is converted to text, and is written into the package's occ_cls / prim_occ; the habitability rule is also handed that value instead of a blank. The roads block two lines below does it correctly (_plain(f[rcls]) decides emptiness).  
  What you see: In the structures layer of the offline package, every building whose source occupancy field is blank shows a placeholder word in occ_cls and prim_occ instead of an empty cell, so Nick cannot select or count the structures with no occupancy recorded, and the package looks as if every building had an occupancy class. The habitable flag itself stays on the safe side (such a building is kept habitable).  
  Fix: oc, po = _plain(f[occ]) if occ in names else None, _plain(f[prim]) if prim in names else None - two words, and the rest of the function is unchanged.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-BV-5.
- **Two run-time models for one number: the window's inline estimate and tool 12 disagree, and tool 12 has no PRESET so its '(as tool 7)' defaults are no longer tool 7's** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/estimate_runtime.py:50`  
  Since the presets were added, tool 7's effective default is the Quick preset (50 m cell, 12 h, 48 h max). Tool 12 copies tool 7's parameters but not PRESET, so its defaults (CELL_FT 33 ft = 10 m, SIMMAX 96 h) describe a run tool 7 no longer makes by default; the labels still say '(as tool 7)'. Meanwhile the Simple tab already answers the same question with a different model (dialog.py:1775 _show_estimate, core/runplan.py:91 seconds_per_dam: log-log anchors by cell size) than tool 12 (core/estimate.py: CFL time steps times a calibration run).  
  What you see: The Simple tab says 'about 12 s/dam' for a county while tool 12 run with its defaults estimates a 10 m grid, 25 times the cells, and hours. The engineer has no way to tell which number to believe.  
  Fix: Add tool 7's PRESET enum to tool 12 (3 lines, same PRESETS list) and apply PRESET_VALS before the estimate so the defaults match tool 7; then make runplan.run_time_text call est.plan_dam / seconds_for with a stored rate and delete runplan.seconds_per_dam and its ANCHORS (about 30 lines), so the window and tool 12 give one number.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-B-10.
- **Tool 3 parses the spill elevation back out of a sentence that core/storage.py wrote, although the number is available there** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/estimate_storage.py:285`  
  core/storage.estimate_storage receives the spill level as a number (line 222) and only puts it into a note string; StorageResult (storage.py:25-36) has no spill field. The tool then recovers the number by splitting the English sentence. The sentence has already been reworded once (storage.py:231 replaces 'storage held at' with 'storage leak at'); the next rewording, or a switch of the note to feet as 0.18 did elsewhere, breaks the parse without any error.  
  What you see: The spill_m column of 3_dams (tool 3's output) goes empty for every dam the moment the note text changes, with no warning in the log; nothing else reads spill_m, so no class moves, but the column Nick sees in the attribute table silently stops being filled.  
  Fix: Add spill_elev: float = None to StorageResult, set it in estimate_storage at line 222 (res.spill_elev = spill), and replace lines 285-286 with spill = res.spill_elev. Removes a 2-line text parse and a cross-module coupling on wording.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-2.
- **Eight tool help texts say 'Not run in a real QGIS' in a release captured in QGIS 3.34** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/find_ponds.py:155`  
  The 1.22 GUI captures come from a real QGIS 3.34, the self-test (14) itself covers tool 16, and the prior-work notes record real QGIS 3.34 runs of the suite. The sentence is left over from early development and is repeated in find_ponds.py:155 and :620 (also in the run log), merge_ponds.py:25, propose_dam.py:103, preflight.py:31, compare_reference.py:55, estimate_runtime.py:40, inventory_diff.py:72, run_chain.py:74 and selftest.py:88.  
  What you see: Every time the engineer opens one of these tools the help panel (and for tool 16 the run log) tells him the tool has never run in QGIS, which is no longer true and undermines trust in results that are fine.  
  Fix: Delete the sentence everywhere; where it matters say which tools the self-test covers ('covered by the self-test (14)' / 'not covered by the self-test').  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-7.
- **27 hidden metre parameters ('before 0.11' / 'before 0.13') are still carried at 0.28.3 and kept alive only by the plugin's own callers** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/hazard_classification.py:179`  
  Every distance and depth parameter has a hidden metre twin (cm.hide at hazard_classification.py:179-184 and triage.py:85-87; cm.distance_params adds one at find_ponds.py:198-232 x6, propose_dam.py:140-150 x3, estimate_runtime.py:50-59 x2, inventory_diff.py:89 x1, selftest.py:98 x1; 27 in the 13 tool modules). They exist for saved models from before 0.11 / 0.13, but the callers that still pass them are the plugin's own: core/selftest.py (tool 14) passes CELL and CLIP_CELL to tools 2, 7 and 16, sensitivity.py:139-140 passes CELL / CORRW / RMAX, and tests test_runchain.py:172 and test_selftest.py:57 pass MOVE_M and CELL. The rule in feet_or_legacy ('the metre key wins only when the feet key is   
  What you see: Not visible in the dialogs (the twins are hidden). Every distance read goes through a two-key rule, and each tool's parameter count in Processing (39 for tool 16, 34 for tool 8) overstates what the user sees.  
  Fix: Change the four internal callers to the _FT keys (core/selftest.py:164,193,216; sensitivity.py:139-140; the two tests), then delete the hidden twins, the second add() in distance_params (_common.py:72-74), the legacy branch of feet_or_legacy (_common.py:46-49) and hide() (_common.py:21-41). Saves 27 parameter definitions and about 60 lines; a model saved before 0.13 would need its distances re-ent  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-B-8.
- **HazardClassification.processAlgorithm is 509 lines with three nested functions and a five-way status branch** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/hazard_classification.py:233`  
  One method holds parameter reading, field resolution, the sampling of five receptor layers, the two-scenario governing rule, the warning-time sampling, the breach-parameter band, the dam.json record and the manifest. The three nested functions close over 20 local variables, so none of them can be called from a test; the only test path for the 0.28.0 band row is the whole tool through the mock (test_v0280.py). `from ..core import manifest as mf` at line 725 is executed once per dam inside the loop.  
  What you see: No direct effect today. A change to any one part (a new receptor kind, a third scenario) means editing a 500-line method, and a mistake in one part is found only when the whole classification is run.  
  Fix: Lift `assess`, `scaled_class` and `band_row` to module level taking a small `Receptors` object (the layers, transforms, field names and thresholds built once at lines 301-361), and move the four fixed result dictionaries for excluded / noflood / triage / nodata (lines 516-550) into a `status_result(status, get)` helper. The method drops to about 200 lines and the band row and the status results ge  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-B-4.
- **Tool 8's breach-parameter row (0.28.0) and tool 9 answer the same question in two column sets; the review queue reads only tool 9's** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/hazard_classification.py:660`  
  Since 0.28.0 every dam gets bp_class_lo / bp_class_hi / bp_stable from tool 8 (a scaled approximation, free) and the chosen dams get class_min / class_max / class_stable from tool 9 (a real re-run). Same concept, two names, and tool 10's review queue and dam page (report.py:127, 290) know only tool 9's names; tool 8's unstable row reaches the queue indirectly, through hz_conf = 'review' (line 666-667), where it reads as 'confidence review' with no mention of breach parameters.  
  What you see: The engineer sees 'class range' twice under different headings on the same dam, and a dam whose class flips in tool 8's band is queued as 'confidence review' while one that flips in tool 9 is queued as 'class changes between sensitivity runs'.  
  Fix: Give tool 9's three columns tool 8's names (class_min -> bp_class_lo, class_max -> bp_class_hi, class_stable -> bp_stable; tool 9 then overwrites tool 8's approximation with the re-run where it ran) and read bp_stable in report.py:127. One concept, one review reason; removes three field names and one FIELDS entry.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-11.
- **Unused constant pins tool 13 to version 0.11** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/inventory_diff.py:20`  
  Neither name is used in this file nor anywhere else in the four plugins (grep over the repo including tests). The version string is stale by 17 releases and invites someone to write it into dam.json later.  
  What you see: None today; a maintenance trap (the real version comes from damrecord_io.plugin_version()).  
  Fix: Delete the line.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-8.
- **inventory_diff.py carries an unused, hard-coded version string '0.11'** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/inventory_diff.py:20`  
  Neither name is referenced anywhere in the four plugins or their tests (grep for TOOL / VERSION from inventory_diff finds only the provider's import of the class). The string pins a release number outside damsuite/versions.py and is 17 releases stale (the plugin is 0.28.3).  
  What you see: None at run time; a reader (or the next audit) is told tool 13 is at 0.11.  
  Fix: Delete line 20. One line, one stale version number gone.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-5.
- **Three different rules name the dam folder for dam.json** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/inventory_diff.py:134`  
  Tool 13 uses the raw ID, tool 8 core/handoff.safe_name (re.sub(r"[^\w\- ]+", "_") keeps spaces), tool 19 core/workflow.safe_name (keeps alnum, '-', '_', '.'; strips leading/trailing '.' and '_'). They agree on DWR-####, POND-######## and DUG-#### but not on an ID with a space, dot or slash.  
  What you see: For such an ID tool 8 writes dam.json in one folder and tool 13 looks in another: the 'inventory_change' note is never written (log: 'folder without dam.json' or nothing) and the suite's 'refresh' hint never appears. For the usual DWR / POND ids nothing is lost.  
  Fix: One safe_name in damsuite (the Runner's rule, which handoff.safe_name already copies) used by tools 8, 13 and 19 and by workflow.one_dam_work.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-9.
- **Tool 19 leaves every downloaded 3DEP tile behind in the terrain folder** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/propose_dam.py:183`  
  With the 3DEP terrain source, tool 19 downloads the LiDAR tiles for each proposed dam into <Terrain clips folder>/_tmp and never deletes them, although the function it calls says the caller must. Tool 16 does it correctly: find_ponds.py deletes the same tiles in a finally block after warping them.  
  What you see: Every run of 'Propose a dam' over the 3DEP service leaves a _tmp folder of raw tiles beside the dam clips, a few megabytes per dam and more for a layer of several crest lines, which nothing ever cleans up; on a laptop with little room left the work folder quietly grows to several times the size it needs.  
  Fix: Keep the list of tiles returned by _source for the 3DEP case and delete them in a finally after the gdal.Warp (as find_ponds._dem_window does), and remove the _tmp folder at the end of the run.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-AV-4.
- **Reports re-read the impacted layer's field list twice per feature** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/reports.py:74`  
  imp.fields().names() builds a new list on every call and is called twice inside the loop over every impacted feature (every wet structure, road, railroad, lifeline and dam of every dam in the county).  
  What you see: Tool 10 takes longer than it needs on a statewide impacted layer; no wrong result.  
  Fix: names_i = imp.fields().names() once before the loop (as `sn` is done for the sensitivity layer on line 64) and test against it.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-A-10.
- **Tool 7's custom defaults (33 ft, 12 h, 96 h) and the preset table are written in four places** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:53`  
  The preset cell sizes live in run_breach_sim.PRESET_VALS and again in core/runplan.PRESET_CELL_M (its comment says 'the figures run_breach_sim.PRESETS shows the user'); the custom defaults 33 ft / 12 h / 96 h are in the tool's initAlgorithm, in workflow.DEFAULT_SETTINGS, and twice more as inline fallbacks in workflow.py (1326-1327, 1442-1443). run_chain.OPTIONAL_LABELS (run_chain.py:16-19) repeats the step labels of workflow.STEPS (workflow.py:69, 92) as well.  
  What you see: The run-time estimate (tool 12, runplan) and the preset the run actually uses can disagree after a one-sided edit, and a changed default in the tool does not reach the window's grid or the headless chain.  
  Fix: runplan imports PRESET_VALS from algorithms/run_breach_sim (or both import one tuple in core/scenarios.py); workflow's 1326-1327 and 1442-1443 fallbacks read DEFAULT_SETTINGS instead of repeating the numbers; run_chain builds OPTIONAL_LABELS from wf.STEPS. Saves three duplicate tables and six literals.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-5.
- **25 release numbers inside the tools' help text: the help reads as a changelog, duplicating metadata.txt** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:142`  
  shortHelpString of 10 of the 12 tools carries release numbers (25 in all: run_breach_sim 11 in a 70-line, 5,189-character help; estimate_storage 3 incl. '0.28.3: ... THIS MOVES THE PEAK BREACH FLOW'; runner_handoff 4 incl. 'v3.1' and 'v0.9'; run_chain 2; download_dem 2; data_package 'v2.10'; prepare_dams, terrain_cache 1 each). metadata.txt already holds the changelog (test_window_028 asserts '0.28.0:' and '0.27.0:' are in it), so the history is kept twice, and the help has to be edited for every release number.  
  What you see: Nick opens the help panel of tool 7 and gets a release history ('0.28.0: the default is ...', 'v0.11, off by default') instead of a description of what the tool does now; the two biggest help texts in the provider (tool 7 and tool 8) are the ones with the most history in them.  
  Fix: Rewrite each help in the present tense (what the tool does, what each option means, what to check) and move the '0.xx:' paragraphs into the metadata.txt changelog. Tool 7's help goes from 70 lines to about 40; no release number is left outside versions.py and metadata.txt.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-B-7.
- **Tool 7 preloads every stream, road, dam line and building within 7.9 miles of every dam before the first run** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:402`  
  reach is the largest allowed clip half-width plus 500 m (12.7 km with the default RMAX), so for each dam the main thread reads, reprojects and converts to vertex lists every flowline, road, dam line and (with BUILDINGS on) building footprint in a 25 km square, whatever the flood will actually reach, and all of it for all dams is kept in `cases` for the whole batch. The work is dams x features-in-the-box, done sequentially before the progress bar moves.  
  What you see: On a county run the tool sits at 0% for minutes while it gathers lines, and with the buildings option on it holds all footprints of the county's 25 km boxes in memory at once.  
  Fix: Gather per dam just before its worker starts (run_batch could take a prepare hook called on the main thread), or gather for the starting radius and refetch only when the clip actually grows; at least limit the buildings box to the pregrow trace extent.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-A-6.
- **A building layer you are not using can stop tool 7 before the first dam** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:478`  
  The check runs whenever a footprint layer was given, not when buildings are actually represented. 'Buildings in the terrain' defaults to 'Not represented' (BUILDINGS, defaultValue=0), and in that case bmode is 'none' and the footprints are never read (rec['_bld'] is None at line 432), yet damsuite.solvegate.layer_overlap returns a FAIL for a layer with no CRS or one that does not reach the dams (damsuite/solvegate.py:107-123) and that FAIL stops the whole tool.  
  What you see: From the Processing Toolbox (or a model) an engineer who fills in the footprint box but leaves buildings at 'Not represented' gets no simulations at all and a message about the buildings layer, for a layer the run would have ignored.  
  Fix: Guard the check with bmode != "none" (the layer is only used then), as the two checks just above it already are.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-AV-4.
- **area_ha counts cells deeper than 5 cm while the flood polygons use the suite wet depth (0.1 ft)** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:648`  
  The flooded area in the results table uses a hard-coded 0.05 m threshold; the extent polygons written for the same run (fp.DEFAULT_WET_M = hz.DEFAULT_WET_M = 0.1 ft = 0.0305 m, flood_polygons.py / hazard.py:35) and tool 8's 'any depth' use the suite wet depth. The two numbers for one dam therefore disagree.  
  What you see: area_ha (and its acres twin) is smaller than the area of the extent polygon of the same dam; an engineer comparing the table with the map sees two different flooded areas with no explanation.  
  Fix: Use fp.DEFAULT_WET_M (or the configured wet depth) in both places, including scen2_area_ha.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-7.
- **The flooded-area column of step 7 uses its own wet depth (0.16 ft), not the suite's 0.1 ft** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:648`  
  area_ha counts cells deeper than 0.05 m, a number written only here, while everything else in the suite calls a cell wet at 0.1 ft (0.03048 m): the flood-extent polygon written by the same run, the arrival and velocity layers, and tool 8's 'any depth'. The same run therefore reports two different flooded areas for one dam, and the log line beside it states the 0.1 ft rule.  
  What you see: The 'Flooded area (ac)' figure in the HTML report (core/report.py:286) and in the Runner-vs-Screener sheet (core/comparison.py:76-77) is smaller than the extent polygon Nick can measure in QGIS from 7_flood_polygons.gpkg, with nothing to say why; on flat ground the fringe between 0.1 ft and 0.16 ft deep is a real share of the area.  
  Fix: Replace both 0.05 literals with the suite value already imported in this file (fp.DEFAULT_WET_M), or better with fp.wet_mask(depth), so the column, the polygon and tool 8 count the same cells.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-BV-4.
- **Resuming tool 7 re-polygonizes every dam that was already done** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_breach_sim.py:651`  
  on_done writes flood polygons for status 'cached' as well as 'ok'. The cached branch of run_scenario (lines 515-524) only re-reads the rasters, so on a resumed county run every finished dam is polygonized again on the main thread and its existing polygons are deleted and rewritten ('an earlier run's replaced').  
  What you see: 'Resuming: 124 of 200 dams already have a result' is followed by minutes of polygon work for those 124 dams before the remaining 76 start producing anything, and the log repeats 124 'flood polygons ... (an earlier run's replaced)' lines.  
  Fix: Skip write_polygons when r['status'] == 'cached' and the GeoPackage already holds polygons for that dam and scenario (fp can report that), or write polygons only for newly run dams.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-A-9.
- **The headless chain re-implements the window's parameter-definition reader and warning-capturing feedback line for line** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/run_chain.py:159`  
  dialog.py:1979-1994 (_definitions) builds the same (name, description, optional, has_default, is_destination) tuple with the same FlagOptional fallback for wf.required_missing, and dialog.py:2013-2023 (_Feedback) subclasses the feedback to capture pushWarning/reportError the same way. Two copies of each, one for the window's chain and one for tool 15, both feeding the same wf.Chain.  
  What you see: The 'X is empty' check that stops a chain can differ between the window and qgis_process after a one-sided fix (the dialog copy has a 'parameters could not be checked' log line the tool copy lacks).  
  Fix: Add wf.parameter_definitions(aid) next to required_missing in core/workflow.py and a wf.capturing_feedback(base_cls, log) factory; dialog.py and run_chain.py call them. Saves about 30 lines and keeps the two chains identical.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-10.
- **The minimum Runner version is hard-coded in core/handoff.py and the help text beside the versions.py table that actually enforces it** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/runner_handoff.py:202`  
  vs.require('ndscreen', 'ndbreach') at line 189 is the check that stops the tool, using versions.REQUIRES (hard 3.1.0, soft 3.7.0). The message at 198-203 and the help at 67 quote a second constant, ho.RUNNER_MIN_VERSION = '3.1', that nothing enforces. Raising the requirement in versions.py (the brief names it as the one place) leaves the message and help saying 3.1.  
  What you see: When the Runner is missing, Nick is told to install 'v3.1 or later' while the suite check a line earlier may demand 3.7.0; two numbers for one requirement.  
  Fix: Delete RUNNER_MIN_VERSION from core/handoff.py; in the message use vs.REQUIRES[("ndscreen", "ndbreach")][0]; drop the number from the help (say 'the version the suite check asks for'). Saves one constant and one drift.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-6.
- **Dead names in three tool modules: QVARIANT, the compare tool's `results` dict, and an unused `src` argument** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/selftest.py:16`  
  QVARIANT is defined and used nowhere in the repository (grep of all four plugins and tests). In compare_reference.py the `results` dictionary is filled per dam and never read; `rows` already carries everything that is written. In find_ponds.py both terrain helpers take a `src` argument that every caller passes as None (lines 461, 580) and that the bodies never touch.  
  What you see: None; dead weight that a reader has to rule out.  
  Fix: Delete selftest.py:16, compare_reference.py's `results` (lines 177 and 218), and the `src` parameter of `_dem_window` and `_write_clip` with its two call sites. About 6 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-6.
- **The All steps tooltip of tool 14 names fewer tools than its help text** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/selftest.py:82`  
  Tool 14 has covered tool 16 since the ponds work (core/selftest.py:213-219 runs findponds), and its own help says so, but the step table in core/workflow.py that feeds the All steps button tooltip still says '1-8 and 10'. Two texts describe one tool.  
  What you see: The engineer reading the All steps tab is told the self-test does not cover ponds; the tool's own page says it does.  
  Fix: Change workflow.py:111 to '1-8, 10 and 16' or, better, build the tooltip from the algorithm's shortHelpString first sentence so there is one text.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-14.
- **The 12 receptor settings of tool 8 are declared three times, under three spellings, in tools 8, 9 and 7b** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:71`  
  sensitivity.py:67-106 re-declares twenty tool-8 parameters (every label says 'as tool 8') only to pass them through at lines 145-156. runner_handoff.py:129-171 declares the same twelve settings a third time with different keys: S_OCC / R_AADT / T_TRAINS / PASSENGER_CODES / LETHALITY / WET_FT where tools 8 and 9 say OCC / ROAD_AADT / RAIL_TRAINS / PAXCODES / CRIT / MIND_FT. Labels have already drifted (tool 8's UNKNOWN_TRAINS says 'at least Medium', tool 9's does not).  
  What you see: Three Processing dialogs ask the same twelve questions with different names; a saved model or a script that works for tool 8 has to be re-typed for 7b; a change in tool 8 (a new default field name, a new word in the uninhabited list) has to be made in three files or the tools disagree.  
  Fix: One `cm.receptor_params(P)` that adds the twelve parameters with tool 8's keys and labels, and one `cm.receptor_values(alg, params, context)` that reads them; use both in tools 8, 9 and 7b. Removes about 45 lines from sensitivity.py and about 45 from runner_handoff.py and ends the three spellings.  
  Effort: medium. Id S3b-B-2.
- **When every sensitivity variant fails, tool 9 leaves the class columns empty and the review queue says nothing** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/sensitivity.py:171`  
  A variant whose flood model failed comes back from tool 8 as hz_class = NoData, which is not in RANK, so ranked is empty and class_min, class_max and class_stable are left NULL. The review queue only reacts to class_stable == "no" (core/report.py:127), so a dam whose every variant failed reads exactly like a dam whose class never moved. Only sens_detail, a 254-character text column in the layer, says NoData.  
  What you see: The engineer runs tool 9 for an hour on the borderline dams; a dam whose eight variants all failed shows blank class columns and is not listed in the review queue, so it looks like a settled dam rather than one that was never tested.  
  Fix: When c is not empty and ranked is, write class_stable = "unknown" (or "not run") and add that value to core/report.py:127 as a review reason, so a dam whose variants failed is queued instead of silently blank.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-BV-5.
- **Tools 2, 4 and 20 have no passing test that runs them; the legacy-unit and last-output helpers have none at all** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/terrain_cache.py:58`  
  Tool 2's processAlgorithm (clip reuse by .json, the 'cut again' path, local vs 3DEP) and tool 4's (structure-ID match, name match, reservoir choice, the 300 m / 'lines share this structure ID' notes) are run by no test; core/breach_dir and core/terrain are, but the glue that chooses the line and the water body is not. Tool 20 runs only inside the two network/suite-folder tests that are known to fail offline. feet_or_legacy's metre branch (B-3) and the 0.21.0 last-output mechanism (_common.py:696-751) are untested, so they could be deleted or broken without a test noticing.  
  What you see: A regression in which dam line tool 4 picks, or in whether tool 2 reuses or re-cuts a clip, reaches Nick's run before any test.  
  Fix: One mock run each in the style of test_v0283's run_mock: tool 2 on a two-tile local folder (first run writes dam_<ID>.tif.json, second run reuses, a changed radius re-cuts); tool 4 on one dam point, two lines sharing a structure ID and one water body (by-ID match, nearest chosen, dir_conf). For tool 20, factor the reuse branch (download_dem.py:101-109) so it is tested without a network. If B-3 is   
  Effort: medium. Id S3a-B-14.
- **Acre and acre-foot constants are redefined in five of these tool files (two precisions) although damsuite/units.py exports them; tool 7's SCENARIOS list is copied too** `nd_dam_screening/algorithms/triage.py:17`  
  damsuite/units.py:14-15 defines ACRE_M2 and AF_M3 for the whole suite (and core/us_units.py is a second copy of that file). Inside nd_dam_screening the same two numbers are written in 14 more places with two different precisions (1233.4818375 and 1233.48183754752); in this unit: triage.py:17, find_ponds.py:41 and 574, propose_dam.py:342-349, compare_reference.py:21. compare_reference.py:20 also re-types tool 7's SCENARIOS list while estimate_runtime.py:13 correctly imports it.  
  What you see: None visible (the two precisions agree to 8 digits). A future change of a label (SCENARIOS) or a unit has to be found in a dozen files.  
  Fix: In these five files import ACRE_M2 and AF_M3 from ..damsuite.units and SCENARIOS from .run_breach_sim; delete the local definitions and literals. Removes 8 definitions here; the same sweep over core/ removes 9 more and lets core/us_units.py become a one-line re-export of damsuite/units.py.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-7.
- **compat.py: three unused names and a docstring pointing at a checker that does not exist** `nd_dam_screening/compat.py:76`  
  FLAG_ADVANCED (76-79), set_advanced (82-89) and WKB_POLYGON (106-107) are referenced nowhere in nd_dam_screening, tests included (grep: 0 hits each). Line 7 says 'tools/enum_scan.py checks that'; no such file exists in the repository (find: none).  
  What you see: None for Nick; 16 lines and a false promise for the next maintainer.  
  Fix: Delete the three names and the sentence.

- **The arrival module's documented contour function is dead; the polygon writer has its own copy with a different list of hours** `nd_dam_screening/core/arrival.py:102`  
  A whole-repo search finds contour_masks called only from tests/test_v0280.py. The production contour layer is built by flood_polygons.arrival_masks, a near-identical function in another module, called with arrival's CONTOUR_HOURS; so the two modules each own a list of hours (0.5, 1, 2 in arrival; 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8 in flood_polygons) and one of the two mask builders is never run.  
  What you see: None directly. A maintainer who changes the contour times in arrival.py, where the module's own documentation points, changes nothing the engineer sees, because the live code path is in the other file.  
  Fix: Delete arrival.contour_masks and the line of its docstring that advertises it, and have flood_polygons keep the one mask builder; or keep contour_masks and have flood_polygons call it. Either way one function and one list of hours, about twelve lines gone.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-BV-5.
- **A second block of the Runner's code is kept in Screening by hand, with instructions to copy it by hand** `nd_dam_screening/core/benchmark.py:45`  
  Lines 45 to 132, 87 lines, are a hand copy of the Runner's comparison metrics with a header asking a human to keep the two equal. I diffed the block against nd_breach_runner/core/benchmark.py: today it is still identical, which is luck, not a mechanism. breach_hydro.py has the same arrangement and has already drifted (finding S1-B-1), so this is the same trap one release away from the same outcome.  
  What you see: None today. The day someone fixes the fit index or the depth classes in one plugin and not the other, a Screening-versus-reference comparison and a Runner-versus-reference comparison of the same two rasters stop being comparable, and nothing in either report says why.  
  Fix: Move the five functions into the shared damsuite package (damsuite/benchmark_metrics.py) and import them in both plugins, as the fix for S1-B-1 proposes for the breach equations. The copy header and both copies then go away and the test shrinks to checking that both modules import the same functions.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-BV-4.
- **Unit constants (ft, acre-foot, cfs, mile) and NODATA defined in 8 files of the unit, the acre-foot at two precisions in one file** `nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py:37`  
  breach_hydro.py alone defines the acre-foot twice (_AF and AF_M3, different precision) and cfs twice (_CFS, CFS_M3S). storage.py:447-448 (AF_M3, FT_M), domain.py:9 (AF_M3), flood_polygons.py:36 (FT), runner.py:40-41 (M3S_PER_CFS, FT), runplan.py:35 (FT), dem_download.py:82/177/338 (MI, FT_TO_M, FT_PER_MI) and benchmark.py:34 (M_PER_FT) add 11 more: 15 definitions of 4 physical constants. damsuite/units.py (and core/us_units.py, which mirrors it) already has FT, AF_M3, CFS_M3S, MILE_M. NODATA = -9999.0 is likewise defined in gridutil.py:10, fv_engine.py:42, solver_io.py:19, flood_polygons.py:37 and arrival.py:29.  
  What you see: None today (the values agree to 1e-8). It is a maintenance trap: one mistyped copy changes one tool's acre-feet or feet and nothing checks it.  
  Fix: Import damsuite.units (with the same ImportError fallback these files already use for netproxy/keepawake) and delete the 15 local definitions; import NODATA from gridutil in the other four modules. About 20 lines gone, one place for each value.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-6.
- **Breach routing drifts from the Runner's by a bottom-width floor and two constants; the docstring claims a difference that does not exist** `nd_dam_screening/core/breach_hydro.py:303`  
  Verdict on the agreement: the breach EQUATIONS have not drifted. froehlich_2008, froehlich_1995, von_thun_gillette (Cb table), mlm_1984, embankment_area_ft2, tr60_peak_cfs and curvilinear_shape give the Runner's numbers (only g 9.81 vs 9.80665, 0.02% in tf, which the test allows). The ROUTING has drifted in three small ways: (1) Screening floors the breach bottom width at 10% of the average width, the Runner at 0; (2) the weir constants are rounded (1.71 / 1.35 vs 1.7114 / 1.3526); (3) the Runner floors the formation time at 60 s, Screening does not. Routing the SAME reservoir curve through both (Runner Reservoir m = 2, Froehlich 2008 piping): a typical dam (8 m, 243 ac-ft) peaks 10,712 vs 1  
  What you see: For most dams the Screening peak and the Runner's agree within 0.3%. For a small pond behind a tall dam the Screening hand-off hydrograph peak is about 4% higher than the Runner would compute for the same storage, and an engineer comparing the two reads the docstring's wrong reason for the gap.  
  Fix: Use the Runner's choices: bb_final = max(bavg - z * hb, 0.0) with the Runner's note when it is clipped, the constants 3.1 * sqrt(0.3048) and 2.45 * sqrt(0.3048), and tf = max(tf, 60.0). Correct the docstring (no tailwater in either). Add a routed-peak comparison on a shared curve to tests/test_tools_breach.py (tolerance 0.5%).  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-3.
- **Comparison sheet inserts dam names and runner text into HTML unescaped** `nd_dam_screening/core/comparison.py:161`  
  name, rn_status, rn_border and rn_gaps are written raw; report.py escapes the same fields. A name with '<' or '&' (e.g. 'Smith & Sons <Lower>') breaks the cell.  
  What you see: runner_vs_screener.html mis-renders rows whose dam name or runner message contains < or &.  
  Fix: Wrap the values in html.escape() in _table_row.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-11.
- **The Runner-vs-Screener comparison sheet writes names and notes into HTML without escaping them, so text containing "<" disappears** `nd_dam_screening/core/comparison.py:161`  
  No value in comparison.py passes through html.escape. A dam name, a runner status or a runner gap note that contains "<" is read by the browser as the start of a tag: everything up to the next ">" vanishes from the page. An "&" produces invalid HTML that most browsers forgive.  
  What you see: On the comparison sheet a dam name or a runner gap note such as "storage < 50 ac-ft" loses the part after the "<", so the engineer reads a shortened note or a shortened name and cannot tell that anything is missing. The per-dam reports in the same folder show the same text correctly.  
  Fix: One import and one line: in _td, `val = html.escape(str(val))` for plain values (pass the few cells that are deliberately HTML as pre-built markup), or render the table through report._page / report.badge as the duplicate-report-writer finding proposes, which escapes already.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-BV-3.
- **Two HTML report writers with two CSS/theme blocks, two class-colour systems and two formatting helpers (report.py and comparison.py)** `nd_dam_screening/core/comparison.py:236`  
  report.py builds every per-dam page and the statewide index with _page(), CSS (44 lines), badge() and CLASS_STYLE. comparison.py (the Runner-vs-Screener sheet written by runner_handoff.py) builds its own page head with a second 37-line stylesheet, a second dark-mode rule set, its own class colours (.cls-h/.cls-m/.cls-l), its own _fmt/_num/_td helpers and a 115-line _table_row of hand-concatenated cells. The sheet therefore looks different from the report it sits next to, and the duplicated _fmt is what produced S2b-B-1.  
  What you see: The comparison sheet and the dam reports in the same folder have different fonts, colours and class badges; a fix to one look (for example the dark-mode colours) never reaches the other.  
  Fix: Render the comparison table through report._page(title, body) with report.CSS and report.badge() for the class cells; delete _HTML_HEAD, _class_css, _agree_css, comparison._fmt and _td (about 70 lines) and keep only build_row, read_runner_manifest and the row-to-cells code. Keep the legend as a <p>. test_comparison.py already checks the row dict, so it needs one string assert changed.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2b-B-5.
- **Unit constants redefined in three files (two spellings of AF_M3) although damsuite/units.py has them** `nd_dam_screening/core/damrecord_io.py:45`  
  damrecord_io.py:45-47 and 167, handoff.py:26-29 and us_units.py:14-17 each define FT, AF_M3, ACRE_M2 (and a cfs factor), while damsuite/units.py:13-16 already holds the exact values and nothing in nd_dam_screening imports it (grep 'damsuite import units' finds no hit outside the damsuite copy). AF_M3 is spelled 1233.4818375 in damrecord_io, handoff, ponds, domain, breach_hydro, triage and breach_hydrograph and 1233.48183754752 in us_units, storage, proposed and damsuite; handoff.py:29 derives cfs as 1/FT**3 while comparison.py:13 rounds it to 35.3147.  
  What you see: No visible difference today (the two AF_M3 values agree to 10 digits), but a wrong factor fixed in one file stays wrong in the others, and a reader cannot tell which copy is the suite's.  
  Fix: In damrecord_io.py, handoff.py and us_units.py replace the constant lines with 'from ..damsuite.units import FT, AF_M3, ACRE_M2, CFS_M3S' (handoff's CFS_PER_M3S becomes 1.0 / CFS_M3S). Deletes 11 lines in this unit; the other core modules can follow the same way later.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-6.
- **County extents kept twice in core/: 53 hand-typed boxes in dem_download and the 53 outlines in nd_counties.geojson** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:26`  
  core/nd_counties.py (identical in the Runner) reads nd_counties.geojson and offers names(), box(counties, pad_deg) and features(); it is the county source the suite pack and workflow.py use. dem_download keeps its own hand-typed, hand-padded boxes plus county_names() and county_features() (rectangles), used by download_dem.py:145 and dialog.py:814/954.  
  What you see: The DEM clip for a county and the county outline the rest of the suite uses come from two lists that nothing checks against each other; the DEM tab draws rectangles where real outlines exist.  
  Fix: download_dem uses nd_counties.names() and nd_counties.box([county], pad_deg=0.05); the dialog draws nd_counties.features(); delete COUNTIES, county_names and county_features from dem_download.py (about 62 lines, one list to maintain).  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-10.
- **A 3DEP download leaves all its raw tiles and the mosaic file in the user's output folder** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:146`  
  download() writes one GeoTIFF per service tile into the folder the user chose, builds a mosaic file over them and never removes either, although the only file anyone needs afterwards is the standardised GeoTIFF that algorithms/download_dem.py:160 writes from them. The other callers of the same function delete their tiles.  
  What you see: After a county download at 10 m the output folder holds about seventy loose tile files plus a .vrt beside the one DEM that matters, roughly doubling the disk the download takes, and a user who tidies up by deleting the tiles also silently breaks the .vrt that still points at them.  
  Fix: In algorithms/download_dem.py, after dd.warp_to_local has written the standardised GeoTIFF, delete the tile files and the .vrt (download() can return the tile list alongside the VRT path, as fetch_3dep already does), or write them into a _tmp sub-folder that is removed at the end.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-AV-5.
- **A failed VRT build is not noticed; the error surfaces one step later with the wrong name** `nd_dam_screening/core/dem_download.py:171`  
  gdal.BuildVRT returns None when it fails (a tile GDAL cannot open, mixed data types, a locked path) and download() returns the VRT path anyway; set_unit_m swallows the open error. build_vrts does not even hold the returned dataset, so the VRT reaches disk only because CPython frees the temporary at once. The caller (algorithms/download_dem.py:159) then fails in warp_to_local with 'GDAL could not warp <stem>.vrt to EPSG:5070'.  
  What you see: When the mosaic step fails, the user is told the warp failed and looks at the wrong step; the tiles that were downloaded are fine and the real cause (which tile GDAL rejected) is never shown.  
  Fix: After each BuildVRT / Translate: `if ds is None: raise IOError("GDAL could not build the VRT over the downloaded tiles: " + gdal.GetLastErrorMsg())`; hold the dataset in a variable and close it with `ds = None` in build_vrts too.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-6.
- **estimate.py copies engine and runner constants instead of importing them, with a different maximum run length** `nd_dam_screening/core/estimate.py:33`  
  CFL 0.45 and the 5 s cap are literals inside fv_engine.Engine.run (run(..., cfl=0.45) and line 292 'dt = min(dt, 5.0, ...)'); 1500 m is runner.DEFAULTS radius_m; 150 m is domain.corridor_mask's keep_radius_m default. plan_dam's default sim_max_s is 36 h while runner.DEFAULTS sim_max_s is 96 h (tool 12 happens to pass 96 h from its own parameter, so the 36 h is a second default for the same setting reachable by direct callers and tests).  
  What you see: If the engine's CFL, the corridor disk or the maximum run length is tuned, tool 12's estimate silently stops describing the run it estimates.  
  Fix: fv_engine exposes CFL = 0.45 and DT_MAX_S = 5.0 as module constants used by run(); estimate imports them, runner.DEFAULTS['radius_m'] and ['sim_max_s'], and domain's keep radius (make it a module constant). Deletes five constants from estimate.py and makes plan_dam's default equal the runner's.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-5.
- **Small helpers written twice: hydrograph end time, download stem, retry loop** `nd_dam_screening/core/estimate.py:55`  
  estimate.hydro_end_s and runner._hydro_end are identical; dem_download.output_stem (138-151) and local_tif_stem (539-552) are identical except the file-name tag; terrain._http_get (108-118) repeats fetch_3dep_tile's retry loop (55-67) with the same back-off.  
  What you see: None directly; three places where a fix has to be made twice.  
  Fix: One hydro_end in domain.py (both callers import domain); one _stem(scope, tag, ...) in dem_download with output_stem and local_tif_stem as two-line wrappers; one _retry(fn) in terrain used by both fetchers. About 30 lines saved.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-13.
- **Runner minimum version hard-coded in handoff.py beside the one in damsuite/versions.py** `nd_dam_screening/core/handoff.py:22`  
  The brief asks for version numbers outside damsuite/versions.py. handoff.RUNNER_MIN_VERSION duplicates REQUIRES[("ndscreen","ndbreach")][0]. algorithms/runner_handoff.py:189 already enforces the requirement with vs.require("ndscreen", "ndbreach") and then, at 199-203, prints 'Install ND Dam Breach Runner v{}+' from ho.RUNNER_MIN_VERSION. When the hard minimum in versions.py moves, the message and the check disagree.  
  What you see: The 'install version X or later' message in tool 7b can name a version older than the one the tool actually refuses.  
  Fix: Delete RUNNER_MIN_VERSION and use vs.REQUIRES[("ndscreen", "ndbreach")][0] in runner_handoff.py:202-203 (or let vs.require's own text carry the version). One constant less.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-7.
- **Small helpers and one check written twice (_num, _nonempty_dir, STORAGE_KEYS, the 10 ft cell warning, the CLIPS_FOLLOW fallback)** `nd_dam_screening/core/handoff.py:77`  
  handoff._num (77-82) equals damrecord_io._num (112-117); workflow._nonempty_dir (310-317) equals selftest._nonempty_folder (116-123); damrecord_io.STORAGE_KEYS (169) restates the field order of handoff.STORAGE_SOURCES (61-64) and says so in its comment; the 'DEM cell > 10 ft with a local DEM folder' warning is written in workflow.py:1278-1281 (a log line when the chain starts) and preflight.py:263-269 (a WARN before the run), with different wording; incomplete.settings_reason (114-121) re-derives the 'follow the terrain source = 3' fallback with a literal 3 instead of wf.CLIPS_FOLLOW, although its only plugin caller (preflight.py:300) already passes the resolved index.  
  What you see: If tool 7b's storage preference changes and tool 8's STORAGE_KEYS does not, the two tools write different storage_acft to dam.json and the record gets a conflict; the two cell warnings can drift apart in advice.  
  Fix: Import one _num; use workflow._nonempty_dir from selftest; set STORAGE_KEYS = tuple(k for k, _f, _d in handoff.STORAGE_SOURCES); delete the workflow.py:1278-1281 log (the preflight already says it before the run); make settings_reason's fallback call wf.larger_clips_index(settings). Saves about 30 lines and three places that must agree.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-12.
- **Three try/except import shims exist so core/ can be imported as a flat folder; two are already unused and the third serves one test** `nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:19`  
  Only tests/test_hazard.py imports hazard and nd_hazard as bare modules; handoff is imported only as nd_dam_screening.core.handoff (dialog.py:994, test_handoff.py:18, test_v0283.py:37) and flood_polygons only as a package module (test_flood_polygons.py:29), so the shims in handoff.py and flood_polygons.py cannot be reached any more. test_nd_hazard_adapter.py already shows the package-style import for the same modules.  
  What you see: None for the user; for the maintainer, every new core module that imports hazard has to repeat the four-line pattern or break one test.  
  Fix: Change test_hazard.py lines 3-5 to `from nd_dam_screening.core import hazard as hz, nd_hazard as nh` (as test_nd_hazard_adapter.py does) and delete the except branches in hazard.py, handoff.py and flood_polygons.py (12 lines, three files).  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-9.
- **MIN_WET_M is below the minimum nd_hazard.Settings accepts** `nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:37`  
  hazard_classification.py lines 181-184 use MIN_WET_M as the minimum of the hidden legacy metre parameters and line 265 accepts any wet depth >= 0.003 m, but classify_nd converts it to 0.00984 ft and Settings.validate rejects anything under 0.01 ft. test_hazard.py:49 asserts the constant is below the limit, so the gap is deliberate but inconsistent.  
  What you see: A saved model or history from before 0.11 with MIND = 0.003 m passes the tool's own check and then crashes in the classifier with a message about feet instead of a parameter error.  
  Fix: MIN_WET_M = nh.MIN_WET_FT * M_PER_FT and update the test.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-12.
- **load_curve ignores the header: a curve in ft/s, ft is read as m/s, m** `nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:115`  
  The header line is skipped without being read, so any two-column CSV is taken as velocity_ms, depth_m. The TM 11 figures and every other engineer-facing value since 0.18.0 are in ft/s and ft, which is what someone digitising a curve will most likely type.  
  What you see: With a ft-unit curve the comparison-only 'curve' criterion treats the boundary as 3.28 times lower than drawn and calls structures lethal at roughly a third of the intended depth, with no message.  
  Fix: Read the header; accept velocity_ms,depth_m and velocity_fps,depth_ft (convert the latter) and raise a clear error for anything else.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-8.
- **Comparison criteria report 'at 0.0 ft/s' when the velocity is unknown** `nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:356`  
  For the feinberg criterion with a known DV but no velocity sample, the code substitutes 0.0 and then writes vel_fts = 0.0 and 'at 0.0 ft/s' into the finding's reason.  
  What you see: hz_reason and the finding row say the structure saw 0.0 ft/s when the velocity was in fact unknown, hiding the data gap the ND path records.  
  Fix: Keep vel_fts None when s.get("vel_ms") is None and word the reason 'velocity unknown, DV {:.0f} ft2/s'.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-9.
- **No test for invdiff.py or the Inventory diff tool, none for review.queue (the Review tab's list), and nd_counties.contains/box/features are untested in the Screening** `nd_dam_screening/core/invdiff.py:99`  
  invdiff.py (174 lines: tolerance logic, filled/cleared detection, the dam.json 'inventory_change' note) and algorithms/inventory_diff.py have no test file at all. review.queue(), which decides which dams appear on the Review tab and joins reasons from four sources, is called only from dialog.py and never exercised by a test (decide/apply/move_xy/read_log/excluded_ids are covered by test_crs_017 and test_popup_013). In the Screening, nd_counties is tested for names/normalize/parse/county_of (test_autofill_0211.py:125-136) but not contains() with holes, box() or features(); the Runner's test_v3131 covers the copy identity only.  
  What you see: A regression in the inventory diff (for example a storage change reported as filled) or a dam dropping off the Review tab would not be caught before a release.  
  Fix: Add tests/test_invdiff.py: two small dict inventories through diff_inventories (new, removed, height +0.6 ft, storage +3 %, moved 60 m, filled/cleared) and record_change; and a queue() test with a fake read_rows returning one low-confidence breach point, one dugout and one borderline hazard row plus a summary.csv. About 60 lines.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2b-B-11.
- **Unit conversion constants are redefined in five core modules with two different acre-foot values and a rounded cfs factor, although us_units.py holds them** `nd_dam_screening/core/inventory.py:6`  
  us_units.py (0.18.0) is the declared home of these factors and report.py already imports from it. inventory.py, invdiff.py, ponds.py and comparison.py each carry their own copies: AF_M3 differs between files (1233.4818375 vs 1233.48183754752, relative 3.9e-11), comparison.py's cfs factor is rounded (35.3147 vs 35.31467, relative 9.4e-7) and its ac/ha is rounded too. ponds.py mixes its local FT (lines 61, 64, 322) with uu.FT (lines 288, 293) in the same function. None of the differences shows at the displayed precision today, but the Runner-vs-Screener sheet and the per-dam report convert the same screener peak with two different factors.  
  What you see: No visible difference now; the risk is a future edit to one copy (for example a storage factor) that moves one table and not another, which is exactly the kind of mismatch the Absaraka comparison was chasing.  
  Fix: In inventory.py, invdiff.py, ponds.py and comparison.py replace the local lines with `from .us_units import FT, AF_M3, ACRE_M2, CFS_M3S` and derive FT_PER_M = 1/FT etc. proposed.py must stay standalone (it is copied to the Runner, which has no us_units), so its three constants stay; nd_hazard.py likewise. While there, the three near-identical float parsers comparison._num (19), invdiff._num (38) a  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-4.
- **inventory.DWR_DEFAULTS repeats the first entry of every FIELD_CANDIDATES tuple** `nd_dam_screening/core/inventory.py:9`  
  Verified: DWR_DEFAULTS == {k: v[0] for k, v in FIELD_CANDIDATES.items()} is True for all ten roles. The two tables are maintained by hand; DWR_DEFAULTS feeds tool 1's parameter defaults (prepare_dams.py:29) and the inventory diff (inventory_diff.py:41), FIELD_CANDIDATES the run-time fallback (resolve_fields).  
  What you see: None today; a new DWR field name added to one table and not the other makes tool 1's default box and its run-time fallback disagree.  
  Fix: Replace lines 9-11 with `DWR_DEFAULTS = {k: v[0] for k, v in FIELD_CANDIDATES.items()}` placed after FIELD_CANDIDATES. Three lines, one table.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-12.
- **nd_hazard's MODULE_VERSION "1.1.0" is pinned as a literal in a Screening test and in a Hazard Helper text string** `nd_dam_screening/core/nd_hazard.py:55`  
  MODULE_VERSION is a format version of the shared rules module, not the release number (0.28.3), so it does not break on a release; but the next change to nd_hazard.py that bumps it fails the adapter test for a reason unrelated to what the test checks (that hz uses the same module object), and the Hazard Helper's chart-file note keeps saying 1.1.0 while chartsign.py:300 already reads the attribute at run time.  
  What you see: After a module bump the Screening's own test suite reports a failure that has nothing to do with the Screening; the Hazard Helper's chart-file note would name the wrong module version in the file it writes.  
  Fix: Trim the assert to `assert hz.nh is nh`; build FORMAT_NOTE from the imported MODULE_VERSION (or drop the number from the text). Two lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-10.
- **Dead constants and functions: DWR_EXPECTED, MB, ACRE_M2 (duplicate of AC_M2), us_units.say/label_pair, incomplete.known_to_priority** `nd_dam_screening/core/preflight.py:43`  
  Repo-wide grep (all four plugins, tests included): preflight.DWR_EXPECTED and preflight.MB (line 38) have zero uses; damrecord_io.ACRE_M2 (line 47) has zero uses while AC_M2 (line 167) holds the same number and is the one used; us_units.say and label_pair (82-97) are called only from tests/test_us_units.py; incomplete.known_to_priority (124-128) is called only from tests. The DWR field check was replaced by inventory.detect_kind/resolve_fields (preflight.py:359-360) in 0.19.0.  
  What you see: None for the user; about 25 lines a maintainer reads and tests for nothing.  
  Fix: Delete DWR_EXPECTED, MB, damrecord_io.ACRE_M2, us_units.label_pair and say (and their test lines), and move known_to_priority into test_v0280.py where it is used. Saves about 25 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-5.
- **Preflight 'Disk space' estimates from every dam in the DWR layer, not the dams the run is about** `nd_dam_screening/core/preflight.py:227`  
  The window never passes n_dams (dialog.py:1965 run_checks(work, S, steps=steps, env=...)), so the estimate is the feature count of whatever layer fills the DWR slot. With the DWR export chosen on the Inputs tab or dropped into the suite folder's dwr/ (the documented way to use the real DWR inventory) that is the whole state, whatever county or ONLY_IDS the run is limited to.  
  What you see: A one-county run with the statewide DWR export reads 'Disk space WARN: 55.0 GB free; roughly 117 GB for 3000 dams at screening cell sizes - Free space on this drive or use a smaller cell size / corridor', a warning about space the run will not use. Engineers learn to ignore the preflight's warnings.  
  Fix: Pass n_dams from the window (wf.dam_count(work) when a step 1/16/18 table exists), and in run_checks fall back to the county clip or the ONLY_IDS count rather than the whole layer; say 'about N dams (whole layer)' when nothing better is known.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-4.
- **Preflight 'Suite set-up' ignores the run's suite_folder (1.22) and checks this computer's QGIS setting** `nd_dam_screening/core/preflight.py:418`  
  0.28.3 added settings['suite_folder'] so a headless run (qgis_process, a script, another computer) can name its suite data folder in the settings JSON; slot_origin honours it through settings_data_folder(). The preflight's 'Suite set-up' line still calls SC.check() with no folder, so it reads the QGIS profile's setting (empty in a headless profile).  
  What you see: Tool 17 run from qgis_process with a settings JSON that names suite_folder reports 'WARN Suite set-up: Suite folder: not chosen ...' while the run it is checking will read that folder and find the pack. The check contradicts the run.  
  Fix: `items = SC.check(folder=wf.settings_data_folder(S) or None)` so the preflight checks the folder the run will read.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-5.
- **The preflight's 'is the Runner installed' check can never run, although tool 17's help promises it** `nd_dam_screening/core/preflight.py:429`  
  Every caller gives `steps` from wf.chain_plan: preflight.py:209 itself, dialog.py:1964 and algorithms/preflight.py:52. chain_plan (workflow.py:1812) drops every key in CHAIN_SKIP, and CHAIN_SKIP (1777) contains '7b', so '7b' is never in steps once the work folder exists. Meanwhile tool 17's own help text (algorithms/preflight.py:27) tells the engineer the check list covers 'the Runner plugin for step 7b'.  
  What you see: The Check before running list never says whether ND Dam Breach Runner is installed, although the tool's description says it does. An engineer who plans to hand the High and Medium dams over finds out only when tool 7b stops with 'ND Dam Breach Runner v3.1+ is not installed'.  
  Fix: Check it whenever a hand-off is possible rather than when 7b is planned: 'if "7b" in steps or "8" in steps:' at preflight.py:429 (the classification is what tool 7b sends on), or drop the promise from the help text. One line.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-BV-3.
- **report.py repeats the same run-status tuple three times, the notes blob four times and a class-rank dict twice (which is nd_hazard.RANK)** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:117`
