  review_reasons() and the inner _run_status() each decide 'done' from a literal tuple of five status words; the proposed-dam test (PROPOSED_PHRASE in blob or source == "proposed") is written out twice (lines 306 and 386) over blobs assembled from the same six fields in different orders; the class rank is spelled out twice and equals nd_hazard.RANK (nd_hazard.py:63). A new status word (0.28.0 added 'excluded' and 'triage') has to be added in three places or the index tile and the review reasons disagree.  
  What you see: Today consistent; the next status or note field added in one place and not the others makes a dam count as 'done' in the status column while review_reasons calls it 'simulation failed', or a proposed dam get its banner on the dam page but not the index.  
  Fix: Add DONE_STATUSES = ("ok", "cached", "triage", "noflood", "excluded") and NOTE_FIELDS = (...) at the top, a 3-line _notes_blob(rec) and _is_proposed(rec), and `from .nd_hazard import RANK`; use them in the eight places. About 12 lines shorter and one place to edit.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-6.
- **Peak (cfs) column formatted with a format string passed as the decimals count** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:425`  
  _fmt's second argument is the number of decimals. qp_cfs is an int from _us(..., 0), so _fmt falls through to str(v) and the format string is ignored; if a float ever reached it, f"{v:,.{nd}f}" would raise 'Invalid format specifier'.  
  What you see: The summary tables show peaks as '12345' while every other number has thousands separators.  
  Fix: qp = f"{r['qp_cfs']:,.0f}" if r.get("qp_cfs") is not None else " ".  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-10.
- **report.summary calls its own _fmt with comparison.py's signature: the Peak (cfs) column loses its thousands separators** `nd_dam_screening/core/report.py:425`  
  Two helpers named _fmt with different second arguments exist in the same package (report.py takes a decimals count, comparison.py takes a format string). summary() passes comparison.py's format string to report.py's helper. Because qp_cfs is an int (set at line 388 by _us(..., 0), which returns int(round(x))), the isinstance(v, float) branch is skipped and the string is ignored, so the value is printed raw. Run: report._fmt(12345, "{:,.0f}") -> '12345'; comparison._fmt(12345, "{:,.0f}") -> '12,345'; report._fmt(12345.0, "{:,.0f}") raises ValueError 'Format specifier missing precision'.  
  What you see: In index.html (review-queue table and all-dams table) the Peak (cfs) column shows 28676 while the ac-ft column next to it shows 1,616: inconsistent and harder to read at a glance. If a record ever reached this line with a float qp_cfs the whole summary page would fail to write.  
  Fix: Replace the call with f"{r['qp_cfs']:,.0f}" (or make _fmt format ints as well as floats) and rename one of the two _fmt helpers, or better, delete comparison._fmt and use report's (see S2b-B-5). Add one assert on the HTML Peak cell to test_report.py.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-1.
- **reservoir.py has no unit test of its own** `nd_dam_screening/core/reservoir.py:37`  
  No test file imports reservoir; its four functions (find_reservoir, seed_upstream, estimate_bed, breach_cells) are reached only when test_runner.py runs the builtin-reservoir engine end to end, which checks that a run completes, not that the reservoir mask stops at the dam line or that the carved bed holds v_below_m3.  
  What you see: A wrong reservoir mask (water leaking round the dam) or a wrong bed depth in the reservoir-in-grid engine would pass the tests as long as the flood run finished.  
  Fix: Add one small synthetic-valley test: find_reservoir must not cross the dam cells and must report 'reaches the terrain edge' when it does; estimate_bed's info['v_below_m3'] must equal the requested volume within 1%; breach_cells must be within bavg/2 of the breach point. About 40 lines of test.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-15.
- **resume.pending is never called: the skip-done rule really lives in tool 7** `nd_dam_screening/core/resume.py:108`  
  Repo-wide grep: pending() is called only from tests/test_v0280.py:143-148. Tool 7 decides per dam and per loading case straight from the rasters (run_breach_sim.py:515: 'if skipdone and all(os.path.exists(pth[k]) for k in need)') and uses the manifest only for the 'Resuming: ...' line (run_breach_sim.py:720). The module's docstring (lines 12-13 and 17-20) nevertheless presents pending as the rule that decides which dams are skipped.  
  What you see: None for Nick. For a maintainer it is a decoy: a change to the documented skip rule in resume.py changes nothing, because the rule that runs is in the tool.  
  Fix: Delete pending (12 lines) and the docstring sentence about it, or have run_breach_sim build its case list through it so there is one rule. Either way one rule instead of two.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-BV-5.
- **'124 of 200 dams already have a result' counts manifest rows whose rasters are gone, so the same dams are then re-run** `nd_dam_screening/core/resume.py:145`  
  summary_text is built from counts(), which only reads the recorded state, while everything that decides what will actually run checks that the dam's rasters are still on disk. A manifest copied from another machine, or a 7_simulations folder cleaned out by hand, makes the two disagree.  
  What you see: Tool 7's log and the window's estimate line say 'Resuming: 124 of 200 dam(s) already have a result' and then model those 124 dams again, with no line saying why; the engineer plans around a number that is not what the run will do.  
  Fix: Give counts() the same raster test (or build summary_text from done_ids(outdir, True, man)) and say how many recorded results lost their rasters, e.g. '124 recorded, 7 of them will be run again (rasters missing)'.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-AV-4.
- **Dead code: review.REV_FIELDS, nd_hazard.ZONE_ORDER, ponds.type_counts (test-only) and an unreachable self-check in proposed.build that is also a NumPy 2 trap** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:31`  
  Grep over all four plugins including tests and the damsuite copies: REV_FIELDS has zero references (set_attributes adds TEXT columns itself at review.py:189); ZONE_ORDER has zero references anywhere (both copies, Runner and Hazard Helper included); type_counts is referenced only by tests/test_ponds.py, never by a tool or the window. In proposed.build, insert_embankment always works on _clean()'s copy (np.array(..., copy=True), proposed.py:37) and then out = natural.copy(), so shares_memory can never be true (verified: False). The guard also calls np.array(dem, copy=False), which in NumPy 2 raises 'Unable to avoid copy' when dem is a list rather than an array (verified on numpy 2.5.3); QGIS 3  
  What you see: None today; it is weight that has to be read and carried in the shared copies. The copy=False line would turn into a crash of tool 19 on a future QGIS with NumPy 2 if a caller ever passed a list.  
  Fix: Delete review.py:31, ponds.py:365-372 (and its two asserts in test_ponds.py), proposed.py:446 and 448-449 (keep `emb = insert_embankment(...)`). ZONE_ORDER lives in the Runner's master copy: delete it there and re-copy nd_hazard.py (the adapter test enforces the copy). About 15 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-8.
- **Review queue silently drops any layer that cannot be read** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:41`  
  _rows turns every read failure (locked or corrupt GeoPackage, a layer with other field names, dialog._read_rows returning None) into an empty list, and queue() has no way to report it.  
  What you see: The Review tab shows '0 dam(s) to look at' and disables its buttons as if nothing needed review, when in fact the breach-point, pond or hazard layer could not be opened.  
  Fix: Collect failures ('{} could not be read: {}') and return them with the queue (or raise), and show them in review_label; keep the per-source try so one bad layer does not hide the others.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-A-5.
- **The Review tab reads only one of the three breach-point layers, so ponds with an uncertain breach direction can be left off the list** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:65`  
  The or-chain stops at the first layer that exists. A work folder where the dams run (tool 4) and the ponds run (tool 16) were both done, but the merge (tool 18) was not, has two breach-point layers; only tool 4's is read. The module's own docstring says all three are considered.  
  What you see: In that folder a pond whose breach direction tool 16 marked dir_conf = low never appears on the Review tab, so nobody is asked to check it and the breach point stays where the terrain trace guessed. The dam-side points are listed, which makes the omission easy to miss.  
  Fix: Read all three: loop over the three paths that exist instead of taking the first. queue()'s add() already keeps one entry per dam and refuses a repeated reason, so the merged layer overlapping the other two is harmless. About three lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-BV-4.
- **review.py and merge.py hand-roll the same GeoPackage SQL (feature-table lookup, PRAGMA table_info, extent reset); six screening modules touch gpkg_contents directly** `nd_dam_screening/core/review.py:176`  
  SqliteApply._table (review.py:175-179) is a copy of merge._feature_table (23-27) including the identical 'no feature table' error; both modules repeat the PRAGMA table_info column scan (review 186, merge 31/47) and the gpkg_contents extent reset. grep shows gpkg_contents SQL in review.py, merge.py, package.py, workflow.py, incomplete.py and preflight.py as well; damsuite/suitegpkg.py has geometry helpers but no table helper.  
  What you see: No user-visible effect today; a GeoPackage quirk fixed in one module (for example a layer whose gpkg_contents row is missing, which both raise on) stays broken in the others.  
  Fix: Put _feature_table(con), _columns(con, table) and _reset_extent(con, table) once in merge.py (or a 25-line core/gpkg.py) and import them in review.py; delete SqliteApply._table and the duplicated lines. Saves about 15 lines in this unit and gives the other four modules a helper to adopt.  
  Effort: small. Id S2b-B-7.
- **runner._simulate is 199 lines and does eight jobs in one loop** `nd_dam_screening/core/runner.py:157`  
  Lines 157-355: terrain load and trace-driven growth, reservoir-in-grid set-up, corridor mask, buildings, source list, the run-until-settled loop, wall/edge growth, cascade and the no-breach baseline, with retry state (radius, corr_hw, use_corr, grew, last_valid, trace_grown, sim_s, extra) threaded through all of it. It is the only function in the unit over 150 lines. It does not mix GUI and computation (no QGIS import in the module).  
  What you see: None directly; it is the hardest function in the plugin to change safely, and it is the one every flood run goes through.  
  Fix: Lift the terrain-load/trace-grow loop (184-222) into _load_clip(...) returning (dem, gt, path, main, radius, grew) and the run-until-settled loop (269-297) into _run_until_settled(...); _simulate drops to about 120 lines with no behaviour change, and test_runner.py already covers the paths.  
  Effort: medium. Id S1-B-12.
- **A flood still at the corridor wall when the attempts run out is reported as finished, with the wrong corridor width** `nd_dam_screening/core/runner.py:326`  
  Every retry (reservoir growth, corridor widening, clip growth, cascade) uses one of max_attempts (8). When the last attempt still ends with the flood on the corridor wall, the `continue` ends the for loop and the code falls through to status 'ok' with the rasters of that last run, corridor_hw_m set to the doubled width that was never run, and no note: only on_edge gets one. Reproduced with max_attempts=2 and the wall reached every time: status ok, notes [], corridor_hw_m 200 m while the rasters came from the 100 m corridor.  
  What you see: Rare (it needs a dam that grows its reservoir, widens its corridor, enlarges its clip and cascades), but when it happens the dam's flood is cut by the corridor wall, the attribute table states a corridor width that was not modelled, and nothing in the notes says the result is truncated; a class read from it can be too low because people outside the corridor are not flooded in the model.  
  Fix: Only set status 'ok' when the loop ended with break (a flag), else keep status ok but append a note 'attempts used up: the flood still reaches the corridor wall - result truncated' and report the width that was actually run (keep the width before doubling, or store it in the result before `continue`).  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-4.
- **The no-breach baseline shares the breach run's time limit, so it is dropped with a note about a limit nobody set** `nd_dam_screening/core/runner.py:427`  
  The time limit (default 60 minutes) is for the whole dam, and t_start is set before the breach run. The baseline simulation, which the tool's own help says roughly doubles the run time, gets only what is left of that one budget, and when the breach run has used it up the engine is given max(remaining, 1.0) = one second and times out at once. The message it raises quotes the leftover budget, not the limit the engineer set, and because the result was already marked "ok" the failure only shows up as a note.  
  What you see: An engineer who ticked "also run a no-breach baseline (increment rasters)" gets a dam marked ok with base_run = no, no increment raster, and a note reading "built-in engine exceeded 0 min" (or 15 min, or whatever was left) although the setting says 60 minutes. Nothing says the baseline is what was skipped.  
  Fix: Give the baseline its own budget (restart the clock for it, or use a separate baseline_timeout_s), and word the engine's timeout as "the run's remaining time of N min (limit M min)". When the baseline cannot run, append a plain note such as "the no-breach baseline did not run: out of time" instead of only the engine's message.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-AV-4.
- **Two run-time estimators (tool 12 and the window label) with duplicated constants and a hand-typed copy of the cell table** `nd_dam_screening/core/runplan.py:41`  
  core/estimate.py (tool 12, calibrates the engine) and core/runplan.py (the window's 'about N min' label) answer the same question with different numbers. runplan copies LISFLOOD_FACTOR (estimate.py:38), fmt_duration (estimate.py:103-112, identical thresholds), PRESET_CELL_M (run_breach_sim.PRESET_VALS) and spells out domain.CELL_TABLE in AUTO_TEXT by hand. domain.CELL_TABLE is marked 'EDIT to taste'; editing it leaves the label wrong. The stated reason, 'Pure Python standard library', is not a constraint inside QGIS, where numpy is always present.  
  What you see: The window says one time and tool 12 another for the same county; if the preset cell sizes or the cell table change, the label keeps describing the old ones.  
  Fix: runplan imports estimate.fmt_duration, estimate.LISFLOOD_FACTOR and run_breach_sim.PRESET_VALS, and builds AUTO_TEXT from domain.CELL_TABLE; delete runplan.py lines 37-38, 41, 45-46 and 119-128 (about 25 lines, two duplicated constants). Next step: have run_time_text call estimate.plan_dam / seconds_for with the last tool-12 calibration rate saved in the settings, so the plugin has one estimator (  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-4.
- **Small functions with no production caller: write_bci/write_bdy, warp_to, worst_band, crop_box** `nd_dam_screening/core/solver_io.py:85`  
  Repo-wide grep: write_bci and write_bdy are called only from three tests (production writes point sources through write_sources, which handles one or many); dem_download.warp_to (522-533) is called only from one test and was superseded by warp_to_local in 0.24.0; arrival.worst_band (116-122) is test-only; gridutil.crop_box (133-140) has no caller at all, not even a test.  
  What you see: None directly. About 45 lines across four modules that look like live API and are not.  
  Fix: Delete the four (and the three test call sites for write_bci/write_bdy switch to write_sources([{pts, t, q}])); 45 lines and one test-only API gone.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-8.
- **Dead helpers kept beside their replacements** `nd_dam_screening/core/storage.py:292`  
  A grep of all four plugins (code and tests) finds scale_to_total and hold_at only in tests/test_dwr_kinds_019.py:116-121, write_bci and write_bdy only in tests (test_chain.py:40-41, test_engines_compare.py:30-31, test_solver_io.py:48-49, 76-77) and crop_box nowhere. set_top (storage.py:432) and write_sources (solver_io.py:102) are what the tools use. Two ways to put the inventory maximum on the curve, one of them dead, is a trap for the next person who changes the storage rule.  
  What you see: None today; a maintainer fixing the storage-at-top rule or the LISFLOOD writer can fix the dead copy and see the tests pass.  
  Fix: Delete scale_to_total, hold_at, crop_box, write_bci and write_bdy; point the listed test lines at set_top and write_sources.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-A-5.
- **storage.scale_to_total and hold_at are dead in production (only tests call them)** `nd_dam_screening/core/storage.py:292`  
  A repo-wide grep (all four plugins, tests included) finds no production caller of either; only test_storage.py and test_dwr_kinds_019.py use them. set_top (line 432, also 0.19.0) is what algorithms/estimate_storage.py uses to honour the inventory maximum storage. 28 lines (292-319) that two tests keep alive.  
  What you see: None directly; two more ways to fit a curve to the inventory maximum that nobody runs, which a maintainer reading storage.py has to tell apart from the live one.  
  Fix: Delete scale_to_total and hold_at and their asserts in the two tests. Saves 28 lines and leaves set_top as the one inventory-maximum rule.  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-7.
- **screen_what and chain are two settings for one choice, kept equal by two sync handlers** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:203`  
  screen_what (0/1/2) is exactly '16 in chain' / '18 in chain'. dialog.py:1441-1442 writes the chain checkboxes from the Simple tab's radio, 1453-1455 writes the radio back from the checkboxes, both values are saved with the project and the settings file (WINDOW_KEYS at 241 lists both), and the include list is computed from chain at 1962 and 2070 but from screen_what at 2233. Two encodings, two sync handlers, two code paths.  
  What you see: A settings file or project where the two disagree (one edited by hand, or an older save) picks one answer on the Simple tab and another in the Advanced chain, and the 2233 path can run a different set of optional steps than the 1962 path for the same window state.  
  Fix: Drop screen_what from DEFAULT_SETTINGS, WINDOW_KEYS and window_settings_from; derive the radio from chain ('18' in chain -> 2, '16' in chain -> 1, else 0) in _apply, keep only the radio->checkbox handler, and compute the include at dialog.py:2233 with the same expression as 1962. Saves 1 setting, one sync handler and one divergent code path (about 15 lines in dialog.py).  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-10.
- **Four settings keys (dugout_records, wb_id, pond_max_ac, wb_search_ft) have no control in the window and are always empty there** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:207`  
  DEFAULT_SETTINGS has 52 keys (42 literal plus the 10 LAYER_SLOTS added at line 294). All 52 are read somewhere, but a repo-wide grep shows these four are read only by defaults() (steps 3 and 16, lines 1286-1287 and 1460-1463) and are never written by dialog.py: no edit box, combo or spin sets them, _apply() does not restore them, and settings() rebuilds the dict from DEFAULT_SETTINGS so they are '' every time. They can only arrive through a settings file's window rows or a headless JSON. suite_folder (the fifth key with no control) is headless-only by design and documented as such.  
  What you see: Nothing the engineer does in the window can set a DWR dugout records file, its ID field, a pond size cap or the step-3 waterbody search distance, although the settings exist and the tools' own dialogs have the same inputs (tool 16: INV, INV_ID, MAX_AC; tool 3: SEARCH_FT). Dead settings that a reader of the code or a settings file believes work.  
  Fix: Remove the four keys from DEFAULT_SETTINGS and the two branches of defaults() that read them (the tool dialogs keep the inputs), or add the controls. Given the brief's aim of fewer settings, remove.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-6.
- **Four settings keys that no screen ever sets (dugout_records, wb_id, pond_max_ac, wb_search_ft)** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:207`  
  DEFAULT_SETTINGS has 52 keys (42 literal at lines 165-219 plus the 10 LAYER_SLOTS added at 294-295). Every key is read by defaults(), but these four are never written by anything: dialog.py settings() and _apply() (1331-1400) enumerate _EDITS, _COMBOS, _CHECKS, _SPINS and name work, package, workers, chain, screen_what, settings_file, reviewer, counties and the layer slots, and none of the four is among them; they are not in WINDOW_KEYS (239-242) so they never reach the settings file either; grep over the whole plugin and its tests finds the quoted key only in workflow.py (definition plus defaults() at 1286-1287 and 1459-1463). The 0.19.0 'DWR dugouts' mode of tool 16 and tool 3's search dis  
  What you see: Nick cannot reach these options from the window, and anyone reading the 52-key settings table thinks the window has a dugout mode and a waterbody search distance that it does not have. A settings file hand-edited to carry them is silently dropped on the next save because WINDOW_KEYS excludes them.  
  Fix: Delete the four keys from DEFAULT_SETTINGS and the seven lines that forward them in defaults() (1286-1287, 1459-1463); tool 16 and tool 3 keep their own dialog parameters for the rare case. Saves 4 settings and about 11 lines. (If the dugout mode is wanted in the window, it needs controls and a WINDOW_KEYS entry, not just keys.)  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-1.
- **The time estimate does not see a proposed dam's table, so it asks for step 1** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:288`  
  dam_count looks for the dam table of steps 18, 1 and 16 but not of step 19, although a proposed dam 'takes the place of 1-4' (STEPS, workflow.py:75-79) and writes 19_proposed.gpkg. Every other place that has to find the dams of a work folder does include it: work_area iterates ('1', '18', '16', '19') at line 674, and defaults' dams3() falls back to o('19') at 1225.  
  What you see: In a work folder made by 'Propose a dam', the Simple tab shows no run-time estimate and instead says 'Run step 1 to see how long a run of this county takes' (dialog.py:1784), which is the one step the proposed-dam path replaces; the remaining-dams count of an interrupted run is not shown either.  
  Fix: for step in ("18", "1", "16", "19"): - one line, and the docstring gains '/ 19'.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-BV-4.
- **ImportError shims for 'core/ on sys.path' are no longer exercised by any test** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:449`  
  workflow._ds (22 call sites), damrecord_io.py:38-42 and 324-327, and handoff.py:13-18 all fall back to a top-level import 'for core/ itself on sys.path (tests)'. No test imports these modules that way: grep '^import (workflow|handoff|damrecord_io|preflight|manifest|selftest|errors|us_units|package|incomplete|resume|settings_groups)' over tests/ finds nothing; the four tests that put core/ on sys.path (test_breach_dir, test_breach_hydro, test_chain, test_dam_lines) import only breach_dir, breach_hydro, solver_io and _dbf; all ten workflow imports are 'from nd_dam_screening.core import workflow as wf'. workflow.py itself uses plain relative imports elsewhere (374, 620, 1114, 1645, 1660, 1734)   
  What you see: None for the user; for a maintainer, 'where does damdata come from' is not greppable (22 indirect lookups) and the shims suggest a test mode that does not exist.  
  Fix: Replace each _ds("x") with a module-level 'from ..damsuite import x' (or one 'from .. import damsuite'), delete _ds and the two try/except blocks in damrecord_io.py and handoff.py, and change test_autofill_0211.py:150 to import offline_pack directly. Saves about 20 lines and 22 indirections.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-9.
- **defaults() is 287 lines and run_checks() 251; steps 15 and 17 repeat the same SETTINGS lines** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1196`  
  workflow.defaults (1196-1482) is one if/elif ladder over 19 steps, 287 lines; preflight.run_checks (199-449) is 251 lines with a 44-line closure inside. Both are over the 150-line mark the brief sets. Lines 1472-1474 and 1476-1478 are identical. workflow.py is 2007 lines in all; it holds no Qt, but lines 417-1011 (suite-data slot resolution: slot_origin, pack/download/nid clips, work_area) are a self-contained 600-line block.  
  What you see: None directly; a per-step parameter bug is hard to find and cannot be tested without running the whole ladder.  
  Fix: Merge steps 15 and 17 into one 'elif step in ("15", "17")' branch (saves 3 lines now). Optionally split defaults() into small per-step functions in a dict {step: fn} and move lines 417-1011 to core/suite_slots.py; that saves no lines but brings workflow.py under 1500 and makes each step's parameters testable alone.  
  Effort: medium. Id S2a-B-11.
- **The plugin key 'ndscreen' is defined in three files and the provider id 'nddam' is spelled out in three** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1596`  
  workflow.PLUGIN_KEY, damrecord_io.TOOL and settings_groups.PLUGIN are the same key, which damsuite/versions.py already holds in PLUGINS; the Processing provider id is workflow.PROVIDER and is also hard-coded in selftest.py:231 and manifest.py:201 (the latter instead of wf.algorithm_id("8")), beside provider.py:153 which is the real one.  
  What you see: None today; a renamed key or provider id (the DamSafety clash the 1.22 summary discusses) would have to be chased through six places.  
  Fix: Keep workflow.PROVIDER and PLUGIN_KEY, delete damrecord_io.TOOL and settings_groups.PLUGIN in favour of them, and use wf.algorithm_id in selftest.py:231 and manifest.py:201. Saves 2 constants and 2 literals.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-13.
- **Step 7 verdict query uses a double-quoted empty string; on SQLite without the legacy fallback the chain stops noticing an all-FAILED flood step** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1873`  
  In SQL a double-quoted token is an identifier; SQLite only reads "" as the string '' through its deprecated double-quoted-string fallback (off when built with SQLITE_DQS=0). `note` has the same form (", ''" is correct there). When the fallback is off the query raises, the broad except returns no counts, step_problem() returns None, and Chain._finished carries on. This is the one function whose job is to stop the chain when every dam's flood FAILED (docstring: 'it would classify NoData and report OK').  
  What you see: On such a build a step 7 in which every flood failed is followed by step 8 and 10 as if fine; the report shows NoData classes with no stop and no warning line. Works today on the SQLite QGIS ships because the fallback is on.  
  Fix: Use single quotes: `COALESCE(sim_status, '')`.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-A-7.
- **No test for the chain's 'every dam FAILED' stop nor for the statewide list's incomplete-run note** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1885`  
  grep of nd_dam_screening/tests for step_problem, step_status_counts, record_note and note_rows finds nothing. step_status_counts/step_problem (1849-1895) read 7_simulations.gpkg with sqlite3 and decide whether Chain._finished (1996-2003) stops the run or only warns; incomplete.record_note/note_rows (167-207) put the 'flood truncated' words into the statewide priority rows, which the module's own docstring calls 'the one grouping that must not be wrong'. Both are plain functions over a small file, so they are cheap to test.  
  What you see: A regression in either goes unnoticed: a chain whose floods all failed would carry NoData into the classification and report OK, or the statewide list would rank a truncated flood as 'agree'.  
  Fix: Add to test_window_028.py: a two-row GeoPackage with sim_status FAILED/ok giving step_problem == (False, '... 1 of 2 ...') and all-FAILED giving (True, ...); a dam folder whose screened result carries sim_notes 'still spreading' giving record_note containing that text and note_rows filling 'note'. About 30 lines of test.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-4.
- **The running-step timer on the Simple tab never appears: the window asks the chain for an attribute it does not have** `nd_dam_screening/core/workflow.py:1978`  
  Chain publishes the running step as `current`; nothing in any of the four plugins defines `_current_step` (a repo-wide search finds only this read). getattr therefore always returns None and _tick returns at once, so the '... 12s' note it is meant to write is never written.  
  What you see: While a step runs, the Simple tab's step line shows no sign of life: no elapsed seconds, nothing changing. During step 7, which can take hours, the only movement is the log pane, so an engineer cannot tell from the step list whether the run is working or hung.  
  Fix: Read `self.chain.current` in dialog._tick (or add a `current_step` property to Chain), and cover it with a test that drives Chain and reads the attribute the window reads.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-AV-3.
- **Stale button name and release notes in user-facing text** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:508`  
  The Simple tab button was renamed 'Run county' at 0.28.0 (433, comment 431-432) but the All steps text still points at 'Run all'. Three tooltips carry version-history sentences that describe old bugs rather than what the control does.  
  What you see: A user looking for 'Run all' on the Simple tab does not find it; tooltips read like a changelog and make a simple box ('Grid size') look dangerous.  
  Fix: Change line 508 to 'Run county'; drop the version sentences from the tooltips at 443, 645-647 and 651-652 (keep the one-line meaning).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-8.
- **Three ways to pick or write the settings file: Simple's File.../New..., the grid's Load.../Save/Save as..., and 'Export settings (JSON)...'** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:529`  
  The Simple tab names the settings file (357-374, File.../New...), the grid below has its own Load.../Save/Save as... (suite_settings.py:166-170) kept in step by _grid_file (1534-1539), and All steps has a JSON export (529-532, _export_settings 2107-2125). The headless tool reads the .xlsx directly (algorithms/run_chain.py:31) and every run already writes <work>/settings_used.xlsx (workflow.py write_run_settings), so the JSON is a third format for the same values; its own tooltip says so.  
  What you see: Nick has three file buttons for one settings file and a JSON he is told not to use.  
  Fix: Delete export_btn and _export_settings (about 25 lines, one button; test_popup.py:523 to drop), and hide the grid's Load.../Save as... in this window since the Simple tab's box owns the file. Saves one format and up to four buttons.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-9.
- **Two county lists from two data sources in one window, and a map button that draws bounding boxes when the outlines exist** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:813`  
  The header County combo (266-277) comes from core/nd_counties.py (nd_counties.geojson, real outlines); the DEM box County (813-815) comes from core/dem_download.COUNTIES, 53 hand-typed lon/lat boxes (dem_download.py:26-83). 'Counties on the map' (831-832, _show_counties 945-976) builds a memory layer of those boxes. The two name sets agree today (53/53) but nothing ties them. _show_counties also uses the absolute import `from nd_dam_screening.core import dem_download` (949) where the rest of the file imports relatively.  
  What you see: Two county pickers on one window that can disagree after an edit to either list; a 'counties' layer of rectangles on the map.  
  Fix: With S4-B-3 the second combo and the map button go. Otherwise derive dem_download's boxes from nd_counties' outlines (box = outline bounds) and delete COUNTIES (about 55 lines).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-11.
- **The DEM box remembers the last dam's ID and folder for the whole QGIS profile, and starts on Cass whatever county is being screened** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:871`  
  The eleven DEM-box values, the dam ID and the output folder among them, are saved in the QGIS profile (_save_dem_box, 906-924) and read back into every new window (_load_dem_box, 879-904), while the rest of the window is saved with the project. Nothing links the box to the dam or the county the window is working on: the hand-off from another plugin fills only the Dams folder (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/suite_home.py:655-658), and line 815 starts the county list on 'Cass' no matter what the header's County combo says.  
  What you see: Nick screens a dam in one county, comes back next week for a dam in another, presses 'Download DEM...', and the box still holds the previous dam's ID and output folder, so the DEM is offered for that dam and would be written into its folder and its dam.json; the county list says Cass even when he is screening Barnes.  
  Fix: Stop remembering dam_id, point and folder across sessions (drop them from _dem_box_widgets), fill them from the chosen dam and the header's County combo instead, or delete the box as S4-B-3 proposes and pre-fill the Processing dialog from the selected dam.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-BV-4.
- **'Counties on the map' adds a new copy of the county layer on every press and uses an absolute package import** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:949`  
  Nothing checks whether an 'ND counties (download clips)' layer is already in the project, so each press stacks another identical memory layer with its own selectionChanged hook (969). The import at 949 is the only absolute import in the file (everything else is relative); it works only because the plugin folder is named nd_dam_screening.  
  What you see: Several identical county layers pile up in the layer tree after a few presses; each must be removed by hand.  
  Fix: Keep a reference (or find the layer by name) and re-use it; use 'from .core import dem_download as dd' like the rest of the file.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-9.
- **'Counties on the map' adds another copy of the layer each press, and picking a county on the map changes the download source behind Nick's back** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:978`  
  Selecting a county on the map sets the Source combo to entry 1, ND DWR LiDAR, and the Area combo to County, whatever Nick had chosen: the handler was written for the DWR case and changes two settings it was not asked to change. _show_counties (945-976) also builds a fresh memory layer and adds it to the project on every press (line 967), with no check for the layer it added last time, and connects one more selection handler to each copy.  
  What you see: Nick chooses USGS 3DEP, presses 'Counties on the map' to find his county, clicks it, and the Source has quietly become ND DWR LiDAR, so the download he starts comes from the wrong service. Press the button three times and the project carries three identical 'ND counties (download clips)' layers, each still switching the source when a county is clicked.  
  Fix: Set only the county (delete the two setCurrentIndex lines), and in _show_counties re-use the layer if one with that name is already in the project instead of adding another. Or delete the button with the DEM box (S4-B-3).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-BV-3.
- **Selecting a county on the map also switches the DEM source to ND DWR LiDAR without saying so** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:982`  
  The hook that follows the map selection changes three boxes, not one: besides the county it forces Source to 'ND DWR LiDAR' and Area to 'County'. The log line written when the layer is added ('Select one and the county list follows it') promises only the county.  
  What you see: A user who had chosen USGS 3DEP and then clicked a county on the map starts a ND DWR LiDAR download instead, with no message; the two sources have different tiles, sizes and run times.  
  Fix: Set only the county name (and the Area, if that is wanted), leave the Source box as the user set it, and say in the log what was changed.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-AV-5.
- **Refresh buttons on All steps and Review repeat what the window does by itself** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1070`  
  refresh() runs after every edit (_changed, 1426-1429), on every window activation (changeEvent, 1896-1905), after every chain step, and it calls refresh_review() itself (1892). The All steps 'Refresh' (545-546) and the Review 'Refresh' (1070-1071) add nothing. Inputs' 'Refresh the layer lists' (project layers change outside the window) and Priority's 'Refresh the list' (a slow folder scan by design) are different.  
  What you see: Two more buttons to wonder about; pressing them changes nothing visible.  
  Fix: Delete refresh_btn and review_refresh_btn (545-548, 1070-1071, 1091). Saves two buttons and six lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-8.
- **The Priority tab's Dams folder is forgotten every time the window is re-opened** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1200`  
  Every other path box in the window is saved with the QGIS project (the _EDITS list at 1332 and settings(), 1343-1344), but priority_edit is in no list, is written to no settings key and is restored by nothing (_apply, 1368-1400). It also has no editingFinished connection, so nothing else in the window notices it. priority_folder (1238-1244) then falls back to the work folder's own Dams folder.  
  What you see: Nick points the Priority tab at the shared statewide Dams folder, closes QGIS, comes back, and the box is empty again: 'Refresh the list' now reads this run's own Dams folder and shows a different, shorter list of dams, with only the folder name in the line above the table to say so.  
  Fix: Add a "priority_folder" key to DEFAULT_SETTINGS and to the window's saved values (settings() and _apply, next to the other path boxes), or drop the box altogether with S4-B-4 and let the suite home's Dams folder be the one place the folder is named.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-BV-2.
- **'What to screen' radios and the 16/18 chain check boxes are one setting with 30 lines of sync code** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1431`  
  The three radios (316-325) and the 'ponds and dugouts (16)' / 'merge dams and ponds (18)' boxes on All steps (514-517) express the same choice; _screen_toggled and _chain_check_toggled (1431-1459) keep them equal with _loading guards.  
  What you see: The same choice appears on two tabs with different words; ticking 18 without 16 flips the radio to 'DWR inventory' and clears the tick again.  
  Fix: Delete the two check boxes from the chain row (keep 0, 6, 9, 19) and _chain_check_toggled; settings() derives chain 16/18 from screen_what. Saves two controls and about 30 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-10.
- **'New...' settings file logs 'written' and makes it current even when the save failed** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1487`  
  SettingsGrid.save returns '' after showing a warning when DS.SettingsError is raised (suite_settings.py:448-456, e.g. the workbook is open in Excel or the folder is read-only). The return value is ignored, so the window still sets the non-existent file as the current path (see S4-A-5) and logs that it was written.  
  What you see: A failed save leaves the window and the suite pointing at a file that does not exist, with a log line saying the opposite.  
  Fix: Check the return of self.grid.save(); on '' leave the previous path in place and log the failure.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-6.
- **Small copies inside dialog.py: _read_rows repeats QgisEnv.rows; two one-line wrappers** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1634`  
  Lines 1634-1639 are the body of QgisEnv.rows (184-188). _point_window (2516-2524) only forwards to suite_home.point_window_at; plugin_version() (83-88) only forwards to damrecord_io.plugin_version.  
  What you see: None for Nick; two places to fix the same reader.  
  Fix: Keep one QgisEnv on the window (self.env) and pass env.rows where _read_rows is passed; inline the two wrappers. About 20 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-17.
- **The window can create a missing work folder, but no button can reach that code** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:1913`  
  _open_step is only reached from the step buttons and 'Open the next step', and refresh() greys all of them out whenever the folder does not exist (`have = bool(work and os.path.isdir(work))`, 1808, then 1831-1833), so this folder-creating branch cannot be reached from the window at all. _run_chain (2064-2066) and _run_one_dam (2197-2199) refuse outright instead. The hint line meanwhile says 'Choose or create a work folder first.' (1855).  
  What you see: Nick types the path of a work folder he has not made yet, and the whole window goes grey with no way to create it: the text tells him to create one, every button that could is disabled, and he has to go back to Browse... and make the folder in the file dialog.  
  Fix: Either create the folder where it is first needed (make 'Run county' and the step buttons stay live when the path is typed but missing, with os.makedirs there), or delete the unreachable branch at 1913-1919 and change the hint to 'Choose an existing work folder (Browse... can make one).'  
  Effort: small. Id S4-BV-5.
- **Two dam pickers: a searchable list for 'Run one dam...' and a check-box list for 'Run selected dams...'** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2209`  
  _run_one_dam (2209-2221) uses QInputDialog.getItem/getText; _run_selected_dams (2159-2181) uses the _DamPicker class (91-134, All/None/OK/Cancel). Both end in _run_one_dam with ONLY_IDS; one tick in the picker is the one-dam case. The two buttons sit side by side on Simple (439-455) with 12 lines of tooltip between them.  
  What you see: Two buttons and two list windows for 'run these dams alone'.  
  Fix: Keep 'Run selected dams...' (rename it 'Run chosen dams...'), delete one_dam_btn and the QInputDialog branch; _DamPicker gets a search box. Saves one button and about 20 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-20.
- **'Add the results to the project' adds a second copy of every result layer each time it is pressed** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2432`  
  The group is re-used when it is already in the project, but the layers in it are never looked at: every result file is opened again and added again. Nothing checks whether a layer with that source is already in the group.  
  What you see: Pressing the button twice (or after a re-run, which is the normal thing to do) leaves two of every result layer in the same group, each drawn on top of the other, and each has to be removed by hand. A one-dam run adds its group automatically at the end (line 2173), so the one-dam groups pile up too.  
  Fix: Before adding, clear the group's existing layers (or skip a result whose file is already a layer in that group), so the button refreshes the group instead of stacking a new copy.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-AV-2.
- **The elapsed-seconds counter never shows: _tick reads chain._current_step, the Chain's attribute is 'current'** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2480`  
  core/workflow.py's Chain sets self.current = step (line 1978) and never defines _current_step (confirmed: hasattr(Chain(...), '_current_step') is False). So _tick returns at the None check every second, and the '… Ns' note on the Simple tab's step line (2489) is never written. The 1 s timer started at 225-228 runs for the life of the window doing nothing.  
  What you see: While a step runs, the Simple tab gives no sign of time passing for that step; the user only learns the duration when the step ends.  
  Fix: Use getattr(self.chain, "current", None) (or add a public step/elapsed accessor to Chain and use it). Add a test that drives a Chain and checks the note text.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-A-3.
- **dialog.py (2,720 lines) mixes widgets with reading GeoPackages and planning runs** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2584`  
  The docstring (lines 10-11) says the file only holds the widgets, but _refresh_dam_table (2566-2642) opens step 8's GeoPackage and guesses column names, _show_counties builds a layer, _zoom_to_selected transforms geometry, _run_one_dam (2192-2257) plans the run folder and steps, refresh() is 92 lines (1804-1895) and _build_run_options 178 lines (606-784). _refresh_dam_table has no direct test (grep: 0).  
  What you see: A change to a step 8 field name breaks the Results table without a test saying so; the file is the hardest one for the next person to edit.  
  Fix: Move the dam-table reader to core/incomplete.py (pure read returning rows, testable), take out the DEM box (S4-B-3) and the Priority tab (S4-B-4), and the file drops to about 2,000 lines with refresh() the only long function left.  
  Effort: medium. Id S4-B-12.
- **'Open Runner...', 'Open Hazard Helper...' and 'Continue' on Results do the same hand-off; a second 'Continue' sits on Simple** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2680`  
  Results has four hand-off buttons (1123-1141): Open Runner, Open Hazard Helper, Continue; Simple has another Continue (459-463). Continue only dispatches to _open_runner / _open_hazard and already knows which plugin the selected dam needs (wf.continue_target). The suite home's Dams tab has 'Open the plugin for the selected dam' for the same purpose.  
  What you see: Three buttons for two destinations on one tab; Nick must know whether a dam goes to the Runner or to Hazard, which Continue was built to tell him.  
  Fix: Delete runner_btn and hazard_btn (1123-1135) and keep the two Continue buttons (or keep one Continue on Results). Saves two buttons and about 14 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-7.
- **'Suite settings...' opens tab number 3 by hard-coded index** `nd_dam_screening/dialog.py:2711`  
  The index is the position in _build (281-288). Hazard's run_settings finds the tab by title (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/plugin.py:168-177). Any of the tab removals above (S4-B-4) or a reorder lands the menu entry on the wrong tab, and the except swallows nothing because setCurrentIndex never raises.  
  What you see: 'Suite settings...' would open Inputs or Review after a tab change, with no error.  
  Fix: self.tabs.setCurrentIndex(self.tabs.indexOf(self.grid.parentWidget())) or look the tab up by its title; one line.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-19.
- **The plugin's own Plugins submenu repeats two entries of the shared ND Dam Suite menu** `nd_dam_screening/plugin.py:81`  
  Plugins > ND Dam Breach Screening holds 'ND Dam Breach Screening' and 'Dam suite home...' (81-88); Plugins > ND Dam Suite holds 'ND Dam Breach Screening...' and 'Dam suite home...' as well (99-103; gui_report menus confirm both lists). unload() spends 199-223 removing the first pair.  
  What you see: Two menus that open the same two windows; Nick is told to use ND Dam Suite and finds a second menu that looks like a different plugin.  
  Fix: Delete the two actions in _add_menu_action and their unload code; keep the suite menu. Saves two menu entries and about 40 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-B-15.
- **The plugin adds its own Plugins submenu whose two entries are already in the shared ND Dam Suite menu** `nd_dam_screening/plugin.py:84`  
  Both menus are built on every start: Plugins > ND Dam Breach Screening (the window and 'Dam suite home...') and Plugins > ND Dam Suite (the same window, the same home, plus the suite settings and suite data). The captured QGIS menus show exactly that, and the other two plugins do the same, so the Plugins menu carries three near-identical submenus on top of the shared one.  
  What you see: Nick sees the same window offered twice in two different places in the Plugins menu, and has to learn which of the two lists is the real front door.  
  Fix: Drop the plugin's own submenu (the two addPluginToMenu calls and the unload code for them) and keep only the shared 'ND Dam Suite' menu, which already has the window, the home, the settings and the suite data. One menu instead of two.  
  Effort: small. Id S4-AV-3.
- **Three plugin.py files with the same skeleton written three ways** `nd_dam_screening/plugin.py:163`  
  initProcessing, initGui, check_versions, _dialog_alive, run_home, run_suite_data, run_settings and unload appear in nd_dam_screening/plugin.py (250 lines), ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/plugin.py (305) and nd_breach_runner/plugin.py (376) with the same job and different bodies: Screening's suite_messages()+_level(), the Runner's version_messages() (a different return shape), Hazard's MSG_* constants; run_settings hard-codes tab index 3 here (dialog.py:2711) and searches the tab title in Hazard (plugin.py:168-177).  
  What you see: A fix to the version warning or the unload path must be made three times; today each plugin warns in a slightly different way.  
  Fix: A damsuite/plugin_base.py with the shared methods, each plugin.py keeping only its names, menu and provider class. About 930 lines become roughly 400.  
  Effort: large. Id S4-B-16.
- **The DEM download tool's run-time choices become settings-file rows, and they land on the 'Reports & checks' tab** `nd_dam_screening/provider.py:29`  
  DownloadDem is made settings-aware like the screening steps, but it has no entry in the tab map, so its parameters are filed under the last tab. The captured Settings tab shows 'USGS 3DEP', 'Dam' and '10 m' combos sitting on 'Reports & checks'. These are per-download choices, not engineer-chosen thresholds, and the window's own 'Download a DEM' box passes its values explicitly (dialog.py:1005-1015), so the rows have no effect on a download started from the window.  
  What you see: The Settings grid's 'Reports & checks' tab shows download rows that belong nowhere near reports, and a value set there is silently ignored when the download is started from the window, so a user who 'fixed' the source or resolution there still gets the box's choice.  
  Fix: Add 'downloaddem' to NOT_IN_GRID in core/settings_groups.py so the download tool keeps its own dialog values and leaves the grid, or, if the rows are wanted, give it a proper entry in FAMILIES (for example Terrain & storage).  
  Effort: small. Id S4-AV-4.
- **proposed.py is a 507-line byte-identical copy in Screening and Runner with no identity test, unlike nd_hazard and nd_counties** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_nd_hazard_adapter.py:29`  
  Three core modules are deliberately duplicated between nd_dam_screening/core and nd_breach_runner/core instead of living in damsuite (nd_hazard.py 864 lines + charts JSON, nd_counties.py 128 + geojson, proposed.py 507). The first two are guarded by a byte-compare test in each plugin; proposed.py (the proposed-embankment rules that breach_hydrograph.py:14 and run_breach_sim.py:18 import for PROPOSED_PHRASE) is not, and FILES.json hashes cover damsuite only, so the two copies can drift without any test or hash noticing.  
  What you see: A fix to the proposed-dam handling made in one plugin and not copied gives a different note or embankment in the Runner than in Screening for the same dam.  
  Fix: Add "proposed.py" to the tuple at test_nd_hazard_adapter.py:29 (one word), or move the three modules and their data files into damsuite so FILES.json hashes cover them in all four plugins and the two ad-hoc identity tests can go.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-15.
- **Tests pin release numbers and a sign-off date that change every release** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_nd_hazard_adapter.py:36`  
  test_nd_hazard_adapter.py pins nd_hazard's MODULE_VERSION to the literal "1.1.0" and the chart sign-off date to the literal "2026-10-12"; test_v3200.py (the test file for lifeloss, cascadecheck and compareruns) pins the Runner release to the literal "3.20.3". Each breaks on the next release or the next chart sign-off without any behaviour having changed, and each duplicates a value that already lives in damsuite (versions.RELEASE, tm11_curves.SIGNED_OFF_ON).  
  What you see: None for Nick; for the developer, red tests at every release that are fixed by editing literals, which teaches people to ignore red tests.  
  Fix: Compare against damsuite.versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"] and tm11_curves.SIGNED_OFF_ON, and drop the MODULE_VERSION pin (R3-B-3 removes the need for it). Three literals fewer to update per release.  
  Effort: small. Id R3-B-17.
- **Screening scripts isolate the suite config by setting HOME, which Windows ignores** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_popup.py:13`  
  damsuite/suitedata.py:49 computes `CONFIG = os.path.join(os.path.expanduser("~"), ".ndsuite", "config.json")`. On Windows (Python 3.8 and later) expanduser uses USERPROFILE and no longer reads HOME, so the override at test_popup.py:13, test_window_028.py:17, test_v0260.py:14, test_v0280.py:23, test_v0281.py:17, test_v0283.py:26, test_pack_0220.py:10, test_nid_clip_0221.py:10 and test_autofill_0211.py:9 does nothing there. Writes are harmless (suitedata._set at :97-101 stores into the mock QgsSettings dict of qgis_mock/qgis/core.py:464 first), but reads fall through `_get` (:82-93) to `_read_config()`, i.e. to the engineer's real %USERPROFILE%\.ndsuite\config.json: a Dams folder or data folde  
  What you see: Run on the engineer's Windows machine, nine Screening scripts read his real suite settings; results then differ from the developer's machine (a configured Dams folder makes the window tests take another path), which looks like a flaky suite.  
  Fix: In each of the nine scripts add `SD.CONFIG = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), "config.json")` after importing suitedata (or a shared tests/_isolate.py helper), and drop the HOME trick.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-8.
- **Two Screening scripts look for data on one developer's machine (/home/claude/...) and the README advertises a check that runs nowhere else** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_suite09.py:46`  
  Section 3 of test_suite09.py (a real ND Dam Hazard export fed to tool 0) needs a `harness/tests/test_v2100_suiteexport.py` and a `harness/fakes` folder that are not in this repository, at an absolute path on one machine; everywhere else it prints `SKIP ND Dam Hazard export: plugin or harness not found` (line 174). nd_dam_screening/README.md:646 nevertheless says the 0.9 checks include 'a real export made by ND Dam Hazard's exporter under its own test harness'. test_inventory.py:31 defaults DAMS_DBF to `/home/claude/data/DamsbyHazardClass.dbf` and skips the whole real-data half when it is absent (line 32-33). test_handoff.py:85 shows the portable way (`os.path.join(PARENT, "nd_breach_runner")  
  What you see: The one test that proves the Hazard export and the Screening data package agree on field names and counts has not run since the repo left that machine, while the README says it has.  
  Fix: Point ND at `os.path.join(PARENT, "ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper")` and move the pack-making helper (T.make_pack / T.dataset) into the repo (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests) so the section runs whenever the sibling is present; drop the DAMS_DBF default path and say in the README which checks need the DWR file.  
  Effort: small. Id T1-T-7.
- **Test files and Qt test doubles are duplicated between Runner and Screening and have started to drift** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_suite_data.py:4`  
  nd_breach_runner/tests/test_suite_data.py and nd_dam_screening/tests/test_suite_data.py are byte-identical (519 lines) but, unlike the damsuite copies, no FILES.json hash guards them, so the next edit to one will not reach the other. The Qt doubles are a second copy: nd_breach_runner/tests/qt_fakes/QtGui.py == nd_dam_screening/tests/qgis_mock/qgis/PyQt/QtGui.py, QtCore.py differs in 10 lines, QtWidgets.py already differs in 107 lines (the Runner copy has QWizardPage/QWizard at :83-114 and QButtonGroup at :386-404; the Screening copy has QPushButton.text/setText at :110-115 that the Runner lacks). The invented ACER test charts are a third copy (nd_breach_runner/tests/test_nd_hazard.py:13-27 a  
  What you see: A fix to a Qt double or to the suite-data checks lands in one plugin and the other plugin's tests keep passing against stale behaviour; the engineer sees two plugins that claim the same shared code is tested while only one copy is.  
  Fix: Keep one copy of test_suite_data.py and the Qt doubles under the master (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/) and copy them with the same FILES.json hash check as damsuite, or have the Screening scripts import the Runner's qt_fakes when the sibling folder is present and fall back otherwise.  
  Effort: medium. Id T1-T-6.
- **A test pins the release string and breaks on every release** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_window_028.py:201`  
  The test reads metadata.txt and asserts the version equals a literal that has to be edited each release (it has gone 0.28.0 to 0.28.3 already). The release number already lives in damsuite/versions.py RELEASE['ndscreen'].  
  What you see: The suite's own test run goes red on the next release until someone edits the test, which teaches people to ignore a red test.  
  Fix: Assert ver == versions.RELEASE['ndscreen'] (one place to bump) and keep the changelog checks; or drop the equality and keep only log.startswith(ver + ':').  
  Effort: small. Id S1-B-14.
- **A test pins the exact release string and will fail on the next release** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_window_028.py:201`  
  test_window_028.py asserts the metadata version equals '0.28.3'. The same check in test_v0260.py:333 was already loosened to 'ver >= "0.26.0"' with the note '0.26.1: no pin', so the suite knows the pattern; this one was missed when 0.28.1-0.28.3 bumped it (the line has been edited for each patch release).  
  What you see: The 0.28.x window test turns red on release 1.23 for no code reason, and someone edits the string again.  
  Fix: Replace with 'assert ver >= "0.28.0" and log.startswith(ver + ":")' as test_v0260.py does. One line.  
  Effort: small. Id S2a-B-8.
- **test_window_028 pins the plugin version to 0.28.3 and breaks on the next release** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_window_028.py:201`  
  The window test asserts the exact version string. The same check in test_v0260.py was deliberately un-pinned ('# 0.26.1: no pin') and test_suite09.py:52 compares against the metadata value. This one pin means release 1.23 fails a window test that has nothing to do with the window.  
  What you see: None for Nick directly; the release script's test run goes red on the first release after this one and the pin has to be bumped by hand.  
  Fix: Change line 201 to assert ver >= "0.28.3" (as test_v0260.py:333), or compare to damsuite.versions.RELEASE["ndscreen"], which the release set already maintains.  
  Effort: small. Id S3a-B-8.
- **A test pins the release number and will fail at the next release** `nd_dam_screening/tests/test_window_028.py:201`  
  The assertion compares metadata.txt's version to the literal '0.28.3'. The next release (0.28.4 or 0.29.0) turns this test red with no defect behind it, and someone edits the literal each time; the project already keeps the number in damsuite/versions.py RELEASE['ndscreen'].  
  What you see: None for the engineer; a false test failure on every release for whoever maintains it.  
  Fix: Replace the literal with `versions.RELEASE["ndscreen"]` (the test then checks that metadata.txt and versions.py agree, which is the useful check) and keep the changelog assertions.  
  Effort: small. Id S3b-B-13.

## Data Updater (73: 5 serious, 28 medium, 40 minor)

### Serious
- **pid_alive uses os.kill(pid, 0), which on Windows terminates the other process instead of asking whether it exists** `nd_data_updater/core.py:107`  
  On Windows, Python's os.kill maps any signal other than CTRL_C_EVENT/CTRL_BREAK_EVENT to TerminateProcess (documented behaviour), so 'signal 0' kills the process whose pid is in the lock file. lock_decision (core.py:153) calls alive(pid) whenever a lock holds another process's pid, which is the designed 'another QGIS is building' case (tests/test_lock.py:94 'refuses while another run holds the lock'). Second half: on Windows a pid that no longer exists raises OSError [WinError 87], not ProcessLookupError, so pid_alive returns True and the 1.4.0 'a killed run no longer blocks later runs' fix does not work there (the lock is refused for six hours). The test test_lock.py:28 `pid_alive(_dead_pid  
  What you see: Two QGIS windows open (two projects), one is in a 90-minute pack build. In the other, Check for Data Updates Now, Fetch again, or the hourly auto check reaches the lock: the first QGIS is killed on the spot, unsaved project edits are lost and the build dies mid-write. And after a real crash the data folder stays locked for six hours on Windows with 'another update is already running'.  
  Fix: In pid_alive, on os.name == 'nt' never call os.kill: use ctypes kernel32.OpenProcess(0x1000 /*PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION*/, False, pid); if it fails with ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER the pid is dead, otherwise GetExitCodeProcess == 259 (STILL_ACTIVE) means alive; close the handle. Keep os.kill(pid, 0) for POSIX only. Add a Windows run of test_lock.py.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-2.
- **_write_folder forces every folder layer back to the work file's copy, overwriting the user's own file and the 3DHP cut from the national file** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1697`  
  At the end of every build, _write_folder walks ALL data sets in the work SQLite (writer.manifest(), not only the ones fetched this run) and rewrites any folder layer whose fetched date or count differs from the work file's copy. The folder's copy differs exactly when it came from somewhere better than the work file: a layer the user replaced with their own file (packsource.replace_layer, flagged replaced=True), the 3DHP flowlines or waterbodies cut from the 12 GB National Map file (_add_3dhp_bulk writes straight to the folder, never to the work file), or an OSM layer added from the Geofabrik files. convert_v1 then calls add_dataset, which replaces the GeoPackage and the manifest entry; the r  
  What you see: The next Updater run after Nick drops in his own county roads or dam file (the 1.4.0 feature) silently puts the old download back and clears the replaced flag, so the layer also returns to the 30-day refresh cycle; the run log says nothing about it. When mode auto switched 3DHP to the National Map file because the service was down, the freshly cut flowlines are replaced by the 30-day-old service copy still in the work file, so the 12 GB download bought nothing and the layer shows the old fetched  
  Fix: Have build() pass _write_folder the set of names it actually fetched in this run (the names _run_one finished), and let _write_folder convert only those plus names whose GeoPackage the folder lacks. Never replace a folder entry that carries replaced=True or whose fetched date is newer than the work file's. With that, the work file is only a resume cache and the folder is the truth.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-1.
- **A failed re-fetch removes a complete folder layer from the manifest when the work file has no usable copy (pack copied from another computer, _work deleted, or layer added from a file)** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1697`  
  PackWriter.fail() records status 'failed' when the work file never held a usable copy of that name (offline_pack.py:639-641). _write_folder then puts the name in todo because its work status is not usable, and convert_v1 pops the folder's manifest entry and lists the layer under left_out, leaving the GeoPackage orphaned on disk. The folder's own copy was complete and is never consulted. Reproduced: a complete 300-feature SARP_dams in the folder plus a 'failed' SARP_dams in the work file ended with SARP_dams gone from the manifest ('failed in the source pack: the service stopped answering') while barriers/SARP_dams.gpkg stayed on disk. The work file lacks a usable copy whenever the pack folde  
  What you see: Nick receives a complete pack from a colleague, clicks Build / update the offline pack in the Hazard Helper (no _work beside it, so every layer is fetched again), one service is down that day, and that layer disappears from the pack as the three plugins see it, though it was fine before. In the Updater, when 3DHP came from the National Map file and a later 30-day refresh goes the web route and fails, the flowlines and waterbodies vanish from the plugins' view; the run reports 'not in the pack: 3  
  Fix: In _write_folder, when the work file's status for a name is not usable and the folder manifest already holds a usable entry whose file exists, leave the folder entry alone and only append the failure note to it (as PackWriter.fail does for the work file). Alternatively pass those names in convert_v1's skip. Also have convert_v1 delete the GeoPackage when it does pop an entry, so no orphan file is   
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-2.
- **The updater's data folder is still a second setting; a suite folder chosen on the Start tab never reaches the updater, and the first quiet run re-points the suite to the updater's default folder** `nd_data_updater/settings.py:26`  
  Settings.data_dir reads only nd_data_updater/data_dir (default ~/Documents/ND Structure Data) and never looks at the suite's ndsuite/data_folder. The sync added in 1.20 is one-way: the updater pushes its folder to the suite (Choose..., the end of every pack run in _share, Import pack), but the suite home's own Suite folder chooser (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/suite_home.py:350) writes ndsuite/data_folder only. The suite home tells a new user to pick the folder there and then open the updater (suite_home.py:80). The updater then still points at its default; auto_check is on by default (settings.py:34) with the pack dataset on by default, so 20 s after QGIS starts a quiet run builds a second pack int  
  What you see: Nick picks his suite folder on the Start tab and drops the DWR exports and DEM into it. The updater silently downloads 10-40 GB for 90 minutes into a different folder, then switches the three plugins to that folder: their dwr/, dem/ and nlcd/ are now the empty ones, and the next classification runs without the DWR files he put in. Nothing on screen says the folder changed.  
  Fix: Make it one setting: Settings.data_dir returns suitedata.data_folder() when it is set (from .ndpack.damsuite import suitedata) and falls back to the old key only for an old profile; the setter goes through plugin.adopt_data_folder. Until a folder has been chosen (no suite folder, no updater folder), auto_check must not start a run; the first run needs a click. Then _share can stop calling adopt_fo  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-1.
- **The updater's data folder and the suite data folder are still two settings** `nd_data_updater/settings.py:26`  
  The updater keeps its own QgsSettings key nd_data_updater/data_dir while the three dam plugins read ndsuite/data_folder. adopt_folder (suitedata.py:127-169) writes only the ndsuite/* keys and nothing in the suite ever writes the updater's key (grep of all four plugins). The two are kept in step only when the folder is chosen through the updater's "Choose…" button (plugin.py:245-248). Three paths break it: (1) the suite home's "Suite folder" chooser in Hazard/Runner/Screening (suite_home.py:329-350) sets the suite folder and leaves the updater on its default; (2) the Settings window's folder field is an editable QLineEdit (settings_dialog.py:73) and Save / "Save and Check All Now" write only   
  What you see: Seen in the configured GUI capture: the suite folder was adopted at .../gui/suite with a pack and a Dams folder, yet the updater's Settings window shows /root/Documents/ND Structure Data and the pack window says "ND_Dam_Suite_Pack - not built yet". From there the auto-check (20 s after start-up, then hourly) builds a second pack in the default folder for 90 minutes and, at the end, adopt_folder silently points the three plugins at that folder, undoing the folder the user chose in the suite home.  
  Fix: Delete the nd_data_updater/data_dir key: make Settings.data_dir return suitedata.data_folder() (DEFAULT_DATA_DIR only when the suite has none) and its setter call SD.adopt_folder, so there is one store with one writer; make the Settings window's folder field read-only like the pack window's (pack_dialog.py:153) so "Choose…" is the only way in; drop plugin.adopt_data_folder's double write (lines 24  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-B-1.

### Medium
- **Every downloaded zip is decompressed twice, with Cancel and the progress bar dead throughout** `nd_data_updater/core.py:173`  
  zf.testzip() decompresses every entry in the archive to compare its CRC, and then the loop below decompresses the same entries again to write them out. No cancellation is checked and no progress is reported during testzip at all, and the extraction loop checks cancellation but never calls feedback.fraction, so the percentage does not move either. (The Geofabrik archive has already been verified against its published MD5 at core.py:262-264, so the CRC pass adds nothing for it.)  
  What you see: On the statewide GeoPackage and USA Structures archives the unzip takes about twice as long as it needs to, and for the whole of it the window shows a frozen percentage and the Stop button does nothing, which is exactly the "the bar sat at 3% for forty minutes" complaint release 1.4.0 set out to fix.  
  Fix: Drop the testzip() pass (a corrupt entry already raises BadZipFile or a CRC error on extraction, which the caller reports) and report progress inside the loop: feedback.fraction((i + 1) / len(members)) beside the existing check_cancelled, plus a feedback.status line naming the archive.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-BV-3.
- **On Windows the staging swap fails whenever a downloaded layer is loaded in the project, and the verified download is thrown away every time** `nd_data_updater/core.py:692`  
  _swap replaces a dataset by renaming its whole folder. On Windows a folder cannot be renamed while any file in it is open; QGIS keeps GeoPackages open through SQLite without FILE_SHARE_DELETE. 'Add North Dakota Layers to Project' (plugin.py:489) loads exactly these files (osm/*.gpkg buildings, microsoft_footprints/*.geojson, nsi/nsi_nd.gpkg). The PermissionError is caught by the broad except, recorded as an error, and the fully downloaded and verified staging folder is deleted in finally, so the next check downloads the same hundreds of MB again.  
  What you see: With the OSM buildings or Microsoft footprints layer in the open project, every check on Windows ends with 'OpenStreetMap (Geofabrik): [WinError 5] Access is denied: ...' in the bar and the Status note, re-downloads the file each day (Geofabrik rebuilds it daily), and the layer never updates. _reload_layers (plugin.py:470) runs only after a successful swap, so it never helps.  
  Fix: Catch PermissionError in _update_one around _swap: keep the verified staging folder as <target>.pending instead of deleting it, and say plainly 'remove the layer <name> from the project (or close the project) and press Check again; the download is kept'. On the next run, install a .pending folder before anything else. Alternatively, before starting the task, on the GUI thread, point project layers  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-A-3.
- **QgisHttp.download holds the whole file in memory (two to three copies) before writing it** `nd_data_updater/http_qgis.py:84`  
  QgsBlockingNetworkRequest buffers the entire reply in memory; reply.content() is that QByteArray and content.data() copies it again into Python bytes. This path serves every HttpFileSource and OsmLayersSource download: the Geofabrik GeoPackage zip and the .osm.pbf (hundreds of MB each), the Microsoft zip, and the user-supplied USA Structures zip, which can exceed 1 GB. There is no size cap and nothing reaches disk until the end. The plugin already has a streaming, cancellable client that honours the QGIS proxy (http_urllib.py:52, 1 MB chunks through netproxy) but plugin.py only ever constructs QgisHttp.  
  What you see: During each of these downloads QGIS's memory grows by two to three times the file size; a large USA Structures zip on a laptop can push QGIS into swapping or out of memory, and a run that stops in the middle leaves nothing to resume from.  
  Fix: Stream to disk: in QgisHttp.download use QgsNetworkAccessManager with readyRead writing chunks to dest (checking qgs_feedback.isCanceled()), or delegate download() to UrllibHttp.download (netproxy.urlopen already applies the QGIS proxy) and keep QgsBlockingNetworkRequest for head() and get_bytes(). Add a sanity cap from Content-Length against free disk space.  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-A-4.
- **Every download through the QGIS client is held whole in memory and cannot resume** `nd_data_updater/http_qgis.py:86`  
  QgsBlockingNetworkRequest buffers the whole reply, so `reply.content()` is the entire file in memory and `content.data()` makes a second Python copy of it before a single byte is written to disk. This is the client every HttpFileSource and the OpenStreetMap pack-layers source use: the Geofabrik GeoPackage zip, the 160 MB North Dakota .osm.pbf, the Microsoft footprints zip and whatever the user pastes as the FEMA USA Structures link. There is no Range or resume here (packsource.download_resumable, which does both, is used only for the 3DHP national file).  
  What you see: A multi-hundred-megabyte dataset needs roughly twice its size in free memory inside QGIS while it downloads, and because nothing is written until the transfer ends, a connection that drops at 95 per cent throws the whole download away: the next run starts again from zero. On the office connection this is the difference between a run that finishes and one that never does.  
  Fix: Stream the reply to the destination instead of buffering it: use a QNetworkAccessManager reply with readyRead writing to the open file (or reuse packsource.download_resumable through the QGIS proxy settings for these sources too), so the file is written as it arrives and a stopped download continues with a Range request like the 3DHP one already does.  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-BV-2.
- **A truncated HTTP response (http.client.IncompleteRead) is not in the retry net and aborts the whole build** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:158`  
  http.client.IncompleteRead (a response body cut off mid-transfer, which r.read() raises on a flaky ArcGIS or Overpass server) and the other http.client.HTTPException classes (BadStatusLine, LineTooLong) derive from Exception, not OSError (checked: issubclass is False), so http_json does not retry them. The exception leaves the worker, _pool re-raises it (only _NET_ERRORS become a result), sets the global _CANCEL flag, _run_one marks the data set 'interrupted', and build() re-raises because its loop catches only _NET_ERRORS. The finally block closes the writer and the function exits before _write_folder, so nothing fetched in this run reaches the pack folder.  
  What you see: One cut-off answer during a 90-minute run ends it. The Updater's update() catches only BuildError / BuildCancelled, so core logs the source as failed with the raw text 'IncompleteRead(123456 bytes read, 7890 more expected)'; the Hazard window shows 'Offline pack FAILED: IncompleteRead(...)'. The layers finished in that run sit in _work and only reach the pack folder when a later run completes; Nick has to notice and start again.  
  Fix: Add http.client.HTTPException to _NET_ERRORS (import http.client), so a truncated answer is retried like a timeout. Consider also catching Exception in build()'s per-data-set loop so one unexpected error fails that layer and the run goes on to the next, as the 'one bad source must not stop the rest' rule in the Updater core intends.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-3.
- **An NSI or Overpass outage has no "the service is down" give-up, so a failing tile is split into 20 more requests and the run crawls for hours instead of failing the layer** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:945`  
  _nsi_tile answers a failed tile by splitting it into four, and each of those into four again, so one dead tile costs 5 + 4 x 5 + 16 x 5 = 105 requests (RETRIES is 5). North Dakota is about 608 NSI tiles at the 0.2 degree size, so a service that is down means roughly 64,000 requests, each waiting the 4, 8, 12 and 16 second back-off (or the 30 to 120 second shared pause on HTTP 429 / 503). The ArcGIS paths guard against exactly this with the _Health counter (OUTAGE_FAILS, "too many = the service is down, and halving chunks would only multiply the failing requests", lines 130-151), but fetch_nsi and fetch_osm create no _Health, and the 5 percent failure test (line 970) is only reached after eve  
  What you see: If the NSI API is down or rate-limiting on the day Nick runs the update, the structures step does not fail in a few minutes; it inches along ("3 of 608 tiles") for many hours while the window says it is waiting on the service, and he has to work out that it is hopeless and press Cancel. Overpass behaves the same way with its 8 state-sized areas and only 2 workers.  
  Fix: Give fetch_nsi and fetch_osm the same shared _Health that fetch_arcgis uses: pass one into _nsi_tile / _osm_tile, count the failures, and once it reaches the limit stop splitting and fail the layer with "the service stopped answering", the way fetch_arcgis does at line 859.  
  Effort: medium. Id U2-AV-4.
- **fetch_nsi leaves a structures layer with unread tiles marked 'complete' (gaps only), so the Updater reports it fine and never retries it** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:978`  
  When up to 5 percent of the NSI tiles fail (each tile is 0.2 degree, roughly 15 x 22 km), fetch_nsi records the holes in gaps but leaves ctx.status at 'complete'; the 1.19 fetch_arcgis_tiles sets STATUS_PARTIAL in the same situation. The manifest entry is therefore status complete with a gaps list. The Updater's plan() marks a layer stale only by status ('not complete') or age, its end-of-run message lists bad layers by status, and runreport never looks at gaps (no mention of gaps anywhere in nd_data_updater). The Hazard window does check gaps (data_tab.py:476-477).  
  What you see: After an NSI run with a few failed tiles, the Updater says '29 layers' with nothing wrong and leaves the holes in place for the whole 30-day refresh interval; LAYERS.txt and the layer table show NSI as complete. The plugins' covers() does honour gaps, so a dam in a hole is told the pack does not cover it and the online service is tried instead, which defeats the point of the pack and surprises an offline user at dam time rather than at build time.  
  Fix: Set ctx.status = STATUS_PARTIAL next to ctx.gaps in fetch_nsi (one line), as fetch_arcgis_tiles does; the Updater then retries it on the next run and reports it as not complete.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-4.
- **The DWR dams and DWR dam lines can be in the download list twice, and the web copy then overwrites the user's DWR export in the same run** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1096`  
  datasets() adds the DWR layers twice under the same name when the user has picked a server for the dwr_dams or dwr_dam_lines role in Plugins > ND Dam Suite > Suite data and servers: once from file_datasets() as a "file" layer (the export the user drops in <data folder>/dwr) and once from suite_datasets() as an ordinary web download. build() then splits them, sends the file one to _add_layer_files (which writes the user's export into the pack folder) and the web one through the normal download, and _write_folder rewrites the folder's copy from the freshly downloaded one, because its date differs. The only built-in choice for that role is the ND GIS Hub HSIP dams layer, whose own entry in dams
