  What you see: Nick picks a DWR dams server in Suite data and servers, which looks like the right thing to do, and from then on every pack build quietly replaces his DWR inventory export with the HSIP copy under the same layer name. The Screening and Breach Runner plugins then read the HSIP dams believing they are the DWR inventory, and the run log mentions both the file and the download without saying one won.  
  Fix: Leave out of suite_datasets() any name that file_datasets() already returns (the DWR ones), or, better, give the suite-chosen version a different layer name so it cannot overwrite the file-based layer. A one-line guard in datasets() that drops later duplicates of a name would also catch any future clash.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-AV-1.
- **When a DWR dams server is picked in Suite data and servers, the pack lists DWR_dams twice and the service download overwrites the DWR export file** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1096`  
  datasets() can hold two entries with the SAME pack name "DWR_dams" (and "DWR_dam_lines"): one of kind "file" from file_datasets(), one of kind "arcgis" from suite_datasets() as soon as the user has chosen a DWR server in Plugins > ND Dam Suite > Suite data and servers. I confirmed this by running suite_datasets() with that choice set: it returns DWR_dams pointing at the ND GIS Hub HSIP dams service. build() then puts the file one in file_todo and the service one in todo: _add_layer_files writes the DWR export into the pack first (l.1255-1257), the service version is downloaded into the working file, and _write_folder / gpkgpack.convert_v1 writes it over the pack's DWR_dams entry because its   
  What you see: Nick puts the DWR inventory export in the data folder's dwr/ folder, the log says "found DWR_dams: <his export>", and the finished pack holds the ND GIS Hub HSIP copy instead - a different vintage with fewer fields (the code's own comment at l.1157-1159 says the built-in DWR choice is the HSIP copy, not DWR's inventory). Screening and the Breach Runner then read the HSIP dams from the pack and nothing in the log says the export was replaced.  
  Fix: In build(), after chosen is computed, drop any non-file entry whose name is already in file_todo (one line: todo = [t for t in todo if t[0] not in {x[0] for x in file_todo}]), and log "DWR_dams: the file in dwr/ is used, the chosen server is not downloaded". Alternatively give suite_datasets' DWR entries their own names (DWR_dams_service) so they cannot land on the file layer.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-BV-1.
- **Building from the command line with --sources throws away the servers chosen in Suite data and servers** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1232`  
  constants.py applies the data-source file and THEN the suite's chosen servers when it loads (l.616-617). build() calls apply_source_overrides again, and that function starts with _restore_source_defaults(), which puts every address back to the built-in one and clears SOURCE_OVERRIDES; apply_suite_services is never called again, so the suite's choices are gone. I checked this by running it: after a second apply_source_overrides call an address that had been set to a chosen server was back to the built-in address and the "suite:" entry had vanished from SOURCE_OVERRIDES.  
  What you see: `python build_nd_pack.py --sources <file>` quietly ignores the servers Nick chose in Suite data and servers and downloads from the built-in addresses instead, while the same command without --sources honours them. The pack's LAYERS.txt then names a source he did not pick, and if he chose a server because the built-in one is dead, the build fails for no visible reason.  
  Fix: Call C.apply_suite_services() right after C.apply_source_overrides(sources_file) in build() (two lines), so the order matches what apply_suite_services documents; or move the --sources handling into main() and leave build() alone.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-BV-2.
- **The 3DHP setting "Web service only" has no effect once the 12 GB National Map file has been downloaded: the builder always looks in the data folder's 3dhp folder itself** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1557`  
  _add_3dhp_bulk does not only search the folders the caller handed it; folders_3dhp always appends the suite data folder's 3dhp sub-folder, and scan_3dhp walks every folder three levels deep for any .zip, .gdb or .gpkg whose name contains "3dhp". The Updater's 3DHP mode is implemented by deciding whether to add that folder to the files list (packsource.py:467-494: mode "service" returns None, and mode "auto" returns None when route_3dhp says the service answers), so the decision is thrown away: the file is found anyway and the flowlines and waterbodies are cut out of it instead of being downloaded.  
  What you see: Nick downloads the 12 GB national file once (or lets "auto" do it), later sets the 3DHP mode to "Web service only" because he wants current data from USGS, and the run keeps cutting the flowlines and waterbodies out of the old file. In "auto" he also pays up to three minutes of probing (the 1.4.0 feature) for a decision that changes nothing. The log line "3DHP national file found ... cut out of it" is the only clue, and the layer's date is the file's date, not today's.  
  Fix: Have _add_3dhp_bulk search only the folders the caller passed in `files` (keep the suite data folder's 3dhp folder as a default only when `files` is empty, as the command-line use needs), so the Updater's mode setting decides. Alternatively give build() an explicit flag such as use_3dhp_file=True/False/auto and let packsource set it.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-AV-2.
- **A failed land-cover step leaves no trace in the manifest, so the end-of-run block says every layer is in the pack** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1616`  
  When the MRLC service fails (a land-cover tile that never answers, so _fetch_nlcd raises), _add_rasters records it only in the in-run `failures` list and the log. No "rasters" entry and no `left_out` entry is written, unlike the two cases above it, so the manifest looks as if the land cover was never part of the build. runreport.layer_rows (runreport.py:188-208) builds its table only from datasets, rasters and left_out, so the land cover has no row at all, and pack_summary_lines (runreport.py:278-280) prints "Every layer the plugins read is in the pack.". The builder does put the failure in manifest["_failures"] (line 1319), but I grepped the whole Updater and nothing reads that key.  
  What you see: After a run in which the land cover failed, the Updater's end-of-run block tells Nick that every layer the plugins read is in the pack, and the pack window's layer table has no land-cover row to say otherwise. The Screening and Breach Runner plugins then fall back to their default Manning's n instead of the real land cover, and nothing in the run report points at the cause.  
  Fix: In the except at line 1616 also set left_out[name] = str(e) (the two cases just above already do this), so the manifest, LAYERS.txt, the layer table and the end-of-run block all name the missing land cover. Having packsource.update mention man["_failures"] in its message would catch the general case.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-AV-3.
- **The Updater ships Hazard's whole constants.py (640 lines) for 32 names, and the copy reads a metadata.txt that is not there** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:29`  
  The builder uses 27 names from constants.py (service addresses, DOWNLOAD_WORKERS/OVERPASS_WORKERS/DOWNLOAD_TILE_DEG, HTTP_USER_AGENT, PLUGIN_VERSION, lifeline_downloads, apply_source_overrides, SOURCE_OVERRIDES) and osm_parse.py 5 (the OSM tag lists). To get them the Updater carries the entire file: about 300 of its 640 lines are Hazard-only (ACER TM 11 curves and TableCurve l.115-236, layer-tree group names and button styles l.32-83, block sizes, ND policy thresholds l.239-275, map-book scales, aerial imagery, ADDRESS_SNAP_FT), plus an import of damsuite.tm11_curves and a README.txt read at import. Beside nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py there is no metadata.txt and no README.txt, so in   
  What you see: Every change to a Hazard-only constant forces a re-sync of the Updater copy or the two drift; the Updater's pack build tells every service it is "ND Dam Hazard Helper 2.28.0" (see U2-B-2); whoever opens the Updater to check how the pack is built meets 300 lines of hazard-classification coefficients that the Updater never uses.  
  Fix: Move the pack-source block into one shared module damsuite/pack_sources.py: the addresses (NSI_API_URL ... DWR_DAMS_URL, LIFELINE_SOURCES, lifeline_downloads), the OSM tag lists (OSM_LIFELINE_TAGS, OSM_CROSSING_QUERIES, OSM_CAMP_TAGS, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT, OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT), DOWNLOAD_* and HTTP_USER_AGENT, and SOURCE_KEYS with apply_source_overrides / apply_suite_services / write_sources_file (  
  Effort: medium. Id U2-B-1.
- **The Overpass filter for mobile-home parks misses names written with a hyphen or underscore, although the matching rule beside it was written to allow them** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/osm_parse.py:156`  
  The query asks the Overpass server for named residential areas whose raw name contains one of four words, with a space in "mobile home". The local rule, is_trailer_park, first replaces underscores, hyphens, slashes, dots and commas with spaces (_norm_name) and only then matches - so it was written for names like "Mobile-Home Park" or "Sunset_Mobile_Home_Park". Those names never pass the server-side filter, so the local rule never sees them. The Updater's own file route does not have this gap: osm_layers.py reads every feature out of the Geofabrik extract and applies is_trailer_park locally (osm_layers.py:311).  
  What you see: A mobile-home park whose OpenStreetMap name is written with a hyphen or underscore (and that is not tagged residential=trailer_park) is absent from the pack's OSM_trailer_parks layer when the layer comes from Overpass, and present when it comes from the Updater's extract files. Two packs built the same week can list a different set of parks, and the EAP notification list for a dam below such a park is short by one site with nothing in the log to say so.  
  Fix: Make the server-side filter tolerate the separators the local rule removes: _TRAILER_NAME_PREFILTER = "mobile[ _-]?homes?|trailer|manufactured|mhp" (Overpass takes POSIX regex, and is_trailer_park still does the exact check, so nothing extra reaches the layer).  
  Effort: small. Id U2-BV-3.
- **osm_parse.py has no test anywhere; the builder's throttling, resume, tile, NSI, Overpass, file, raster and CLI routes have none; the Updater's tests fake the builder** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/osm_parse.py:194`  
  No test in any plugin calls overpass_to_features, crossing_features, camp_features, trailer_park_features (with the _join_rings / _in_ring multipolygon assembly), place_features, is_trailer_park or the query builders (339 lines). In build_nd_pack.py no test touches NetSession (acquire/release/backoff/beat), _retry_after_s, _pool, the chunk resume (_resume_ids, _resume_tiles, _fetch_arcgis_pages), fetch_arcgis_tiles (the 0.25-degree 3DHP route added in 1.19), fetch_nsi, fetch_osm, _run_one, _add_layer_files, _add_rasters/_fetch_nlcd, _write_zip or main(). The Hazard tests do cover packfiles well (classify, scan, add_files, scan_3dhp, add_3dhp, folders_for, missing_text) and 14 builder functio  
  What you see: A regression in how a mobile-home park's multipolygon is assembled, or in the throttling that halves the workers on HTTP 429, would reach Nick inside an hour-long pack build instead of in the test suite.  
  Fix: Add one osm_parse test with a small canned Overpass answer per layer (node, closed way, open way, multipolygon with an inner ring; expected polygons and categories) and one NetSession test with a fake clock (429 halves the limit, Retry-After honoured, RECOVER_AFTER successes give one back, cancel wakes the waiters). In the Updater run the Hazard builder tests against ndpack (import test_v2290 / te  
  Effort: medium. Id U2-B-13.
- **No test for the default NSI merge, the download sources, the staging swap or build_sources** `nd_data_updater/nsi_merge.py:128`  
  The 75 tests cover the 1.4.0 additions (lock, 3DHP cap, run report, USB copy, service check, build plan, Qt6 spelling). Nothing tests nsi_merge.match_points / merge_versions (the default NSI dataset since 1.1.0), HttpFileSource / NsiApiSource / OsmLayersSource / GitHubRepoSource, Updater._update_one and _swap (the "never leaves half-written data" promise), extract_zip, sources.build_sources, settings.source_config, http_qgis or osm_layers.extract / _classify (Hazard's test_osm_layers.py only feeds hand-made GeoPackages to the builder). The one version-pinning test is test_qt6.py:52 (see U1-B-2).  
  What you see: A regression in match_points (the 15 m category penalty, the grid cell size) changes how many 2022-only houses are kept in the merged inventory and so the structure count behind a hazard class, and nothing catches it; a mistake in _swap could delete a working local copy on a failed download with no test to notice.  
  Fix: Add tests/test_nsi_merge.py (fold within 30 m, keep a 2022-only point with nsi_version 2022, mobile flag, the renamed field, the one-schema guarantee) and tests/test_core.py (Updater._update_one with a fake http: current / updated / offline keeps the local copy / _swap restores .previous on failure; build_sources from Settings-shaped config). About 120 lines.  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-B-9.
- **osm_layers.py retypes the Hazard tag rules the plugin already ships byte-identical under ndpack/** `nd_data_updater/osm_layers.py:24`  
  ndpack/constants.py and ndpack/osm_parse.py are byte-identical copies of the Hazard master (cmp), import nothing from qgis, and already define OSM_LIFELINE_TAGS, OSM_CAMP_TAGS, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT, TRAILER_PARK_CATEGORY, TRAILER_PARK_NAME, _DEAD_RAIL and _circle_ll. osm_layers.py lines 27-49 and 139-145 retype all of them by hand and pin the copy to "2.29.0" (Hazard is 2.30.3); they match today (checked line by line) but nothing checks the next change. Line 16 hand-computes ND_BBOX as offline_pack.ND_BBOX ± DEFAULT_PAD_DEG (-104.0489-0.1 etc.), and the docstring (line 7) points at a stand-alone nd_osm_download.py that is not in the repo.  
  What you see: When the Hazard Helper changes a lifeline or campground tag (as 2.5 did with schools and child care), the pack's OSM_lifelines layer built here keeps the old rule, so the pack route and Hazard's own Overpass route count different facilities for the same dam, and nothing says so.  
  Fix: from .ndpack.constants import OSM_LIFELINE_TAGS, OSM_CAMP_TAGS, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT; from .ndpack.osm_parse import TRAILER_PARK_NAME, TRAILER_PARK_CATEGORY, _DEAD_RAIL, _circle_ll; ND_BBOX from ndpack.damsuite.offline_pack padded by its DEFAULT_PAD_DEG. Delete lines 16, 27-49, 139-145 and the stale docstring reference. Saves ~35 lines and the drift.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-6.
- **Two windows and six menu entries offer the same actions two to four times; the Set-up tab repeats the suite home** `nd_data_updater/pack_dialog.py:169`  
  GUI report: the Settings window is 674x858 with 28 controls (8 buttons); the pack window has 3 tabs; the menu has 6 entries. The same action appears in several places: the data folder chooser in both windows (one editable field, one read-only, see U1-B-1); "Rebuild only missing layers" in both windows; "Stop the update" in both windows plus a menu entry plus the QGIS task bar; "Open Data Folder" as a menu entry, a Settings button, a Pack "Open" button and ten "Open folder" buttons; "Check for Data Updates Now" (menu, toolbar) beside "Save and Check All Now". The Set-up tab (_setup_tab, _fill_ready, _fill_folders, show_setup_text) draws SC.check / step_rows / Open folder / text_report exactly  
  What you see: A novice meets the same button in three windows and two folder fields that can disagree; the Set-up tab repeats what the suite home front door (built in 1.20) already says, with a next step pointing back at the window he is in.  
  Fix: One window with three tabs (Settings | Pack layers | Services): delete the Set-up tab (~75 lines of pack_dialog) because the suite home owns that; keep one Stop button and one Open button; drop the Settings window's "Rebuild Only Missing Layers" and "Offline Pack…" buttons; drop the "Check when this window opens" checkbox and setting (keep "Check now"); menu down to Check now, Add layers, the wind  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-B-8.
- **Import pack and Replace with my file are not blocked while a build is running; an import renames and deletes the pack folder under the running builder** `nd_data_updater/pack_dialog.py:551`  
  fetch_again and rebuild_missing refuse to start while plugin.is_running(); import_pack and replace_layer do not. import_pack ends in packmove._install, which renames the live pack folder to .previous, puts the imported copy in its place and then deletes .previous (packmove.py:203) while the background PackSource.update may be writing layers and, at the end, the manifest into that path. replace_layer writes a GeoPackage and the manifest that the same run may be rebuilding. In addition _file_job pumps QCoreApplication.processEvents() (pack_dialog.py:494) for the whole copy, so the plugin's hourly auto_check timer can start a build in the middle of an import.  
  What you see: On a Mac or Linux the running build keeps writing into the moved folder and then into the freshly imported pack, so the imported pack ends with layers of two vintages and a manifest that does not match its files; the half-built pack is deleted. On Windows the rename fails and the import is refused with a raw OSError. A file put in with Replace with my file during a refresh of that layer is overwritten minutes later without a word.  
  Fix: Add the same guard to import_pack and replace_layer: `if self.plugin.is_running(): self._info('An update is already running; wait for it or stop it first.'); return`. Set a plugin flag (self.file_job_open) around _file_job and make auto_check and start_update return while it is set.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-6.
- **Copy pack to USB hashes 10-40 GB back from the stick on the GUI thread with no progress and no Cancel** `nd_data_updater/packmove.py:225`  
  The copy phase reports progress and pumps events through _file_job, but the checksum phase that follows reads every copied byte again from the USB stick through sha256_file with no progress callback, so no processEvents() runs and the Cancel button is never consulted. At USB stick speeds this is minutes to tens of minutes for a 30 GB pack.  
  What you see: The progress dialog freezes at 100% with 'Copying the pack to ...', QGIS goes 'Not responding', Cancel does nothing, and the user cannot tell whether it hung. Pulling the stick at that point leaves a .incoming folder on it and no checksum file.  
  Fix: Call checksums(incoming, progress, cancelled) with a second label ('Checking the copy...'), or compute the sha256 while copying (read the source in chunks, write and hash the same chunk) so the data is read once; verifying the written copy can then be a sampled read-back or left to the import.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-7.
- **Release number hard-coded in four places, three different User-Agent versions, one test pins the version** `nd_data_updater/packsource.py:83`  
  The version lives in metadata.txt:8, packsource.UPDATER_VERSION, damsuite/versions.py RELEASE["ndupdater"] and the test literal; the user agent is typed three times with three numbers. servicecheck is already stale at 1.4.0 and the two HTTP clients at 1.0 since the first release, which shows the hand-bump is missed. The Hazard Helper solved this in 2.28 with constants._version_from_metadata (15 lines) reading metadata.txt once.  
  What you see: packsource.load_builder stamps the pack manifest's "created by" with UPDATER_VERSION (line 90); a release that bumps metadata.txt but not packsource.py leaves every pack built afterwards claiming the old updater version, which the change report and anyone debugging a pack then trust. test_qt6 fails at the next release until someone edits the literal.  
  Fix: One PLUGIN_VERSION read from metadata.txt (copy the Hazard _version_from_metadata, or use ndpack.damsuite.versions.RELEASE["ndupdater"]) and one USER_AGENT in core.py imported by http_qgis, http_urllib and servicecheck; change test_qt6.py:52 to compare the metadata version with versions.RELEASE["ndupdater"] instead of a literal. Removes three constants and one hand-edit per release.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-2.
- **Stop promises the finished work is kept, but for every dataset except the pack it is deleted** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:293`  
  Only the offline pack is built in place and resumes (PackSource.in_place, core._update_in_place, whose own cancel message correctly says "the finished layers are kept"). Every other source downloads into .staging, and when the user cancels, the finally clause removes that source's staging folder and check_and_update then removes the whole .staging tree. Nothing was partially kept and nothing resumes, yet the message bar line and the Stop button's tooltip say the opposite without qualification.  
  What you see: A user who stops a run twenty minutes into the 53-county NSI download, or most of the way through the statewide OpenStreetMap extract, is told the work so far is kept and that the next run continues from there. It is not: the next run downloads all of it again from the beginning, and he has no way to know that from anything the plugin said.  
  Fix: Say what is true: make the Stop message and the tooltip read "the offline pack keeps the layers it finished; a download that was in progress starts again next time", or make the message depend on whether the running source is the pack (plugin.task_status already knows which source is running).  
  Effort: small. Id U1-BV-4.
- **When the pack cannot be refreshed because there is no internet, the message bar says the pack "has not been downloaded yet", even when a full pack is sitting in the folder** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:400`  
  The code decides "this dataset has never been downloaded" by looking for the exact words "using local copy" at the start of the message. The offline pack writes a different wording, "using the local copy", so the pack is always put in the never-downloaded list whenever a check runs without internet.  
  What you see: A user with a complete 30 GB pack opens QGIS on a laptop with no connection and gets the warning "No internet connection, and these datasets have not been downloaded yet: Offline pack for the ND dam plugins", while the end-of-run block in the same window says the local copy is still being used. One of the two is wrong and the user cannot tell which.  
  Fix: Stop matching on message text. Have the engine mark a result that fell back to local data (for example a flag on Result set in both core.py:640 and core.py:675), and have plugin.py:400 read that flag.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-AV-1.
- **With the pack dataset turned off, the pack buttons do nothing and report that all data is up to date** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:424`  
  "Rebuild only missing layers" (both copies of it), "Fetch again" on a layer row and "Replace with my file" all start a run with only={"pack"}. If the "Offline pack" checkbox in the settings window is unticked, build_sources never makes a pack source, so the filter at core.py:572 selects nothing and the run returns an empty result list. The guard at plugin.py:341 does not catch this because it asks whether *any* dataset is enabled, not whether the ones named in `only` are. The buttons stay enabled whatever the checkbox says.  
  What you see: The user unticks the offline pack (or has it off from an earlier session), presses "Rebuild Only Missing Layers", and gets a green "All North Dakota datasets are up to date" plus a pack summary, while nothing at all was built and nothing says the pack is switched off. He has no way to tell the difference between "there was nothing to do" and "this button is doing nothing".  
  Fix: In start_update, after building the sources, check that the ids in `only` are among them and otherwise push a warning naming the setting ("The offline pack is turned off in Settings and Status; turn it on first"), and grey out the pack buttons in both windows when settings.enabled("pack") is false.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-BV-5.
- **The "small question" the service check asks the NSI is a whole county of structures** `nd_data_updater/servicecheck.py:46`  
  There is no HEAD request and no Range header anywhere in this module: kind "head" only means "do not parse the answer as JSON" (the `if kind == "json"` at line 106 is the only place kind is read). So the NSI probe is a plain GET of the full structure inventory of county 38015, of which the first 64 KB is read and the rest hung up on, under a 6-second timeout (TIMEOUT, line 34) and a 2.5-second slow threshold. The server still has to build the whole county answer before the first bytes arrive.  
  What you see: The Services tab, and the warning line it pushes into the QGIS message bar, can say the NSI is "slow" or "down" when the API is working normally and merely takes more than six seconds to assemble a county. That is the one service every hazard class is counted from, so the user is warned off a run, or told to expect a failure, on the strength of a probe that asked the wrong question.  
  Fix: Point the nsi row at a metadata address, or keep the county query but add a row limit and a Range header and give it the longer timeout the 3DHP statewide probe effectively gets; and either make kind "head" issue a real HEAD (urllib.request.Request(url, method="HEAD")) or rename it, so the kind no longer promises something the code does not do. Correct the module text at line 14 either way.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-BV-1.
- **A data folder typed into the Settings box and saved is not adopted by the dam plugins; Choose... applies before Save and Cancel does not undo it** `nd_data_updater/settings_dialog.py:266`  
  Two code paths set the same folder differently. Choose... calls plugin.adopt_data_folder immediately (writes the updater key and the suite settings, creates sub-folders) before the user presses Save, so Cancel leaves the change in place. Typing or pasting a path into the editable QLineEdit and pressing Save writes only nd_data_updater/data_dir; the three dam plugins keep their old folder, which is the drift 1.20 set out to remove. The button tooltip ('One choice: ... the three dam plugins all follow this folder') is true for one path and false for the other. The pack window avoids this by making its box read-only (pack_dialog.py:153).  
  What you see: A user who pastes a network path into the box and saves gets the updater building in one folder and the plugins reading another, with no message; a user who presses Choose... then Cancel has changed the suite's folder anyway.  
  Fix: In save(), when the box text differs from s.data_dir, route it through self.plugin.adopt_data_folder(text) and show the returned note; or make the box read-only like the pack window and let only Choose... set it. Make _browse only fill the box and apply on Save.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-5.
- **With the default settings the whole North Dakota structure inventory is downloaded twice, and the NSI version and match-distance settings never reach the copy the dam plugins actually read** `nd_data_updater/sources.py:56`  
  Both datasets are on by default and both download all 53 counties of the NSI, into <data folder>/nsi and into the pack. The pack's copy, which is the one the Hazard Helper, Runner and Screening read, uses the unversioned API address and ignores the "NSI version" and "Same structure within" settings in the Settings window; those two controls only change the separate copy in nsi/, which nothing but "Add North Dakota Layers to Project" ever opens.  
  What you see: Every full run spends the download time and the disk space for the state's structures twice. And a user who sets "NSI version: 2022 only" or changes the match distance sees no change at all in the structures a hazard class is counted from, because the pack's layer was fetched without those choices.  
  Fix: Make one of the two the real one. Either drop the standalone nsi dataset and point "Add North Dakota Layers" at the pack's NSI layer, or pass the NSI mode and match distance into the pack build so the pack's NSI_structures is the merged layer the settings describe. Until then the Settings window should not offer the two NSI choices as if they affected the plugins.  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-AV-2.
- **Ticking "USA Structures" without pasting a link does nothing and nothing says so** `nd_data_updater/sources.py:78`  
  The dataset is dropped from the run when the link box is empty, and no code path tells the user. The run reports success, the status row shows "not downloaded", and the only hint is the tooltip on the tick box.  
  What you see: A user ticks USA Structures, presses Save and Check All Now, is told the datasets are up to date, and never gets the building outlines. Nothing on screen connects that to the empty link box.  
  Fix: When the tick is on and the link is empty, say so once where the user is: a line under the tick box in the Settings window, and a warning from start_update ("USA Structures is turned on but has no download link").  
  Effort: small. Id U1-AV-5.

### Minor
- **extract_zip reads every archive twice (testzip then extract) and Stop is not checked during the first pass** `nd_data_updater/core.py:173`  
  testzip() decompresses and CRC-checks every member, then extract() decompresses them again; zipfile already raises on a CRC mismatch while extracting, so the first pass only doubles the time for the Geofabrik GeoPackage zip and the Microsoft zip (hundreds of MB) and runs with no cancel check.  
  What you see: A Stop pressed while the OSM zip is being verified waits until the whole archive has been read once more before the run stops; each OSM update takes roughly twice as long to unpack as it needs to.  
  Fix: Drop the testzip() pass (keep the path check and the per-member cancel check); a corrupt member still raises zipfile.BadZipFile during extract, which the except already turns into UpdateError.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-13.
- **The OSM pack layers source resets the run's progress range, so the task bar jumps back to 0% mid-run** `nd_data_updater/core.py:347`  
  Feedback.set_range takes absolute percentages of the whole run. OsmLayersSource.fetch overwrites the slice it was given with 0-30 and 30-95, so while it runs the QgsTask progress is reported as if this source were the whole run; the next source's set_range snaps it back.  
  What you see: During a check the bar in the task manager goes from, say, 60% back to 0%, climbs to 95%, then drops to 80% ('did it start over?'). The status text is right, the bar is not.  
  Fix: Scale inside the current slice: compute the sub-range from the current base and span (add Feedback.sub_range(lo, hi) that sets base+span*lo .. base+span*hi, and restore after), or report fraction(0.3 * f) during the download and fraction(0.3 + 0.65 * f) during the build.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-8.
- **Fetch again and Rebuild only missing layers do nothing, then say 'All North Dakota datasets are up to date', when the Offline pack dataset is unticked** `nd_data_updater/core.py:572`  
  The pack window's buttons call start_update(only={'pack'}), but the source list is filtered by the enabled flags first; with 'Offline pack for the ND dam plugins' unticked (a user who turned off the automatic refresh but keeps the pack) the run has no sources, returns [] and the success message is shown. pack_dialog has just said 'Fetching <layer> again'.  
  What you see: Pressing Fetch again on a layer reports success in a few seconds and the layer is unchanged; nothing explains why.  
  Fix: When `only` names a dataset, build it regardless of its enabled flag (pass enabled overrides for the ids in `only` into _config), or have start_update warn 'turn on the Offline pack dataset in Settings first' when only names a disabled source.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-12.
- **Updater._update_one / _update_in_place and packmove.copy_pack / import_pack repeat their tails** `nd_data_updater/core.py:649`  
  The two update paths share about 20 lines of error handling and manifest bookkeeping; packmove.copy_pack (216-232) and import_pack (259-274) share the dest / .incoming / _install / rmtree-on-failure block (about 15 lines). A fix to one copy (for example recording last_error) has to be made twice.  
  What you see: None today; a future fix to the manifest bookkeeping or the install step can land in one path and not the other.  
  Fix: One Updater._record(source, entry, config_key, outcome) and one try/except wrapper used by both paths; one packmove._stage_and_install(src, dest_root, name, sums, progress, cancelled, log) called by copy_pack and import_pack. Saves ~30 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-12.
- **Three hand-kept version strings; two User-Agent strings are stale (1.0 and 1.4.0 for a 1.4.3 plugin)** `nd_data_updater/http_qgis.py:18`  
  The version lives in metadata.txt, packsource.UPDATER_VERSION (also written into the pack manifest's 'created by'), damsuite/versions.py:42, and two User-Agent literals that were not bumped. ndpack/constants.py:29 already shows the right pattern (_version_from_metadata).  
  What you see: A support question or a server log cannot tell which updater asked (requests from the same install identify as 1.0, 1.4.0 and 1.4.3), and the next release has to touch four files to bump one number.  
  Fix: Read the version once from metadata.txt in __init__ or a tiny version.py (as ndpack/constants.py does) and build every USER_AGENT and UPDATER_VERSION from it.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-11.
- **Two compat shims target QGIS and GDAL versions below the plugin's minimum** `nd_data_updater/http_qgis.py:59`  
  QgsBlockingNetworkRequest.head() has existed since QGIS 3.18 and gdal.VectorTranslateOptions(addFields=) since GDAL 2.1; the plugin requires QGIS 3.28, which ships GDAL 3.x, so neither branch can be taken. The hasattr check also hides the real HEAD fallback behind a fake "HTTP 405" error string that the except clause then pattern-matches.  
  What you see: None at run time; the fake-405 trick makes the HEAD fallback harder to read than it is.  
  Fix: Delete lines 59-60 and 109-110 and pass addFields=i > 0 directly. Saves 4 lines and one misleading error string.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-13.
- **http_urllib.py is never used by the plugin; it exists for one test assertion** `nd_data_updater/http_urllib.py:1`  
  The docstring promises a fallback, but plugin.py imports QgisHttp unconditionally and nothing in the four plugins imports http_urllib except tests/test_proxy.py:41 (UrllibHttp()._open), and that test already proves the same proxy path through packsource.get_json and servicecheck._default_opener in the next two lines. The module also carries a third copy of the User-Agent and a second copy of the HEAD-then-Range fallback (http_qgis.py:56-69).  
  What you see: None at run time; 70 lines and a file that look like a live code path and are not.  
  Fix: Delete http_urllib.py and the one assertion in test_proxy.py:41-42. Saves 70 lines and one file.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-4.
- **The ndpack copy says it is kept byte-identical by 'the suite sync' but nothing checks it (damsuite copies are hash-checked, these four files are not)** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/__init__.py:1`  
  Today the five files (build_nd_pack.py, constants.py, packfiles.py, osm_parse.py, offline_pack.py) are byte-identical to ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/ (cmp verified), and both copies are live: the Hazard imports its root copies (data_tab.py:454, datafolder.py:889, chain_algorithms.py:220, download.py:46), the Updater imports .ndpack (packsource.py:89, 373, 481). The shared damsuite package is protected by FILES.json hashes checked in damsuite/__init__.py; the ndpack copy has no FILES.json, no test, and no script in the repo that compares it with the master (grep for ndpack outside the Updater finds only the Updater's own imports and README).  
  What you see: A fix made in one plugin's builder (for example U2-A-1 or U2-A-2) can ship in release N+1 for the Hazard Helper but not for the Updater, and the two plugins would then build different packs from the same window without any warning; nothing would tell Nick which builder made the pack he has.  
  Fix: Add the four files to a FILES.json-style hash list checked at import like damsuite, or a one-line test in both plugins' test sets that cmp's each file against the master. Longer term, move the builder into damsuite so there is one copy.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-6.
- **The ndpack copies have no hash manifest; damsuite copies do** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/__init__.py:1`  
  damsuite carries FILES.json with a sha256 per file (identical in ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite and the three copies), so a drifted damsuite copy can be detected. The five ndpack files (build_nd_pack.py, constants.py, packfiles.py, osm_parse.py, offline_pack.py) have no FILES.json and nothing in the repo checks them; they are identical today (cmp) only because the sync was run. No sync script is in the repo.  
  What you see: A fix made in Hazard's build_nd_pack.py and not re-synced would leave the Updater, the scheduled builder, building packs with the old code, and no check would say so.  
  Fix: Either add nd_data_updater/ndpack/FILES.json to the same hash scheme, or (better, with U2-B-1) move build_nd_pack.py, packfiles.py and osm_parse.py into damsuite/ so the existing hash list covers them and the ndpack folder disappears; the Runner and Screening then have the builder too and suite_data.py's "needs ND Dam Hazard installed" refusal (l.689-695 in each copy) can go. Saves a folder and a   
  Effort: medium. Id U2-B-14.
- **ArcGIS layer description, paging and tile-failure handling each written twice** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:756`  
  fetch_arcgis_tiles (l.756-763) and fetch_arcgis (l.802-810) repeat the layer-description, field, oid and uid block; _arcgis_tile_job (l.734-745) and _fetch_arcgis_pages (l.900-909) build the same offset-paged query and the same exceededTransferLimit test; fetch_arcgis_tiles (l.770-790) and fetch_nsi (l.960-980) have the same `got` callback and the same "more than max(2, tiles//20) failed -> fail, else WARNING + gaps" tail. The two tails already differ by one line: the tile route marks the data set partial (l.788), the NSI route leaves it "complete" with gaps (l.978), which is the kind of drift a shared helper prevents.  
  What you see: None directly; each ArcGIS paging quirk has to be fixed in two places, and the NSI and 3DHP tile routes already disagree on whether a data set with unreadable tiles counts as partial.  
  Fix: Helpers `_layer_info(url, out_fields, log) -> (out_fields, oid, uid, max_records)`, `_page_params(bbox, oid, out_fields, precision, offset, size)` with `_exceeded(d)`, and `_run_tiles(ctx, tiles, keys, have, job, uid, label) -> note` used by fetch_arcgis_tiles and fetch_nsi. Saves about 40 lines and settles the partial/complete difference in one place.  
  Effort: medium. Id U2-B-8.
- **fetch_arcgis's id_grid branch and _grid() can never run** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:812`  
  No data-set spec sets "id_grid": a repo-wide grep (both copies, all tests) finds only the parameter (l.793), its use (l.812-814) and the spec.get default (l.1411). The 3DHP layers took the other route in 1.19 (tile_deg=0.25 in _hydro_spec, l.1121), so the branch and _grid() (l.723-727) are unreachable.  
  What you see: None at run time; 14 lines a reader must understand that never execute, right beside the tile route that does handle 3DHP.  
  Fix: Delete _grid() (l.723-727), the id_grid parameter (l.793), the if-branch (l.812-818, keep the else body) and spec.get("id_grid", 1) at l.1411. Saves 14 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-4.
- **build()'s sources_file does nothing inside QGIS, and the "N address(es) changed" log line is skipped there too** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1231`  
  Inside QGIS the module is imported as a package, so __package__ is set and sources_file is ignored (the comment at l.1229-1230 explains: the plugin applied the file at import). The only plugin caller that passes it, data_tab.py:454-460, computes a path for nothing. The same condition skips the log line, so no in-QGIS build (either Hazard button, ndhazard:buildpack, the Updater) ever reports that the data-source file changed addresses; only the CLI does.  
  What you see: A user who edited the data-source file (Hazard Readme tab, "Edit data sources") and then builds the pack from any window gets no line in the run log saying which addresses were overridden; when an override points at a dead server, the log shows the failure but not why that address was used.  
  Fix: Remove the sources_file parameter (l.1217-1218, 1231-1235) and the two data_tab lines; in its place log `C.SOURCE_OVERRIDES` whenever it is non-empty, unconditionally ("Data-source file: 2 address(es) changed - NHD_FLOWLINE_URL, OVERPASS_URL"); the CLI keeps --sources by calling C.apply_source_overrides in main(). Removes one dead parameter and makes the override visible in every build.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-5.
- **The progress bar fills to 100 percent at the end of the downloads and then jumps back to 95 percent for the GeoPackage writing** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1273`  
  The download loop spreads the whole 0 to 100 range over the layers, so the last layer's own 100 percent is reported as 100 percent of the build; the folder-writing step then reports 95 to 99 percent and the raster step 96 to 99 percent. The bar therefore reaches the end and goes backwards while minutes of GeoPackage writing are still to come.  
  What you see: At the end of a long run the Updater's bar fills completely, so Nick thinks it is finished, and then drops back to 95 percent with "Writing the GeoPackages...". It looks like something went wrong or restarted.  
  Fix: Scale the download loop into 0 to 95 instead of 0 to 100 (lo, hi = 95.0 * i / n, 95.0 * (i + 1) / n), so the bar rises once from 0 to 100 across the downloads, the GeoPackage writing and the rasters.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-AV-5.
- **The _work SQLite copy of the whole pack is never removed and is what decides 'already in the pack', so the suite folder holds the state twice and deleting it causes a full re-download** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1290`  
  Every web layer is stored in ND_Dam_Suite_Pack_work/nd_offline_pack.sqlite as compressed JSON and then written again as GeoPackages into the pack folder; nothing in the builder or any caller (grep for _work in all four plugins) deletes or shrinks the work file after a complete build. 'Already in the pack' is answered from the work file (writer.done), not from the folder manifest. The builder's own text (lines 38-39) tells the user to delete that folder to start over, and the Updater's pack move does not move it.  
  What you see: The suite data folder carries two copies of the statewide data (a few GB extra on a laptop) with no mention in any window. If Nick deletes the _work folder to free space, or moves the pack with the Updater, the Hazard Helper's 'Build / update the offline pack' fetches every layer again (an hour or more) although the folder is complete, and U2-A-2 becomes reachable for every layer.  
  Fix: After a successful _write_folder with no failures or leftovers, remove the work file (or keep only the chunks / stages tables for resume); decide 'already in the pack' from the folder manifest for folder packs.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-8.
- **Three file-source routes open with the same 14 lines and two end with the same 5** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1464`  
  _add_layer_files (l.1460), _add_osm_files (l.1504) and _add_3dhp_bulk (l.1539) each begin with the same block: import suitedata with the package/script fallback, data_folder(), normalise extra_folders, layer_files_folder(), PF.folders_for. _add_osm_files and _add_3dhp_bulk also end with the same filter of `todo` against found/added/have/problems (l.1535-1536 vs l.1569-1570).  
  What you see: None at run time; a change to where layer files are looked for (as 1.4.0 made when it added the Updater's osm_layers folder) has to be made three times or the DWR/HIFLD, OSM and 3DHP routes search different folders.  
  Fix: One helper `_search_folders(extra_folders) -> (data_folder, folders)` and one `_without_supplied(todo, names, found, added, have, problems)`; the three functions call them. Saves about 35 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-7.
- **The land-cover tile retry sleeps with time.sleep, so Cancel is ignored for up to 40 s per tile** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1673`  
  Everywhere else the builder waits with cancel-aware sleeps (_sleep_plain, NetSession.sleep, which watch the _CANCEL event). _fetch_nlcd uses a plain time.sleep of 4, 8, 12, 16 s between tries, and checks cancel only once per tile; the WCS read itself may take up to TIMEOUT_S (180 s).  
  What you see: When the MRLC service is slow and Nick presses Cancel during the land-cover step, the button appears dead for up to 40 s of back-off plus the running request before the run stops.  
  Fix: Replace time.sleep(...) with _sleep_plain(...), which raises BuildCancelled when the cancel flag is set.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-10.
- **Cancel is ignored for up to 40 seconds a tile while the land-cover download waits to retry** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1673`  
  Every other wait in the builder is cancel-aware: _sleep_plain and NetSession.sleep wake up on the cancel flag in quarter-second slices. The land-cover retry uses a plain time.sleep, and _backoff_s("error", attempt) gives 4, 8, 12 and 16 seconds over the five tries, so one unlucky tile can hold the build for 40 seconds with no cancel check in between, and the progress callback (the other thing that notices a cancel) is only called before the retries start.  
  What you see: Nick presses Cancel in the Updater's pack window, or Cancel in the Processing dialog, while the land cover is being fetched from a slow MRLC service, and nothing happens for up to 40 seconds a tile - the window looks frozen and he is likely to press it again or kill QGIS.  
  Fix: Replace the one line with _sleep_plain(_backoff_s("error", attempt)), the helper already in this file, and add _check_cancel() inside the retry loop. Two lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-BV-5.
- **The one-file .zip pack is still written, reachable only from the CLI, with a README that names a group box that no longer exists** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1732`  
  Every in-QGIS caller passes a folder (data_tab.py:445-447, suite_data.py:699, chain_algorithms.py:230, packsource.py:547), so zip writing is reachable only by `python build_nd_pack.py --out x.zip`. The route is about 80 lines: _write_zip (l.1732-1772), the two zip branches of build() (l.1246-1251 and l.1323-1346, the second a copy of _report() l.1715-1729), `import zipfile` (l.68), write_clean_copy (imported l.82-95; damsuite/offline_pack.py:680 has no other user), docstring lines 7-9, 28, 34-36. The README it writes into the zip says to open "Data & Output tab > 7. Offline data pack"; that group is now "7. Data source - offline pack or download" (data_tab.py:235). Reading old zips (gpkgpack  
  What you see: Someone running the CLI with a .zip name gets a pack the plugins treat as "an older nd_offline_pack.zip" (data_tab.py:260 placeholder) and a README pointing at a control that is not there; anyone reading build() follows two output paths and two end-of-run reports.  
  Fix: Drop zip WRITING only: delete _write_zip, the two branches, the zipfile and write_clean_copy imports, write_clean_copy in damsuite/offline_pack.py, the "(or .zip)" in chain_algorithms.py's PACK parameter text and the docstring lines; make `--out x.zip` an error ("the pack is a folder since 2.24.0"). Saves about 80 lines here, one damsuite function and one of two report blocks.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-6.
- **The pack folder name "ND_Dam_Suite_Pack" is defined five times** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/build_nd_pack.py:1779`  
  The same folder name is a constant in damsuite twice (gpkgpack.PACK_FOLDER, suitedata.PACK_NAMES[0]), in the Updater twice (packsource.PACK_FOLDER, packmove.PACK_FOLDER) and the CLI default once; chain_algorithms.py:230 even hedges with `SD.PACK_NAMES[0] if hasattr(SD, "PACK_NAMES") else "ND_Dam_Suite_Pack"`. All equal today.  
  What you see: None today; renaming the pack or adding a second accepted name means five edits, and one miss would make a plugin look for a folder the Updater does not write.  
  Fix: Keep damsuite.gpkgpack.PACK_FOLDER; suitedata.PACK_NAMES = (gpkgpack.PACK_FOLDER, ...); packsource.py and packmove.py import it from .ndpack.damsuite.gpkgpack; the CLI default reads it; drop the hasattr hedge. Saves 3 constants and 1 literal.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-11.
- **Updater copy identifies itself as 'ND-Dam-Hazard-Helper-QGIS-plugin/2.28.0' (no metadata.txt beside the copied constants; User-Agent fixed at import)** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:29`  
  _version_from_metadata reads metadata.txt next to constants.py; in nd_data_updater/ndpack/ there is none, so PLUGIN_VERSION falls back to the hard-coded '2.28.0' (Hazard is 2.30.3, the Updater 1.4.3). Confirmed by importing the copy: PLUGIN_VERSION 2.28.0, HTTP_USER_AGENT ND-Dam-Hazard-Helper-QGIS-plugin/2.28.0, README_TEXT 'README.txt is missing from the plugin folder.'. packsource.load_builder() patches PLUGIN_VERSION after import, which fixes the pack's created_by but not HTTP_USER_AGENT, which _ua() uses on every request. The .zip README written by _write_zip (lines 1745-1749) still sends the user to 'Data & Output tab > 7. Offline data pack', a control that no longer exists under that n  
  What you see: Every request the Updater's builder makes tells USGS, SARP, the ND GIS Hub and Overpass it is a two-releases-old Hazard Helper, which makes server-side troubleshooting ('which version hit us') misleading; a .zip pack's README gives an outdated instruction.  
  Fix: In load_builder() also set C.HTTP_USER_AGENT (or make _ua() build the string at call time from C.PLUGIN_VERSION); better, give the copied constants a version source that does not depend on a metadata.txt beside them. Update the _write_zip README text to the current window names.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-5.
- **Five constants nothing reads, 33 lines, including the pre-2.11 quartic curve fits** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:164`  
  A whole-repo grep (four plugins, tests, README.txt, metadata.txt, all .py/.txt/.md/.json) finds no reader for QUARTIC_COEFFICIENTS_V2_10 (26 lines; the comment at l.125-128 says it is kept "for the record and for comparisons", but no comparison code uses it), CURVE_FIGURE (l.223-225), ND_ADDRESS_POINTS_ITEM (l.300-301), TRAVEL_MAX_MI (l.455) or FLOWLINE_SNAP_FT (l.456). (VEHICLE_CURVE_KEYS, _FT_PER_METER and the SOURCE_* names are used inside the file or elsewhere and are fine.)  
  What you see: None at run time; 33 lines of numbers that look like live hazard-curve inputs but are not, in the file an auditor opens first to check the curves, shipped twice (Hazard and the Updater copy).  
  Fix: Delete the five definitions; if the record matters, put the two sentences about the pre-2.11 quartic fits and their 0.10 / 0.18 ft difference into metadata.txt's 2.11.0 changelog entry. Saves 33 lines in each copy.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-12.
- **Release numbers and User-Agent strings typed by hand in six places; the Updater's pack build identifies as Hazard 2.28.0** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:380`  
  damsuite/versions.py holds RELEASE for all four plugins, yet version literals live in: constants.py:29 fallback "2.28.0" (two releases behind 2.30.3, and in the Updater it is always the value used, see U2-B-1); packsource.py:83 "1.4.3"; servicecheck.py:37 "ND-Data-Updater-QGIS/1.4.0" (stale); http_urllib.py:11 and http_qgis.py:18 "ND-Data-Updater-QGIS/1.0"; build_nd_pack.py:97 "1.3". HTTP_USER_AGENT is built once at import (l.380); packsource.py:90 patches C.PLUGIN_VERSION afterwards, which fixes the pack's created_by but not the User-Agent that _ua() (build_nd_pack.py:405) sends.  
  What you see: Every request the Updater's pack build sends to USGS, USACE, Esri, ND GIS Hub and Overpass says "ND-Dam-Hazard-Helper-QGIS-plugin/2.28.0 offline-pack-builder/1.3"; the Updater's service check says 1.4.0 and its file downloads 1.0. A service operator asked to whitelist or trace the suite's traffic sees three names, none of them the installed release.  
  Fix: One USER_AGENT per plugin built from damsuite.versions.RELEASE (Updater: "ND-Data-Updater-QGIS/" + RELEASE["ndupdater"], used by servicecheck, http_urllib, http_qgis and packsource); _ua() builds its string at call time from C.PLUGIN_VERSION so packsource's patch takes effect; constants.py's fallback reads RELEASE["ndhazard"] instead of a literal. Removes five literals and two stale numbers.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-2.
- **The Updater vendors the entire Hazard constants module (ACER curves, button CSS, layer-tree names, README reader, import-time settings reads) to get about 25 addresses and 4 OSM tag tables** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/constants.py:616`  
  build_nd_pack and osm_parse need the service URLs, the OSM tag lists, DOWNLOAD_WORKERS / OVERPASS_WORKERS / DOWNLOAD_TILE_DEG and HTTP_USER_AGENT. The copied file also carries the TM 11 curve objects, Qt stylesheet strings, Hazard layer-tree group names and output sub-folders, reads README.txt (missing in the copy), and at import time reads the Hazard data-source file and the suite services.json. 640 lines are copied and must stay in sync for roughly 60 lines of use; any Hazard change to the constants file (every release) changes the Updater's copy too.  
  What you see: No direct effect today; it is the reason the copy is 3,100 lines instead of a few hundred and the reason U2-A-5 happens (the version fallback is a Hazard concern that leaks into the Updater).  
  Fix: Split the download addresses, the OSM tag tables and the download tuning into a small damsuite module (sources.py) that both the Hazard and the builder import; keep the Hazard-only constants in the Hazard. The data-source override functions move with the addresses.  
  Effort: medium. Id U2-A-7.
- **The 11-line offline_pack.py shim is synced into the Updater for one import** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/offline_pack.py:4`  
  In the Updater the shim's only importer is build_nd_pack.py:82-84; the script branch two lines below (l.93-95) already imports damsuite.offline_pack directly. In Hazard the shim still serves nine other import sites (estimate.py:293,320; suite_algorithms.py:89,364; data_tab.py:395; runnerculverts.py:1118; suiteexport.py:88; download.py:637; checks.py:183) and its twin suitegpkg.py (11 lines) serves three (suite_algorithms.py:87, runnerculverts.py:104, suiteexport.py:86).  
  What you see: None at run time; one more file to keep in sync and two indirections for anyone tracing where PackWriter lives.  
  Fix: Change build_nd_pack.py:82 to `from .damsuite.offline_pack import (...)` and delete nd_data_updater/ndpack/offline_pack.py; in Hazard retarget the nine `.offline_pack` and three `.suitegpkg` imports to `.damsuite.<name>` and delete both shims. Saves 3 files, 33 lines and one synced file.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-10.
- **osm_parse.py: _bbox() bypassed twice and the node-or-centre position rule written three times** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/osm_parse.py:24`  
  overpass_query (l.24-25) and camps_query (l.136-137) re-implement _bbox(); overpass_to_features (l.39-45), camp_features (l.99-102) and crossing_features (l.309-314) each repeat "lat/lon on the element, else its center"; with_timeout re-imports re.  
  What you see: None at run time; this is the file that decides which OpenStreetMap features reach the pack, and three copies of the position rule is where a node-versus-way difference would creep in unnoticed (no test covers the file, U2-B-13).  
  Fix: Use _bbox() in both queries, add `_position(el) -> (lat, lon) or None` used by the three parsers, drop the inner import. Saves about 15 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-16.
- **The four kinds of populated place are listed twice: once as a setting, once hard-coded in the query** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/osm_parse.py:331`  
  places_query writes the four place kinds into the query text, while place_features decides what to keep from the keys of OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT in constants.py. The two other query builders in the same file do it properly: crossings_query builds itself from OSM_CROSSING_QUERIES and camps_query from OSM_CAMP_TAGS.  
  What you see: None today, the two lists agree. Adding a kind to OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT (a suburb or a locality, for the travel-time table) would change nothing, because the query would still not ask for it, and no message would say why the new kind never appears.  
  Fix: Build the pattern from the setting: '^(' + '|'.join(sorted(OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT)) + ')$'. One line, and the two lists can no longer disagree.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-BV-4.
- **A DWR / HIFLD file re-exported on the same day as the pack's copy is treated as 'not newer' and kept out** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/packfiles.py:155`  
  The comparison is at day resolution: the file's modification date against the manifest's fetched date (also a date). A corrected export dropped into dwr/ on the day the pack took the previous one compares equal, so it is skipped unless replace (refresh) is set. The Updater's _file_todo uses the same day comparison (packsource.py:387-388), so it does not even plan the layer.  
  What you see: Nick fixes a DWR export and drops it in the same afternoon; the run says 'DWR_dams: already in the pack - kept (the file DWR_Dams.gpkg is not newer)' and the pack keeps the wrong one until the next day or until he uses Fetch again on that layer.  
  Fix: Store the source file's modification time (or size + mtime) in the manifest entry when a file is added, and compare against that instead of the fetched date.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-A-9.
- **packfiles.py: the 3DHP file route is a second copy of the layer-file route (walk, folder list, adder)** `nd_data_updater/ndpack/packfiles.py:205`  
  scan (l.99-126) and scan_3dhp (l.205-229) are the same directory walk with a different file test; folders_for (l.129-137) and folders_3dhp (l.232-237) the same list builder with a different sub-folder; add_files (l.140-176) and add_3dhp (l.258-288) the same load-manifest / keep-if-complete / add_vector_file / record-problem loop with a different info dict.  
  What you see: None at run time; a fix to one route (the depth limit, treating .gdb as a folder, the "needs GDAL" message) has to be repeated in the other.  
  Fix: One `_walk(folders, depth, accept) -> [(path, mtime)]` used by both scans; `folders_for(data_folder, extra, subdirs=("dwr", "hifld", "osm_layers"))` with the 3DHP caller passing ("3dhp",); one `_add_one(pack_folder, man, name, path, info, **kw)` shared by add_files and add_3dhp. Saves about 40 of 288 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-15.
- **The Updater's osm_layers.py re-declares the OSM tag rules and bbox that its own ndpack already holds** `nd_data_updater/osm_layers.py:24`  
  osm_layers.py defines LIFELINE_TAGS, CAMP_TAGS, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT, PLACE_KINDS, TRAILER_PARK_CATEGORY, TRAILER_PARK_NAME, DEAD_RAIL (l.27-49), is_trailer_park (l.130-136) and the padded ND_BBOX (l.16) while the same plugin folder carries them in ndpack/constants.py (OSM_LIFELINE_TAGS l.356-372, OSM_CAMP_TAGS l.426, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT l.428, OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT l.431), ndpack/osm_parse.py (l.55, 150-153, 163-172) and ndpack/damsuite/offline_pack.py (ND_BBOX plus DEFAULT_PAD_DEG). I compared every pair: identical today. The comment pins "2.29.0" (the plugin is 2.30.3) and no Updater test imports osm_layers, so a tag added in Hazard would silently stop reaching the files the Updater builds  
  What you see: The pack's five OSM layers are built from one list and the plugins' Overpass fallback reads with another; when Hazard adds a tag (as 2.5 added schools and child care) the Updater's files lack it until someone remembers the copy, and nothing reports the difference.  
  Fix: Import instead of copy: `from .ndpack.constants import OSM_LIFELINE_TAGS as LIFELINE_TAGS, OSM_CAMP_TAGS as CAMP_TAGS, CAMP_POINT_RADIUS_FT, OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT` (PLACE_KINDS = tuple(OSM_PLACE_RADIUS_FT)), `from .ndpack.osm_parse import TRAILER_PARK_CATEGORY, TRAILER_PARK_NAME, _DEAD_RAIL as DEAD_RAIL, is_trailer_park`, ND_BBOX from damsuite.offline_pack padded by DEFAULT_PAD_DEG; delete l.16, 23-  
  Effort: small. Id U2-B-9.
- **Building the OpenStreetMap layers changes two GDAL settings for the whole QGIS session and never puts them back** `nd_data_updater/osm_layers.py:219`  
  These two switches are process wide, not private to this step, and they stay on for the rest of the QGIS session. One allows up to a gigabyte of memory for reading OpenStreetMap files; the other changes how any OpenStreetMap file is read, in a mode where the ordinary one-layer-at-a-time read can stop early.  
  What you see: After a pack run that built the OpenStreetMap layers, a .osm or .osm.pbf file the user opens in the same QGIS session can come in with only part of its features, and QGIS may hold a lot more memory than before, until QGIS is restarted.  
  Fix: Read the two values first, set them around the read, and put the old values back in a finally block (or pass them as open options to gdal.OpenEx instead of setting them globally).  
  Effort: small. Id U1-AV-4.
- **"Replace with my file" does the whole GDAL import on the window's own thread, so QGIS freezes with no progress and no way out** `nd_data_updater/pack_dialog.py:451`  
  Everything else in this window that takes time runs in front of a progress box with a Cancel button, which the file's own notes at the top state as a rule. This one reads the user's file, clips it to the pack's area and writes it into the pack GeoPackage with nothing but a wait cursor.  
  What you see: A user who drops in a statewide roads or structures file watches QGIS go grey and "Not responding" for minutes, with no bar, no message and nothing to press, and cannot tell a slow import from a hang.  
  Fix: Run it through the same _file_job progress box used for the USB copy and the import, passing a progress and a cancel handle into add_vector_file, or at least show a modal "Putting your file in the pack" box while it runs.  
  Effort: medium. Id U1-AV-3.
- **Eight helpers are defined and called only by their own tests** `nd_data_updater/packmove.py:277`  
  Grep of all four plugins (tests included) finds no caller outside the updater's own tests for: packmove.adopt (duplicates plugin.adopt_data_folder and contradicts its module docstring), servicecheck.text_report / worst / blocking / STATES (the window uses only check and warning_text), runreport.save_report (TextWindow._save writes its own file) and pack_age_days, packsource.service_answers (its test says "for callers that only want yes or no"; there are none), and the packsource.vintage_rows / layer_rows pass-through wrappers (pack_dialog already imports runreport). PackSource.plan(pack, force=False) never reads force.  
  What you see: None at run time; about 75 lines of product code and 25 of tests that test nothing the user can reach.  
  Fix: Delete the eight helpers and the force parameter, and their tests (test_runreport.py:167, 209-215; test_servicecheck.py:98, 118-119, 128-129; test_3dhp.py:117-119; test_packsource.py:134-141); pack_dialog.py:350 calls runreport.layer_rows directly. Saves ~100 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-5.
- **The BlockingRun fallback can never run: QgsTask is imported unguarded by qtcompat first** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:33`  
  plugin.py line 25 imports qtcompat, whose line 15 imports QgsTask without a guard, before the guarded import at line 33. If QgsTask were missing the plugin would already have failed to load, so the ImportError branch, class BlockingRun (lines 89-133), _run_blocking (369-379), the `if UpdateTask is None` branch (357-358) and the QProgressDialog / QCoreApplication imports (20, 22) are unreachable. The module docstring (lines 8-10) advertises a mode that does not exist, and QgsTask has been in QGIS since 3.0 anyway (minimum here is 3.28).  
  What you see: None at run time. A reader, and every later reviewer, has to understand a second run path that cannot happen.  
  Fix: Delete lines 33-36, 85-133, 357-358 and 369-379, the two imports and the docstring sentence; keep UpdateTask as a plain class. Saves about 65 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-3.
- **all_sources() invents a placeholder URL so build_sources will emit a Source for the status table** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:228`  
  The Settings status table only needs each dataset's name and folder, but it gets them by forcing every source on and feeding a fake https://example.invalid URL through build_sources, which constructs a PackSource and an NsiApiSource (and imports nsi_merge) just to read .name and .folder. The folder names live only inside build_sources, so SOURCE_INFO cannot be iterated directly.  
  What you see: None at run time (the placeholder is never fetched); a reader of build_sources must know it is also called with made-up input.  
  Fix: Add the folder name as a fourth field of SOURCE_INFO and have refresh_status iterate SOURCE_INFO; delete all_sources() and the fake URL (8 lines), and build_sources reads the folder from SOURCE_INFO too (one less literal per source).  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-14.
- **The pack's completeness is summarised three ways and the pack manifest is read by two readers** `nd_data_updater/plugin.py:273`  
  plugin.pack_summary (261-274), PackSource.update's result message (559-566) and runreport.pack_summary_lines (268-296) each count the layers and the incomplete ones from the manifest with their own wording ("all complete" / nothing / "N of M layers built"). runreport.read_manifest (107-114) is a second reader of the same manifest.json that gpkgpack.read_manifest (used by plugin.pack_summary and PackSource._manifest) already reads.  
  What you see: The Settings status table, the end-of-run message and the plain-words block can describe the same pack differently; a change to what counts as complete has to be made three times.  
  Fix: One runreport.pack_status(man) returning (n, bad, left_out) used by plugin.pack_summary, PackSource.update and pack_summary_lines; replace runreport.read_manifest with ndpack.damsuite.gpkgpack.read_manifest. Saves ~20 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-10.
- **The end-of-run block says 'the copy already on this computer is still used' for datasets that were never downloaded; the bar says 'this Mac' on every platform** `nd_data_updater/runreport.py:323`  
  run_summary writes the same sentence for every offline or error result, including a source whose Result message is 'not downloaded yet (...)' (core.py:641). plugin._on_task_done distinguishes the two cases (lines 400 and 409) but the summary logged and kept for the windows does not, so the two messages contradict each other. The bar text also names a Mac for Windows users.  
  What you see: On a first run without internet the log's 'what this run did' block claims a local copy is in use for NSI, Microsoft and OSM when none exists, right under a bar message saying they have not been downloaded; a user trusts the wrong one.  
  Fix: Pass the Result message through: when r.message starts with 'not downloaded yet' write '<name>: could not be reached and has not been downloaded yet', otherwise the current sentence. Replace 'this Mac' with 'this computer'.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-9.
- **The statewide 3DHP count URL, the state bbox, the md5 sidecar parser and two folder names are each defined twice** `nd_data_updater/servicecheck.py:38`  
  servicecheck.py:38-41 and packsource.py:70-77 build the same statewide count request with the parameters in a different order, and the bbox string appears a third time in packsource.TNM_PRODUCTS (82). core.py:239-244 (HttpFileSource) and 336-340 (OsmLayersSource) parse a .md5 sidecar with five identical lines. PACK_FOLDER is declared in packsource.py:58, packmove.py:37 and ndpack.damsuite.gpkgpack:40; MANIFEST_NAME in runreport.py:49, packmove.py:35 and gpkgpack:39.  
  What you see: None today; the service check and the build can start asking different questions when one URL is edited, so the check stops predicting the build.  
  Fix: servicecheck imports packsource.SERVICE_PROBE_STATE and a STATE_BBOX constant kept once; a core._md5_sidecar(http, url) helper used by both sources; import PACK_FOLDER and MANIFEST_NAME from ndpack.damsuite.gpkgpack. Saves ~15 lines and four constants.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-11.
- **The NSI service probe is a full county data query for Burleigh County, not the metadata question the module promises** `nd_data_updater/servicecheck.py:46`  
  fips=38015 is Burleigh County (Bismarck), the state's most populous county, and fmt=fc asks for the whole FeatureCollection; probe() reads 64 KB and closes, but the server has to start producing the county and the measured time is the time to its first bytes under load. The 6 s timeout and the 2.5 s 'slow' threshold were set for metadata requests.  
  What you see: The Services tab can show NSI as 'slow' or 'DOWN' when the API is fine, which the warning text then turns into 'those layers would be left out of the pack'; and every check costs the USACE server a county export.  
  Fix: Probe with the smallest county instead (fips=38007, Billings County, a few hundred structures) or the API root, and keep kind 'head'.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-10.
- **Choice lists and defaults typed in two or three modules** `nd_data_updater/settings.py:59`  
  The NSI mode list is typed in core.NSI_MODES, settings.py:59 and settings_dialog.NSI_MODE_LABELS (25-29); the 3DHP mode list in settings.py:92 and packsource.MODES_3DHP. Defaults are repeated: NSI 30 days (settings.py:50, sources.py:59, core.py:394), pack 30 days (settings.py:83, sources.py:89, packsource.py:265), 30 m (settings.py:67 vs nsi_merge.DEFAULT_MATCH_M), 3 minutes (settings.py:101 vs packsource.DEFAULT_3DHP_MINUTES).  
  What you see: Adding an NSI version (nsi2030) or changing a default means editing three files; miss one and the settings reader silently snaps the saved choice back to "merged" / "auto" or a different file's default wins over the window's.  
  Fix: Settings imports core.NSI_MODES, packsource.MODES_3DHP, nsi_merge.DEFAULT_MATCH_M and packsource.DEFAULT_3DHP_MINUTES for its checks and defaults; build_sources passes config values through with no second default; settings_dialog builds the NSI combo labels from one table kept in core. Removes about ten duplicated literals.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-B-7.
- **Saving the Settings window silently clears a pending 'Fetch every pack layer again at the next check'** `nd_data_updater/settings_dialog.py:169`  
  The box is always drawn unticked instead of from settings.pack_full, but save() writes its state back. The flag is only consumed when a run starts (plugin.py:361), so a user who ticks it, presses Save, and later re-opens the window to change anything else and saves again has the one-shot cleared without noticing (Offline Pack... also calls save(), line 252).  
  What you see: The full refresh they asked for never happens and nothing says so.  
  Fix: Initialise the box from settings.pack_full (`self.pack_full.setChecked(settings.pack_full)`), or only write the flag when the box is ticked.  
  Effort: small. Id U1-A-14.

## Shared damsuite package (57: 0 serious, 20 medium, 37 minor)

### Medium
- **The sync script the package depends on is not shipped, and selfcheck() runs only in tests** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/__init__.py:8`  
  `sync_damsuite.py` exists nowhere in the four plugins (find) nor in /mnt/project-files (text search). The four FILES.json files (27 hashes each) are therefore rebuilt by a tool nobody can see, or by hand. And `selfcheck()` (line 85), the only reader of FILES.json, is called by three Runner tests (test_v320.py:950, test_v3120.py:176, test_v3130.py:153) and by no plugin at start-up, so a drifted copy is never reported to a user. The mechanism costs 4 JSON files, 37 lines x 4 copies, and 4 hand edits per release, and protects only the test run.  
  What you see: An engineer who changes one damsuite file (say a threshold in loss.py) in the Hazard master has no shipped way to push it to the other three plugins or to rebuild FILES.json; the three other copies silently keep the old value, so the same dam gets two answers from two windows.  
  Fix: Either ship the sync script in the repo (copy master to the three plugins, rewrite the four FILES.json; it is also the natural place to write RELEASE and the four metadata.txt version lines), or drop FILES.json, selfcheck and package_files and replace them with a 10-line test that diffs the four damsuite folders byte for byte, which is what actually matters. Dropping saves 4 files and 37 lines x 4  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-3.
- **Cancelling 'Get data for this dam' throws away the record of the files already fetched** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/damdata.py:581`  
  step() is called at the top of get_role() outside its try/except, so a cancel (or any DataError from step) leaves get_data_for_dam before the final rec.save() at line 639. The set_entry() calls for the roles already written, and the dam values offered from the NID/DWR record at line 567, live only in memory. The one earlier save (line 616) runs only on the suite-package path, before the package step. The windows catch the exception and log just 'cancelled' (suite_data.py:816-818).  
  What you see: The files are in <dam>/data/ but dam.json does not point at them, so the tools' autofill does not offer them and the next Get data (exists() reads the record, lines 544-545) fetches and rewrites every role again; for a live-service role that is a repeat download, and the log never says the bookkeeping was lost.  
  Fix: Wrap the role loop in try/finally and call rec.save() in the finally (every entry written so far points at a finished file), or save after each successful set_entry(). Also append 'cancelled after N of M roles; what was fetched is recorded' to out["lines"].  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-4.
- **A local DEM that does not reach the dam fails the step instead of downloading terrain** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/damdata.py:654`  
  A DEM in dem/ always wins, because until the engineer picks a server by hand catalog.choice has no "dem" entry and chosen() just returns the first built-in 3DEP service (services.py:718-726). raster_window then raises "<file> does not cover the dam's area" (damdata.py:491) and get_role records that as a failed role (damdata.py:603) without ever trying the terrain service that was ready; _get_nlcd has the same shape at damdata.py:673-677.  
  What you see: An engineer who drops one county's LiDAR into dem/ sees "DEM: FAILED - county.tif does not cover the dam's area" for every dam outside that county, and Get data finishes with no terrain for those dams although 3DEP would have fetched it. The message does not say what to do, so the user has to guess that taking their own DEM out of the folder, or choosing a server by hand, is the way out.  
  Fix: In _get_dem (and _get_nlcd) catch the DataError from raster_window, log one line saying the local DEM does not cover this dam, and fall through to the service path; keep the hard failure only when no service is chosen.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-AV-3.
- **A pack layer whose file is missing is dropped without a word, while the dialog still counts it** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/gpkgpack.py:637`  
  GpkgPackReader.__init__ silently leaves out every data set whose .gpkg is not on disk (a pack folder copied halfway, a file deleted or lost on a share). Nothing records that it was listed in manifest.json. offline_pack.describe() (offline_pack.py:1030-1042) reads only the manifest, so the Data tab / Suite data text still says 'N data sets' including the missing one, and LAYERS.txt lists it too. When a tool then asks for that layer in offline mode, ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/download.py:726 raises 'not in the offline data pack (offline mode)' because reader.has() is False.  
  What you see: Nick sees a pack described as complete, then a run that stops with 'NSI_structures is not in the offline data pack' for a layer the pack's own listing shows. Nothing tells him the file structures/NSI_structures.gpkg is missing from the folder, so he looks for a settings problem instead of re-copying the pack.  
  Fix: Keep the missing entries (e.g. self.missing = {name: e['file']}), make describe() check the files of a format-2 folder and append ' - file missing: structures/NSI_structures.gpkg', and have the not-in-pack error say 'listed in the pack but its file <rel> is missing: copy the pack again' when the name is in reader.missing.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-A-2.
- **A structure with no arrival time is counted in the 'over 60 minutes' warning band** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/loss.py:176`  
  warning_band() returns "" when a wet structure has no arrival time (loss.py:78-80), and estimate() then files that structure under "over60", the longest warning and the lowest fatality rates. The Runner hands such rows in: consequences.py:558 starts arrival_hr as NaN and lifeloss.rows_from_evaluate (lifeloss.py:251) passes it through, so any structure the depth raster wets but the arrival raster does not cover lands in this band. The Runner's own guard (lifeloss.complete_cells, lifeloss.py:21-25) checks only that the cells the flood fills have a rate; it cannot see that people were moved into the wrong cell.  
  What you see: The report line 'by warning time: under 15 minutes 0, 15 to 60 minutes 0, over 60 minutes N' states as fact a warning time that was never computed, and once an engineer signs a rate table those people get the most favourable rate, so the loss-of-life number comes out too low. lifeloss.py:23 says a number too low 'is the one direction this suite does not allow'.  
  Fix: Keep a fourth bucket for a missing arrival time: add "unknown" to WARNING_BANDS/WARNING_LABELS, put got["warning"] or "unknown" in the cell key, print it in estimate_text, and let a cell with band "unknown" have no rate (so the total is None and the text says why). If a conservative default is preferred, use "under15" and say so in the line.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-1.
- **Structures with no arrival time are reported as having over 60 minutes of warning** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/loss.py:176`  
  warning_band() returns "" when there is no arrival time (and, for a not-a-number arrival, falls through both comparisons and returns "over60" outright at line 85), and estimate() then files those people under "over60" - the most favourable band, the one with the lowest fatality rate in any Graham / RCEM table.
