Arrival is optional in the Runner's own consequences run ("velocity and arrival are optional", nd_breach_runner/core/consequences.py:696, NaN where missing), and the Runner's PAR report prints the split as fact: "by warning time: under 15 minutes 0, 15 to 60 minutes 0, over 60 minutes N" (core/lifeloss.py:395) with no note that no arrival time was known.  
  What you see: A run without an arrival-time raster produces a Population at Risk report saying everybody has over an hour of warning, which an EAP reviewer would read as a safe dam. With a signed rate table the loss-of-life number is also the lowest the table can give, which is the one direction the suite says it never errs in.  
  Fix: Keep the unknown band: add "unknown" to WARNING_BANDS / WARNING_LABELS ("warning time not known") and file an empty band there instead of in "over60", give warning_band an explicit not-a-number guard, and treat an unknown band like a missing rate in the total (no fatality figure until the arrival times are there).  
  Effort: medium. Id D1-BV-3.
- **loss.estimate keeps a total when a rate cell is missing: the line meant to void it is a no-op** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/loss.py:193`  
  `total = None if total is None else total` changes nothing, so a signed-off rate table that lacks one (severity, band) cell still returns a number: the people in that cell are left out and rates_ok stays True. Ran it: two structures of 10 and 40 people, table missing high/over60 -> fatalities 5.0, estimate_text prints 'loss of life (signed-off rates): 5.0'. The Runner knows: nd_breach_runner/core/lifeloss.py:21-25 says 'damsuite.loss.estimate adds up only the cells that have a rate ... a number too low' and adds complete_cells() to refuse the number; test_v3200.py:374 pins that workaround. Hazard's pipeline.py:3169 always passes rates=None, Screening uses warning_band only.  
  What you see: No shipped path shows the wrong number today (the Runner guards it, Hazard never passes a table). The next window or report that hands loss.estimate a signed table with a gap prints a loss-of-life figure that is too low and labelled as signed off, which is the one direction the suite says it never errs in.  
  Fix: Replace line 193 with `total = None` and delete the now redundant `if total is not None:` guard two lines down (net 1 line). Then lifeloss.complete_cells is only the message naming the empty cells, not the thing that keeps the arithmetic right.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-1.
- **The pack is described from manifest.json alone, so ten layer files missing from the real capture pack are reported as present and current** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/offline_pack.py:1033`  
  describe() reads only manifest.json and counts the data sets it lists; it never checks that each layer's GeoPackage is on disk. suitedata.pack_datasets (suitedata.py:498) does the same, and suitedata.stale (515) only flags a data set whose status is not "complete" or whose date is old, so a layer whose file is gone but whose manifest entry says "complete" today is counted as present and current. GpkgPackReader (gpkgpack.py:636-638) meanwhile skips exactly those data sets, so reader.has("NSI_structures") is False. packdiff.diff has the same blind spot: it compares manifests, so when the missing part is finally copied in, the change report sees no change and names no dam.  
  What you see: On the pack the GUI captures were taken with, the Data tab says "33 data sets ... built 2026-10-12" and the suite check says "33 layers, 1 to refresh", while the structures and roads layers are not in the folder at all. With "Offline only" ticked the run then stops with "not in the offline data pack" for ten data sets, and the advice the check gives ("it fetches only what is missing or stale") will not fetch them, because the manifest says they are complete. The engineer is told the pack is fine  
  Fix: In GpkgPackReader's loop keep the data sets whose file is missing in a separate dict (say self.absent) instead of dropping them silently, and have describe() and suitedata.pack_datasets check os.path.isfile on each entry's "file" (about 6 lines) so a missing layer is reported as missing and suitedata.stale lists it for the Updater to re-fetch.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-BV-1.
- **packdiff's layer-role prefix table duplicates gpkgpack.GROUPS and has drifted: campgrounds are 'not used by the classification'** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/packdiff.py:32`  
  Two tables classify the same pack layer names by prefix: gpkgpack.GROUPS (lines 43-55, the pack's own folder layout) and packdiff.ROLES (lines 32-43). They are maintained separately and disagree. packdiff.role("OSM_campgrounds") returns ("other", "not used by the classification") because no CONSEQUENCE prefix matches it, although OSM_campgrounds is a pack layer (build_nd_pack.py:1088, Updater packsource.py:64) and the Hazard classification counts campgrounds as inundated receptors (classification.py:400-413, criterion 5.2.1.1). packdiff also carries prefixes for layers the pack never had (MS_BUILDINGS, E911, PARCEL, FEMA_PARCEL, AADT, OSM_PARKS). A third copy of the same grouping, nd_data_up  
  What you see: After the Updater refreshes the pack, its run report names the classified dams whose class may be out of date (runreport.py calls packdiff.stale_dams). A refresh in which the campground layer changed lists no dam, so the engineer keeps a hazard class that counted the old campgrounds and is told nothing needs re-running.  
  Fix: Derive the role from the pack's own table: role(name) = a 7-entry map from gpkgpack.group_of(name) (structures -> consequence, roads / railroads -> access, lifelines -> lifeline, dwr / barriers -> dams, hydro -> dams for NID_dams else model, landcover -> model) and delete the five prefix tuples (lines 32-38, about 8 lines) and osm_layers.GROUP (2 lines). The quickest fix is one token, "OSM_CAMP",   
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-1.
- **stale_dams names every classified dam after every refresh, because the only caller never passes the flood footprints the function was built around** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/packdiff.py:223`  
  Without footprints the function has no way to tell one dam from another, so it returns every row that has a computed class as soon as any consequence, access, lifeline or dam layer changed. The Updater's refresh is statewide, so the structures, address and road layers change on essentially every run, and runreport.py:397 is the only production caller and never supplies footprints. The module docstring sells the opposite ("the question nobody could answer before is so which of the dams I have already classified would come out differently now?").  
  What you see: After every pack refresh the run report says "N classified dam(s) may be out of date" and lists all of them, whichever dam the changed features are actually near. An engineer with fifty classified dams gets the same fifty names every time, so the list stops being read, and the one refresh where it matters looks like all the others.  
  Fix: Either state it plainly in the report ("every classified dam is listed: the features were not tested against each dam's flood") in one line of runreport.py, or narrow it with what the manifest already holds: compare each dam's study box against the changed data sets' own bounding boxes from the manifest and drop the dams no changed layer reaches, about 15 lines in packdiff with no QGIS needed. Del  
  Effort: medium. Id D2-BV-3.
- **Review list flags a dam classified from HEC-RAS rasters as 'incomplete: no model run'** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/priority.py:74`  
  has_rasters is true only for <dam>/Depth (Max).tif (suite_home.py:25, 55) and has_manifest only for _breach_model/ or _screening_model/run_manifest.json (suite_home.py:56-57). ND Dam Hazard writes a real 'classified' status for every classification (pipeline.py:1279) including one made from a HEC-RAS export, whose depth raster is 'Depth (Max).vrt' (autodetect.py:4) and which has no manifest. verdict() then groups the dam as 'incomplete' whenever its class does not exceed the record (priority.py:92-93).  
  What you see: In the Screening's statewide review list a dam that was fully modelled in HEC-RAS and classified by the Hazard Helper reads 'computed High but the run is incomplete', flag 'no model run', and sits in the attention list above dams that really need work; the summary line counts it as a run whose class is 'a floor, not an answer'.  
  Fix: Count a non-preview 'classified' status written by ND Dam Hazard as a modelled dam: in suite_home._fill_from_record set has_rasters when the classified result exists and the dam folder holds any Depth (Max).* (tif or vrt), or add a row field 'classified_by_hazard' that _flags() checks before appending 'no model run'. Keep the flag for a class that came from the Screening's triage only.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-5.
- **Feature download skips records when a server's page cap is below 1000** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/services.py:626`  
  fetch_features asks for 1000 records a page and sets the next offset to n * 1000. An ArcGIS server never returns more than its own maxRecordCount: a layer capped at 500 answers 500 records with exceededTransferLimit=true, the loop then asks for offset 1000, and records 500-999 (and every second block after them) are never fetched. The loop does not notice because got is not empty and 'more' is true. The offset must be the number of records already received (len(feats)), not n * page. Checked live on 2026-10-13: the built-in layers are all at or above 1000 (3DHP flowlines 2500, FEMA NFHL 2000, ND Hub HSIP dams 1000, TIGERweb 100000, ND Hub roads / USA Structures / NID / NBI 2000), so today th  
  What you see: 'Get data for this dam' and Data source = Download (damdata.fetch_role) return a layer with roughly half its buildings, roads or dams missing, with no warning. The run finishes and the counts and class come out low.  
  Fix: Use the received count as the offset: `raw = http_get(query_url(lay, box_lonlat, offset=len(feats), count=page))`. Add a test page set where the server returns fewer records than asked with exceededTransferLimit=true and assert every record arrives.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-A-1.
- **A services.json the plugin cannot read empties the user's server list in memory, and the next click in the servers window writes that empty list over the file** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/services.py:805`  
  When services.json cannot be read, load() throws the whole file away and builds the Catalog from a dict holding only _problem, so self.user is empty. Nothing stops a save after that: Suite data and servers calls catalog.save() the moment the user picks anything in a dropdown (suite_data.py:538, 591, 643, 677, 683), and save() writes to_dict(), whose "servers" list is now empty, over the file. The docstring's promise "left untouched (never overwritten until the user saves)" is kept only until the first click. The only warning is a sentence in a note label (suite_data.py:530).  
  What you see: The suite folder is shared, so this happens when a newer release writes services.json and an older plugin opens it (format_version too new), or after a crash truncates the file. The engineer opens Suite data and servers, sees the built-in servers and a note, changes one dropdown, and every server the office ever added to that suite folder is gone from disk with no confirmation and no way back.  
  Fix: Record the failure on the Catalog and refuse to write over it: in save(), `if self.problem: raise ServiceError(self.problem + " - fix or rename the file before saving")`, and in the servers window show that message instead of a silent overwrite. About 4 lines. Better still, rename the unreadable file to services.json.bad before the first save so nothing is lost.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-BV-2.
- **A settings value the suite cannot use is dropped without a word and the tool runs with its shipped default** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/settings.py:545`  
  coerce() returns None plus a problem for a stored value that is out of range, misspelled for an enum or not a number, and apply_defaults then skips it. Every tool of the three plugins is wrapped by suite_settings_tools.settings_aware, which stores the refusals on the tool object as `self._nd_refused` (suite_settings_tools.py:161) - and nothing reads that name anywhere except two Screening tests. Screening's preflight only counts how many values are set (core/preflight.py:443). The only place that tells the user is the Suite settings window when it happens to load the file (suite_settings.py:441), which does not run when the engineer edits damsuite_settings.xlsx in Excel - the way the file is  
  What you see: An engineer types a value into the signed settings spreadsheet, runs the tool, and the run silently uses the plugin's own starting value instead. The log says nothing, the report says nothing, and the number in the report is not the number in the settings file.  
  Fix: In settings_aware's initAlgorithm, push the refusals into the tool's log (feedback.pushWarning or the plugin's log_message) as "settings file value for X not used: <problem>; the starting value <v> is used", so the refusal appears beside the run it affects. About five lines in each plugin's suite_settings_tools.py.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-BV-2.
- **Two tile-size limits and two download paths for the same 3DEP terrain request (solvegate.TILE_PX 1200 vs terrain_cache.TILE_PX 2000)** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/solvegate.py:37`  
  The biggest exportImage tile the suite asks the USGS 3DEP server for is defined twice with different values. The Screening's direct download (core/terrain.py tile_extents / fetch_3dep_tile, used when no suite folder is set) tiles at 1,200 px, retries 3 times and gives up; the suite terrain cache (terrain_cache.get, used when a suite folder and an ImageServer / WCS are chosen) tiles at 2,000 px and on failure re-asks the tile as 2 x 2 quarters (fetch_split). The Screening file also has two HTTP paths: fetch_3dep_tile calls netproxy.urlopen with its own retry loop, _http_get wraps services.urllib_get with a second retry loop. test_damsafety_021.py:82 pins SG.TILE_PX == 1200.  
  What you see: The same dam gets different tile counts and different failure behaviour depending only on whether a suite folder is set: a slow 3DEP answer that the cache path survives (quarters) stops the direct path with '3DEP request ... failed after 3 tries'. Whoever tunes the tile size after the next USGS timeout has two numbers to find.  
  Fix: Route the Screening's direct download through terrain_cache.get with a throw-away cache folder (out_dir/_tmp), so one module owns the tile size, the retries and the quarter split. Delete tile_extents and fetch_3dep_tile (core/terrain.py:24-70, about 47 lines), solvegate.TILE_PX and its comment (solvegate.py:35-37), and the pin in test_damsafety_021.py:82.  
  Effort: medium. Id D2-B-2.
- **Two settings for one data folder: adopt_folder never writes the Updater's own key** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitedata.py:127`  
  adopt_folder writes ndsuite/data_folder, pack_path, data_source and dams_folder, but not the Updater's folder. The Updater reads its folder from nd_data_updater/settings.py:25-27 (QgsSettings key `nd_data_updater/data_dir`, default ~/Documents/ND Structure Data) for pack_path (plugin.py:234), the updater core (plugin.py:277) and the folders table of its pack window (pack_dialog.py:338 `SD.subfolder_info(self.settings.data_dir)`), while the readiness lights in the same window (pack_dialog.py:317) come from suitecheck, i.e. suitedata.data_folder(). Only the Updater's adopt_data_folder (plugin.py:236-249) writes both keys; nothing in Hazard, the Runner or Screening writes the Updater key (grep)  
  What you see: A user who presses 'Set up this folder' in the Hazard, Runner or Screening home window (the button is in all three, gui clean/*_home) and then opens step 1, the Updater, sees 'Data folder: ~/Documents/ND Structure Data', builds the pack there, and the Updater's own lights, read from the suite folder, say no pack is found. When the Updater later shares the pack (packsource.py:628) it calls adopt_folder on its own folder and moves the plugins' suite folder under them.  
  Fix: Delete the Updater's key: make nd_data_updater Settings.data_dir read `suitedata.data_folder() or DEFAULT_DATA_DIR` and its setter call suitedata.adopt_folder; drop plugin.adopt_data_folder (14 lines). One setting, one field, one default, and the lights and the folder table in the Updater window agree.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-6.
- **Two or more rasters in dem/ (no .vrt) are reported as 'Your DEM: none' and every run downloads terrain** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitedata.py:438`  
  local_dem() returns "" as soon as dem/ holds two rasters without a .vrt mosaic. Nothing downstream distinguishes 'empty' from 'several files': describe() prints 'Your DEM: none (dem/ folder)' (suitedata.py:594), suitecheck prints 'none (each run downloads a DEM instead)' with the fix 'Put a statewide or county DEM (.tif or .vrt) in dem/' (suitecheck.py:175-179) while subfolder_info() in the same window counts the files (suitedata.py:316), and damdata._get_dem (damdata.py:648-657) goes to the terrain service without a word about the files it ignored.  
  What you see: An engineer who drops two county LiDAR tiles into dem/ is told there is no DEM and asked to put one there; Get data and the Runner then download coarser 3DEP terrain for every dam, and the lights say the DEM row is not set up, which contradicts the file count shown beside it.  
  Fix: Return a third state. In status()/describe()/suitecheck: when rasters(folder, DEM) has more than one file and no .vrt, say 'N rasters in dem/ but no .vrt mosaic: build one (Raster > Miscellaneous > Build virtual raster) and put it in dem/, or keep a single file' and make the row 'warn'. Alternatively have local_dem() build dem/_mosaic.vrt from the tiles with gdal.BuildVRT when GDAL is available.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-3.
- **The suite home says a pack layer is "to refresh" when it can never be refreshed** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitedata.py:520`  
  stale() calls a layer stale when its status is anything other than "complete", so a layer the source itself cannot deliver in full counts as needing a refresh for ever. The Updater applies the opposite rule to the same manifest (nd_data_updater/plugin.py:273 and damsuite/offline_pack.py:1037 both accept "partial" and "not available"). The pack built for the GUI captures shows it: OSM_lifelines has status "partial" with the note "PARTIAL: Geofabrik free shapefile has no electric substations", so suitecheck's pack row (suitecheck.py:133) reads "33 layers, 1 to refresh - Run the ND Data Updater: it fetches only what is missing or stale (OSM_lifelines)" in all three home windows of scratchpad/gu  
  What you see: The suite home shows a yellow warning light the user cannot clear. Running the ND Data Updater again downloads the layer again and leaves it "partial", so the warning comes back; meanwhile a real stale layer hides in the same row.  
  Fix: Use one rule: in stale(), treat a layer as stale only when it is older than max_age_days or its status is neither "complete", "partial" nor "not available" (offline_pack.USABLE_STATUS plus "not available"), and let suitecheck say "1 layer incomplete at the source" as information rather than something to fix.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-BV-1.
- **Each release needs seven hand edits in five files plus a FILES.json rebuild and three copies; only the Runner's tests check any of them** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/versions.py:41`  
  Per release, by hand: (1) versions.RELEASE, four entries; (2) damsuite/__init__.py:64 SUITE_VERSION; (3) version= in the four metadata.txt, whose changelog lines also hard-code the sibling versions and 'damsuite 1.16.3'; (4) nd_data_updater/packsource.py:83 UPDATER_VERSION, a second copy of the updater's version that becomes the pack's 'created by' (packsource.py:90); (5) damsuite/FILES.json regenerated because versions.py changes every release, with sync_damsuite.py (named in __init__.py:8) not in the repo; (6) the package copied to nd_breach_runner/damsuite, nd_dam_screening/damsuite and nd_data_updater/ndpack/damsuite. Only the Runner's tests tie its metadata to RELEASE (test_v320.py:948,  
  What you see: A missed edit is invisible until a user sees 'X installed, Y is this release' from suitecheck for a plugin that is in fact current, or a pack labelled as built by an older updater; a stale FILES.json fails no runtime check at all.  
  Fix: Make RELEASE the one source: a small release script (or the missing sync_damsuite.py) that writes version= into the four metadata.txt from RELEASE, bumps SUITE_VERSION, regenerates FILES.json and copies the package; derive UPDATER_VERSION from metadata.txt as the Runner's workflow.VERSION = metadata_version() and the Hazard's constants.PLUGIN_VERSION already do; and add one test per plugin asserti  
  Effort: medium. Id D1-A-9.
- **A release needs about ten hand edits plus five test edits; versions.RELEASE could drive them all** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/versions.py:41`  
  From `git show --stat` of the 1.22 and 1.21.1 commits, each release is edited by hand in: (1) versions.RELEASE; (2) damsuite/__init__.py:64 SUITE_VERSION (1.16.2 -> 1.16.3; read only by Runner tests test_v3120.py:173, test_v3130.py:152, test_v3140.py:134 as '>= 1.8.0 / 1.10.0'; the damsuite code itself dates features by release-set numbers such as '1.19.0', suitedata.py:41, not by SUITE_VERSION); (3) damsuite/FILES.json; (4) the four metadata.txt version= and changelog lines; (5) nd_breach_runner/core/workflow.py:58 `VERSION_FALLBACK = "3.20.3"`; (6) nd_data_updater/packsource.py:83 `UPDATER_VERSION = "1.4.3"`; (7) tests that pin the literal: ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/tests/test_suite116.py:29-30  
  What you see: None directly. Each release risks one forgotten edit: a suite-home warning that names the previous release as 'this release', a Runner report carrying a stale fallback version, or a test run that goes red for a number rather than a bug.  
  Fix: Make versions.RELEASE the single source. workflow.py: `VERSION_FALLBACK = damsuite.versions.RELEASE["ndbreach"]`; packsource.py: read the Updater's metadata.txt as Hazard (constants._version_from_metadata), the Runner (workflow.metadata_version) and Screening (damrecord_io.plugin_version) already do, or use RELEASE["ndupdater"]; delete SUITE_VERSION and change the three Runner tests to assert the   
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-2.

### Minor
- **The package map omits seven modules and still says nothing has run in a real QGIS** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/__init__.py:12`  
  The list at lines 12-43 names 19 of the 26 modules; cascade, loss, priority, suitecheck, packdiff, transformguard and hydro3dhp are missing, and six of them are also absent from the imports (lines 56-62) and __all__ (67-70) although all three plugins import them directly. Line 49 and versions.py:22 still say nothing has run inside a real QGIS, which the 1.20+ GUI runs disprove.  
  What you see: None for the engineer running tools; the first page a maintainer reads is wrong about what the package holds and how tested it is.  
  Fix: Add one line per missing module (or drop the list, since every module opens with its own one-line docstring) and delete the two 'never run in QGIS' sentences.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-13.
- **Cascade check ray-casts every inventory dam against every flood vertex; bbox() is defined but not used** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/cascade.py:109`  
  downstream() calls in_flood() for every dam, and in_ring() walks every vertex of every ring in pure Python (with an _on_segment test per edge). The module defines bbox(rings) (line 191) but never uses it to reject dams outside the footprint first. The Runner passes the Dams folder's dams (cascadecheck.dams_from_folders) and the flood extent GeoJSON, whose outline at model resolution can carry tens of thousands of vertices.  
  What you see: With a few hundred dam folders and a long flood outline the cascade step at the end of a run takes tens of seconds for a question whose answer is almost always 'none'; a statewide inventory passed as `dams` would take minutes.  
  Fix: Compute box = bbox(rings) once and skip a dam when its point is outside the box (four comparisons) before in_flood(); optionally a per-ring bbox inside in_flood.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-8.
- **chain.py carries a hand-kept copy of the Runner's digest code, with no test that proves equality** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/chain.py:52`  
  chain.py:32-91 (FULL_HASH_LIMIT, PIECE, MANIFEST_NAME, _cache, file_digest, _canonical, digest_of) repeats nd_breach_runner/core/provenance.py:19-87 line for line; chain.py:8-12 says 'if the runner's algorithm changes, change it here too' and that 'the suite's tests prove the equality against the runner's module', but no test imports both modules (grep). provenance.py does not import damsuite.chain although the Runner ships the package.  
  What you see: None today. If the Runner's digest ever changes without the mirror edit, the Hazard chain check reports every raster 'DIFFERENT' for runs that are intact, and the submittal says the chain is not complete.  
  Fix: In provenance.py replace lines 19-53 and 81-87 with `from ..damsuite.chain import FULL_HASH_LIMIT, PIECE, MANIFEST_NAME, file_digest, _canonical, digest_of`. About 45 lines removed, one algorithm, no mirror rule.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-5.
- **The datum words of a shared dam.json field are defined in the Runner only; the shared doc does not know 'DEM estimate'** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/dam_record.py:79`  
  The allowed values and their meaning live in nd_breach_runner/core/damjson.py: :41 `LOCAL_DATUMS = ("local",)`, :49 `DEM_ESTIMATE_DATUM = "DEM estimate"` (added in 1.22), :91-108 local_datum() / dem_estimate() that decide when a stored top of dam may be used as an elevation. The shared FIELDS text still lists three words. Screening (core/comparison.py:50 reads top_of_dam_ft; algorithms/propose_dam.py:418 writes datum 'DEM') and Hazard (reviewpkg.py:581 copies top_of_dam_ft) read the field with no datum rule.  
  What you see: None today. The next tool in Hazard or Screening that reads top_of_dam_ft from dam.json will take a Runner estimate from a coarse DEM as a surveyed crest, the exact mistake 1.22 fixed inside the Runner.  
  Fix: Move LOCAL_DATUMS, DEM_ESTIMATE_DATUM, DEM_ESTIMATE_NOTE, local_datum(rec) and dem_estimate(rec) (about 20 lines) into dam_record.py next to FIELDS, update the FIELDS text, and import them in damjson.py. No lines saved; one definition for the three readers.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-7.
- **The one-year staleness threshold is written twice** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/dam_record.py:422`  
  suitedata.py:61 `STALE_DAYS = 365` (a pack data set older than this is offered for refresh) and this literal are the same rule in two modules with no link; only Hazard's portfolio.py:918 passes data_age_days.  
  What you see: None today (both 365). A later change to one leaves the pack row and the dam's next-action disagreeing on what 'stale' means.  
  Fix: `STALE_DAYS = 365` in dam_record.py (the lower module) and `from .dam_record import STALE_DAYS` in suitedata.py; one line each.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-12.
- **Three different rules for 'which sub-folders are dams'** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/damdata.py:768`  
  damdata.dam_folders takes a folder with dam.json or dam_location.json and skips names starting with '.' or '_'; suite_home.scan (lines 107-123) also takes a folder with 'Depth (Max).tif' or a model folder and has no name filter; Hazard's watch.dam_folders (watch.py:90-95) takes every sub-folder except '_', '.' and 'outputs', and series.py:349 and portfolio.py:933 wrap that one.  
  What you see: The suite home table, Hazard's portfolio and the 'every dam' batch in Suite data (suite_data.py:482/775, suite_data_tools.py:311 use dam_folders) can list different dams for the same Dams folder: a folder with Hazard rasters but no dam.json is a row in the home table and the portfolio but is skipped by the batch; an '_old' folder with a dam.json is a home row but not a portfolio row. Counts differ between windows with no message.  
  Fix: One `is_dam_folder(path)` in dam_record (dam.json, legacy location, final depth raster or model folder; skip '.', '_' and 'outputs') used by suite_home.scan, damdata.dam_folders and watch.dam_folders; delete the two extra predicates and the two wrappers (about 20 lines).  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-11.
- **gdalsafe.open_raster builds a broken sentence into the error the user sees** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/gdalsafe.py:41`  
  checked() appends " failed" to whatever `what` says, so the `what` passed here produces "GDAL cannot open C:/dem.tif - opening failed: <message>". The exception-mode branch two lines above (line 40) already formats the same case correctly as "GDAL cannot open C:/dem.tif: <message>".  
  What you see: When a DEM or a terrain tile cannot be opened, the message box and the run log show "GDAL cannot open C:/dem.tif - opening failed: ...", which reads as two sentences stuck together and makes the engineer unsure which file the problem is in.  
  Fix: Pass the plain subject: `checked(ds, "opening {}".format(path), gdal)`, which reads "opening C:/dem.tif failed: <message>". One line.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-BV-4.
- **Every feature of a pack layer is read and decoded twice while the layer is written** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/gpkgpack.py:209`  
  write_layer reads the whole data set once to work out the column types and once more to write the rows, and its callers never pass fields/geometry: add_dataset (line 303) and convert_v1 (line 364) both leave them out. For convert_v1 the feature source is _v1_features, which opens the old pack database and unzips and JSON-decodes every stored feature, so the whole state-wide layer is decompressed twice; add_vector_file reads the source file through OGR twice, with the reprojection and the property copy done twice as well.  
  What you see: Building the pack and the "convert the old pack" step take roughly twice as long as they need to (the biggest layers, NSI structures and the address points, are hundreds of thousands of features), with the progress bar sitting still for the extra pass.  
  Fix: Let write_layer plan the fields from the rows it is already writing: write with a first pass that buffers the features in batches, or accept fields/geometry from the caller (convert_v1 can read them from the source pack's own datasets record, which already holds the count and geometry type).  
  Effort: medium. Id D2-AV-2.
- **The 3DHP service address is typed in five files while hydro3dhp.BASE / FLOWLINE_URL / WATERBODY_URL are never used** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/hydro3dhp.py:24`  
  hydro3dhp.py defines the 3DHP MapServer address and both layer URLs (lines 24-28), but nothing imports them. The same address is spelled out literally in services.py:191 and 196 (BUILTIN usgs_3dhp_flowline / waterbody), ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/constants.py:438 and 441 (and the Updater's identical copy of constants.py), nd_data_updater/servicecheck.py:39 and nd_data_updater/packsource.py:70. Only FLOW_FIELDS / WB_FIELDS of hydro3dhp are used (build_nd_pack.py:1120).  
  What you see: None today (all five agree). When USGS moves or renames the service, a fix in one place leaves the others pointing at the dead address: the Updater's service check can say 'up' while the pack build or the Hazard download fails, or the reverse.  
  Fix: Use hydro3dhp.FLOWLINE_URL / WATERBODY_URL in services.BUILTIN, set constants.NHD_FLOWLINE_URL = hydro3dhp.FLOWLINE_URL (constants.py already imports damsuite modules), and import them in servicecheck.py and packsource.py. Four literal copies go; one address, one edit.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-3.
- **normalise_flowline and normalise_waterbody are the same 20 lines twice** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/hydro3dhp.py:106`  
  Lines 106-128 and 131-151 differ only in the third argument to ftype_of (True / False) and in whether the featuredate loop is guarded by `if "featuredate" in low` (the unguarded loop does the same work). Every fix to one must be repeated in the other.  
  What you see: None now; a later change to how a 3DHP feature is given its NHD names (say a new field) is easy to make in one copy and forget in the other, and streams and ponds would then read differently.  
  Fix: One `_normalise(feature, flowline)` holding the body, with `normalise_flowline = lambda f: _normalise(f, True)` and the same for waterbodies. Saves about 20 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-6.
- **Loss-of-life total silently leaves out cells that have no rate (no-op line)** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/loss.py:193`  
  `total = None if total is None else total` changes nothing, so the `if total is not None` guard below it is dead. A signed rate table that lacks a cell gives a total made of the other cells only, with no sign in the result that people were dropped. The docstring (loss.py:16-18) promises fatalities is None until a table covers the estimate. The only live caller, the Runner's lifeloss.assess, had to add its own complete_cells check (lifeloss.py:21-25) to work around this.  
  What you see: Any future caller of estimate() (the Hazard Helper imports loss too, pipeline.py) that trusts est["fatalities"] prints a loss-of-life figure that is too low when a table is incomplete; today the Runner's extra guard hides it.  
  Fix: Replace the line with `total = None` (and keep the `if total is not None` guard), or raise/record a problem so the caller cannot miss it.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-2.
- **A program started by the plugin still gets the computer's proxy for an address QGIS is told to reach directly** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/netproxy.py:109`  
  proxy_for has three answers: a proxy address, None (leave urllib's own choice alone) and NO_PROXY, the empty string, meaning "QGIS connects to this address directly" because it is on QGIS's no-proxy list. env_for tests `if proxy:`, so the empty string is treated the same as None and nothing is changed, leaving whatever HTTP_PROXY / HTTPS_PROXY the computer has in the environment of the program that is started. urlopen (line 101) does handle this case correctly, which is what makes the difference visible: the Python downloads obey the no-proxy list and the started program does not.  
  What you see: On a network where QGIS is set to reach the package server directly, the Breach Runner's ANUGA / micromamba install (core/anuga_setup.py:169) still tries the computer's proxy and can fail with a connection error that the suite's own downloads do not show.  
  Fix: Handle the direct case: when proxy == NO_PROXY, remove the four proxy variables from env (and set no_proxy / NO_PROXY to "*") instead of leaving them untouched.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-AV-4.
- **Helpers and constants defined twice inside damsuite: point-in-ring, box overlap, the EPSG:5070 WKT and the acre-foot** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/offline_pack.py:290`  
  offline_pack._point_in_ring (290-300) is the same ray-casting test as suitegpkg.point_in_ring (252-262); offline_pack.boxes_touch (359-360) is solvegate.boxes_meet (102-104) without the None guards; terrain_cache.WKT_5070 (37-46) is identical to suitegpkg.WKT[5070] (113-121, checked by parsing both files); solvegate.CFS / ACFT (33-34) restate units.AF_M3 and 1 / units.CFS_M3S, and nd_breach_runner/core/units.py:8-9 holds them a third time. suitegpkg imports nothing from damsuite, so offline_pack and terrain_cache can import it without a cycle.  
  What you see: None now. A fix to one copy (for example the zero-division guard in the ring test, or a changed acre-foot rounding) silently misses the other, and the pack reader and the export would then disagree on which features touch the study box.  
  Fix: offline_pack imports point_in_ring from suitegpkg and boxes_meet from solvegate (or keeps one); terrain_cache uses suitegpkg.WKT[5070]; solvegate imports units.AF_M3 and units.CFS_M3S. About 28 lines and four places to keep in sync go.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-7.
- **The format-1 one-file pack (.zip of nd_offline_pack.sqlite) can still be written although the suite reads and ships format 2** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/offline_pack.py:680`  
  Since 2.24 / damsuite 1.10 the pack is a folder of GeoPackages (gpkgpack) and the Updater only writes folders (packsource.py), but build_nd_pack.build still writes the older one-file zip when --out ends in .zip (build_nd_pack.py:1246-1247, 1322-1330, _write_zip 1732-1773) and offline_pack.write_clean_copy (680-696) exists only for that branch. The README written into that zip (1748-1752) sends the user to 'Data & Output tab > 7. Offline data pack' of the Hazard plugin alone. Reading old zips (open_pack / _unzip / PackReader) is a separate matter and should stay; the format-1 PackWriter also stays, it is the builder's resumable work file.  
  What you see: Two pack formats can still be produced. An engineer who follows the docstring's `--out D:/nd_pack.zip` gets a pack whose layers cannot be dragged into QGIS, has no LAYERS.txt, and whose README describes one plugin's old tab.  
  Fix: Drop the .zip output branch of build_nd_pack (lines 9, 28, 1246-1247, 1322-1330, 1732-1773) and offline_pack.write_clean_copy; about 65 lines and one CLI option. Keep reading old zips.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-9.
- **PackReader and GpkgPackReader repeat five methods; _unzip and _unzip_folder repeat the target-probe loop** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/offline_pack.py:787`  
  The two pack readers (format 1 SQLite and format 2 GeoPackage folder) each carry the same `created` property, `_real` (the NHD / 3DHP alias), `has`, `info` and `covers` (about 30 lines). `_unzip_folder` (942-974) and `_unzip` (977-1013) each loop over `_extract_target`, compare the manifest's `created`, probe the folder with a `.write_test` file and log the same 'unpacking ... (once)' line (about 20 lines twice).  
  What you see: None now. The 1.19 alias fix (`_real`) had to be written twice; the next change to how a pack is located or to which names a layer answers to has to be made in two readers and two unzippers, and a user with an old format-1 pack would see different behaviour from one with a format-2 pack.  
  Fix: A small `_BaseReader` (in gpkgpack, which offline_pack already imports late) that both readers inherit, and one `_pick_target(zip_path, manifest, log)` that returns the writable folder. Saves about 45 lines.  
  Effort: medium. Id D2-B-8.
- **Two QGIS sessions unpacking the same pack zip write the same .part file** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/offline_pack.py:999`  
  _unzip (and _unzip_folder at line 968, dst + '.part') uses a fixed side-file name. The unpack target is 'next to the zip when that folder can be written' (_extract_target), so two people opening the same zipped pack from a shared folder at the same moment both write nd_offline_pack.sqlite.part, each open() truncating the other's bytes, and the first os.replace puts a damaged database in place; the 'created' check in read_manifest then accepts it. _OPEN_LOCK only serialises threads of one QGIS. terrain_cache._part_name (line 53-56) already solves the same problem with a pid/thread suffix.  
  What you see: Rare: a pack opened by two users at once from a share can come out corrupt, and both then get 'the offline data pack could not be read' until someone deletes the unpacked folder.  
  Fix: Name the side file with os.getpid() (tmp = '{}.{}.part'.format(db, os.getpid())) in _unzip and _unzip_folder, as terrain_cache does.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-A-6.
- **The shared review-list call cannot see a cut-off flood: the flags come from a field the folder scan never fills** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/priority.py:69`  
  _flags hunts the run words in "problem", "next_text", "note", "incomplete" and "flags", but a suite_home.scan row carries none of those except "problem", which holds only a dam.json read error (suite_home.py:43-46). So scan_and_rank, the one-call entry point this module's own docstring offers, can never report "flood cut off" or "truncated at the terrain edge". Only the Screening works, by filling "note" from each dam.json first (nd_dam_screening/core/incomplete.py:198-207, dialog.py:1253), and it falls back to the bare scan_and_rank inside a catch-all (dialog.py:1254-1255).  
  What you see: On that fallback path every dam whose flood was cut off is ranked "agree - both High" and drops out of the attention list, which the Screening's own notes call the one grouping that must not be wrong, and the next window to use the shared call inherits the same silent miss.  
  Fix: Give suite_home.dam_row a "note" field filled from the status results it already reads (the keys core/incomplete.record_note uses: note, notes, hz_notes, sim_notes, flags, problem), so the flags work for every caller, and drop the plugin-side patch-in.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-AV-4.
- **Version numbers hard-coded in User-Agent strings and SUITE_VERSION, all stale and outside versions.py** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/services.py:59`  
  The suite is at release 1.22 and the Updater at 1.4.3, but the User-Agent sent to every server says 'ND-Dam-Suite/1.5' (services.py:59, also used for QgsBlockingNetworkRequest in the three suite_data_tools.py), 'ND-Data-Updater-QGIS/1.4.0' (servicecheck.py:37) and 'ND-Data-Updater-QGIS/1.0' (http_urllib.py:11, http_qgis.py:18); only packsource.py:84 builds its string from UPDATER_VERSION. damsuite/__init__.py:64 SUITE_VERSION = "1.16.3" is read only by three Runner tests as a >= check. These four Updater modules are also four thin wrappers (Request + User-Agent + netproxy.urlopen) around what services.urllib_get already does.  
  What you see: A server log or the agency proxy shows the suite as version 1.5 and the Updater as 1.0; a support question 'which plugin version hit our server' cannot be answered from the logs. No functional effect.  
  Fix: One services.USER_AGENT built from versions.RELEASE (or with no number at all), imported by servicecheck.py, http_urllib.py and http_qgis.py; have the Updater's three openers call services.urllib_get. Delete SUITE_VERSION or set it from versions.py. Saves four constants and three copies of the same 3-line opener.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-4.
- **A settings value of 'inf' crashes coerce() instead of being reported, and the tool then drops all its file defaults** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/settings.py:222`  
  _from_text() (line 258) turns the CSV cell 'inf' or 'infinity' into float('inf'); coerce() tests only for NaN (v != v) and then calls int(v), which raises OverflowError (verified: coerce(_from_text('inf'), number setting) raises 'cannot convert float infinity to integer'; enum_index(float('inf'), options) at line 180 does the same). The plugins' settings_aware.initAlgorithm catches everything (suite_settings_tools.py:160-163) and sets _nd_applied = [] for the whole tool.  
  What you see: One bad cell makes the tool open with its built-in defaults for every parameter, not just the bad one, with no message; the settings windows call the same coerce through Settings.changed (suite_settings.py:245, 348).  
  Fix: Add `or not math.isfinite(v)` to the test at line 214 (return the 'is not a number' problem) and guard enum_index with math.isfinite before int(value).  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-6.
- **Settings file is written in place, not atomically like dam.json and the manifests** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/settings.py:367`  
  Settings.save() opens the target file for writing directly (CSV here; xlsx_lite.write does zipfile.ZipFile(path, "w") at xlsx_lite.py:152). dam_record.save, suitedata._set and suitedata.write_manifest all write <path>.tmp then os.replace; this one file, the engineer's hand-tuned table of every threshold, does not. A disk-full error or a crash mid-write leaves a truncated file, and load_current() then reports 'cannot be read' and every tool silently falls back to built-in defaults.  
  What you see: The one file the engineer edits by hand can be lost by a failed save; nothing else in the suite has that gap.  
  Fix: Write to path + '.tmp' and os.replace() in save(), for both the .xlsx and .csv branches (xlsx_lite.write can take the tmp path).  
  Effort: small. Id D1-A-7.
- **settings.current_path re-implements suitedata's setting store without its outside-QGIS tier** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/settings.py:477`  
  Lines 477-504 (current_path, set_current_path) repeat suitedata.py:82-111 (_get/_set: environment variable, then QgsSettings, then ~/.ndsuite/config.json) for one more key, but without the config.json tier; set_current_path(..., remember=True) outside QGIS swallows the ImportError at lines 499-503 and remembers nothing.  
  What you see: Outside QGIS (the headless chain, scripts, tests) the chosen settings file is forgotten between runs while the data and Dams folders are remembered; inside QGIS no difference.  
  Fix: current_path: return `_current["path"]` when set, else `SD._get(SETTINGS_KEY, ENV_PATH)`; set_current_path: `SD._set(SETTINGS_KEY, path)` when remember. About 20 lines removed, one store for every suite-wide setting.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-9.
- **A dam.json that cannot be read is treated as a dam with no record, so the home table advises redoing the work** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suite_home.py:69`  
  dam_row sets row["problem"] when DR.load raises (lines 48-53) but leaves rec as None, and the next action is then worked out as if the folder had no record at all: "screen" for a dam with no rasters (dam_record.py:397) or the line above for one with rasters. The text "Flood rasters without a dam record" is untrue - there is a record, it just cannot be read - and the one piece of advice that would help, fix or restore dam.json, is never given.  
  What you see: A dam whose dam.json is damaged (a half-written file, a hand edit) appears in the home table with "screen" or "classify" as its next action, so the user may redo screening or classification that was already done, instead of restoring the file.  
  Fix: In dam_row, when row["problem"] names an unreadable dam.json, set next to "none" with the text "dam.json cannot be read: fix or restore it before anything else" rather than falling through to the no-record branches.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-BV-5.
- **The suite home's "Installed" line never mentions the ND Data Updater, which is step 1** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suite_home.py:163`  
  installed_plugins() walks VS.PLUGINS (three plugins) while suitecheck walks VS.ALL (four, the Updater included), and the home window draws its one-line summary from installed_plugins (ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/suite_home.py:470). The captures show the result in all three windows: "Installed: ND Dam Hazard Classification Helper 2.30.3; ND Dam Breach Runner 3.20.3; ND Dam Breach Screening 0.28.3." - the plugin that step 1 of the same window sends the user to is not in the list.  
  What you see: A user whose ND Data Updater is missing reads a line that says everything is installed; only the step 1 row elsewhere in the window says otherwise.  
  Fix: Use VS.ALL in installed_plugins (one word), so the line names all four and says "not found" for the Updater when it is missing.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-BV-4.
- **Compat shims for a versions.py that cannot be older, an unreachable import fallback, and an uncalled method** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitecheck.py:258`  
  Lines 258, 260 and 364 guard against a versions.py without ALL or RELEASE, i.e. older than 1.16.0, which cannot happen: versions.py sits in the same package copy that FILES.json pins. Same kind: damdata.py:700-705 `_hydro()` falls back to `import hydro3dhp` 'outside the package', unreachable because damdata.py:49-53 already fails outside the package on its relative imports; settings.py:302 `Settings.for_plugin` has no caller anywhere (grep, tests included).  
  What you see: None.  
  Fix: Use VS.ALL and VS.RELEASE directly (3 places), replace _hydro() with `from . import hydro3dhp` at the top of damdata.py, delete for_plugin. About 14 lines.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-8.
- **"Next:" line names the step the user just finished, not the next one** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitecheck.py:391`  
  The loop returns the first step that is NOT ready, the opposite of the docstring and of the name, and when every step is ready it falls through to steps[0], which is always "1. Data". The only caller is the ND Data Updater's window, which handles both answers (nd_data_updater/pack_dialog.py:320-328) and so prints "Next: 1. Data in ND Data Updater. Nothing is missing here." for a fully set-up suite.  
  What you see: Once the suite folder and the offline pack are in place, the one line in the Updater window that is supposed to say where to go next keeps pointing back at step 1, the window the user is already in, instead of step 2 Screen. A user following that line never leaves the first step.  
  Fix: Return the first step that is ready and whose work is not done yet (for the Updater: the step after its own), and when nothing is blocked say so plainly; or rename it blocked_step and have the window print "nothing is blocking you" plus the next numbered step itself.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-AV-1.
- **The GeoPackage writer uses one fixed side-file name and deletes any other writer's copy of it** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitegpkg.py:623`  
  Every GpkgWriter for the same output file writes <file>.part, and on top of that it removes an existing .part before it starts. Two runs that write the same pack layer at the same time (the suite data folder on a shared drive, the Data Updater running in two QGIS sessions, or a second run started after the first was left going) therefore pull the file out from under each other: the first writer's database is deleted mid-write, and whichever finishes last renames a file the other was still filling. terrain_cache._part_name (line 53) solves the same problem with a process and thread suffix.  
  What you see: Two pack writes at once can leave a layer's .gpkg truncated or missing while the manifest says it is complete, which then shows up as a layer the pack lists but cannot open.  
  Fix: Name the side file with the process and thread, as terrain_cache does (self.tmp = "{}.{}_{}.part".format(path, os.getpid(), threading.get_ident())), and remove only that file instead of any .part lying around.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-AV-3.
- **Writing a pack layer always demands SQLite's rtree module and dies with a raw SQLite message without it** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/suitegpkg.py:798`  
  Every pack layer is written with spatial_index=True, and _write_rtree creates the R*Tree table with no check and no try/except. The older pack code treats a Python whose sqlite3 was built without the rtree module as a real possibility: offline_pack.rtree_available() (line 83) probes for it and _build_index (line 132) simply gives up and lets the pack work without an index. The format-2 reader is just as tolerant (GpkgPackReader.index_kind returns "scan" when there is no R*Tree), so only the writer is strict. On such a computer the CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE raises sqlite3.OperationalError("no such module: rtree") inside GpkgWriter.close, write_layer aborts the file and the error travels up unchang  
  What you see: On a QGIS whose SQLite lacks the rtree module, building the offline pack or converting an old pack to the new one-file-per-layer form stops on the first layer with the message "no such module: rtree" and no pack, although the plugins can read a pack that has no index at all.  
  Fix: In gpkgpack.write_layer pass spatial_index=offline_pack.rtree_available(), or wrap the CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE and the trigger block in suitegpkg._write_rtree in try/except sqlite3.Error and carry on without the index (the reader already copes, reporting index_kind "scan").  
  Effort: small. Id D2-AV-1.
- **normalize leaves the source tile open and its temp file behind when the warp fails** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/terrain_cache.py:166`  
  If gdal.Warp returns None (or raises in GDAL's exception mode) the RuntimeError leaves `src` open and `tmp` on disk; there is no try/finally. get() catches the error (line 216), adds the tile to `failed` and tries os.remove(part) (line 218), but on Windows the still-open GDAL handle makes that remove fail, which is swallowed by `except OSError: pass`.  
  What you see: After a failed tile the terrain cache folder keeps a <tile>.<pid>_<thread>.part and a .part.n.tif that nothing cleans except clear(); the request is reported as failed correctly, but the leftovers accumulate and the tile is re-downloaded next run even if only the rewrite step failed.  
  Fix: Wrap the warp in try/finally that sets src = None and removes tmp when the warp did not succeed; or call normalize on the part through a helper that closes the dataset before os.replace.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-A-4.
- **A dead network costs up to half an hour per terrain tile before the error, and Cancel waits as long** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/terrain_cache.py:208`  
  get() tries every tile before raising, and for each tile a failure first runs the fetcher's retries and then fetch_split's four quarter requests with the same retries. The Screening's fetcher (nd_dam_screening/core/terrain.py _http_get) is 3 attempts x 120 s timeout + 12 s of sleeps, about 6 min; the split adds 4 x 6 min, so one tile on a network that silently drops packets takes about 30 min to fail, and a 4-tile request about 2 h. cancel() is read only at the top of the tile loop (line 194), never inside the retries or the split.  
  What you see: With the terrain server unreachable through a firewall that drops packets, a Screening or Runner terrain step appears to hang for a long time before 'N of M terrain tile(s) could not be downloaded', and pressing Cancel does not stop it until the current tile has exhausted all its retries.  
  Fix: Stop at the first failed tile (the user is told to run again anyway), pass cancel into fetch_split and check it between quarter requests, and do not attempt the 2 x 2 split when the first failure was a connection error rather than a timeout or a server 'size too large' answer.  
  Effort: medium. Id D2-A-5.
- **About 100 lines of public functions and constants in these modules have no caller, or only a test** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/tm11_curves.py:164`  
  Grepping all four plugins including tests and the damsuite copies: no caller at all for tm11_curves.check (164-182) and STATED_ACCURACY_FT (39), offline_pack.build_spatial_index (197-214), services.Catalog.is_default (728-731), services.DISPLAY_KINDS and VECTOR_KINDS (62, 64), hydro3dhp.normaliser_for_name (183-187), suitegpkg.SegmentGrid.keys_near (405-406), transformguard.repairs (25-26, with the 'repaired' / 'failed' counters it reports), terrain_cache.clear (327-338). Test-only callers for keepawake.status (228-233), solvegate.one_crs (47-59), packdiff.affects_result (60-62), hydro3dhp.first_available (198-204), terrain_cache.summary (341-353), PackReader.index_kind (766-769) and GpkgPac  
  What you see: None directly. tm11_curves.check promises a consistency check of the danger-curve table that never runs anywhere; terrain_cache.clear offers a 'clear the terrain cache' that no window exposes. A maintainer reading the module docstrings is told about features that do not exist in the GUI.  
  Fix: Delete the listed names (about 100 lines across nine files). Keep tm11_curves.check only if one line `assert tm.check() == []` is added to test_v2281.py; otherwise delete it too. Where a test is the only caller (one_crs, affects_result, first_available, status, summary, index_kind) delete the function and the test lines together.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-5.
- **transformguard is loaded twice over, so the copy anyone imports normally never installs the guard and its repair counters are always zero** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/transformguard.py:21`  
  Each of the three plugins loads the file with module_from_spec, which does not register the module in sys.modules, so every load makes a fresh module with its own _state. The first one installs the guard and owns the "repaired" / "failed" counters; the others see _nd_guarded on the replaced object and return True without doing anything. A normal `from .damsuite import transformguard` creates yet another module whose install() is a no-op and whose repairs() is always (0, 0). The replacement itself (line 81) also swaps a core QGIS class for a plain function for the whole QGIS session, so any other plugin that writes `isinstance(x, QgsCoordinateTransform)` or subclasses it stops working, and in  
  What you see: No effect today, because nothing reads repairs(). It is a trap for the obvious next step: adding "transform repairs: N" to the Runner's report through the ordinary import would always print 0, so the engineer would believe the guard never fired when it may have fired hundreds of times, and a conflict with another plugin would leave nothing in the log.  
  Fix: Import it normally (`from .damsuite import transformguard; transformguard.install()`) and add it to damsuite/__init__.py, which keeps one module and one set of counters; the "before any plugin module imports QgsCoordinateTransform" requirement is still met because __init__.py runs first. Log the counts once at the end of a run, and log one line when install() fails instead of swallowing it. About   
  Effort: small. Id D2-BV-5.
- **damsuite/units.py is dead in all four plugins, and its comment claims a user it does not have** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/units.py:20`  
  Nothing in the four plugins or their tests calls any function or constant of this module: the only mentions outside the file are the import and the __all__ entry in damsuite/__init__.py:56 and 68 and its line in the package docstring. The settings grid named in the comment does not use UNIT_FACTORS (settings.py keeps its own _UNITS table at settings.py:44), and each plugin converts units with its own code (nd_breach_runner/core/units.py:13, nd_dam_screening/core/hazard.py:40).  
  What you see: Nothing for the user today, but it costs work every release: the file is one of the 27 the package hash list covers and is copied into all four plugins, and it advertises one shared place for unit conversion that no plugin reads, which is exactly where a future "fix the conversion" edit would land and do nothing.  
  Fix: Delete damsuite/units.py, its line in the package docstring and its name in the import and __all__ of damsuite/__init__.py, then regenerate FILES.json and the three copies. The same sweep can drop loss.WARNING_MINUTES (loss.py:38), suite_home.CLASS_FILES (suite_home.py:26), priority.GROUP_TEXT (priority.py:37) and cascade.bbox (cascade.py:191), none of which is read anywhere.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-AV-2.
- **damsuite/units.py is used by nothing in any plugin or test** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/units.py:64`  
  No line outside damsuite imports damsuite.units (grep of every .py in the four plugins and their tests for a line naming both), and inside the package no function of it is called (ft_to_m, m_to_ft, acres_to_m2, af_to_m3, cfs_to_m3s, cell_label, UNIT_FACTORS: 0 uses each; the 8 `cell_label` hits are Screening's own core/runplan.cell_label(settings)). The settings grid the comment names uses its own table, settings.py:46 `_UNITS`. Each plugin keeps its own unit module with the same four factors (nd_breach_runner/core/units.py 38 lines, nd_dam_screening/core/us_units.py 97 lines, Hazard constants), and damdata.py:85 repeats `MI = 1609.344` beside units.MILE_M.  
  What you see: None. 68 lines ride in four zips and take a FILES.json entry each.  
  Fix: Delete damsuite/units.py, its import in __init__.py:56 and its name in __all__; keep damdata.MI. Saves 68 lines x 4 copies and 4 FILES.json entries.

- **Two rules say whether the plugins match: pairwise REQUIRES minimums and the RELEASE set** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/versions.py:44`  
  check(), require() and report_lines() apply the hard/soft minimums (2.9.1/2.17.0, 3.1.1/3.7.0, 3.1.0/3.7.0, 0/2.17.0) and are called at start-up by all three plugin.py files (Hazard :139-140, Runner :33-34, Screening :25,172) and by the two hand-offs (runner_handoff.py:189, core/handoff.py:90). suitecheck._plugin_items (suitecheck.py:269-274) separately warns when a sibling is below this release's RELEASE ('{} installed, {} is this release'). The minimums are many releases behind, so any sibling old enough to trip them trips both, in two wordings; the 18 lines of `reason` text describe features from 2.9 to 3.7.  
  What you see: With, say, Runner 3.6 beside Hazard 2.30.3 the user reads at start-up 'works best with ND Dam Breach Runner 3.7.0 or newer; 3.6.0 is installed (hand-off passes the dam folder only from 3.1.1; ...)' and in the suite home '3.6.0 installed, 3.20.3 is this release': two warnings about one thing.  
  Fix: Keep RELEASE as the one rule: a sibling below this release set is a warning ('install the matching zip'), below the oldest version that writes dam.json format 1 (one number per plugin) is an error. Delete REQUIRES' soft tier and reason text, check()'s warning branch and report_lines' 'needs X+, recommended Y+' (about 50 lines over versions.py and suitecheck.py); the start-up log then shows one lin  
  Effort: medium. Id D1-B-10.
- **The hand-off version gate and the raster chain check have no test** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/versions.py:166`  
  Across the four plugins' tests no file calls versions.require (the gate in nd_dam_screening/algorithms/runner_handoff.py:189 and nd_breach_runner/core/handoff.py:90), chain.compare_rasters or chain.manifest_ok (the chain check behind the Hazard submittal), suite_home.installed_plugins, settings.list_presets, settings.apply_defaults, damdata.dam_location or dam_record.inventory_change (0 test files each by name). versions, suitecheck, cascade and chain are each exercised by a single test file (test_suite116.py; Screening test_popup.py for chain). The tests that do exist for versions pin the release literals listed in D1-B-2 and go red every release.  
  What you see: A change to the hand-off gate or to the digest/name/role matching of compare_rasters passes the test suite unnoticed; the only test that watches versions breaks on every release for a reason that is not a bug.  
  Fix: Add to test_suite116.py: require() raising below the hard minimum and passing when the other plugin is absent; compare_rasters on a two-entry manifest giving MATCH, DIFFERENT, NOT IN MANIFEST and MISSING; dam_location from x/y in EPSG:5070; inventory_change before and after a later status. About 60 lines, and replace the literal RELEASE dict at :29 with a read of the four metadata.txt files.  
  Effort: small. Id D1-B-14.
- **xlsx_lite and transformguard have no test of their own; xlsx_lite's docstring claims tests that do not exist** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/xlsx_lite.py:14`  
  No test file in any of the four plugins imports xlsx_lite or openpyxl (grep over all tests/ folders). The module is exercised only indirectly, through damsuite.settings (the settings workbook) in test_popup_013.py and damsuite.priority.write_xlsx in test_window_028.py, neither of which reads an .xlsx back. transformguard.py (85 lines, patches qgis.core.QgsCoordinateTransform at plugin start in all three plugins) has no test at all, and its only observable output, repairs(), is never read (see D2-B-5).  
  What you see: A regression in the workbook reader (every suite setting goes through xlsx_lite.read via settings.py) would show up only as a broken Settings window or silently reset settings; a regression in the transform guard would show up as the 'everything outside the study area' failure it was written to prevent, with nothing in the log.  
  Fix: One round-trip test (write a sheet with None, bool, int, float, text and a 35-character sheet name, read it back, compare) in test_v2290.py or a new test_damsuite file, about 15 lines; delete the docstring sentence or make it true. For transformguard, either log repairs() once at the end of a run (one line in the Runner's workflow report) so a user can see it worked, or accept it is untested and s  
  Effort: small. Id D2-B-11.
- **The settings workbook is truncated before it is rewritten** `ND_Dam_Hazard_Helper/damsuite/xlsx_lite.py:152`  
  write() opens the target file itself in 'w' mode, which empties the existing workbook at once. If writing then fails (disk full, a network share that drops, the computer going to sleep mid-save), the old settings file is already gone and the new one is a broken zip. settings.Settings.save (damsuite/settings.py:365) catches the OSError and reports it, but cannot restore the file. Every other writer in the package writes a side file first and renames it (services.Catalog.save lines 797-800, gpkgpack.write_manifest lines 287-289, suitegpkg.GpkgWriter .part).  
  What you see: A failed save of the suite settings workbook (or priority.write_xlsx) destroys the previous copy; the next load says the file cannot be read and the saved tool settings are lost.  
  Fix: Write to path + '.tmp' and os.replace(tmp, path) after the ZipFile block closes, removing the .tmp on failure, as services.Catalog.save does.  
  Effort: small. Id D2-A-3.
